Unit-tested for the block-boundary and all-same-value-in-a-block (`bitsPerValue == 0`) shapes with hand-built bytes (`varying_bpv_blocks_decoded_across_two_blocks`, `varying_bpv_block_all_same_value`). **Per-field doc-values skip index decode (task #185, this row's prior gap): ported.** A field whose `FieldInfo` carries a non-`None` `doc_values_skip_index_type` now gets its `.dvm` `DocValuesSkipperEntry` (`Lucene90DocValuesProducer.readDocValueSkipperMeta`: offset/length into `.dvs`, min/max value, doc count, max doc id, max value count) parsed into `DocValuesSkipperMeta` (`DocValuesMeta::skippers`, keyed by field number, orthogonal to the field's doc-values *type*) instead of the previous `Error::UnsupportedSkipIndex` rejection, and `parse_skip_index` decodes the full multi-level per-interval structure out of `.dvs` (`Lucene90DocValuesConsumer.writeLevels`'s inverse: a level-count byte then, per level coarsest-first, `maxDocID`/`minDocID`/`maxValue`/`minValue`/`docCount`) into a `DocValuesSkipIndex`/`Vec<SkipIndexInterval>`, eagerly rather than real Lucene's lazy `advance()`-driven read. **Decode-only, not yet wired into a range-query scan** (`search/doc_value_query.rs::search_numeric_range` still does a full sweep — see that row below and the follow-up tracked as "Doc-values skip index in range query scan"); this is a deliberate scope cut, not a gap discovered late. Fixture-verified: `fixtures/src/GenDocValuesSkipIndex.java` writes 36,000 docs to a `NumericDocValuesField.indexedField` field (past both the 4096-doc base-interval size and the 8-interval level-1 grouping threshold, so the fixture has a genuine 2-level interval, not just 1-level rows), and `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/doc_values_skip_index_fixtures.rs` compares the parsed meta and every decoded interval/level against a manifest built by manually walking the same raw `.dvs` bytes in Java (not by re-deriving them from `writeSkipIndex`'s grouping decisions, which this port doesn't reimplement). Unit-tested for truncated/corrupt input: zero and above-`SKIP_INDEX_MAX_LEVEL` level counts, a truncated level body, an out-of-range offset/length, and a `.dvs` header/id mismatch. **M1.6 (performance, not format):** `numeric_value` re-read the per-field jump table and block header for every value, where `Lucene90DocValuesProducer.VaryingBPVReader.getLongValue` opens with `if (this.block != block)` and keeps the decoded block. Two identical timings -- 13.08 ns for 16,384 consecutive in-block reads and 13.09 ns for a block crossing on every call -- were the proof that nothing was cached. New `NumericReader` cursor holds what Lucene's per-field readers hold (the decoded block, plus a sparse field's `IndexedDISI` list decoded once instead of per call): **13.08 -> 8.16 ns on sequential access**, and 0.78x on an all-cache-miss pattern that Lucene's forward-only `DocIdSetIterator` contract cannot express. `numeric_value` is unchanged for single-lookup callers, and the two share one block-header parser. The fixture test asserts they agree document-for-document in forward, backward and strided order. `sorted_ord`/`sorted_numeric_values`/`binary_value` keep the free-function shape; the sparse cost there wants a real `IndexedDISI` cursor rather than an owner each -- see `docs/sweep/findings.md`. **m2 sweep (b6-docvalues)**: two gaps closed against `Lucene90DocValuesProducer`. (1) `inferMaxValueCounts` -- a `.dvm` older than `VERSION_SKIPPER_MAX_VALUE_COUNT` stores no `maxValueCount`, and Java post-processes the skipper entries to fill in `1` for a type that is single-valued by construction (NUMERIC/SORTED) or by its own count equality (SORTED_NUMERIC/SORTED_SET with `numValues == numDocsWithField`); this port left every such entry at the `-1` "unknown" sentinel, so a caller could never take a single-valued fast path on an older segment. Now ported as `infer_max_value_counts`. (2) `DocValuesSkipper` itself -- `parse_skip_index` decoded the intervals but there was no cursor with Java's `advance` semantics, so the decoded index could not actually be used to skip. `DocValuesSkipper` now reproduces `getSkipper`'s exact state machine: per-level arrays that **persist across intervals** (a plain level-0 interval leaves the coarser levels holding the last interval that carried them, which is what makes the `while (levels < MAX && maxDocID[levels] >= target) levels++` widening meaningful), a level's `maxDocID` stored *before* it is tested, and a bail-out at level `L` jumping the whole `8^L`-interval subtree (Java's `SKIP_INDEX_JUMP_LENGTH_PER_LEVEL[L]` byte jump is exactly that subtree's byte size) rather than walking it. Also `advance(long, long)` as `advance_range`. Verified against the real 36,000-doc `.dvs` fixture: the skipper walks all 9 intervals, and every interval's own `.dvd` values are asserted to fall inside its level-0 min/max with an exact `docCount` match, plus a unit test whose covered intervals carry an impossible `max_doc_id` so a linear scan (rather than a subtree jump) fails. Also: `.dvs` now rejects a `VERSION_START` header, matching Java's `checkIndexHeader(skipIn, ..., VERSION_SKIPPER_SEPARATE_FILE, ...)` -- a `.dvs` file does not exist before version 1. |
| `util/packed/DirectReader`/`DirectWriter` (bit-packed integer array read + write) | `lucene-codecs/src/direct_reader.rs` | **both directions ported**, as **one generic bit-position formula** each (`get`/`encode`), not Java's thirteen width-specialized `DirectPackedReaderN`/encode-path classes — those exist for JIT monomorphism, a concern this port doesn't have yet. `encode`'s output round-trips through `get` for every `DirectWriter`-supported width (unit-tested); `padding_bytes_needed`/`unsigned_bits_required` (`DirectWriter.paddingBytesNeeded`/`unsignedBitsRequired`) are also ported, needed for wire-compatible writer output even though this port's own bounds-checked `get` never needs the padding itself. **M1.6:** `get` now issues a single 8-byte little-endian load whenever eight bytes are in range -- which is what `DirectWriter`'s padding is for, and what Java's width-specialized classes each do with one `readInt`/`readLong`. It previously assembled the word one byte at a time, so the read got linearly slower with the width; measured at **0.82x -> 1.82x of Lucene** (median over all fourteen widths) and now flat at ~1.23 ns rather than 1.96-5.25 ns. See `scripts/bench-micro.sh --bench direct_reader` and `docs/sweep/findings.md`. **M2 sweep `b2-packed`:** `get` now rejects a `bits_per_value` outside `DirectWriter.SUPPORTED_BITS_PER_VALUE` (Java's `DirectReader.getInstance` throws for the same set), where it previously computed `1u64 << bits_per_value` -- a debug-build panic and a silently masked shift in release. Reachable from disk via `doc_values`' varying-bits-per-value block header. |
| `util/packed/DirectMonotonicReader`/`DirectMonotonicWriter` (monotonic sequence read/write, blocks of min/avg/bit-packed-delta) | `lucene-codecs/src/direct_monotonic.rs` | **both directions ported.** Read side used by BINARY doc values' variable-length address blocks, SORTED_NUMERIC/SORTED_SET's multi-value address ranges, and (structurally, values discarded) by the terms dictionary. Write side (`write`) returns owned `(meta_bytes, data_bytes)` rather than streaming to an `IndexOutput` (no on-disk `IndexOutput` yet); round-trip-tested through the read side for linear, irregular, single-value, and empty sequences, plus one hand-verified case matching Java's exact `flush()` arithmetic. **M2 sweep `b2-packed`:** `Meta` now holds one array of per-block structs instead of Java's four parallel arrays (one bounds check and one cache line per `get`, not four of each), `load_meta` rejects a block `bitsPerValue` outside `DirectWriter`'s supported set the way Java's eager `DirectReader.getInstance` per block does, an out-of-range index is an error rather than a slice-index panic, and the final `min + avg*i + delta` wraps like Java's `long` arithmetic instead of panicking on overflow. Net **72.3 us -> 55.4 us (-23%)** on `direct_monotonic/get_block` (`benches/hot_paths.rs`) -- most of it from replacing eager `ok_or(Error::Eof{..})` with `ok_or_else`, since `lucene_store::Error` is 32 bytes and was being materialized on every happy-path lookup. Still not ported: `binarySearch`/`getBounds` (bounds-first search that dodges page faults) and `Meta.SINGLE_ZERO_BLOCK` (all-zero metadata collapses to one shared block) |
| `codecs/lucene90/Lucene90DocValuesProducer.TermsDict` (ordinal → term bytes, 64-term LZ4-compressed blocks + prefix compression) | `lucene-codecs/src/terms_dict.rs` | ported as **a forward cursor plus a materializer over it**: `TermsCursor::open`/`next_term` walks the dictionary one term at a time into a reused buffer (`TermsDict.next()`), and `decode_all_terms` is that cursor collected. Still not Java's *seekable* `TermsEnum` — the block-address array and reverse index exist purely to support random seeking, which neither a walk nor a one-shot decode needs; both are parsed structurally (to keep the `.dvm` cursor aligned) but their values are discarded, same trade-off as `IndexedDISI` and stored fields. Noted in passing during the doc-values write-side slice: this file's `cargo llvm-cov` **region** coverage sits at 92.15% (below this project's 95% bar), though its **line** coverage (96.27%, the metric the pre-commit gate actually enforces via `--fail-under-lines`) clears it — pre-existing, not introduced by that slice; worth closing the region gap in a future pass but not gating anything today. | **M2 sweep (b4-fst-blocktree).** `read_term_dict_entry` matches `Lucene90DocValuesProducer.readTermDict` field for field. `decode_all_terms` gained three corrupt-input guards where Java throws and this port panicked or aborted: a prefix length longer than the previous term (`previous[..prefix_len]` panicked), a first-term length past the terms region (`vec![0u8; n]` on a negative `vint` aborts), and an unbounded block-body `vint` (the shift overflowed on the sixth byte, where `DataInput.readVInt` throws "too many bits"). Also dropped the per-term `term.clone()` -- every term was allocated and copied twice -- and the block body's second allocation, so the decode is now one `Vec` per term rather than two plus a copy.
| `codecs/lucene90/Lucene90NormsConsumer` (write side) | `lucene-codecs/src/norms.rs::write_single_dense_field` | **write path started -- scoped to exactly one shape: a single norms field, DENSE (every doc `0..maxDoc` has a value, no `IndexedDISI` sparse structure), at most 1 byte per doc** -- the `numDocsWithValue == maxDoc` branch of `addNormsField` feeding `numBytesPerValue`'s `0` (all values equal, `min >= max`, Java's constant-value encoding) and `1` (`Byte.MIN_VALUE..=Byte.MAX_VALUE`) cases only; a range needing 2/4/8 bytes returns `WriteError::RangeTooWide` rather than silently truncating. Returns `(meta_bytes, data_bytes)` — norms have no third (`.dvs`-style) file, unlike doc values. **Explicitly deferred, not silently missing**: sparse fields (`IndexedDISI.writeBitSet`, the `numDocsWithValue != maxDoc` branch), 2/4/8-byte-per-doc widths, and multiple fields in one `.nvm`/`.nvd` pair — all separate future slices. Unit-tested by round-tripping through this port's own existing reader (`parse_meta`/`norm_value`/`check_data_header_footer`): varying signed-byte values, an all-equal-values (constant-encoding) set, the non-dense-input rejection path (`WriteError::NotDense`), and the range-too-wide rejection path (`WriteError::RangeTooWide`). **Verified against real Lucene in the write→read direction**: `crates/lucene-codecs/examples/write_norms_fixture.rs` writes two segments and `fixtures/src/VerifyNorms.java` opens each directly through `Lucene90NormsFormat.normsProducer` (hand-built `SegmentInfo`/`FieldInfos`, no `.si`/`.fnm` writer needed), confirming every doc's value matches the manifest via real `NumericDocValues.nextDoc`/`longValue` iteration: `_0` (varying small signed values, the real `bytesPerNorm == 1` path) and `_1` (all-equal values, the `bytesPerNorm == 0` constant path, added from the start per the doc-values write-side review's finding rather than shipping it unverified against real Lucene) — both passed on the first real-Lucene run. **Now wired into `IndexWriter`'s normal indexing chain** (`crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::set_norms_field`/`build_norms_output`): before this, `write_single_dense_field` was only ever exercised directly by `merge.rs`'s merge-side tests and this module's own unit tests — a caller building an index through `IndexWriter::add_document`/`commit()` had no way to get norms written at all. `set_norms_field(Some(field_name))` opts one field (single-field-per-commit, same scope as `set_doc_values_field`) into automatic norm computation: each pending doc's field length is derived from `crate::indexing_chain::invert_documents`'s own per-term occurrence counts (summed across all terms for that doc — real Lucene's `FieldInvertState.length`, total token count not distinct-term count), then encoded with `lucene_util::small_float::int_to_byte4` — the exact inverse of this port's own existing `lucene_search::similarity::decode_norm` read-side (`byte4_to_int`), not re-derived from Java source. No caller-supplied norm values are involved; `commit()` computes them from actual indexed text. Still scoped: one field per commit (no `add_norms_field` yet), and a doc missing the field gets a `0`-length norm rather than being excluded (the writer's output is always dense). End-to-end proven by `crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::commit_with_norms_field_writes_readable_length_dependent_norms_for_multiple_docs` — three docs with different body-field token counts, added via plain `add_document`/`commit()`, decode to three different, exactly-correct norm bytes through the unmodified reader stack. See `PLAN.md`'s "Wire norms computation into the `IndexWriter` indexing chain" entry for the full gap analysis and what this does/doesn't unblock for postings-writer competitive-impact computation **m2 sweep (b6-docvalues): the three deferred items above are now ported, and `write_single_dense_field` is a wrapper over the general `norms::write_fields`.** (1) All five of `numBytesPerValue`'s widths (constant, 1, 2, 4, 8 bytes per doc) instead of constant-and-1-byte-only; `WriteError::RangeTooWide` is gone. (2) **Sparse fields** (`write_single_sparse_field`): the `numDocsWithValue != maxDoc` branch, with an `indexed_disi`-backed docs-with-field structure and a rank-indexed (not doc-id-indexed) value array. Two shapes degenerate on purpose, branching on the *count* exactly like `addNormsField` rather than on how the caller phrased it -- an empty list writes the `-2` "no document has this field" marker, and a list covering every doc writes the `-1` dense marker. (3) **Several fields in one `.nvm`/`.nvd` pair** (`write_fields`), which is what every real multi-normed-field segment looks like. All three are verified against real Lucene: `write_norms_fixture.rs` now writes seven segments (`_0` 1-byte, `_1` constant, `_2` 2-byte, `_3` 4-byte, `_4` 8-byte, `_5` sparse with a leading, interior and trailing gap, `_6` three fields -- dense/sparse/constant -- interleaved into one pair) and `VerifyNorms.java` iterates each field through real `NumericDocValues`, checking that docs the manifest marks absent really are skipped. **m2 sweep (`c35-norms-and-sort`)**: the `IndexWriter`-side wording above is history -- `set_norms_field`/`add_norms_field` are gone and norms are written for every indexed non-`omitNorms` field, sparse where a document does not carry the field. See the `index/IndexingChain` norms row below. |
| `codecs/lucene90/IndexedDISI.writeBitSet` (sparse norms/doc-values write side) | `lucene-codecs/src/indexed_disi.rs::{write, write_with_dense_rank_power, create_rank, add_jumps}` | **ported, complete.** All three block shapes, the DENSE rank table at `DEFAULT_DENSE_RANK_POWER`, and (since `c39-codecs-readpath`) the trailing block jump table with `writeBitSet`'s `short` return value. See the `IndexedDISI` row above for the evidence in both directions. |
| `codecs/lucene90/Lucene90DocValuesConsumer` (write side) | `lucene-codecs/src/doc_values.rs::{write_single_dense_numeric_field, write_single_dense_binary_field, write_single_dense_sorted_numeric_field, write_single_dense_sorted_field, write_single_dense_sorted_set_field}` | **write path covers four of the five doc-values kinds now (task #77 added BINARY and SORTED_NUMERIC to task #11's original NUMERIC-only write side, task #99 added SORTED, an earlier task added SORTED_SET) -- all four share the same scope: DENSE only** (every doc `0..maxDoc`/every doc for SORTED_NUMERIC has at least one value, no `IndexedDISI` sparse structure), **no varying-bits-per-value blocks.** `write_single_dense_numeric_field`/`write_numeric_values_body` (this task): the per-value encoding choice (plain delta, GCD-delta, or table compression) now matches `Lucene90DocValuesConsumer.writeValues` exactly -- a running GCD of every value's offset from the first value seen (with Java's own `[i64::MIN/2, i64::MAX/2]` overflow guard), a `<=256`-entry distinct-value set (abandoned past that cap, same as Java's `uniqueValues`), then table compression wins only if it needs strictly fewer bits than GCD/plain-delta (`unsignedBitsRequired(uniqueValues.len() - 1) < unsignedBitsRequired((max - min) / gcd)`); the `min >= max` (all-equal) case is still handled as `bitsPerValue = 0` constant encoding. The on-disk distinguishing fields are exactly Java's: the `tableSize` i32 in the meta (`-1` = no table, `>= 0` = table entry count followed by that many `i64` table values) and the `gcd` i64 right after `bitsPerValue`/`min` (`1` = no GCD scaling, matching plain delta). No new read-side work was needed -- `read_numeric_entry`/`decode_value` already fully decoded both table and GCD entries (ported read-only, real-Lucene-fixture-verified, before this task's write-side work existed). `write_single_dense_binary_field`: fixed-length (`ordinal * length` indexing) and variable-length (a `direct_monotonic::write`-backed end-offset array, `block_shift = 0`, same simplicity-over-ratio choice `term_vectors.rs`/`stored_fields.rs`'s own monotonic writers already made) — an empty-length value is a present value, not "no value". `write_single_dense_sorted_numeric_field`: flattens every doc's values into one shared array (reusing the NUMERIC-entry encoder verbatim via the newly-extracted `write_dense_numeric_entry_body` helper) plus a per-doc address range, **except** when every doc has exactly one value, where it must (not may) omit the address array entirely to match what `read_sorted_numeric_entry` infers from `num_docs_with_field == numeric.num_values` — that equality isn't a stored flag, so writing addresses when it holds would desync the two sides. All three functions return `(meta_bytes, data_bytes, skip_index_bytes)`, same shape, and always emit a valid (if empty) `.dvs` skip-index file. **SORTED now has a write side too (this task)**: `write_single_dense_sorted_field` builds the sorted, deduplicated distinct-value dictionary from the field's raw per-doc bytes (`Vec<Vec<u8>>` in, `sort_unstable`+`dedup`), maps each doc to its term's ordinal (written as a plain dense NUMERIC entry via the same `write_dense_numeric_entry_body` the other three functions share — matches real Lucene's `doAddSortedField`, which always wraps a `SortedDocValues` as a singleton `SortedNumericDocValues` and so never needs an address array), then writes the dictionary itself via a new `write_terms_dict` helper — a from-scratch port of `Lucene90DocValuesConsumer.addTermsDict`/`writeTermsIndex` (64-term LZ4-compressed prefix-compressed blocks, a `DirectMonotonicWriter`-backed block-address array, and the coarser every-1024th-ordinal reverse index with `StringHelper.sortKeyLength`-style disambiguating prefixes) that real Lucene shares byte-for-byte between `addSortedField` and `addSortedSetField`, so it's already positioned to serve a future SORTED_SET writer unchanged. `write_terms_dict` uses `lz4::compress` (no preset-dictionary variant, unlike real Lucene's `compressWithDictionary`) on each block's prefix-compressed body alone rather than the first term acting as a preset dictionary — still format-compatible, since the decompressor only ever *permits* back-references into the dictionary region, never requires them, and the dictionary bytes are already present verbatim right before the compressed body starts (see `write_terms_dict`'s own doc comment). Both writer's `DirectMonotonicWriter` block shift is fixed at `0` (real Lucene always uses `16`), the same simplicity-over-compression-ratio choice `write_single_dense_binary_field`'s address array already made — the format stores `block_shift` per-field, so any value round-trips correctly through this port's own reader. **SORTED_SET now has a write side too (this task)**: `write_single_dense_sorted_set_field` builds the dictionary from every value of every doc (not one value per doc, since a doc can carry more than one), sorted and deduped the same way `write_single_dense_sorted_field` does, then writes each doc's ordinals following `write_single_dense_sorted_numeric_field`'s exact collapse rule: when every doc ends up with exactly one distinct value it writes a plain `SortedEntry` (`multiValued = 0` flag byte, per-doc ordinal via `write_dense_numeric_entry_body`, no address array), otherwise a true multi-valued form (`multiValued = 1`, flattened ordinals plus a `direct_monotonic`-backed address-range array), matching what `read_sorted_set_entry` actually checks (the `multiValued` flag byte, not a count equality like SORTED_NUMERIC's read side uses). The dictionary itself goes through the same `write_terms_dict` helper, unchanged. Docs with zero values are rejected with `WriteError::EmptyMultiValuedDoc`, same contract as SORTED_NUMERIC. **Explicitly still deferred for the four kinds that do have a write side** (see the module's own doc comments): sparse fields (`IndexedDISI`-backed docs-with-value) for BINARY/SORTED/SORTED_NUMERIC/SORTED_SET (NUMERIC sparse writing exists, see below), the varying-bits-per-value block split (`doBlocks`), per-field doc-values skip indexes (`writeSkipIndex`/`.dvs` content), (multiple fields in one `.dvm`/`.dvd`/`.dvs` triple is no longer deferred: `write_dense_fields` writes any number of distinct dense fields, of the same or different DocValuesTypes, into one container, and the five `write_single_dense_*_field` functions are now thin one-field wrappers over it -- see its own doc comment and unit tests in `doc_values.rs`; `IndexWriter::commit()` and `lucene-index/src/merge.rs`'s merge path do not yet call it with more than one field each, a documented follow-up, not a format limitation). GCD compression and table compression are no longer deferred -- both are now chosen automatically by `write_numeric_values_body` (see above). **Now wired into `IndexWriter::commit()` for all five DocValuesTypes -- NUMERIC, BINARY, SORTED, SORTED_NUMERIC, and SORTED_SET** (SORTED wiring added by task #99, SORTED_SET wiring by the following task, BINARY/SORTED_NUMERIC wiring added by this task): `IndexWriter::set_doc_values_field(Some(field_name))` (`crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs`) opts a writer into calling `write_single_dense_numeric_field`/`write_single_dense_binary_field`/`write_single_dense_sorted_field`/`write_single_dense_sorted_numeric_field`/`write_single_dense_sorted_set_field` (dispatched on `DocValuesFieldConfig::doc_values_type`, cached at `set_doc_values_field` time) for exactly one field of every segment `commit()` flushes. NUMERIC sources per-doc `i64` values from `FieldValue::Int`/`FieldValue::Long`; BINARY sources per-doc raw bytes from `FieldValue::String` (UTF-8 bytes) or `FieldValue::Binary`, one value per doc, via the new `build_binary_doc_values_output` (same accepted-value shape as SORTED, but no terms dictionary/dedup/ordinals -- BINARY stores every doc's bytes verbatim); SORTED sources per-doc raw bytes the same way via `build_sorted_doc_values_output`; SORTED_NUMERIC sources per doc *every* `StoredField` entry carrying that field's number as `FieldValue::Int`/`FieldValue::Long` (a doc opts into multiple values by repeating the field), via the new `build_sorted_numeric_doc_values_output`; SORTED_SET sources per doc *every* `StoredField` entry carrying that field's number as raw bytes (same repeated-field convention, real Lucene's own `SortedSetDocValuesField`/`SortedNumericDocValuesField` convention), via `build_sorted_set_doc_values_output`. **All five now dispatch dense vs. sparse per field, per commit (follow-up task)**: each `build_*_doc_values_output` helper collects only the docs that actually supplied a value; if every pending doc has one, it calls the existing dense writer unchanged (byte-for-byte identical output to before this task); if some but not all do, it calls the corresponding `write_single_sparse_*_field` (all five of which already existed in `doc_values.rs` from earlier tasks, just previously unreachable from `IndexWriter`) with only the present `(doc_id, value)` pairs; if none do, it's still `Error::MissingDenseDocValue` (nothing to encode) -- so this is strictly an addition, not a replacement, of the old dense-only behavior. A wrong-typed value still fails the whole commit with `Error::NonNumericDocValue` (NUMERIC/SORTED_NUMERIC) or `Error::NonBinaryDocValue` (BINARY/SORTED/SORTED_SET), same "atomic, not best-effort" contract as before. A doc with an explicit empty/zero value is still present, not absent -- absence means the doc never supplied the field's `StoredField` entry at all. Tested for all five types (`commit_writes_sparse_{numeric,binary,sorted,sorted_numeric,sorted_set}_doc_values_when_a_pending_doc_has_no_value`), each round-tripping the middle of three docs as correctly absent (`None`/empty, not a stale or zero value) through the existing unmodified sparse reader. **Known limitation, pre-existing and unrelated to sparse support**: `doc_values_field` is `Option<DocValuesFieldConfig>` (singular), so a "one field dense, another field on the same docs sparse" mixed-commit scenario isn't expressible yet -- only one doc-values field can be configured per `IndexWriter` at a time. **This task is IndexWriter wiring only, not new codec logic**: `write_single_dense_binary_field`/`write_single_dense_sorted_numeric_field` themselves (the on-disk byte encoding) were already implemented and already had real-Lucene fixture verification from an earlier task (see the `write_doc_values_fixture.rs`/`VerifyDocValues.java` paragraph below, segments `_3`-`_6`) -- this task only adds the `IndexWriter::set_doc_values_field`/`build_doc_values_output` call sites and `IndexWriter`-level tests (add_document/commit/read-back through the existing reader) for those two already-verified write functions; no new real-Lucene fixture was added by this task. **Merge-policy interaction, applied proactively from the postings/term-vector features' own review findings**: `execute_merge`/`merge_stored_only_segments` have no `.dvd`/`.dvm`/`.dvs` awareness either, so `segment_stats()` also excludes any segment whose `.si` lists a `.dvd` file from `find_merges`' candidate pool, covered by `segments_with_doc_values_are_never_automatically_merged_away`. **Postings + term vectors + doc values together, tested and found to work correctly**: all three setters can be configured at once (to different fields in the same commit) -- each builds its own in-memory output before `dir` is touched, and each `.si`-patching helper reads back whatever the others have already written rather than clobbering it, so a segment ends up with all ten files correctly listed; covered by `postings_term_vectors_and_doc_values_configured_together_all_write_correctly`. Required end-to-end proof: `commit_with_doc_values_field_writes_readable_numeric_values_for_multiple_docs`/`commit_with_doc_values_field_writes_readable_binary_values_for_multiple_docs`/`commit_with_doc_values_field_writes_readable_sorted_values_for_multiple_docs`/`commit_with_doc_values_field_writes_readable_sorted_numeric_values_for_multiple_docs`/`commit_with_doc_values_field_writes_readable_sorted_set_values_for_multiple_docs` (`lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs`) add documents via `IndexWriter::add_document`/`commit()` and read the written `.dvm`/`.dvd` back via the existing unmodified `lucene_codecs::doc_values::{parse_meta, numeric_value, binary_value, sorted_ord, sorted_numeric_values}`/`terms_dict::decode_all_terms`, asserting the exact per-document values (SORTED/SORTED_SET: resolved ordinal(s) -> term bytes) come back; `commit_with_doc_values_field_rejects_non_binary_sorted_value`/`commit_with_doc_values_field_rejects_non_binary_binary_value` cover `Error::NonBinaryDocValue`, and `commit_with_doc_values_field_rejects_non_numeric_sorted_numeric_value`/`commit_with_doc_values_field_rejects_doc_with_no_sorted_numeric_values` cover `Error::NonNumericDocValue`/`Error::MissingDenseDocValue` for the SORTED_NUMERIC wiring added by this task. Unit-tested by round-tripping through this port's own existing, unmodified reader (`parse_meta`/`numeric_value`/`binary_value`/`sorted_numeric_values`/`sorted_ord`/`terms_dict::decode_all_terms`/`check_data_header_footer`): NUMERIC -- a mixed-magnitude value set, an all-equal-values (constant-encoding) set, negative/large values, and the non-dense-input rejection path (`WriteError::NotDense`); BINARY -- fixed-length, variable-length (including an empty-string value), and the non-dense-input rejection path; SORTED_NUMERIC -- varying per-doc value counts (1-3 values/doc), the all-single-valued collapse case (confirms no address array gets written), the all-same-value case (confirms the flattened array still gets constant-value encoding), and the empty-per-doc-value rejection path (`WriteError::EmptyMultiValuedDoc`); SORTED (task #99) -- a small dictionary with repeated values (confirms dedup + ordinal reuse), an all-docs-share-one-value set (confirms constant-ordinal encoding), an empty-`Vec<u8>` value (a present, distinct value, not "no value"), a 300-distinct-term dictionary reversed into descending input order (exercises 4 full + 1 partial 64-term LZ4 block; only the first, `ord == 0` reverse-index sample fires at this size), a separate 2200-distinct-term dictionary that crosses two 1024-ordinal reverse-index sample boundaries (exercising both the `sampled_previous` capture and a non-first sample's `common_prefix_len` computation, which the 300-term case doesn't reach), and the non-dense-input rejection path; SORTED_SET (this task) -- a small dictionary shared across docs with varying, overlapping value sets, an all-docs-single-value set (confirms the collapse to a plain `SortedEntry` with no address array), varying per-doc value counts (1-3 values/doc), intra-doc duplicate raw values collapsing to one ordinal reference per doc, a 2200-distinct-term dictionary reversed into descending input order that crosses many 64-term LZ4 blocks and two non-trivial 1024-ordinal reverse-index sample boundaries (matching SORTED's own large-dictionary term count -- a smaller count such as 2000 only reaches the first non-trivial sample, never the second), the empty-per-doc-value rejection path (`WriteError::EmptyMultiValuedDoc`), and the non-dense-input rejection path. **Verified against real Lucene in the write→read direction, all five DocValuesTypes now (task #11 covered NUMERIC only; this task extends to BINARY/SORTED_NUMERIC/SORTED/SORTED_SET)**: `crates/lucene-codecs/examples/write_doc_values_fixture.rs` writes **ten** segments and `fixtures/src/VerifyDocValues.java` opens each directly through `Lucene90DocValuesFormat.fieldsProducer` (hand-built `SegmentInfo`/`FieldInfos`, no `.si`/`.fnm` writer needed), dispatching per segment on a `<segment>.type` manifest key to the matching production-facing read API -- `NumericDocValues`, `BinaryDocValues`, `SortedNumericDocValues`, `SortedDocValues`, or `SortedSetDocValues` -- and confirming every doc's value(s) match the manifest: `_0`/`_1`/`_2` are the original NUMERIC segments (mixed values, the min-shift-drop regression case, and the all-equal constant-encoding case); `_3`/`_4` are BINARY (fixed-length direct addressing, and variable-length including an empty value via the `DirectMonotonicReader` address block); `_5`/`_6` are SORTED_NUMERIC (every doc exactly one value, the address-array-collapse case, and 1-3 values/doc forcing the real address-range array); `_7` is SORTED (repeated terms over a 3-term dictionary, exercising both the ordinal decode and the terms-dict decode); `_8`/`_9` are SORTED_SET (every doc one distinct value, the `multiValued = false` collapse case, and 1-2 distinct values/doc sharing a dictionary including a doc whose raw values dedup to one ordinal). All ten passed on the first real-Lucene run. **Scope note**: `_7`'s SORTED and `_8`/`_9`'s SORTED_SET dictionaries are deliberately small (3 terms), so this real-Lucene verification exercises `write_terms_dict`'s single-64-term-block path only -- it does **not** reach the multi-LZ4-block / multi-1024-ordinal-reverse-index-sample paths a large dictionary forces (those remain covered only by the 2200-term unit tests against this port's own reader, described above; no fixture has yet forced real Lucene to open a multi-block dictionary this port wrote). **Sparse NUMERIC write support added (this task)**: `write_single_sparse_numeric_field(field_number, doc_values: &[(doc_id, value)], max_doc, ...)` is the first write-side function in this module that doesn't require every doc to have a value. It builds the `IndexedDISI`-backed docs-with-field structure via the new `indexed_disi::write` (see that module's own doc comment) and reuses the exact same per-rank value encoding (`write_numeric_values_body`, factored out of what was `write_dense_numeric_entry_body`'s tail) that dense NUMERIC already uses -- a sparse field's value array is indexed by rank-among-present-docs, exactly what the pre-existing read side (`numeric_value`'s sparse branch, `indexed_disi::rank_of`) already expects, since that reader has supported sparse NUMERIC/BINARY/SORTED_NUMERIC since task #4/#21. **Scope, deliberately narrow**: NUMERIC only -- BINARY/SORTED/SORTED_NUMERIC/SORTED_SET sparse writing is still deferred (the shared `write_numeric_values_body`/`indexed_disi::write` machinery would extend to them the same way `write_dense_numeric_entry_body` already does for dense, but wiring that up for the other four kinds, plus `IndexWriter` integration, is left for a future task). `write_dense_numeric_entry_body` and all four other write functions are completely unmodified in behavior (`write_single_dense_numeric_field_still_dense_after_sparse_addition` pins its output byte-for-byte identical to before this change). **Block-shape coverage**: `indexed_disi::write` picks a shape per 65536-doc block by density, exactly like real `IndexedDISIBuilder` -- `<= 4095` present docs -> SPARSE (explicit low-16-bit doc ids), `== 65536` -> ALL (no data), otherwise -> DENSE (a 65536-bit bitset); all three are exercised by `write_single_sparse_numeric_field_uses_all_three_block_shapes` (block 0 forced SPARSE with 10 docs, block 1 forced ALL with every doc present, block 2 forced DENSE at 50% density). Deliberately **not** written: the DENSE rank table (`denseRankPower` is always `0xFF`/no-rank, matching every other sparse entry this port already writes) and the trailing jump table -- both pure random-access speedups the existing whole-structure-decode reader doesn't use. Unit-tested by round-tripping through this port's own existing reader (`parse_meta`/`numeric_value`): `write_single_sparse_numeric_field_round_trips_through_own_reader` (200,000 docs, every 3rd present, forcing multiple DENSE blocks) and `write_single_sparse_numeric_field_uses_all_three_block_shapes` (above) both check a stride sample of docs rather than every doc, since `numeric_value` re-decodes the whole `IndexedDISI` structure per call (see `indexed_disi.rs`'s doc comment) and checking all ~200K docs individually would be quadratic; `write_single_sparse_numeric_field_rejects_duplicate_doc_id`/`_rejects_out_of_range_doc_id` cover the two new `WriteError` variants (`DocIdsNotAscending`, `DocIdOutOfRange`). **Verified against real Lucene in the write→read direction (follow-up task)**: `crates/lucene-codecs/examples/write_sparse_numeric_doc_values_fixture.rs` writes two segments (`_0`: 20 docs with values missing interspersed throughout, not just trailing; `_1`: 200,000 docs, 1 of every 3 present, the same DENSE-block-shape case the unit test above covers) and `fixtures/src/VerifySparseNumericDocValues.java` opens each directly through real `Lucene90DocValuesFormat.fieldsProducer` (hand-built `SegmentInfo`/`FieldInfos`, same division of labor as `VerifyDocValues.java`), then calls real `NumericDocValues.advanceExact` for every doc id from `0` to `max_doc - 1` -- not just iterating present docs via `nextDoc()` -- confirming docs with a value return the correct value and docs without one correctly report absent. Both segments passed on the first run. This closes the gap the earlier task's note flagged: `write_single_sparse_numeric_field`'s output is now confirmed genuinely valid, real-Lucene-readable NUMERIC sparse doc-values, not just self-consistent with this port's own reader.  **Skip-index scope revisited (this task), deferral confirmed unchanged**: checked whether this session's multi-field `write_dense_fields`/all-five-type merge/NRT doc-values wiring made real per-field skip-index (`Lucene90DocValuesSkipIndex`) write support tractable. It does not change anything -- every `FieldInfo` constructor in `lucene-index` (`segment_writer.rs`, `update_document.rs`, `index_writer.rs`, `merge.rs`, `check_index.rs`) still hardcodes `doc_values_skip_index_type: DocValuesSkipIndexType::None`, so the writer never even decides a field should carry one; `doc_values.rs`'s shared `finish_field_list_and_footers` still emits only an index-header-plus-footer stub for every write path; the read side (task #185, after this note was written) now decodes any field claiming one instead of rejecting it, but nothing in `lucene-index`'s writers ever sets a non-`None` `doc_values_skip_index_type` to begin with, so this port's own writer output never carries one to decode; `doc_value_query.rs`'s `search_numeric_range` still does a full sweep regardless. Implementing real skip-index *write* bytes in isolation would still be dead serialization code with no writer ever requesting it -- the same unconsumed-accessor trap flagged elsewhere this session -- so the write side remains deferred; only the decode side changed. **m2 sweep (b6-docvalues)**: three fixes. (1) **`DIRECT_MONOTONIC_BLOCK_SHIFT` is now 16, Java's value, not 0.** Every address array this format writes (BINARY's variable-length end offsets, SORTED_NUMERIC/SORTED_SET's per-doc value ranges, the terms dict's block and reverse-index addresses) was written with a block shift of 0, i.e. one block per value. That round-trips -- the shift is stored per array -- but it made `.dvm` grow *linearly with the value count*: 21 bytes of metadata and one eagerly-materialised `direct_monotonic::Meta` block per address, so a 1M-doc variable-length BINARY field produced ~21 MB of `.dvm` and ~32 MB of heap on open where Java produces ~336 bytes and 16 blocks. Regression-tested by `direct_monotonic_block_shift_keeps_meta_size_constant` (100-doc and 1000-doc fields must produce the same `.dvm` size). (2) **Constant-encoding bytes now match Java exactly**: `writeValues` runs its GCD scan *before* the `min >= max` branch and writes whatever it produced (`0` for an all-equal array, `1` when the overflow guard tripped), and writes `Long.MAX_VALUE` as `min` for an empty array; this port wrote a hardcoded `gcd = 1` and `min = 0`. Neither is read back (`bitsPerValue == 0` short-circuits), but the writer is now byte-identical here. (3) **The terms dictionary is finally verified against real Lucene at a size where its structure is non-trivial.** The write-path fixture's only SORTED/SORTED_SET dictionaries were 3-5 terms -- one LZ4 block, one empty reverse-index sort key -- so `addTermsDict`'s flush-the-previous-block path, `writeTermsIndex`'s real `StringHelper.sortKeyLength` sort keys, and both of the prefix/suffix escape thresholds (`prefixLength >= 15`, `suffixLength >= 16`) had never been read by Java. New segment `_10` is a 1500-term dictionary (24 LZ4 blocks, past `TERMS_DICT_REVERSE_INDEX_SHIFT`), and `VerifyDocValues.java` now also round-trips **every** distinct term through real `SortedDocValues.lookupTerm` -- the only API that exercises the reverse index and block seek, which `lookupOrd` never touches -- plus `getValueCount` and a negative-insertion-point check for an absent term. This is also the first real-Lucene confirmation that this port's LZ4 (which has no preset-dictionary compressor, emitting a plain block where Java emits one compressed against the block's first term) is accepted by Java's `TermsDict.decompressBlock`. **`c17-index-sort` (2026-08-29)**: `write_dense_fields` gained a `DenseField::SparseNumeric` variant -- `(doc_id, value)` pairs written through the same `IndexedDISI` body `write_single_sparse_numeric_field` uses. A multi-tier index sort needs every tier's column in one `.dvm`/`.dvd`, and a sort field whose documents may lack a value is the normal case (`SortField.setMissingValue`), so a dense-only multi-field writer could not express the sorts Lucene routinely writes. `IndexWriter` correspondingly gained `add_doc_values_field`, so a segment can carry more than one doc-values field (previously one per commit). **`c27-arith-codecs-2` (2026-08-29)**: `write_dense_fields` gained the four remaining sparse variants -- `DenseField::{SparseBinary, SparseSorted, SparseSortedNumeric, SparseSortedSet}` -- and the four `write_single_sparse_*` functions became thin wrappers over it, the same shape the five dense ones already had. This closes the deferral noted earlier in this row and unblocks c26's merge finding: `DocValuesConsumer.getMerged*DocValues` drops a source reader that has no column for the field, so an ordinary index that starts writing a doc-values field partway through its life produces a *sparse* column for every one of the five types, and `execute_merge` had to fail such a merge outright for the four non-NUMERIC ones. Found while doing it: **none of the four sparse writers emitted Java's `-1` dense docs-with-field marker**. `Lucene90DocValuesConsumer` writes it whenever `numDocsWithField == maxDoc` (`addBinaryField` inline, the other four through `writeValues`); this port wrote a full `IndexedDISI` region for a complete column, which round-trips through its own reader -- which is exactly why nothing caught it -- but is not a byte sequence real Lucene ever produces. Now shared in `write_docs_with_field`, and pinned by `a_sparse_column_that_covers_every_document_writes_javas_dense_marker`, which asserts the sparse and dense writers emit identical bytes. Still deliberately divergent: a sparse SORTED_SET never collapses to `multiValued = 0` when every present doc has exactly one ordinal (a size optimization real Lucene applies and `SortedSetKind::Multi` does not require). |
| `codecs/lucene90/Lucene90CompoundFormat`/`Lucene90CompoundReader` (`.cfs`/`.cfe` read) | `lucene-codecs/src/compound_format.rs` | ported (read-only), fixture-verified against a real `useCompoundFile=true` IndexWriter segment (12 packed sub-files); preserves Java's exact-version cross-check between `.cfs`/`.cfe` and the total-length-vs-entries-table sanity check  **M2 sweep (b3-compression).** Compared method-for-method against `Lucene90CompoundReader`/`Lucene90CompoundFormat`; the `.cfe` entry table, the exact-version cross-check, the max-extent-plus-footer length check and the 64-byte start alignment all agree. Fixed: the expected-length computation added `offset + length` straight off a possibly corrupt `.cfe`, which is a debug-build overflow panic in Rust where Java's `long` silently wraps -- now saturating, so it lands in the existing `WrongLength` error, with tests for an overflowing extent and a negative offset. Java's `Directory`-shaped surface (`listAll`, `fileLength`, `getPendingDeletions`, `checkIntegrity`) is deliberately not ported: this port exposes `names()`/`FileEntry`/`open_input` instead of a read-only `Directory` implementation. See `docs/sweep/m2/b3-compression.md`. |
| `codecs/lucene90/Lucene90CompoundFormat.write` | `lucene-codecs/src/compound_format.rs::write` | **write path ported**, given already-written sub-files as `(stripped_id, complete_bytes)` pairs -- each sub-file must already be a complete standalone codec file (its own `IndexHeader` sharing the segment's id, a body, and its own `Footer`), exactly what this port's other write-side modules (`field_infos::write`, `stored_fields::write_best_speed`, etc.) already produce, so no new file-on-disk plumbing was needed to compose them. Verified precisely against Java rather than assumed simple: `Lucene90CompoundFormat.writeCompoundFile` isn't a bare concatenation -- it (1) packs sub-files smallest-first (`Comparator.comparingLong(length)`), (2) pads each sub-file's start offset up to a 64-byte boundary, and (3) "verifies and copies" each sub-file's header (checking the object id matches) then re-derives its footer from the *verified* checksum rather than copying the footer bytes outright -- but since that checksum is definitionally the value already stored in the sub-file's own footer, the net result is byte-identical to a verified verbatim copy, which is what this port does (validate header id + footer checksum, then copy the whole sub-file). No per-sub-file checksumming beyond that verification step; the `.cfs` file's own footer covers the whole packed blob via a single CRC, same as every other framed format in this port. Unit-tested by round-tripping through this port's own existing reader (`parse_entries`/`check_data_header_footer`/`open_input`) with three differently-sized/differently-codec-named sub-files, confirming ascending-size ordering, 64-byte-aligned offsets, and byte-for-byte sub-file recovery, plus rejection of a wrong segment id, a bad magic number, and a corrupt footer checksum. **Verified against real Lucene in the write→read direction**: `crates/lucene-codecs/examples/write_compound_format_fixture.rs` packs a `.fnm` (`field_infos::write`) and a `.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm` triple (`stored_fields::write_best_speed`) -- four distinct sub-files -- into a `_0.cfs`/`_0.cfe` pair, and `fixtures/src/VerifyCompoundFormat.java` opens it through real `Lucene90CompoundFormat.getCompoundReader` with a hand-built `SegmentInfo`, confirms the sub-file list and lengths, then re-decodes the `.fnm` through real `Lucene94FieldInfosFormat` and the `.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm` triple through real `Lucene90StoredFieldsFormat` -- both read *through* the compound reader, not the raw sub-file bytes directly, so a byte-offset bug would fail this check even if the entries table alone looked correct. Passed on the first real-Lucene run. **Wired into the write path (task #38):** `lucene-index/src/segment_writer.rs::flush_stored_only_segment` now actually calls this `write` function when its caller passes `use_compound_file: true`, packing a flushed segment's `.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm`/`.fnm` into `<name>.cfs`/`.cfe` and setting `SegmentInfo.is_compound_file` accordingly, instead of leaving this codec's write half unreferenced by any writer. See that row below for the wiring's own tests. **Write-side edge-case audit (later task):** zero/one/many (200, stressing the `.cfe` entry-count vint's 128-entry width boundary) sub-files, a zero-length sub-file body, unusual/empty/non-ASCII sub-file names, and duplicate-name detection on read were all checked against the actual code -- every case was already handled correctly (no production bug found); only test coverage was missing, now closed by `write_single_sub_file_round_trips`, `write_zero_length_body_sub_file_round_trips`, `write_many_sub_files_stresses_entry_count_vint_boundary`, `write_unusual_sub_file_names_round_trip`, `write_rejects_completely_empty_sub_file_bytes`, and `write_duplicate_sub_file_names_detected_on_read` (see `PLAN.md` for the full per-case writeup). |
| `codecs/lucene90/Lucene90StoredFieldsFormat`/`Lucene90CompressingStoredFieldsReader`/`FieldsIndexReader` (`.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm` read, **both `Mode.BEST_SPEED` and `Mode.BEST_COMPRESSION`**) | `lucene-codecs/src/stored_fields.rs` | ported (read-only), fixture-verified against a real IndexWriter for both modes: BEST_SPEED (6 docs, one field of each of the 6 stored-field types, varying lengths so the chunk uses the bulk multi-doc framing) and BEST_COMPRESSION (same shape, forced via `Lucene104Codec.Mode.BEST_COMPRESSION`, with a long repeated-text field so the DEFLATE dictionary + multi-sub-block path is actually exercised); the mode is detected from the `.fdt` data codec name itself (`...FastData` vs `...HighData`), and `chunk_size` is read from `.fdm` rather than hardcoded, since BEST_COMPRESSION's is ~480KB vs BEST_SPEED's ~80KB **`c27-arith-codecs-2` (2026-08-29)**: `token >>> 2` was ported as a signed `>>` in both `read_chunk_header` and `copy_chunks`, so a corrupt token with its sign bit set gave a *negative* `chunkDocs` where Java gets a large positive -- and `chunk_docs as usize` on a negative is ~2^64. `chunkDocs` is now additionally capped at the format's `maxDocsPerChunk` (1024/4096, pinned by `Lucene90StoredFieldsFormat.impl` for the two codec names `open` matches); it had been bounded only by `maxDoc`, itself a raw `i32` off `.fdm`, so a 60-byte file could reserve 17 GB -- reproduced as a real SIGABRT under `ulimit -v`, as was `numStoredFields` sizing one `StoredField` slot per *claimed* field (171 GB). Eleven more `.fdm`/`.fdx`/`.fdt` values bounded, including `maxPointer` (whose `as usize + 16` wrapped to 15, so the `.fdt`-length cross-check panicked before it could reject anything), a negative per-document length that made the offsets array *decrease*, and a chunk's claimed decompressed length (now capped by the LZ4/DEFLATE worst-case expansion of the bytes that remain). Re-signed byte-flip sweeps: `.fdm` 269/282, `.fdx` 114/126, `.fdt` 624/904, zero panics. **`c42-readpath-perf`**: `StoredFieldVisitor`/`StoredFieldVisitor.Status` and `DocumentStoredFieldVisitor` are now ported (`stored_fields::{StoredFieldVisitor, VisitStatus, DocumentVisitor}`), with `StoredFieldsReader::visit_document` reproducing `document(int, StoredFieldVisitor)`'s loop including `skipField` and the "don't skipField on the last field value" shortcut; `parse_document`/`document` are that loop run with a `DocumentVisitor::all`, so there is one field-decode path and not two. Values reach the visitor **borrowed** (`&str`/`&[u8]`), so a visitor that does not keep one never allocates it. Java's `FieldInfo` argument is a field *number* here: this reader decodes `.fdt` alone and is never handed a `FieldInfos`. |
| `util/compress/LZ4.decompress`/`LZ4.compressWithDictionary` | `lucene-codecs/src/lz4.rs` | decompress ported (read-only, as before) **and a real compressor is now ported** (`lz4::compress`): a scoped-down port of `compressWithDictionary` with a zero-length dictionary only (preset-dictionary compression is a separate, larger piece -- cross-block dictionary reuse -- and remains unported; `decompress`'s dictionary *read* support is unaffected) and always using the simpler `FastCompressionHashTable` match-finding strategy (a single last-occurrence-per-hash table with a fixed hash-table size) rather than `HighCompressionHashTable`'s hash-chain "try a better match" search (`compressHC`, unported) -- every match this compressor emits is a real, verified back-reference within `MAX_DISTANCE`, just not necessarily the longest one available at each position the way Lucene's own search would find. Verified both by round-trip through this port's own `decompress` (empty input, single byte, below-match-threshold-length, highly repetitive input, pseudo-random incompressible input, matches at the very start/end, and long input forcing the extended-length continuation encoding) and cross-engine (see the `stored_fields.rs::write_best_speed` row below)  **M2 sweep (b3-compression).** Compared method-for-method against `util/compress/LZ4.java`. The compressor is now a **complete** port, not a scoped-down one: `compress_with_dictionary` takes the preset-dictionary window Java does (`dictOff`/`dictLen`, `initDictionary`) and runs the `ht.previous()` "try to find a better match" loop, and **both** hash tables are ported -- `FastCompressionHashTable` (`CompressionMode.FAST`, `LZ4WithPresetDictCompressionMode`) and `HighCompressionHashTable` (`Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter`'s suffix compression, `CompressionMode.FAST_DECOMPRESSION`), the latter with Java's `MAX_ATTEMPTS = 256` chain walk and its two-branch `reset` (partial chain-table wipe below 64kB, full wipe above). The tables are reusable across calls and are never cleared, exactly as Java's are. The previous fixed `2^17`-slot `i64` table (1 MiB allocated and filled *per compress call*) is gone -- sizing now follows Java's `hashLog = MEMORY_USAGE + 3 - bitsPerOffsetLog` (13 below 64kB, 12 above); measured, that table cost 13 us per call on its own, against 71 us for compressing a whole 64kB block. `decompress` copies matches with bulk `copy_within` runs of at most `matchDec` bytes instead of a bounds-checked byte loop (Java's `System.arraycopy`-or-byte-loop split): measured ~2x faster on text (57 us vs 103 us per 64kB) and ~6% on run-length data. `commonBytes` compares 8 bytes at a time, matching what `Arrays.mismatch` intrinsifies to. `lz4` is now a `pub` module (as `LZ4` is a public class in Lucene) with a criterion bench, `crates/lucene-codecs/benches/lz4_codec.rs`. Not ported, and not reachable from any Lucene codec: nothing -- `HashTable.assertReset` is assertion-only. See `docs/sweep/m2/b3-compression.md`. |
| `codecs/lucene90/LZ4WithPresetDictCompressionMode` (dictionary + fixed-size sub-blocks) | `lucene-codecs/src/stored_fields.rs::decompress_unit` | ported as an eager full decode (no partial/lazy read path — this port hands back a materialized `Document`, not a streaming reader); every unit's *compressed*-length vint (the dictionary's, then each block's) is batched up front in this format, before any of the actual compressed bytes -- they exist in Java only to support seeking without decompressing everything before it, so they're read here to keep the cursor aligned and discarded  **M2 sweep (b3-compression).** **The eager full-chunk decode is gone.** `decompress_unit` now takes the `(offset, length)` pair Java's `Decompressor.decompress(in, originalLength, offset, length, bytes)` takes and decompresses only the sub-blocks that range intersects, skipping the rest by their recorded compressed length -- which is what those lengths are recorded for. The dictionary is still always inflated (the sub-blocks back-reference into it), so fetching one document out of a full 80kB BEST_SPEED chunk now costs the dictionary plus one ~8kB sub-block instead of all ten. Both modes' skip paths are covered (`every_document_position_inside_a_multi_sub_block_chunk_reads_back` and its DEFLATE twin), and the real-Lucene-written fixtures still read back byte-identically. Also fixed: a `.fdt` unit header claiming `blockLength == 0` with bytes still to cover used to spin forever in the block-count loop (Java divides by it and throws), and a `dictLength` larger than the unit would underflow the block loop -- both are now `Corrupted`, with tests. See `docs/sweep/m2/b3-compression.md`. |
| `codecs/lucene90/DeflateWithPresetDictCompressionMode` (raw DEFLATE, dictionary + fixed-size sub-blocks) | `lucene-codecs/src/deflate.rs` | decode ported as an eager full decode, same shape as the LZ4 sibling but a real semantic difference caught by a real fixture: DEFLATE isn't self-terminating from an output-length count alone, and (unlike LZ4) each unit's compressed-length vint sits **immediately before its own compressed bytes**, not batched up front -- getting this backwards (by over-generalizing from the LZ4 format) produced a `MalformedVarint` against real Lucene bytes, caught and fixed before commit. Built on `miniz_oxide::inflate::core`'s low-level API (raw deflate, no zlib header/Adler-32) rather than its `decompress_to_vec` convenience wrappers, which don't support a preset dictionary; the preset-dictionary trick is the same one `lz4::decompress` uses -- pre-write the dictionary bytes into the output buffer before `d_off` and let back-references look behind it. **A real DEFLATE compressor is now ported too** (`deflate::compress`): unlike LZ4 (no compress-capable crate was already a dependency, so this port hand-wrote a real greedy-match encoder), `miniz_oxide` -- already a dependency for the decode side -- exposes `miniz_oxide::deflate::compress_to_vec`, a real, already-vetted raw-DEFLATE encoder (no zlib wrapper to strip; that's the sibling `compress_to_vec_zlib`), so this port reuses it directly at level 6 rather than hand-writing Huffman+LZ77 from scratch -- only a *correctly decodable* stream is required, not byte-identical output to Java's own `Deflater`. Fixed a real latent bug while wiring this up: `decompress`'s `decompressed_len == 0` fast path returned without consuming `compressed_len` bytes from the shared reader, silently desyncing every subsequent unit in a chunk whenever a dictionary (or sub-block) happens to be zero-length but still has nonzero *compressed* bytes on the wire (a real DEFLATE stream, even of empty content, still emits a final-block marker) -- caught by `write_best_compression`'s own round-trip tests, not a real-Lucene fixture, but the same bug could in principle have hit a real BEST_COMPRESSION file whose dictionary happens to size out to 0 bytes |
| `codecs/lucene90/compressing/StoredFieldsInts` (bulk per-doc field-count/length arrays) | `lucene-codecs/src/stored_fields.rs::read_bulk_ints`/`write_bulk_ints` | **both directions ported**, `Mode.BEST_SPEED` only for the write side so far. Read: exact on-disk transposed-block layout preserved (values decode identically to Java's SIMD-shaped bulk reader), decoded via a plain per-value loop rather than replicating the bulk/SIMD machinery itself. Write: **complete as of 2026-08-28** -- `write_bulk_ints` is now the exact inverse of `read_bulk_ints`, bit-transposing every whole 128-value block across i64 words and writing only the remainder value-by-value. Previously scalar-tail only, which capped a chunk at 127 documents and so capped the entire write path there (see the `Lucene90CompressingStoredFieldsWriter` row below); round-tripped at 127/128/129/200/256/300 values across all three widths. **Audited 2026-07-17**: only bits-per-value 0/8/16/32 are real -- confirmed against `StoredFieldsInts.java`'s `writeInts`, which only ever emits those four values (constant-fill, or the smallest of 8/16/32 that fits `max`), and whose own `readInts` throws `IOException("Unsupported number of bits per value: " + bpv)` for anything else. This port's `Error::UnsupportedBulkIntWidth` for other byte values is a faithful match of Java's own defensive check, not a gap; left as-is, with `read_bulk_ints_unsupported_width_rejected` widened to sweep the whole invalid space rather than assert a single sentinel value |
| `codecs/lucene104/Lucene104PostingsWriter` + `codecs/lucene103/blocktree/Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter` (write side) | `lucene-codecs/src/postings_writer.rs` | **write path ported; made readable by real Lucene 2026-08-28.** Four defects were live at once, each invisible to this port's own reader and to all thirteen single-format cross-engine checks (see `docs/sweep/findings.md`, O27/O29/O30/O31). (1) The `.psm` metadata file was never written; Lucene fails the segment without it. (2) `.tmd`'s `indexLength`/`termsLength` excluded the footer, where Java writes each footer *then* records `getFilePointer()` and feeds both to `CodecUtil.retrieveChecksum`. (3) A field whose terms spanned more than one leading byte was split into one leaf block per byte under a `SIGN_MULTI_CHILDREN` trie root with no output of its own -- but Lucene's terms enum starts by loading the root *block*, so `loadBlock` got `-1`; two terms (`apple`, `banana`) sufficed. Now every term goes in one leaf block under a single-output root, the shape real Lucene already validates; **scope limit**: no non-leaf blocks with sub-block entries, so term lookup within a field is a block scan rather than a trie descent (filed as the block-tree navigation performance item). (4) The level-0 `numSkipBytes` was written as `0` because this port's reader re-derives that position from `blockLength`; Lucene's `skipLevel0To` *seeks* by it, so a zero sent it backwards and then off the end of the file -- reachable only for `docFreq >= 256`. **Impacts**: this writer emits, per level-0 block and per level-1 span, the competitive `(freq, norm)` frontier `CompetitiveImpactAccumulator` computes (`postings_writer::CompetitiveImpactAccumulator`, ported including the `otherFreqNormPairs` overflow set for norms wider than a byte). `write_fields_with_norms` takes each field's per-document norm column, exactly as `Lucene104PostingsWriter` gets it from the `NormsProducer`; `write_fields` (no norms) is Java's `fieldHasNorms == false` path and still writes the single `(maxFreq, 1)` pair, byte for byte as before. A bound must be an over-estimate -- a loose one costs pruning, a low one drops hits -- which is why the frontier is computed rather than the `(maxFreq, minNorm)` corner. Previously an empty region, which Lucene rejects outright ("Got empty list of impacts on level 0"). `.psm`'s four maxima are computed to match, since Lucene sizes its impact-decoding buffers from them before reading any block. **Verified end to end**: `crates/lucene-index/examples/write_full_segment_fixture.rs` writes 2,500 documents through the real `IndexWriter` (501 terms spanning many leading bytes, one term in every document and so spanning ten postings blocks) and `fixtures/src/VerifyFullSegment.java` opens it with `DirectoryReader`, counts `body:shared` exactly with `IndexSearcher.count`, and runs `CheckIndex` at full level -- 20,000-term and 2,500-document indexes both report no problems  **m2 sweep (c20-postings-skip)**: the `.pos`/`.pay` skip sub-fields of every level-0 block header and level-1 span entry are now emitted (`flushDocBlock`'s `posOut.getFilePointer() - level0LastPosFP` / `posBufferUpto` pair and `writeLevel1SkipData`'s), so the `docFreq < BLOCK_SIZE`-when-positions-are-indexed ceiling (`Error::DocFreqTooLargeForPositions`) is gone -- a positions-indexing term of any `docFreq` now round-trips. This writer builds each file whole rather than interleaving the three as Lucene does, so it lays `.pos`/`.pay` out first and reconstructs the samples Lucene takes live (`PositionLayout`); the flush schedule is arithmetic (one `.pos` block per 256 occurrences, doc-boundary-agnostic), so the reconstruction is exact. Pinned by `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/postings_skip_pointers.rs`, whose expectations come from the whole-term reader that ignores skip data entirely -- a writer-side error cannot cancel against a reader-side one. **Still no cross-engine verifier for the postings/term-dictionary write path** (T3.1); real Lucene has never read this port's skip records. |
| `index/IndexingChain` norms (write side) | `lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::{norms_field_configs, omit_norms_field, build_norms_output}` | **done (m2 sweep `c35-norms-and-sort`).** Norms follow `IndexingChain.writeNorms`' rule exactly: every `FieldInfo` with `omitsNorms() == false && getIndexOptions() != NONE` gets a column, with no opt-in anywhere, and `IndexWriter::omit_norms_field` is the opt-*out* (`FieldType.setOmitNorms(true)`). The column is **sparse**, matching `NormValuesWriter`: `PerField.finish(docID)` runs only for a document that carries the field, and writes an explicit `0` when it carries it but produced no tokens -- so an absent field and an empty one are distinguishable in the `.nvd`, which they were not while the writer was dense-only. Each value is the document's own `FieldInvertState.length` through `small_float::int_to_byte4`, computed from the one shared invert pass. **Verified against real Lucene**: `write_full_segment_fixture` indexes a `title` field *nothing* opts into norms for, with documents that lack it entirely and documents that carry it empty, and `fixtures/src/VerifyFullSegment.java` reads every document's norm back through `MultiDocValues.getNormValues` and compares it to `SmallFloat.intToByte4(tokenCount)`, gaps included. **History**: until c35 this port required a `set_norms_field`/`add_norms_field` call per field and rewrote every other indexed field's `.fnm` entry as `omit_norms: true` -- self-consistent and openable, but a different schema than the caller asked for, and BM25 then scored those fields against a constant length instead of each document's own. |
| `index/IndexingChain` vectors (write side) + `KnnFloatVectorField`/`KnnByteVectorField` + `KnnFieldVectorsWriter` + `PerFieldKnnVectorsFormat` | `lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::{set_vector_field, add_vector_field, set_hnsw_parameters, add_document_with_vectors, DocumentVector, VectorValue, build_vectors_output, write_vector_files}` | **ported (batch c10).** `IndexWriter` can now index vector fields: a caller opts a field in (its `FieldInfo` supplies the dimension, `VectorEncoding` and `VectorSimilarityFunction`, so the `.fnm` and the `.vemf` cannot disagree -- which is what `Lucene99FlatVectorsReader.FieldEntry` cross-checks), adds documents through `add_document_with_vectors`, and each flush writes `<segment>_Lucene99HnswVectorsFormat_0.{vec,vemf,vem,vex}` plus the `PerFieldKnnVectorsFormat.format`/`.suffix` attributes and the four `SegmentInfo.files` entries. Both encodings, all four similarities, dense and sparse fields, and `shouldCreateGraph`'s tiny-segment skip. Vectors live in a buffer parallel to the document buffer rather than inside `stored_fields::Document`, because that type is this port's *stored-fields* document and Java's `KnnFloatVectorField` is not stored. Per-document validation (dimension, encoding, a field named twice, a field not opted in) happens at `add_document_with_vectors` as Java's does in the field constructor; `VectorUtil.checkFinite` runs at flush, where the whole field is visible. A field no document carried has its `.fnm` `vector_dimension` zeroed -- real Lucene raises no error for a field that claims vectors but has no registered format, it simply reads back empty. **Verified end to end**: `crates/lucene-index/examples/write_vector_segment_fixture.rs` writes a 3000-document, four-vector-field index through the real `IndexWriter`, and `fixtures/src/VerifyVectorSegment.java` opens it with `DirectoryReader`, checks every ordinal's components and doc id, runs `KnnFloatVectorQuery`/`KnnByteVectorQuery` against Lucene's own brute-force top-k (recall@10 0.91-1.00; the sub-threshold field, searched exhaustively, matches **exactly**), and runs `CheckIndex` at `MIN_LEVEL_FOR_SLOW_CHECKS` -- `scripts/verify-write-path.sh` 17/17 -> **18/18** with this case. **Measured**: below `HNSW_GRAPH_THRESHOLD` a 128-dim vector per document is free (median 22.0 vs 20.6 us/doc over five interleaved pairs, the vector arm faster in two of them); above it the graph is essentially the whole cost -- 50k documents at dim 16/64/128 cost 85.5/142.9/203.8 us/doc (interleaved medians) against a 25.8 us/doc vector-free baseline, on uniform-noise vectors -- the ANN worst case. **Not wired**: the index-sorted flush path (`writeSortingField`/`reconstructAndWriteGraph`) -- and note that once `IndexWriter` gains a sorted flush, `build_vectors_output` **must** remap ordinals through the sort map or every vector lands on the wrong document, silently and `CheckIndex`-clean (see `docs/sweep/m2/c10-vectors-wiring.md` finding 11), and the merge is not wired into `merge.rs` (the codec entry points exist; see `docs/sweep/m2/c10-vectors-wiring.md`) |
| `util/packed/DirectMonotonicWriter` | `lucene-codecs/src/direct_monotonic.rs::write` | **write path ported; a precision divergence fixed 2026-08-28.** Java computes each block's linear estimate as `(long) (avgInc * (long) i)` where `avgInc` is a **float**, so the product is float arithmetic; this port's writer used `f64` while its own *reader* correctly used `f32`. Writer and reader therefore disagreed with each other, and the writer with Lucene, once a block held enough values for the two precisions to truncate differently. Latent rather than new: every caller passed `blockShift = 0`, so each block held one value, `i` was always 0, and the two agreed trivially -- switching stored fields to Lucene's real `blockShift = 10` exposed it as a `.fdx` chunk lookup landing on the wrong chunk. Regression-tested at the 1024-value block size real formats use with an average increment having no exact `f32` representation, and confirmed to catch the old behaviour |
| `codecs/lucene90/compressing/Lucene90CompressingStoredFieldsWriter` (write side), `Mode.BEST_SPEED` (LZ4) only | `lucene-codecs/src/stored_fields.rs::write_best_speed` | **write path ported, and chunking is now real as of 2026-08-28.** Documents were previously *all* placed in one single un-sliced chunk, which combined with `write_bulk_ints`' scalar-tail-only encode (row above) to make committing 128 or more documents a hard panic -- the write path could not index a realistic corpus at all, and no check caught it because every fixture was a handful of docs. A shared `write_chunked` now ports `Lucene90CompressingStoredFieldsWriter`'s buffer/`triggerFlush`/`flush` cycle with Lucene's own constants (`10*8*1024` bytes or 1024 docs for BEST_SPEED, whichever trigger fires first; `blockShift = 10`), including the **dirty** token bit and `.fdm` dirty-chunk/dirty-doc tallies for a final chunk that ran out of documents before either trigger, and the **sliced** path for a chunk holding a single outsized document. Measured against real Lucene once it could run at all: 25708 ns/doc against 10299, a real 2.5x gap at a 1.05x noise floor (`scripts/bench-micro.sh --bench index`). The chunk's payload is now compressed with **real LZ4 back-reference matching** (`crate::lz4::compress`, see that row above) rather than the original `encode_literal_lz4` literal-only stub -- the empty zero-length dictionary unit that precedes it is still emitted via `encode_literal_lz4` since a real compressor over zero bytes degenerates to the same single token. Numeric field encodes (`write_zint`/`write_zfloat_full`/`write_tlong_full`/`write_zdouble_full`) still always emit the full/worst-case encoding rather than Java's shortest-of-several choice (still correct, just not size-optimal). **Verified against real Lucene in the write→read direction** (the reverse of this repo's usual differential-testing pattern): `crates/lucene-codecs/examples/write_stored_fields_fixture.rs` writes a `.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm` triple -- now including a field whose value is a phrase repeated 50 times, specifically to prove genuine compression (the resulting `.fdt` is ~222 bytes for a doc set whose one repeated field alone is 2250 bytes, well under a literal-wrapped size) -- and `fixtures/src/VerifyStoredFields.java` opens it directly through `Lucene90StoredFieldsFormat.fieldsReader` (with a hand-built `SegmentInfo`/`FieldInfos`, so this slice doesn't also need `.si`/`.fnm` writers) and confirms every document's fields, including the repeated-phrase one, match byte-for-byte -- passed on the first real-Lucene run after the compressor was wired in  **M2 sweep (b3-compression).** **The chunk payload is now framed exactly as `LZ4WithPresetDictCompressor` frames it**, rather than as one zero-length dictionary plus one block covering the whole chunk: `dictLength = min(64kB, len / (NUM_SUB_BLOCKS * DICT_SIZE_FACTOR))`, `blockLength = ceil((len - dictLength) / NUM_SUB_BLOCKS)`, the dictionary compressed first and each sub-block compressed against it via `lz4::compress_with_dictionary`, with every unit's compressed length written up front and the bytes after. That is both a better ratio (the dictionary is a real preset dictionary now) and what makes the reader's sub-block skipping possible on files this port writes. One `FastCompressionHashTable` is allocated per call and reused for every unit of every chunk, as Java's compressor does. `writeZFloat`/`writeZDouble`/`writeTLong` are now the real Java encodings rather than always emitting the widest form: small integral floats/doubles collapse to one byte, an `f32`-exact double to five, and a whole-second/hour/day timestamp picks the matching scale header (a 1-day timestamp is now 1 byte, was 10). NaN is canonicalized the way `Float.floatToIntBits` does. Re-verified end to end against real Lucene 10.5.0 (`scripts/verify-write-path.sh`). See `docs/sweep/m2/b3-compression.md`. |
| `codecs/lucene90/compressing/Lucene90CompressingStoredFieldsWriter` (write side), `Mode.BEST_COMPRESSION` (DEFLATE) | `lucene-codecs/src/stored_fields.rs::write_best_compression` | **write path ported**, sharing `write_best_speed`'s `write_chunked` loop (at its own `10*48*1024`-byte / 4096-doc triggers) and `.fdx`/`.fdm` assembly (factored into a shared `write_index_and_meta` helper -- both modes produce an identical index/meta shape, differing only in the recorded `chunkSize`, and both now write one `.fdx` entry per chunk plus the trailing sentinel rather than a hardcoded two), but with its own `.fdt` payload framing since the two modes' compression-unit shapes genuinely differ (BEST_SPEED: one zero-dictionary LZ4 unit; BEST_COMPRESSION: a dictionary prefix plus several fixed-size DEFLATE sub-blocks, real Lucene's own `dictLength = len/(NUM_SUB_BLOCKS*DICT_SIZE_FACTOR)`/`blockLength = ceil((len-dictLength)/NUM_SUB_BLOCKS)` sizing formula reused verbatim, `NUM_SUB_BLOCKS=10`/`DICT_SIZE_FACTOR=6`) -- sharing one function across both would have needed a mode-branch threaded through the framing itself, so two functions sharing only the common suffix was the more readable split. Each sub-block (and the dictionary prefix) is compressed independently via `deflate::compress` with no cross-unit back-referencing (`miniz_oxide` has no preset-dictionary compress API) -- still a valid decode per `decompress_unit`'s contract, just a smaller compression ratio than real Lucene's writer, not a correctness gap. **Verified against real Lucene in the write→read direction**: `crates/lucene-codecs/examples/write_stored_fields_fixture.rs` now writes *two* segments, `_0` (BEST_SPEED, unchanged) and `_1` (BEST_COMPRESSION, one field repeating a phrase ~2000 times, ~90KB, forcing a real dictionary plus several sub-blocks rather than one trivial unit), and `fixtures/src/VerifyStoredFields.java` was extended to loop over both segments (a `segments=_0,_1` manifest key, each with its own `<seg>.mode` attribute value) -- both pass against real Lucene on the same run. A third segment `_2` was added 2026-08-28 carrying `2*1024 + 37` documents -- two full BEST_SPEED chunks plus a dirty tail -- checked document by document by real Lucene via a `{i}`-templated manifest line (per-doc lines would swamp the file for a segment whose whole point is its document count); confirmed to have teeth by mutating the expectation and watching it fail inside the dirty chunk  **M2 sweep (b3-compression).** Now shares `write_best_speed`'s chunking and the shared `NUM_SUB_BLOCKS`/`DICT_SIZE_FACTOR` constants, and emits Java's bare `vInt(0)` for a zero-length dictionary unit rather than a real (2-byte) empty DEFLATE stream -- which real Lucene does in fact still accept, verified deliberately, so this is framing fidelity and not a corruption fix. A new fixture segment (`_3` in `write_stored_fields_fixture.rs`) covers that zero-length-dictionary shape, which every previous BEST_COMPRESSION fixture was too large to reach. Still unported and now stated precisely: the *encoder* side of the preset dictionary -- `miniz_oxide` has no `deflateSetDictionary`, so each sub-block is a self-contained stream and the ratio is worse than Java's; the decoder side back-references into the dictionary correctly either way. `deflate::compress` uses level 6 because that is the level `DeflateWithPresetDictCompressionMode` itself passes (the previous doc comment claimed Lucene used level 9). See `docs/sweep/m2/b3-compression.md`. |
| `codecs/lucene90/Lucene90TermVectorsFormat`/`Lucene90CompressingTermVectorsReader` (`.tvd`/`.tvx`/`.tvm` read) | `lucene-codecs/src/term_vectors.rs` | ported (read-only), fixture-verified against a real IndexWriter (a repeated-term field with positions/offsets/payloads on some occurrences but not others, a multi-field doc, and a doc with no term vectors at all); positions/offsets reset to a fresh delta chain at each **term's** first occurrence rather than one chain per field (reverse-engineered from the reader's exact loop bounds, not its doc comment — see the module doc); the LZ4 unit's term-suffix and payload bytes are interleaved **per document** (each doc's suffixes then its payloads, not two global regions), a second reverse-engineered detail the first fixture run caught; uses two more bit-packing conventions alongside `direct_reader`: `packed_ints` (generic MSB-first bitstream, for the distinct-field-numbers array) and `block_packed` (64-value blocks built on `packed_ints`, for the field/term bookkeeping arrays)  **Sweep b7**: added the third dirty-chunk corruption check Java makes (`numDirtyDocs < numDirtyChunks` -- "Cannot have more dirty chunks than documents within dirty chunks"); the other two were already present **`c27-arith-codecs-2` (2026-08-29)**: `chunkDocs` was decoded with a *signed* shift. `Lucene90CompressingTermVectorsReader:380` is `readVInt() >>> 1` and `Lucene90CompressingTermVectorsWriter.copyChunks:858` is `code >>> 1`; both were `>>` here. The difference shows only for a negative vint -- only on a corrupt `.tvd` -- but it is the difference between an unconstrained negative `chunkDocs` and one as large as `i32::MAX`, and the latter is what makes `docBase + chunkDocs` genuinely overflow the guard that exists to catch it. |
| `util/packed/PackedInts.Format.PACKED`/`BulkOperationPacked` (generic MSB-first bit-packed array, arbitrary width) | `lucene-codecs/src/packed_ints.rs` | **both directions ported.** Read as before; `encode` is the exact bitwise inverse of `get`, any width `0..=64` (this convention has no whitelist of supported widths, unlike `direct_reader`) |
| `util/packed/BlockPackedReaderIterator`/`BlockPackedWriter` (64-value self-describing blocks) | `lucene-codecs/src/block_packed.rs` | **both directions ported.** Read as before (a single `decode_all`, not Java's seekable `next`/`skip` iterator), with the block `minValue` now read through the same negative-capable 9-byte varint `BlockPackedReaderIterator.readVLong` uses rather than the generic `DataInput.readVLong` (M2 sweep `b2-packed`; the generic one mis-parses a `minValue` whose ninth byte has its top bit set). Write (`encode_all`) is now a full port of `BlockPackedWriter.flush`, per-block min-value delta encoding included -- it previously hardcoded `minValue = 0` and sized `bitsPerValue` from the block max alone, which silently encoded any block containing a negative value wrong (all-negative blocks collapsed to `bitsPerValue = 0`, i.e. all zeros); no caller feeds negatives yet, so it was latent |
