| `codecs/lucene90/compressing/Lucene90CompressingTermVectorsWriter` (write side) | `lucene-codecs/src/term_vectors.rs::TermVectorsWriter` (+ the `write_best_speed` convenience wrapper) | **write path complete on chunk geometry as of sweep batch `c8-tv-chunking`.** `TermVectorsWriter` is the streaming, chunk-flushing writer: `add_document`/`flush(force)`/`finish` with Java's own two triggers -- `chunkSize` 4096 buffered term-suffix+payload bytes or `maxDocsPerChunk` 128 documents (`Lucene90TermVectorsFormat`'s `super(..., 1 << 12, 128, 10)`) -- plus `TermVectorsWriter::with_geometry` for `Lucene90CompressingTermVectorsFormat`'s parameterised constructor, `numDirtyChunks`/`numDirtyDocs` accounting, `tooDirty`, and `copy_chunks` (`Lucene90CompressingTermVectorsWriter.copyChunks`). The `.tvx` `DirectMonotonicWriter`s use Java's `blockShift = 10`. Feature matrix: **positions, offsets, and payloads are all supported** (task follow-up; originally positions-only, see git history). Offset deltas are patched against the real `flushOffsets` `charsPerTerm` ratio (`sumOffsets / sumPos`, both summed over each term's last occurrence per distinct field number), as of `c8` -- it was a fixed `1.0` before, which round-tripped but compressed worse. Payload bytes are appended into the same LZ4 unit as term suffixes, grouped **per document** (all of that doc's fields' suffixes, then that doc's PAYLOADS fields' payloads) to match the read side's decompression slicing. Terms are prefix-compressed against the previous term in the same field (`startTerm`'s `StringHelper.bytesDifference`), as of `c8`; `flushFlags`' `nonChangingFlags` per-field-number encoding (selector 0) is emitted too, and `flushFieldNums` sorts the distinct field numbers as Java does. Every `block_packed`/`direct_reader`/`packed_ints` array uses the exact bit width its own values need (worst case, not minimized across blocks/chunks). LZ4 is the same zero-dictionary, literal-only "compression" as stored fields' write side (`encode_literal_lz4`, a term-vectors-local copy since this format's LZ4 unit has no dict/block-length wrapper to begin with). **Verified against real Lucene in the write→read direction**: `crates/lucene-codecs/examples/write_term_vectors_fixture.rs` writes a `.tvd`/`.tvx`/`.tvm` triple (3 docs, a multi-field doc, a doc with no term vectors, repeated terms with multi-occurrence positions) and `fixtures/src/VerifyTermVectors.java` opens it directly through `Lucene90TermVectorsFormat.vectorsReader` (hand-built `SegmentInfo`/`FieldInfos`, no `.si`/`.fnm` writer needed) and confirms every doc's terms/freqs/positions match via real `Terms`/`TermsEnum`/`PostingsEnum` -- passed on the first real-Lucene run. **Two review-pass fixes landed before this slice's commit**, both worth naming durably rather than leaving only in a source comment: (1) `bits_per_field_num` (the bit width `field_nums`/`allFieldNumOffs` are packed at) is floored at 1, never 0 -- a chunk where every field across every doc has `field_number == 0` (an entirely ordinary shape, e.g. any single-field index) previously computed a 0-bit width, which this port's own reader tolerates (an all-zero chunk just decodes as all-zero) but real Lucene's reader does not: it unconditionally indexes `packedBulkOps[bitsPerValue - 1]`, throwing `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` on a 0-bit width, independent of Java's `assert` (stripped without `-ea`, the default). The fixture now writes a second segment (`_1`) specifically shaped this way (every field number 0 across two docs) since the primary fixture's mixed field numbers 0/1 never exercised this; both segments pass against real Lucene. (2) the `.tvm` meta stream's `packedIntsVersion` field must be written as `2` (`PackedInts.VERSION_CURRENT`/`VERSION_MONOTONIC_WITHOUT_ZIGZAG`) -- this port's own reader never validates that field (a wrong value round-trips fine through it), but real Lucene's `BlockPackedReaderIterator` does validate it, so this is a genuine cross-engine-only requirement a purely-Rust test suite could never catch. Real LZ4 back-reference compression remains unimplemented on the write side (each chunk is one literal run); every array still uses its own exact bit width rather than a cross-chunk minimisation. Multi-chunk segments are covered by a third real-Lucene fixture segment (`_2` in `write_term_vectors_fixture`/`VerifyTermVectors`): 400 documents across a dozen-odd chunks with offsets, payloads and prefix-shared terms, checked occurrence by occurrence through real `PostingsEnum.ALL`. **Offsets/payloads write support (later follow-up task)**: round-trip proven via direct `TermVectorField` construction + read-back through the unmodified reader (`write_best_speed_offsets_only_round_trips`, `write_best_speed_payloads_only_round_trips`, `write_best_speed_positions_offsets_payloads_round_trips_multi_term_multi_doc`, `write_best_speed_positions_and_offsets_freq_three_round_trips` in `term_vectors.rs`'s own test module) -- **review-confirmed bug fixed before landing**: the offset-delta correction used each occurrence's absolute position instead of the position *delta* the read side actually applies as its own correction input, which only coincidentally agreed for `freq <= 2` or a first occurrence at position 0 (exactly the shape every other test here happens to use) -- silently corrupting the 3rd+ occurrence's decoded start/end offsets for any term repeated 3+ times with positions+offsets both enabled. Fixed to track the same delta-from-previous-occurrence stream `positions_flat` itself encodes; `write_best_speed_positions_and_offsets_freq_three_round_trips` is the regression test (freq 3, non-uniform position gaps) that would have caught it. **Merge path now passes offsets/payloads through (later follow-up task)**: `merge.rs`'s `write_merged_term_vectors` reads each source's already-decoded `TermVectorsDocument` (offsets/payloads included) and appends it to the merged doc list with only a field-number remap -- no cross-source term-level combination is needed (term-vector data is entirely doc-local, unlike postings), so lifting the earlier `Error::TermVectorOffsetsOrPayloadsNotSupported` rejection required no new combination logic, just removing the guard. Covered by `term_vectors_merge_carries_offsets_and_payloads_through` (`lucene-index/src/merge.rs`): two sources, three docs total, each with a distinct term/offset/payload, merged and read back through the real reader with exact values checked. **Still not wired end-to-end**: `IndexWriter`'s term-vector commit path (`build_term_vectors_output` in `index_writer.rs`) does not yet construct a `TermVectorField` with `has_offsets`/`has_payloads` set, so no real flush produces one today (tokenizer offsets/payloads through to `TermVectorField` construction is a separate follow-up); only a merge of already-flushed segments or a hand-built `TermVectorsDocument` can exercise this today. **Now wired into `IndexWriter::commit()`** (follow-up task, see PLAN.md): `IndexWriter::set_term_vector_field(Some(field_name))` (`crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs`) opts a writer into calling this function for exactly one field of every segment `commit()` flushes, sourcing per-document `term -> (freq, positions)` data by regrouping `crate::indexing_chain::invert_documents`'s term-keyed inverted index by doc ID (term vectors need the transpose of what the postings writer wants) -- the same single-chunk/positions-only scope above, unchanged, now reachable from a real `IndexWriter::add_document`/`commit()` call. Requires `store_term_vectors == true` on the field's `FieldInfo` (checked, `Error::UnsupportedTermVectorField` otherwise). **Now wired for more than one field per commit too (same follow-up task as `set_postings_field`):** `IndexWriter::add_term_vector_field(field_name)` opts a writer into additional term-vector fields on top of whatever `set_term_vector_field` already established (same "replace, don't accumulate" semantics for `set_term_vector_field` itself). This was a genuine `IndexWriter`-side gap, not a `term_vectors::write_best_speed` limitation -- that function already accepted multiple `TermVectorField` entries per `TermVectorsDocument` before this task; `build_term_vectors_output` simply never built more than one. Now every configured field with content for a given doc contributes its own `TermVectorField` to that doc's `TermVectorsDocument`, all written in one `write_best_speed` call. Proven end-to-end by `crates/lucene-search/tests/index_writer_term_vectors_fixtures.rs::two_distinct_term_vector_fields_in_one_commit_are_both_readable`. **Merge-policy interaction, applied proactively from the postings feature's own review finding**: `execute_merge`/`merge_stored_only_segments` have no `.tvd`/`.tvx`/`.tvm` awareness either, so `segment_stats()` also excludes any segment whose `.si` lists a `.tvd` file from `find_merges`' candidate pool, keeping it permanently un-mergeable rather than mergeable-with-silent-data-loss. Covered by `segments_with_term_vectors_are_never_automatically_merged_away`, mirroring the postings version. **Postings + term vectors together, tested and found to work correctly**: `set_postings_field` and `set_term_vector_field` can both be set (to the same field) in one commit -- each builds its own in-memory output before `dir` is touched, and the `.si`-patching helper for each reads back whatever the other has already written rather than clobbering it, so a segment ends up with all seven files correctly listed; covered by `a_field_with_both_postings_and_term_vectors_configured_together_produces_both_correctly` (`lucene-search`) and its `lucene-index`-side unit-test counterpart. Required end-to-end proof: `crates/lucene-search/tests/index_writer_term_vectors_fixtures.rs::documents_added_via_index_writer_have_readable_term_vectors` adds documents via `IndexWriter::add_document`/`commit()` (not a hand-built fixture) and reads them back via the existing unmodified `lucene_search::term_vector_for_doc`, asserting the exact per-document term/freq/position data.  **Sweep b7 fix (CORRECTNESS)**: the `charsPerTerm`+offset streams and the payload-length stream were gated on "some field carries the OFFSETS/PAYLOADS flag", but both readers gate on `totalOffsets > 0`/`totalPayloads > 0`, which are sums of term *frequencies*. A flagged field with zero terms therefore made the writer emit blocks the reader never consumes, desynchronizing every following byte of the chunk. Now gated on the flattened arrays' lengths (regression test `write_best_speed_flagged_but_termless_field_round_trips`). Caller contract worth knowing: real `CheckIndex` iterates term-vector fields in document field order and requires ascending field *names*, so callers must add each document's fields name-sorted -- `lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::build_term_vectors_output` now does (sweep batch `c34-ledger-reconcile`: it sorts its `TermVectorFieldConfig`s by name, so a caller who calls `add_term_vector_field` out of name order no longer produces a segment real `CheckIndex` rejects; pinned by `term_vector_fields_are_written_in_ascending_field_name_order`). **Merge fast path (sweep batch `c8-tv-chunking`)**: `Lucene90CompressingTermVectorsWriter.merge`'s `MatchingReaders`-gated BULK path is ported -- a source with identical field numbering, no deletions, the same chunk geometry and acceptable dirtiness has its compressed chunks copied byte for byte (`copy_chunks`, with Java's two `CorruptIndexException` guards and its own `checkIntegrity` run first, since a byte copy writes a fresh valid footer over copied bytes); every other source is decoded through a `ChunkCursor` and re-encoded. Proven end to end by `write_merged_segment_fixture`/`VerifyMergedSegment`, which now stores term vectors on its indexed field, recomputes every one of the 7 176 merged documents' vectors independently and runs real `CheckIndex`. |
| `codecs/lucene90/Lucene90PointsFormat`/`Lucene90PointsReader`/`util/bkd/BKDReader` (`.kdm`/`.kdi`/`.kdd` read, block KD-tree point values -- `IntPoint`, `LongPoint`, `LatLonPoint`, etc.) | `lucene-codecs/src/points.rs` | ported (read-only), fixture-verified against a real IndexWriter (2000 docs, a `LongPoint` field on two-thirds of them so leaves have non-continuous doc ids, forcing several leaves past the default 512-point-per-leaf threshold); only the current on-disk BKD version (10, vectorized BPV24 + BPV21) is accepted, older versions rejected outright rather than replicated. **Decode-fully, no query pruning yet**: the packed index tree is walked in full left-to-right order to recover every leaf's file pointer rather than replicating Java's bounding-box-pruned seek/skip navigation -- the index's `leftNumBytes` skip-ahead hint is read and discarded, since a full recursive left-then-right traversal naturally consumes exactly that many bytes and needs no seeking at all; every `DocIdsWriter` doc-id encoding is now supported, including the legacy vint-delta marker (byte `0`, `LEGACY_DELTA_VINT`): continuous run, bitset, 16/21/24/32-bit delta-packed all fixture-verified against a real writer, plus the legacy delta-vint decode covered by hand-built unit tests only, since no Lucene 10.5.0 writer (real or this port's) can produce that marker -- it predates the 16/21/24/32-bit encodings and real Lucene's own `DocIdsWriter` source comments it as "legacy, should no longer be used in the writing side." Any other marker byte remains a rejected `Error::UnsupportedDocIdsEncoding`. **`num_index_dims != 1` leaf layout bug fixed (task #22)**: `read_leaf_block`'s per-leaf min/max bounding box (present whenever `numIndexDims > 1`) had been decoded in the wrong position relative to the compressed-dimension marker byte -- it read the box *before* the marker, matching real `BKDReader.visitDocValuesNoCardinality`'s (older, no-longer-written) leaf layout instead of `visitDocValuesWithCardinality`'s (the layout every current-version, `VERSION_LOW_CARDINALITY_LEAVES`-and-up tree actually uses), which reads the marker *first* and only reads the box afterward, and only when the marker isn't `-1`. This went unnoticed for a while since the only test ever exercising it was a hand-built unit test (no real multi-dimension tree had ever been read *or* written by this port), and was only caught once this task's new write side produced a real 2-dimension `.kdd`/`.kdi` pair and `fixtures/src/VerifyPoints.java` tried to open it through real `Lucene90PointsFormat` (`CorruptIndexException: Got compressedDim=127`) -- exactly the kind of bug the differential-testing skill's fixture-first rule exists to catch. Fixed in both `read_leaf_block` (order swapped, box now conditioned on `compressed_dim != -1`) and this port's own `write_leaf` (marker written before the box, matching). **Compressed-dimension boundary re-validated, found already correct (task #191)**: cross-checked `read_leaf_block`'s rejection condition (`compressed_dim < -2 || compressed_dim as i32 >= field.num_dims`) and the real-dimension-index (`>= 0`) branch's `compressed_byte_offset = compressed_dim * bytes_per_dim + common_prefix_lengths[compressed_dim]` math against real `BKDReader.readCompressedDim`/`visitDocValuesWithCardinality` (`util/bkd/BKDReader.java` lines ~974-980, ~944-950) -- both match exactly, no off-by-one, no fix needed. The prior gap was fixture coverage, not correctness: the existing `points_index` fixture's single-dimension `val` field can only ever produce `compressedDim == 0` (there's no dimension 1 to pick), so the offset multiplication was previously only ever exercised at its trivial zero case. `fixtures/src/GenPoints.java` now also writes a second, 2-dimension `IntPoint` field (`multi`: dim0 a bijective multiplicative hash of the doc id spread across the full 32-bit range, dim1 only 4 distinct values), engineered so real `BKDWriter` picks dim1 as `sortedDim`/`compressedDim` in every leaf -- a naive sequential dim0 turned out to be the wrong shape (BKDWriter's recursive range-narrowing squeezed dim0's in-leaf cardinality down to 1-2, which tied or beat dim1's fixed <=4 and kept `compressedDim` at 0 in every leaf; verified this was actually happening, not just theorized, via a temporary debug print in `read_leaf_block`). The generator now mechanically confirms `compressedDim >= 1` was really written, independent of this port's own decoder, via `CompressedDimSpy.java` (lives in `org.apache.lucene.util.bkd` to reach `BKDReader`'s package-private fields, and does its own from-scratch walk of the packed index tree and leaf header) and records the observed per-leaf byte in the `multi_leaf_compressed_dims` manifest key; `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/points_fixtures.rs`'s `parses_real_multi_dim_points_and_matches_lucene_values` test asserts on that recorded value directly (so it fails, not just passes vacuously, if a future regeneration stops exercising the branch) and also confirms this port decodes the fixture identically to real Lucene -- proving the nonzero-index offset math against real bytes, not just hand-built ones.  **Sweep b7 additions**: (a) `PointValues.intersect` is now ported -- `PointsReader::intersect` + `Relation` + `IntersectVisitor`, plus a `range_query` convenience implementing `PointRangeQuery`'s visitor. This is the real pruning traversal: `CELL_OUTSIDE_QUERY` skips a whole subtree without reading any of its `.kdd` bytes (and, for a left child, without parsing its packed-index bytes either -- the `leftNumBytes` skip-ahead vint, previously read and discarded, is what makes the seek to the right sibling possible), `CELL_INSIDE_QUERY` decodes doc ids only via a port of `BKDPointTree.addAll`, and only `CELL_CROSSES_QUERY` decodes packed values. Cell bounds are maintained exactly like `pushBoundsLeft`/`pushBoundsRight`/`popBounds`, and split values are reconstructed from the packed index's prefix/first-diff-byte/`negativeDeltas` coding exactly like `BKDReader.readNodeData` -- the read-side inverse of `pack_index`, and the first code here that *uses* split values rather than skipping them. Verified against real Java-written `.kdi` bytes for all three fixture fields (1-dim `LongPoint`, 2-dim `IntPoint`, 4-dim/2-index-dim shape) in `tests/points_fixtures.rs::intersect_over_real_lucene_packed_index_matches_brute_force`. Measured 577x vs decode-everything-and-filter on a 200k-point/391-leaf tree at 0.1% selectivity (`cargo bench -p lucene-codecs --bench hot_paths -- points/`). `lucene-search/src/points_query.rs` and `lucene-index/src/points_delete.rs` have **not** been migrated to it yet. (b) `BKDConfig`'s constructor bounds (`numDims` 1..16, `numIndexDims` 1..8 and <= `numDims`, `bytesPerDim` > 0, `maxPointsInLeafNode` > 0), `numLeaves > 0`, and `BKDReader`'s per-dimension `minPackedValue <= maxPackedValue` `CorruptIndexException` are now enforced on read -- previously a corrupt `.kdm` could drive a ~2^64-byte allocation **c19 sweep**: `check_config` now also rejects a `numDims x bytesPerDim` product that overflows an `i32`, and `parse_field` refuses a packed min/max value length the `.kdm` does not actually contain. Lucene bounds `numDims` and `numIndexDims` but puts **no** upper bound on `bytesPerDim`; in Java the product wraps an `int` and `new byte[negative]` throws `NegativeArraySizeException`, while in Rust it panicked, and a large-but-not-overflowing product (`numIndexDims=1, bytesPerDim=2^30`) asked `vec![0u8; n]` for two 1 GB buffers -- an **abort** no `catch_unwind` at the FFI boundary can intercept. Both reproduce Java's outcome without Java's mechanism. See `docs/arithmetic-gate.md`. **`c27-arith-codecs-2` (2026-08-29)**: `DocIdsWriter.readInts21`'s top field is `(int) (l >>> 42)`, a 22-bit **zero-extended** field; the port had `(l >> 42) as i32`, so a corrupt `.kdd` word with its sign bit set decoded to a *negative doc id* instead of the in-range value Java yields -- a different answer, not a rejected one. Every `>>>` in `BKDReader`/`BKDWriter`/`DocIdsWriter`/`BKDUtil` was then swept; this was the only divergence. Ten more `.kdm`/`.kdi`/`.kdd` values that reached an allocation, an index or a shift unbounded were bounded, among them `numLeaves` (b7's sibling, a 17 GB reservation reproduced as a real SIGABRT under `ulimit -v`), the leaf point `count`, a common prefix wider than `bytesPerDim` (which decoded silently wrong point values for an all-equal leaf), the split descriptor, and `nodeID * 2`, whose wrap let a deep packed index overflow the *stack*. A re-signed byte-flip sweep over all three files (1 014 flips) went from 8 panics to 0. **M2 sweep `c39-codecs-readpath`**: `PointValues.estimatePointCount(IntersectVisitor)` is ported (`PointsReader::estimate_point_count`, plus `estimate_point_count_bounded` for `isEstimatedPointCountGreaterThanOrEqualTo`'s early stop and `estimate_range_point_count` for `PointRangeQuery`'s own visitor). It is the same descent as `intersect` with the two expensive steps removed: an entirely-inside cell contributes `BKDPointTree.size()` derived from the node id alone (`PointsField::subtree_size`, Java's `leftMostLeafNode`/`rightMostLeafNode` walk and its `lastLeafNodePointCount` correction -- only the unbalanced arm, because `isTreeBalanced()` is `false` outright for every BKD version this reader accepts), a leaf the walk cannot descend past contributes `(size + 1) / 2`, and no `.kdd` byte is read on any path. `intersect_node` and `estimate_node` now share one `read_inner_node` so the descent cannot drift between them. Ground truth is real Lucene's own `estimatePointCount` over fourteen boxes on the committed `points_index` (`fixtures/src/AppendPointEstimateManifest.java`, appended without regenerating the index), chosen so the estimate and the exact match count differ -- 768 vs 674, 256 vs 8, 744 vs 251 -- since a test written against the exact answer could not tell a correct tree walk from a wrong one. Both halves negative-controlled: dropping the last-leaf correction gives 1 536 where Lucene says 1 333, and dropping the half-leaf assumption gives 1 024 where Lucene says 768. Lucene's `estimateDocCount` over the same boxes is asserted alongside, but pins less than it looks: all three fixture fields are single-valued (`size == docCount`), so every box takes branch 1 or 2 and `.docs == .points` throughout. **Branch 3** -- the multi-valued urn approximation `D * (1 - ((N - n) / N)^(N/D))`, the one place float order-of-operations could diverge from Java -- has no cross-engine ground truth; closing that needs a fixture field with more points than documents, and its arithmetic is meanwhile unit-tested against hand-computed values. **`c43-final-cleanup`**: the tree walk no longer allocates per node. `read_inner_node` owned a `saved_split_tail` and a `split_value` `Vec`, and both callers additionally `to_vec()`'d the cell bound they were about to overwrite -- four heap allocations per inner node visited, against Java's `BKDPointTree` preallocating per-level stacks (`splitValuesStack`, `splitDimValueStack`, `negativeDeltas`) sized once from `getTreeDepth`. `IntersectCtx::stack` is those stacks, grown on demand one entry per level reached rather than sized up front from `num_leaves` (sizing it up front would let a corrupt `bytesPerDim` multiply a plausible allocation by the tree depth, the shape [`docs/arithmetic-gate.md`](arithmetic-gate.md) singles out as the one that aborts), and `InnerNode` is offsets only. **Measured** with the min-of-40 alternating A/B, both arms in one process from one build (`crates/lucene-codecs/examples/bkd_walk_scratch.rs`, arm selected by `intersect_with_scratch`'s `reuse_scratch`): `estimate_point_count` -- the walk whose whole justification is being cheap, since it reads no `.kdd` at all -- **1.27x to 1.68x**; `intersect` 1.01x to 1.04x, dominated by leaf decode, which is the honest report of it. `points::tests::the_two_scratch_arms_visit_exactly_the_same_documents` pins that the two arms are the same walk. |
| `codecs/lucene90/Lucene90PointsWriter`/`util/bkd/BKDWriter` (write side) | `lucene-codecs/src/points.rs::write` | **write path now supports any number of dimensions, any number of leaves** (task #22 extends the prior one-dimension-only, multi-leaf write path to `numDims > 1`, e.g. `LatLonPoint`-shaped 2D fields; `numIndexDims` may be less than `numDims`, i.e. trailing data-only, non-indexed dimensions are supported, e.g. a `LatLonShape`-style bounding box). **Split-dimension selection**: at every recursive split, [`widest_dim`] picks the dimension with the widest value range (`max - min`, computed via unsigned big-endian byte subtraction so it works for any `bytesPerDim`, not just ones that fit a native integer) across the current point subset, ties toward the lowest dimension index -- for `numDims == 1` this always picks dimension 0, so the original single-dimension path/behavior is unchanged. This is a real, deliberately-chosen heuristic, not a wild deviation from real `BKDWriter`: real Lucene's own multi-dimension `build()` picks its split dimension by computing each dimension's cardinality/range at the current node too (see `BKDWriter.build`'s `usedBytes`/cardinality-driven choice for leaf-level dimension selection, and its inner-node `splitDim` selection based on recomputed per-dimension bounds) -- this port's version is a simpler, still-real range comparison rather than replicating the exact cardinality-counting algorithm, documented as a deliberate scope reduction. Because a different dimension can be chosen at every recursion level, `compute_leaf_plan` now consumes and re-sorts an owned `Vec` at each split node (`Vec::split_off`) rather than indexing into one shared, globally-presorted array the way the single-dimension predecessor did -- still no per-node `radix select` the way real `BKDRadixSelector` does, a plain `sort_by` per node is enough at this port's sizes. Real Lucene picks the leaf-split shape via `getNumLeftLeafNodes` (fill the deepest full binary-tree level, half its leaves go left, then push any remainder left too) and `mid = numLeftLeafNodes * maxPointsInLeafNode`; this port's `compute_leaf_plan` uses the exact same two formulas (verified against `BKDWriter.getNumLeftLeafNodes`'s source, not approximated), so the resulting tree shape matches real Lucene's for any point count/leaf-size combination. `packIndex`/`recursePackIndex`'s general case (split descriptor + left/right children + `leftNumBytes` skip-ahead) is implemented (`pack_index`), matching real Lucene's node layout exactly, **now including the split *dimension*, not just the split value**: the packed-index code formula `(firstDiffByteDelta * (1 + bytesPerDim) + prefix) * numIndexDims + splitDim` (already documented from task #21) now genuinely varies `splitDim` per node rather than always encoding `0`, and the split-value delta-coding's `lastSplitValues`/`negativeDeltas` state (`last_split_values`/`negative_deltas` in this port) is now **one array slot per index dimension** (real `BKDWriter.recursePackIndex`'s own per-dimension arrays) instead of a single dimension-0-only pair -- only the dimension a given node actually split on has its slot touched/saved/restored around that node's children, every other dimension's slot is inherited unchanged from the parent, exactly like real Lucene's shared per-dimension arrays. Leaf data now carries all dimensions per point (`numDims * bytesPerDim` bytes), and each leaf also writes its own (tighter-than-field) per-dimension min/max bounding box whenever `numIndexDims != 1`, matching what the read side ([`read_leaf_block`]) already unconditionally decoded in that case (see the read-side row above for the ordering bug this surfaced and fixed). Leaf-encoding choices remain freely made (not replicating real `BKDWriter`'s size-driven heuristics): common-prefix length always 0 per dimension, compressed-dimension marker always `-2` with every run forced to length 1, doc ids `CONTINUOUS_IDS` or plain `BPV_32`. Unit-tested: `widest_dim`/`unsigned_byte_sub` in isolation (widest-range selection, tie-breaking, multi-byte borrow), `compute_leaf_plan`'s point-distribution/balance invariants (now leaf-content-based rather than range-based, since dimension changes at each recursion level, and confirmed no longer a globally-presorted single order), two dedicated multi-dimension `pack_index`/split-value-reconstruction tests (a 3-dimension, 7-leaf tree whose split dimensions are confirmed to vary across nodes, decoded by a generalized `reconstruct_split_values` mirror of real `BKDReader.readNodeData`), and full write→read round trips through this module's own reader for a 2D `LatLonPoint`-shaped 100-point/25-leaf tree and a 3D 2-byte-per-dim, non-continuous-doc-id, 8-leaf tree, on top of the pre-existing 1D single/multi-leaf coverage. 99.5%+ line coverage on `points.rs`. **Verified against real Lucene in the write→read direction**: `crates/lucene-codecs/examples/write_points_fixture.rs` now writes *three* segments -- `_0` (single-leaf, 1D, unchanged), `_1` (multi-leaf, 1D, unchanged, including its pre-existing narrow-bounding-box pruning query), and **`_2` (new: 2-dimension, `LatLonPoint`-shaped, multi-leaf, with the two dimensions deliberately correlated so a real bounding-box query narrow on both axes actually finds leaves fully `CELL_INSIDE_QUERY`, not just `CELL_CROSSES_QUERY`/`CELL_OUTSIDE_QUERY`)** -- and `fixtures/src/VerifyPoints.java`'s new `verifySegment2D`/`verifyPruningQuery2D` open `_2` directly through real `Lucene90PointsFormat.fieldsReader`, confirm every point via a full-decode `CELL_CROSSES_QUERY`-only pass, **and then run a real two-dimension bounding-box query that prunes across *both* dimensions** (asserting at least one `CELL_OUTSIDE_QUERY` and one `CELL_INSIDE_QUERY` cell actually occur, then checking the returned point set against the manifest) -- all three segments and both pruning queries (1D and 2D) passed against real Lucene, proving real `BKDReader`/`IndexTree` accepts this port's multi-dimension packed-index encoding and correctly reconstructs per-dimension split values/dimensions from it, including under real pruning. **Data-only dimensions now supported (task #192)**: `WritePointsField::num_index_dims` may be less than `num_dims` -- [`widest_dim`]'s split-dimension selection and [`pack_index`]'s prefix-coding/`lastSplitValues`/`negativeDeltas` state are restricted to the first `num_index_dims` dimensions only (matching real `BKDWriter.split`/`recursePackIndex`, which only ever range over `config.numIndexDims()`), while the per-doc packed value written to every leaf, and read back by [`read_leaf_block`], remains the full `num_dims`-wide value including the trailing data-only dims -- `write_field` rejects `num_index_dims` outside `1..=num_dims` (`Error::InvalidNumIndexDims`). **Explicitly deferred, not silently missing**: real query-driven pruning on this port's *own* read side (still decode-fully, see the read-side row) -- multi-dimension write is otherwise no longer a scoped-out gap. **Multi-leaf/multi-dimension edge case audit**: systematically re-checked the max-points-per-leaf boundary (`count == max` stays one leaf, `count == max + 1` splits into exactly 2), the all-points-identical degenerate case (no dimension has variance -- `widest_dim`'s tie-break still returns a valid dimension and the recursion terminates purely by leaf count, never by value variance), the only-the-last-dimension-varies case (`widest_dim` correctly finds it via unsigned-byte-range comparison, not a naive dimension cycle), `num_dims == 1` vs. `>= 2`, exact-multiple-of-max vs. partial-last-leaf point counts, and deep recursion/many-leaf trees -- all already correct with no production bug found; the audit's only gaps were test coverage at the exact boundary/degenerate cases, closed with five new tests in `points.rs` (`write_then_read_exactly_at_max_points_per_leaf_stays_single_leaf`, `write_then_read_one_over_max_points_per_leaf_splits_into_two`, `write_then_read_all_points_identical_degenerate_case`, `widest_dim_picks_last_dim_when_only_it_varies`, `write_then_read_last_dim_only_varies_multi_leaf_round_trips`). `num_index_dims != num_dims` was re-confirmed as a genuine, pre-existing scope boundary at the time of this audit -- since closed by task #192 (see above: `WritePointsField::num_index_dims` now exists and `write_field` validates/uses it instead of hardcoding `num_index_dims = num_dims`).  **Sweep b7**: `write` now validates the same `BKDConfig` bounds the read side does -- `max_points_in_leaf_node == 0` previously reached `count.div_ceil(0)` and panicked. Still divergent from `BKDWriter` by design (recorded in `docs/sweep/m2/b7-points-fields.md`): `widest_dim` omits `split`'s `parentSplits` fairness pass, `compute_leaf_plan` sorts per node instead of `BKDRadixSelector`'s O(n) select, leaves always use prefix 0 / `compressedDim = -2` / run length 1, and doc ids use only `CONTINUOUS_IDS`/`BPV_32` -- all legal, all costing size rather than correctness |

| `codecs/lucene104/Lucene104PostingsReader`/`Writer`, `PostingsUtil`, `ForUtil`, `PForUtil` (`.doc`/`.pos`/`.pay` files, `PostingsEnum` in `DOCS`/`DOCS_AND_FREQS`/`DOCS_AND_FREQS_AND_POSITIONS`/`DOCS_AND_FREQS_AND_POSITIONS_AND_OFFSETS`/`DOCS_AND_CUSTOM_FREQS` mode, incl. payloads) | `lucene-codecs/src/postings.rs` (+ `lucene-codecs/src/for_util.rs`) | **ported: sequential decode (a full forward scan, `nextDoc()`-equivalent -- no skip-ahead/`advance()`) of any `docFreq > 1` up to `LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` (32 * `ForUtil.BLOCK_SIZE` = 8192), `IndexOptions::Docs`/`DocsAndFreqs`.** Three writer-side shapes now all decode: `docFreq == 1` is pulsed entirely into the term dictionary (`Lucene104PostingsWriter.finishTerm`, no `.doc` bytes at all -- `postings::singleton_postings` reconstructs `(docID, freq)` purely from `TermMetadata::singleton_doc_id` + `totalTermFreq`); `1 < docFreq < BLOCK_SIZE` takes `PostingsUtil.writeVIntBlock` (group-varint doc-delta/freq-bit packing + a plain-vint tail of freq exceptions, no skip data, no impacts), decoded via `DataInput::read_group_vints` (`postings::read_tail_block`); and **`docFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE`** now decodes one or more full 256-doc blocks (`postings::read_full_block_header` + `postings::decode_full_block_body`) followed by that same tail-block encoding for the `docFreq % BLOCK_SIZE` remainder. Each full block is `ForUtil`/`PForUtil`-bit-packed -- `for_util.rs` is a direct transliteration of `ForUtil.decode1..decode16`/`decodeSlow` (the lane-interleaved-per-byte/halfword SIMD-shaped bit-packing for `bitsPerValue <= 16`, plain 32-bit-wide packing above that) and `PForUtil.decode` (the token byte + up to 7 byte-patched exceptions), independently round-tripped against a hand-written test encoder for every `bits_per_value` in `1..=32` (not just what one fixture term happens to need) -- and the full block's doc-delta section additionally supports the "all-256-consecutive" (`bitsPerValue == 0`) and dense unary bit-set (`bitsPerValue < 0`) encodings the writer picks for storage efficiency (the bit-set path is not a corner case: it's what real Lucene chooses for this port's own dense `big`/"everywhere" fixture term, confirmed by regenerating against the real writer). Also ported (previously skipped by `blocktree.rs`, see that row): the per-term postings-metadata bytes (`Lucene104PostingsReader.decodeTerm`, no-positions subset) -- `postings::decode_term_metadata`, threaded across a `.tim` block's entries exactly like `SegmentTermsEnumFrame` threads `IntBlockTermState`. `blocktree::FieldTerms::postings(term, doc_in)` wires this up end-to-end: `seekExact` -> stats + metadata -> singleton reconstruction or `.doc` decode. **`advance()`: an "advance()-shaped API, not real skip-ahead" shim, landed this slice.** `postings::PostingsCursor` (`next_doc()`/`advance(target)`/`doc_id()`/`freq()`) gives `PostingsEnum`-style interface parity, but it is a binary search over `DocInput::read_postings`'s already-fully-materialized `Postings.docs`/`freqs` `Vec`s -- **not** a lazy on-wire jump using the level-0/level-1 skip pointers (those are still parsed-and-discarded, exactly as before this slice; see the deferred bullet below, unchanged). This was a deliberate scope choice per this slice's own design brief: a real lazy skip-ahead would need a new stateful `DocInput`-like type tracking on-wire position and decoding blocks on demand, which is a materially larger and riskier change than a cursor over data this file already decodes eagerly by design (the same tradeoff every other decoder in this file already made -- `BlockTree`'s `TermsEnum`, `IndexedDISI`, the terms dictionary, `BlockPackedReaderIterator`). Fixture-verified against real `PostingsEnum.advance(target)` output (`fixtures/src/GenBlockTree.java::appendAdvanceManifest`, `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_fixtures.rs::{big,body}_field_advance_matches_real_lucene_postings_enum_advance`) for both "big"/"everywhere" (`docFreq == 300`, so some targets cross the full-block/tail-block boundary) and "body"/"cat" (`docFreq == 2`, single tail block), across before-first-doc/exact-match/between-docs/next-doc-after-a-match/last-doc/past-last-doc targets -- matched on the first real-Lucene run, no decode bugs caught (unsurprising, since the shim never touches the wire format at all, only the already-verified decoded `Vec`s). Unit tests (`postings.rs`) additionally cover: advancing to a target at or before the current doc ID (documented no-op, not an error or rewind -- real Lucene's contract technically forbids this, but the shim's binary-search-from-current-position implementation makes a no-op the natural, safe behavior to document rather than leave accidental), advance on empty postings, advance past the last doc (`NO_MORE_DOCS`), and advance immediately followed by `next_doc()` continuing from the landed position. **Deferred** (rejected with `Error::Unsupported`, or in the skip-ahead case simply not implemented as an API): (1) **real lazy skip-ahead** -- the level-0/level-1 skip pointers (`level0NumBytes`/`blockLength`/level-1 header vlongs) are parsed in wire order for correctness but never used to jump forward or to avoid decoding a block; every block between the start and the target is fully decoded by `read_postings`, and `PostingsCursor` only adds a post-hoc binary-search cursor on top of that (see above) -- correct but not fast for a searcher that only wants a subrange (fine for a full-scan/merge consumer, or a conjunction/phrase query over small-enough postings lists that eager decode is cheap); a stateful decode-on-demand `DocInput` that actually jumps past undecoded blocks using the level-0 pointers remains future work; (2) **`docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS`** (8192) -- level-1 skip entries start appearing inline in the `.doc` stream every 32 full blocks, not yet parsed; (3) positions/offsets/payloads (`.pos`/`.pay`, `IndexOptions::DocsAndFreqsAndPositions` and up -- `decode_term_metadata` doesn't even parse the `posStartFP`/`payStartFP`/`lastPosBlockOffset` fields those index options add to the wire; being worked on separately, this slice deliberately did not touch `.pos`/`.pay`); (4) impacts/competitive-scoring metadata (`ImpactsEnum`, `CompetitiveImpactAccumulator`, needed for WAND/MAXSCORE in PLAN.md Phase 3) -- full-block impact bytes are parsed-and-discarded (skipped by length), never surfaced; (5) ~~`IndexOptions::DocsAndCustomFreqs` -- rejected outright, real Lucene never writes it for a normal text field~~ **now ported** (see the read/write postings-codec support note near the end of this row): it's wire-identical to `DocsAndFreqs` (real Lucene derives `indexHasFreq`/`writeFreqs` from `IndexOptions.subsumes(DOCS_AND_FREQS)`, which is `true` for both), so `read_postings`/`lazy_cursor`/`validate_field` now accept it directly alongside `DocsAndFreqs` with no new decode/encode path. **Positions/offsets/payloads are now ported** (`.pos`/`.pay`, `IndexOptions::DocsAndFreqsAndPositions` and `...AndOffsets`), sequential-only (no skip-ahead, same scope as the `.doc` side): `decode_term_metadata` now parses `posStartFP`/`payStartFP`/`lastPosBlockOffset` (`Lucene104PostingsReader.java:237-250`), `read_full_block` parses (but doesn't act on) the extra level-0 pos/pay skip-pointer fields those index options add to a full doc block's header (`Lucene104PostingsReader.java:754-761`), and the new `PosInput`/`PayInput` + `read_positions` decode every position/offset/payload occurrence for a term as **one flat sequence of `totalTermFreq` occurrences** (not one block per doc -- the writer buffers 256 occurrences at a time across doc boundaries, only resetting the position/offset accumulator per doc, `Lucene104PostingsReader.java:1298-1304`) -- full `ForUtil`/`PForUtil` blocks (payload lengths and offset start-deltas/lengths bulk-encoded, payload bytes batched per block) for `totalTermFreq / BLOCK_SIZE` full blocks, then a `refillLastPositionBlock`-style vint tail (payload bytes inlined in `.pos`, lengths only re-written when they change) for the remainder, re-chopped into per-doc groups via `Postings::freqs`. `blocktree::FieldTerms::positions(term, doc_in, pos_in, pay_in)` wires this up end-to-end alongside `postings()`. Fixture-verified against real `PostingsEnum.nextPosition()`/`startOffset()`/`endOffset()`/`getPayload()` output via a new "pos" field in `fixtures/src/GenBlockTree.java` (`DOCS_AND_FREQS_AND_POSITIONS_AND_OFFSETS`, a repeated term across two docs with a payload on only one occurrence, exercising the vint tail's payload-length-change bit) -- matched on the first real-Lucene run, no decode bugs caught this time. **Deferred**: the full-`ForUtil`/`PForUtil`-block position path (`totalTermFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE`) is only unit-tested (`postings::tests::read_positions_exactly_one_full_block_boundary`), not fixture-verified against real Lucene bytes, since reaching it needs 256+ real token occurrences of one term that this port's small hand-built fixtures don't produce; `docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` interacts with positions the same way it does with docs (not supported, same `Error::Unsupported` gate on `read_postings`, which `positions()` also goes through via `postings()`). Fixture-verified against real `PostingsEnum.nextDoc()`/`freq()` output (not just aggregate `docFreq`/`totalTermFreq` stats) via `fixtures/src/GenBlockTree.java`'s extended manifest: "body" (`docFreq == 2` for every term, `.doc` group-varint decode), "id" (`docFreq == 1`, singleton path, no `.doc` access at all), and **"big"/"everywhere" (`docFreq == 300`, one full `PForUtil`-bit-set-encoded block + a 44-doc group-varint tail, added specifically to force the multi-block path past real `Lucene104PostingsWriter`/`IndexWriter`)** -- see `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_fixtures.rs`. Hand-built unit tests cover edge cases no small real fixture reaches: `docFreq == BLOCK_SIZE` (256, exactly one full block, no tail bytes at all), `docFreq == BLOCK_SIZE + 1` (257, one full block + a 1-doc tail), the full-block bit-set (unary) doc-delta decode and its too-few-set-bits corruption check, `docFreq == BLOCK_SIZE - 1` (255, the largest size the non-multi-block path supports), all-docs-freq-1 (group-varint bit-packing path with zero trailing freq-exception vints), `docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` rejection, and `decode_term_metadata`'s absolute-vs-delta and docStartFP-delta-vs-singleton-zigzag-delta branches. `for_util.rs`'s own unit tests independently round-trip `bits_per_value` `1..=32` (both the lane-interleaved `<=16` paths and the `decodeSlow` `17..=32` path) and the `PForUtil` all-equal/exceptions fast paths, via test-only encoders mirroring `ForUtil.collapse8/16`+`encode`/`PForUtil.encode`. **Post-landing review fixes**: (a) `decode_term_metadata` now correctly zeroes `doc_start_fp`/`pos_start_fp`/`pay_start_fp` before applying deltas when `absolute` is set (`Lucene104PostingsReader.decodeTerm`'s own reset), instead of basing them on whatever `prev` the caller passed in -- invisible today since every current caller passes a fresh `TermMetadata::EMPTY` as `prev` on `absolute`, but load-bearing once multi-block BlockTree threads `prev_meta` across blocks; covered by `decode_term_metadata_absolute_resets_fps_even_with_stale_prev`; (b) `read_positions`'s per-doc re-chop loop now returns `Error::Store(Corrupted(_))` instead of panicking via out-of-bounds indexing when a corrupted `.doc`/`.tim`/`.tmd` makes `freqs` sum to more or less than `total_term_freq` (covered by two new rejects-mismatch tests); (c) closed two coverage gaps the ≥95%-per-file bar didn't catch: the packed (`bitsPerValue > 0`) `.doc` doc-delta branch and the full-`PForUtil`-block position path *with offsets* (as opposed to payloads) were previously exercised by neither the fixture nor a unit test -- both now have dedicated unit tests (`read_full_block_packed_encoding_decodes_bit_packed_deltas`, `read_positions_full_block_with_offsets_no_payloads`), reusing `for_util`'s own test-only bit-packing encoder (later promoted to the production `for_util::for_encode` function, see below, so this cross-module reuse point moved with it) **Real lazy skip-ahead is now ported** (`postings::LazyDocsCursor`, `DocInput::lazy_cursor`, `blocktree::FieldTerms::lazy_postings`): a decode-on-demand `next_doc()`/`advance(target)` iterator that decodes one `.doc` block at a time, replacing the eager whole-term materialization `PostingsCursor` sits on top of (that shim is unchanged and still exists for callers that want the simpler already-decoded path -- see the module doc for which to pick). **The honest boundary of what is actually skipped**: `read_full_block_header` was split out of `read_full_block` (now a thin wrapper over `read_full_block_header` + `decode_full_block_body`) to read a full block's level-0 header (`level0NumBytes`/`docDelta`/`blockLength`/impacts/pos-pay fields) *without* running `ForUtil`/`PForUtil` decode on its body; the header alone gives the block's last doc ID (`prev_doc_id + docDelta`) and its on-wire byte span. When `advance(target)` finds `target > header.last_doc_id`, it `seek()`s straight to the block's end and moves to the next block, skipping the actual bit-unpack entirely -- the expensive part of a block. This works for **every full block a term has, at any `docFreq`, not just `docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS`**: level-0 skip-past-one-block only requires the term to have at least one full 256-doc block (`docFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE`), which is unrelated to the level-1 (8192-doc) threshold -- level-1 exists to skip past *32 full blocks at once* without reading their individual level-0 headers, a coarser optimization this port still does not need since it already reads every level-0 header cheaply one at a time. The **tail block** (the `docFreq % BLOCK_SIZE` remainder, or the whole term when `docFreq < BLOCK_SIZE`) carries no skip data on the wire at all -- reaching it always means decoding it in full, lazy cursor or not, matching real `Lucene104PostingsReader.refillRemainder`. `docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` is still rejected by `lazy_cursor` with the same `Error::Unsupported` as `read_postings` -- unchanged from before this slice; the lazy cursor does not add level-1 support, it only makes level-0 skip actually *used* instead of parsed-and-discarded. Differential-tested against real `PostingsEnum.advance(target)` output for the same target set as `PostingsCursor` (`crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_fixtures.rs::{big,body}_field_lazy_advance_matches_real_lucene_postings_enum_advance`) plus a full `next_doc()` walk compared against the eager `read_postings` result byte-for-byte (`big_field_lazy_next_doc_matches_eager_read_postings`) -- all three matched on the first real-Lucene run. A hand-built unit test (`lazy_cursor_advance_skips_corrupted_earlier_block_without_decoding_it`) proves the skip is real, not just correct by coincidence: it builds a first full block whose body is deliberately corrupt (a bit-set with fewer than `BLOCK_SIZE` bits set, which `decode_full_block_body` rejects with a `Corrupted` error) followed by a valid second block, and confirms `advance()` to a target in the second block succeeds (the corrupt first block is never decoded) while `advance()` to a target in the first block surfaces the corruption error (confirming the earlier success wasn't accidental). Other unit tests cover sequential multi-block decode matching `read_postings`, the no-op advance-to-current-or-earlier contract, advance past the last doc, and the `docFreq <= 1` rejection mirroring `read_postings`'s own. **`docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` (8192) level-1 skip data is now ported for both paths, closing the last remaining `docFreq` gap** (supersedes the several "rejected with `Error::Unsupported`" / "not yet parsed" mentions of that threshold above, which described the state before this slice). Above `LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` the `.doc` stream interleaves a level-1 skip entry (`postings::read_level1_entry`, the vInt span-last-doc delta + vLong `level1DocEndFP` + `indexHasFreq`-gated `readShort` skip1EndFP/numImpactBytes + impacts bytes + pos/pay sub-fields, reverse-engineered from `Lucene104PostingsReader.skipLevel1To`, `Lucene104PostingsReader.java:674-719`) before every span of `LEVEL1_FACTOR` (32) full level-0 blocks, for as long as at least `LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` docs remain. `read_postings` (eager) consumes each entry's bytes purely to stay byte-aligned and decodes its 32 blocks like any other; `LazyDocsCursor` (lazy) tracks `level1_last_doc_id`/`level1_doc_end_fp`/`level1_doc_count_upto` and, via the new `skip_level1_to` method (mirroring Java's `skipLevel1To`, wired into `advance` exactly like Java's `moveToNextLevel0Block`/`doAdvanceShallow` call it first), `seek()`s straight to `level1_doc_end_fp` to jump a whole 32-block span whose last doc is behind the target -- never reading even the individual level-0 headers of the skipped span, the coarser counterpart to the level-0 skip-past-one-block above. Positions inherit level-1 support automatically, since `positions()` no longer hits a `docFreq` gate. Fixture-verified against real `PostingsEnum` via a new "l1" field in `fixtures/src/GenBlockTree.java` ("l1term" in 8250 docs, one level-1 span + a 58-doc remainder): full `nextDoc()`/`freq()` iteration over all 8250 docs for both eager and lazy paths, plus eager and lazy `advance()` at the exact span boundaries (occurrence 8191 = span's last doc, 8192 = first remainder doc reached only by jumping the whole span, `appendLevel1AdvanceManifest`) -- matched on the first real-Lucene run, no decode bugs caught. Hand-built unit tests cover the level-1 entry decode in isolation (all freq/pos/pay sub-fields + the docs-only short form), an eager span+tail decode, a lazy sequential walk matching eager, and a "provably skipped" test corrupting an entire 32-block span to confirm `advance()` past it never touches the corrupt bytes while a control target into the span still surfaces the corruption. **Impacts/competitive-scoring metadata (`ImpactsEnum`/`CompetitiveImpactAccumulator`) are now decoded, for both level-0 (per-block) and level-1 (per-span) impact byte runs** -- this was the last thing on the postings wire that was parsed-and-discarded purely by length rather than decoded. `postings::Impact { freq: i32, norm: i64 }` (a direct port of `Impact`'s two fields) plus `postings::decode_impacts(bytes)` implement `Lucene104PostingsReader.readImpacts`'s decode exactly: a flat sequence of vint `freqDelta`s (each storing `impact.freq - previous.freq - 1`, i.e. `+1` is added back on decode) whose low bit selects between an implicit `normDelta == 0` (the common "norm only increases by 1" case, zero extra bytes) and an explicit zigzag `normDelta` vlong. `read_full_block_header` (level-0) and `read_level1_entry` (level-1) both now decode their impacts byte run into `FullBlockHeader::impacts`/`Level1Entry::impacts` instead of `skip()`-ing past it -- the length-prefix parsing (vint for level-0, `readShort` for level-1) is unchanged, only the payload is now real. **Exposed on both cursors** (impacts are inherently a "what's coming up from here" query that only makes sense against a position, matching real `ImpactsEnum.getImpacts()` -- both `LazyDocsCursor` and `PostingsCursor` have one): `LazyDocsCursor::level0_impacts()`/`level1_impacts()` for the genuinely lazy, decode-on-demand cursor, `PostingsCursor::level0_impacts()`/`level1_impacts()` for the eager, already-materialized one (a lookup into `Postings::level0_impacts`/`level1_impacts`, see below): `level0_impacts()` returns the current level-0 block's competitive pairs (empty once positioned in the tail block, which carries no level-0 impacts on the wire at all -- deliberately *not* replicating Java's synthetic "everything is competitive" `(Integer.MAX_VALUE, 1)` sentinel for that state, since this port exposes decoded wire data rather than reconstructing `ImpactsEnum`'s full multi-level fallback semantics) and `level1_impacts()` returns the current level-1 span's merged pairs (empty below `LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` docs or past the last span). Differential-tested against real `TermsEnum.impacts(PostingsEnum.FREQS)`/`ImpactsEnum.getImpacts(level)` ground truth (`fixtures/src/GenBlockTree.java::appendImpactsManifest`, `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_fixtures.rs::{big,l1}_field_impacts_match_real_lucene_impacts_enum`) for "big"/"everywhere" (level-0 only, three occurrences within its one full block, freq/norm both varying 1..4) and "l1"/"l1term" (level-0 varying across different blocks of the same level-1 span, level-1 merged and identical across all three sampled occurrences since they share one span) -- matched on the first real-Lucene run. Unit tests for `decode_impacts` itself cover: empty input, a single implicit-norm-delta entry, multiple entries mixing implicit and explicit (including a full-width `i64::MAX` norm) deltas via a test-only encoder mirroring `Lucene104PostingsWriter.writeImpacts` exactly, and two truncation/corruption cases (a freqDelta vint missing its continuation byte, an explicit-norm-delta flag with no following zlong). **Impacts are now threaded through the eager path too**: `DocInput::read_postings` captures each level-0 block's and level-1 span's already-decoded impacts (no second decode pass -- it just retains what it was discarding) into `Postings::level0_impacts`/`level1_impacts` (`Vec<(last_doc_id_covered, Impacts)>`, ascending/contiguous so a lookup is a single `partition_point` binary search), and `PostingsCursor::level0_impacts`/`level1_impacts` look up the entry covering `doc_id()` the same way `LazyDocsCursor::level0_impacts`/`level1_impacts` report the block/span covering its own current position -- same query, same empty-cases contract (tail block, below `LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS`, no freqs, not yet/no-longer positioned), just against pre-materialized data instead of decode-on-demand state. Verified against the same real-Lucene ground truth as the lazy path (`crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_fixtures.rs`'s `big_field_impacts_match_real_lucene_impacts_enum`/`l1_field_impacts_match_real_lucene_impacts_enum`, extended to assert `PostingsCursor`'s new accessors too, not just cross-check against `LazyDocsCursor`). **Still deferred**: no `ImpactsSource`/full `ImpactsEnum` trait hierarchy (`numLevels()`, `getDocIdUpTo()`) is implemented for either cursor, only the two concrete accessors this port actually needs. **Query-execution-side consumer of these impacts (this task)**: `lucene-search/src/similarity.rs::max_score_for_impacts` computes the true upper-bound BM25 score obtainable from any one `(freq, norm)` pair in a block's/span's competitive impacts list (max over entries of `idf * tf_norm`, not just the list's last entry -- freq and decoded field length move the score in opposite directions, so the true max can be an interior entry; see that function's doc comment and its `max_score_for_impacts_takes_the_true_max_not_just_the_last_entry` test). This is deliberately scoped to **the bound computation itself plus a test-only demonstration harness** (`similarity.rs`'s `assert_block_pruning_matches_brute_force`, exercised by `block_pruning_via_max_score_matches_brute_force_top_1`/`_top_2_spans_both_blocks`) that proves, for a synthetic `Postings` list, that skipping a whole level-0 block once its bound can't beat a `TopDocsCollector`'s current worst kept score never changes the top-`n` result -- **not** wired into the production `term_doc_scores`/`search_term_query_scored` path in `lib.rs`. That path calls `DocInput::read_postings` (the eager, whole-term-materializing decode), so every block's docs/freqs are already fully decoded before scoring even starts; real pruning there would additionally need switching that loop onto `LazyDocsCursor`'s decode-on-demand blocks so a skip actually avoids `ForUtil`/`PForUtil` work, not just a re-score. **Full multi-clause WAND/MAXSCORE dynamic pruning across a `BooleanQuery`'s clauses (maintaining a running minimum-competitive-score threshold across clauses) remains substantial, wholly unimplemented future work**, matching PLAN.md's Phase 3 notes -- that earlier task only reached the single-clause, single-block bound-and-prove-safety increment. **`max_score_for_impacts` is now wired into a real, narrow single-clause query-execution path** (a later task): `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::search_term_query_scored_maxscore` is a `TopDocsCollector`-only sibling of `search_term_query_scored` that streams a `TermQuery`'s postings through `LazyDocsCursor` instead of `DocInput::read_postings`'s eager, whole-term materialization, and, once `TopDocsCollector::min_competitive_score()` (new method) reports the collector is holding a full top-`n`, skips a whole level-0 block outright (`advance()`-ing straight past it, never running `ForUtil`/`PForUtil` decode on it) whenever `max_score_for_impacts`'s bound for that block can't beat the threshold -- real MAXSCORE-shaped dynamic pruning at query-execution time, not just the bound proven in isolation. Falls back to calling `search_term_query_scored` verbatim (never a silently different codepath) whenever the fast path isn't available: no `.doc` file opened, `docFreq <= 1` (no `.doc` bytes at all for a singleton, so nothing to stream lazily), or `IndexOptions::None` (the only index option `LazyDocsCursor` doesn't support, now that `DocsAndCustomFreqs` is accepted, see the postings-codec `DocsAndCustomFreqs` support note above). Differential-tested against the checked-in real-Lucene `big`/"everywhere" fixture (`docFreq == 300`, real Java-written impacts, see above) with real per-doc norms opened (`crates/lucene-search/src/lib.rs::tests::maxscore_lazy_path_matches_eager_path_on_real_fixture_and_actually_skips_blocks`) -- deliberately not `norms: None`, since the impacts' bound is only a valid upper bound against the score formula that consumes those same real norm bytes, and comparing it against the unrelated `UNNORMED_FIELD_LENGTH` fallback would be an unproven mix rather than a documented approximation. Asserts byte-for-byte-identical `TopDocsCollector::top_docs()` between the eager and maxscore paths across several `top_n` values, plus a test-only skip counter (`test_only_maxscore_block_skip_counter`, compiled only under `cfg(test)`, incremented at the real skip call site) proving real block skips happened for small `top_n` and proving zero skips happened when `top_n` equals the full `docFreq` (nothing safely skippable there) -- ruling out both a silent correctness change and a vacuously-passing "skip branch never taken" test. **Deliberately not attempted**: multi-clause `BooleanQuery` WAND (unchanged, see above) and any collector besides `TopDocsCollector` (no min-competitive-score threshold exists for a plain `Collector`/other `ScoringCollector` impl, so there is nothing to prune against for those callers). **`for_util.rs` now has real, production (not test-only) encode-side primitives**: `for_util::for_encode` (`ForUtil.encode`, bit-pack 256 already-fit values at a caller-chosen width) and `for_util::pfor_encode` (`PForUtil.encode`, the full histogram-based bit-width search that picks the smallest width keeping at most `MAX_EXCEPTIONS` (7) patched outliers, plus the all-equal vint fast path) -- both are direct ports of the real Java encoders, independently round-tripped in `for_util.rs`'s own tests (exactly-256-entries no-exceptions, all-zero, full-31-bit, few-outliers-with-patched-exceptions, and a direct `pfor_encode`-then-`for_decode` cross-check). **Not yet wired into `postings_writer.rs`**: the writer's `write_single_field`/`write_fields` still reject any term with `docFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE` or `total_term_freq >= BLOCK_SIZE` (`Error::DocFreqTooLarge`/`Error::TotalTermFreqTooLarge`, see that module's own doc comment) -- calling `for_encode`/`pfor_encode` from the writer to actually emit full 256-entry blocks (matching what `postings.rs`'s reader already fully decodes, see above) additionally needs the writer to build the level-0 skip-list metadata (`level0NumBytes`/`blockLength`/impacts bytes) the reader expects to find alongside each full block, which the writer does not build today. That remaining wiring is out of scope for this slice and tracked here rather than silently left unimplemented. **M2 sweep `b2-packed`:** three fidelity/robustness fixes in `for_util.rs`. (1) `bits_required` now returns at least 1, matching `PackedInts.unsignedBitsRequired`'s documented floor; with the old 0-for-zero, `pfor_encode`'s bit-width search could settle on `bitsPerValue == 0` for any block whose non-zero values number at most 7 and fit in 8 bits, where real `PForUtil` stops at 1 -- both decode to the same values and Lucene reads either, but the bytes differed from what Lucene writes for the same input. (2) `ForUtil::decode` rejects a `bits_per_value` outside `1..=32` instead of panicking in `decode_slow`'s slice split -- `postings`' full-block header is a signed byte straight off disk, so a corrupt `.doc` could reach it with up to 127. (3) `for_encode`/`ForUtil::encode` now bit-pack **in place** into the caller's array, as `ForUtil.encode(int[], ...)` does, instead of copying it; every encoded block was paying a 1 KiB memcpy Java does not. `ForUtil::encode` also reuses the instance's scratch buffer (Java's `ForUtil.tmp`) rather than zeroing a stack-local 1 KiB array per block -- `postings_writer` still calls the one-shot free functions per block, so it does not yet get that second saving; see `benches/for_util_decode.rs`'s `for_util/for_encode` `oneshot` vs `reused` arms. Still not ported: `PForUtil.skip` (no caller -- this port never skips a block without decoding it). **M2 sweep (b5-postings).** Two `LazyDocsCursor` state-machine defects fixed. (1) `next_doc()` answered out of the previously decoded block after an `advance_shallow()` left a block positioned-but-not-unpacked, handing back a document behind the shallow position while `level0_impacts()` already described the *new* block -- Lucene gates its `nextDoc` on exactly this (`if (doc == level0LastDocID || needsRefilling)`); `advance()` already carried the guard and `next_doc()` was missed when `advance_shallow` landed. (2) `advance()` indexed one past the 256-entry block buffer -- a panic, not an error -- when a corrupt `.doc` block body's decoded doc IDs fall short of the `docDelta` its own level-0 header claims (nothing on the wire ties the two together); now `Corrupted`. Both regression-tested and both confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code. Also **the tail block no longer allocates**: `read_tail_block` decodes into caller-owned `&mut [i32]` slices (the cursor's own `block_docs`/`block_freqs`, Lucene's `refillRemainder`-into-`docBuffer` shape) and reads group-varints into a stack array, removing three heap allocations and two 256-entry copies *per term* -- a term with `docFreq < BLOCK_SIZE` is entirely a tail block, so this was on the common path. New `postings/lazy_cursor/{tail_block,full_blocks}` criterion group: 200-doc walk 1.27 -> 1.00 us, 2600-doc walk 5.43 -> 4.75 us (noisy machine, +/-10% CIs). **`PostingsEnum` flags ported by sweep batch `c8-tv-chunking`** (b5's F6): `postings::PostingsFlags` is the one distinction `Lucene104PostingsReader` derives from the flag mask before touching `.doc` (`needsFreq = indexHasFreq && featureRequested(flags, FREQS)`) -- the rest gate `.pos`/`.pay`, which are a separate call here. `DocInput::{read_postings_with_flags,lazy_cursor_with_flags}` and `blocktree::FieldTerms::{postings_with_flags,lazy_postings_with_flags}` take it; with `PostingsFlags::DocsOnly` every full block's frequency block is stepped over via the new `for_util::pfor_skip` (`PForUtil.skip`) and the tail block skips its freq-exception vints (`PostingsUtil.readVIntBlock`'s `decodeFreq == false` branch), and every reported frequency is 1. Wired into `term_delete::resolve_term_doc_ids`; the `lucene-search` constant-score/filter call sites are left to a batch that owns that crate. **Recorded open**: level-1 impacts are decoded even for spans being skipped (one per 8192 docs, bounded). Confirmed with `ls`/grep that Lucene104 has **no** `MultiLevelSkipList`/`skipOffset` -- the inline level-0/level-1 structure this file implements is the whole skip story. See `docs/sweep/m2/b5-postings.md`. **c15 sweep**: added `postings::read_occurrences_for_docs` and `blocktree::FieldTerms::{occurrences_for_docs, occurrences_for_doc}` -- `PostingsEnum.advance(doc)` followed by `nextPosition()`/`startOffset()`/`endOffset()`/`getPayload()` for one document (or a sorted batch), which is what `PostingsOffsetStrategy` and the phrase paths actually want. `read_positions_for_docs` was reimplemented on the same walker, which now steps over a `.pos`/`.pay` block holding no wanted occurrence with `PForUtil.skip` and stops reading entirely once the last wanted document is behind it (43x on a five-million-document term, measured). Still unported and recorded there: the `.pos`/`.pay` file pointers in `.doc`'s level-0/level-1 skip records, which is why a wanted-documents walk still needs the term's whole doc list. **c20 sweep**: the `.pos`/`.pay` file pointers in `.doc`'s level-0/level-1 skip records are now **read and used**, and written. `read_full_block_header`/`read_level1_entry` retain `posEndFPDelta`/`posBufferUpto`/`payEndFPDelta` instead of discarding them; `LazyDocsCursor` accumulates them exactly as `BlockPostingsEnum` does (`skipLevel1To`'s `level0PosEndFP = level1PosEndFP`, `skipLevel0To`'s pre-header snapshot, `readLevel0PosData`) and exposes the result as `position_origin()` -- `seekPosData`'s arguments plus `accumulatePendingPositions`' in-block frequency sum. `postings::read_occurrences_for_doc` (behind `blocktree::FieldTerms::occurrences_for_doc`) is Java's `advance(doc)` + `skipPositions` + `nextPosition` trio: it reads `.doc`'s skip records down to one 256-document block and the `.pos`/`.pay` blocks holding that document's occurrences, and nothing else -- no doc-list decode at all. `TermMetadata::last_pos_block_offset` (`lastPosBlockFP`) becomes load-bearing on the read side for the first time, since a walk that jumps into the middle of `.pos` cannot re-derive the full-block/vint-tail split from a running occurrence count. The batch form (`read_occurrences_for_docs`/`read_positions_for_docs`) keeps its frequency-sum addressing on purpose: its callers intersect doc lists first, so they have already decoded the list the sum walks. Pinned by `fixtures/data/postings_skip_index` (`GenPostingsSkip.java`: 8500 documents, a dense term and a sparser one, with offsets and payloads, so a level-1 entry, 33 level-0 headers and a vint tail all exist, and so the level-1 `posBufferUpto` is 253 rather than the 0 a degenerate frequency cycle would have produced), whose ground truth is Java's own fresh-`PostingsEnum`-per-document `advance(doc)`. b5's F7 (level-1 impacts decoded for every span being skipped) is closed in the same batch: `Level1Entry` now holds the impacts run undecoded, as `FullBlockHeader` already did, and `skip_level1_to` decodes it only on the span it stops in (`skipLevel1To`'s `needsImpacts && level1LastDocID >= target`) -- with the pointers wired up it had become the *dominant* cost of a skip-driven `advance`, since reaching the last document of a five-million-document term crosses 610 level-1 entries. See `docs/sweep/m2/c20-postings-skip.md`. **`c27-arith-codecs-2` (2026-08-29)**: two decode defects. A `.pay` payload length is a `PForUtil`-decoded `u32` held as `i32`, so a corrupt block yields a *negative* one; `payload_upto + len` either panicked outright or **wrapped below `payload_upto`, passed the `end > payload_bytes.len()` guard**, and panicked in the slice -- reachable from the public `read_positions` on any payload-carrying field with two full `.pos` blocks. And `readImpacts`' `freq += 1 + (freqDelta >>> 1)` had the `>>>` right but the `1 +` as a plain `i32` add, so a five-byte varint decoding to `-2` panics in a debug build on every freqs-indexing term, before the accumulator's existing `wrapping_add` ever sees it. `ForUtil::encode` separately turned out to be a **release-mode infinite loop** at `bitsPerValue == 0` -- a faithful transliteration of Java's `for (shift -= bitsPerValue; shift >= 0; shift -= bitsPerValue)`, and the one failure `catch_unwind` at the FFI boundary cannot convert into a Java exception. `PForUtil`'s `static { assert ForUtil.BLOCK_SIZE <= 256 }`, which is what makes `pfor_decode`'s `ints[read_byte() as usize]` in bounds, was never ported and is now a `const _: () = assert!(...)`. Re-signed `.doc` byte-flip sweeps reject 589/744 and 49/60 with zero panics. |
| `util/fst/FST` (`FST.Arc`/`BytesReader`/`readArc`/`findTargetArc`/`readMetadata` -- on-heap arc-lookup read side) | `lucene-codecs/src/fst.rs` | **ported, scoped to lookup-only.** Lives in `lucene-codecs`, not `lucene-util` as PLAN.md §1's crate table originally sketched -- FST metadata parsing needs `CodecUtil.checkHeader` (`lucene-store::codec_util`), and per the `architecture` skill's strict downward dependency graph (`util ← store ← codecs`), `lucene-util` cannot depend on `lucene-store`, so a module needing both belongs in `lucene-codecs`. Ported: `Fst::read` (metadata + on-heap body, `OnHeapFSTStore`'s single-`Vec<u8>` case, copying the body into a freshly-allocated `Vec<u8>`) and `Fst::read_borrowed` (identical metadata parsing, but the body is a zero-copy `&'a [u8]` borrowed straight out of the caller's own buffer via `SliceInput::slice`/`seek`, no allocation for the body at all). **What "off-heap" honestly means here**: this port's `Directory` abstraction already has a real zero-copy backend, `lucene_store::directory::MmapDirectory` -- an actual OS `mmap(2)` via `memmap2`, returning `Input::Mapped` which `Deref`s to `&[u8]` without ever materializing an owned `Vec<u8>` copy of the file. `Fst::read_borrowed` is the missing FST-level plumbing that lets an `Fst<'a>` be constructed directly over such a buffer without `Fst::read`'s unconditional extra copy of the body -- for an `Fst` opened over an `MmapDirectory`-backed `Input`, that reproduces real Lucene's `OffHeapFSTStore` cost model: bytes stay in the OS page cache, faulted in lazily as arcs are actually visited, never doubled in the process heap. This module itself performs no `mmap(2)` call -- that already exists in `lucene-store` -- so the precise framing is "a borrowing/zero-copy FST constructor that composes with this port's existing mmap `Directory` backend," not "this file re-implements memory-mapping." `Fst::read` and `Fst::read_borrowed` are proven to agree on every lookup for the same bytes (`crates/lucene-codecs/src/fst.rs::tests::read_borrowed_matches_read_for_same_bytes`, `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_fixtures.rs::read_borrowed_matches_read_on_real_fixture` against the real Lucene-written fixture), and an end-to-end test opens a real `MmapDirectory`-backed file this way (`read_borrowed_over_a_real_mmap_directory_input`). The reverse `BytesReader` cursor (`ReverseBytesReader`), and `Fst::get` (`Util.get(FST, BytesRef)`-equivalent: walk `findTargetArc` once per key byte, then check `arc.is_final()`). Single output type: `ByteSequenceOutputs` (`Vec<u8>` in Rust) -- the type real Lucene's term index FST actually uses to map term-prefix byte sequences to concatenated block-pointer bytes, confirmed by checking `ByteSequenceOutputs.java` directly rather than assumed. `Fst::read_label` decodes all three `INPUT_TYPE`s (`BYTE1`: raw byte; `BYTE2`: fixed 2-byte value, little-endian from `VERSION_LITTLE_ENDIAN` on and byte-swapped before that, matching `FST.readLabel`'s own version-gated branch; `BYTE4`: `vint`, not a fixed 4-byte value), and `find_target_arc`'s label-domain arithmetic (binary search / direct addressing / continuous dispatch) never assumed a byte-sized alphabet in the first place. `Fst::get`/`Fst::iter` (the `Vec<u8>`-keyed convenience API real Lucene's `BytesRefFSTEnum`/`Util.get(FST, BytesRef)` mirror) still reject non-`BYTE1` FSTs -- not an arbitrary restriction, but because a `u8` key element genuinely cannot represent a UTF-16 code unit (`BYTE2`) or a full Unicode code point (`BYTE4`). `Fst::get_labels`/`Fst::seek_exact_labels` (`Util.get(FST, IntsRef)`-equivalent) and `Fst::iter_labels`/`FstEnum::next_labels`/`seek_ceil_labels`/`seek_floor_labels`/`seek_exact_labels` (`IntsRefFSTEnum`-equivalent full walk/seek, `i32`-keyed) now widen lookup and enumeration to any `INPUT_TYPE`, reusing the exact same `find_target_arc`/`FstEnum` engine `BYTE1` callers already exercise -- `FstEnum`'s internal `labels`/`target` storage widened from `u8` to `i32` to stop truncating labels outside a byte's range; the existing byte-keyed public methods narrow back to `u8` only at their own boundary, which `Fst::iter`'s `BYTE1`-only guard makes lossless. **Verified against real Lucene-written `BYTE2`/`BYTE4` fixtures, not just hand-built ones**: `fixtures/src/GenFstWideInputTypes.java` builds two real on-heap FSTs via `FSTCompiler.Builder`'s public `INPUT_TYPE` parameter and `FSTCompiler.add(IntsRef, T)` (a genuinely wider-than-byte alphabet, unlike every other `GenFst*.java` generator here, which derives `IntsRef` labels from a `BytesRef`) -- one `BYTE2` FST with UTF-16-range labels (including combining-mark and CJK code points, up to `0xFFFF`) and one `BYTE4` FST with full-Unicode-range labels (including supplementary-plane code points up to `0x10FFFF`, well past `BYTE2`'s own ceiling). `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_wide_input_types_fixtures.rs` opens both through this port's unmodified `Fst::read` and confirms `get_labels`/`seek_exact_labels` resolve every present key and reject absent ones, `iter_labels` enumerates every key in ascending order, `seek_ceil_labels`/`seek_floor_labels`/`seek_exact_labels` agree with the full enumeration, and the byte-keyed `get`/`iter` reject both fixtures outright. Hand-built unit tests in `fst.rs::tests` (`read_label_decodes_byte2_little_endian`, `read_label_decodes_byte2_legacy_big_endian`, `read_label_decodes_byte4_as_vint`) additionally pin down `read_label`'s exact byte-level encoding for each `INPUT_TYPE`/version combination in isolation, including the pre-`VERSION_LITTLE_ENDIAN` byte-swapped `BYTE2` case no real fixture from this Lucene version can exercise (current-version `FSTCompiler` only ever writes `VERSION_CURRENT`). **What's still not ported**: real Lucene's genuinely wider-alphabet `FSTCompiler`/`build_fst` construction for `BYTE2`/`BYTE4` (this port's own `build_fst`/`TrieNode` remain hardcoded to single-byte labels, so only the *read* side is widened) -- see `IntsRefFstEnum`'s own doc comment (below) for why building a genuinely wide-alphabet `Fst` from scratch here remains deferred. **FST construction (`build_fst`/`write_fst`, same file) is now ported, scoped to a simplified builder, not real `FSTCompiler`.** Real Lucene's `FSTCompiler` is an *incremental*, memory-bounded builder (a stack of `UnCompiledNode`s, a node hash table for suffix sharing/minimization, output pushing down shared prefixes) -- reproducing that algorithm exactly remains deferred, much larger future work. What's ported instead: `build_fst(&[(Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>)])` takes the *entire* sorted key/output sequence in memory, builds an ordinary trie, and serializes it bottom-up directly into the same list-encoded (variable-length arc) node/arc byte format `Fst::read`/`find_target_arc`/`read_arc` (above, unmodified) already parse -- explicit `vlong` targets everywhere (no `BIT_TARGET_NEXT` physical-adjacency compaction), each key's whole output placed on the single arc leading to its accepting node (no output-pushing down shared prefixes). **Suffix sharing / minimization is implemented**, though: `build_node` maintains a `NodeHash` (`HashMap<Vec<ArcSignature>, i64>`) keyed on each node's fully-resolved arc signature (labels, finality/final-outputs, child target addresses) and, before writing any node's bytes, checks whether a structurally-identical node has already been frozen elsewhere in the tree -- if so, its existing address is reused instead of writing a duplicate copy. This achieves the same *result* as real `FSTCompiler`'s incremental `NodeHash`-based freeze-and-dedup (two keys sharing a suffix, e.g. `"cat"`/`"bat"` sharing `"at"`, collapse to one shared node reachable from both `'c'` and `'b'`), just discovered by hash-consing the whole in-memory trie at once rather than incrementally as keys stream in -- the incremental/memory-bounded construction itself remains the deferred part, not the dedup algorithm. Proven with three tests in `fst.rs::tests`: `build_fst_shares_identical_suffix_node_across_two_prefixes` (`"bat"`/`"cat"`, asserting the node reached after `'b'` and after `'c'` are the literal same address), `build_fst_shares_classic_mop_pop_stop_top_suffix_node` (real Lucene's own canonical `FSTCompiler` suffix-sharing example -- `mop`/`moth`/`pop`/`star`/`stop`/`top` -- asserting the nodes after `"po"`/`"sto"`/`"to"` are all the same shared address while the node after `"mo"` is correctly distinct, plus full get/seek/enumerate regression over that now-shared-node FST), and `build_fst_dedup_keeps_compiled_size_sublinear_in_repeated_suffix_count` (8 distinct 2-byte prefixes sharing one 44-byte tail; asserts the compiled size is far below the naive no-sharing linear lower bound and that every prefix's node address is identical). `write_fst(&Fst)` serializes a built `Fst` back through the exact codec-header + `FSTMetadata.save` layout `Fst::read` parses, for a full round trip through the unmodified reader rather than only the in-memory `Fst` struct. **Verified against real Lucene, not just self-round-trip.** In addition to the self-round-trip unit tests in `crates/lucene-codecs/src/fst.rs::tests` (7-key set matching `fixtures/src/GenFst.java`'s shape, a single key, an empty key set, a key set including the empty-string key, a proper-prefix-of-another-key case, a 200-key set forcing multi-byte `vlong` targets, and unsorted/duplicate-key input rejection -- every case opens via the real unmodified `Fst::read` and confirms every present key resolves correctly and every absent key is rejected via `Fst::get`), the reverse direction is now covered too: `crates/lucene-codecs/examples/write_fst_fixture.rs` builds the same 7-key set via `build_fst`/`write_fst` and writes it to disk, and `fixtures/src/VerifyFst.java` opens that file through real Lucene's `FST.read(Path, ByteSequenceOutputs)` and looks up every key (7 present, 8 absent) via real `Util.get(FST, BytesRef)`.
