A second, larger fixture (`large/`, 200 keys) is also written and verified, forcing the same multi-byte-`vlong`-node-address-target encoding `build_fst_many_keys_forces_multi_byte_vlong_targets` stress-tests via self-round-trip -- previously never checked against a real Lucene reader, since the small 7-key set is too small to reach that path. Both ran and passed: `VerifyFst OK (<dir>): 7 present keys resolved, 8 absent keys rejected` and `VerifyFst OK (large): 200 present keys resolved, 3 absent keys rejected` -- concrete proof that `build_fst`'s simplified, non-minimizing, non-suffix-sharing construction is still a wire-format-valid FST that unmodified Lucene application code accepts (at both small and larger scale, with and without multi-byte node-address targets), not just bytes this port's own reader happens to tolerate. **Fixed-length-arc node decoding: `ARCS_FOR_BINARY_SEARCH` is now supported on read.** `Fst::find_target_arc` ports `FST.java`'s `ARCS_FOR_BINARY_SEARCH` branch verbatim: node header (`numArcs`/`bytesPerArc` vints), then a sparse binary search over fixed-size arc slots addressed as `posArcsStart - bytesPerArc * idx`; `read_arc`'s `BIT_TARGET_NEXT` branch also knows this encoding's "skip to before the arcs array" target rule (`Arc` grew `bytes_per_arc`/`num_arcs`/`pos_arcs_start` fields, defaulting to the list-node case). **Verified against a real Lucene-written fixture, not just a hand-built one**: `fixtures/src/GenFstBinarySearch.java` builds an `FST<BytesRef>` with `allowFixedLengthArcs(true)` and seven single-byte root labels spread widely enough (1, 40, 80, 120, 160, 200, 240) that real `FSTCompiler`'s own cost heuristic (`shouldExpandNodeWithDirectAddressing`) picks binary search over direct addressing for the root node -- the generator self-checks this by reading the root arc back and asserting `arc.toString()` contains `"(bs)"` before ever writing the fixture, so a future Lucene version quietly changing that heuristic and picking a different encoding would fail fixture generation loudly rather than silently testing the wrong thing. `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_binary_search_fixtures.rs` opens that fixture through this port's unmodified `Fst::read`/`Fst::read_borrowed` and confirms all 7 present keys resolve and 8 absent keys (labels strictly between/outside the chosen ones) are rejected. Hand-built node tests (`fst.rs::tests::binary_search_node_finds_every_label`/`binary_search_node_rejects_absent_labels`) additionally pin down the byte-level field layout in isolation. **`ARCS_FOR_DIRECT_ADDRESSING` is now also supported on read.** `Fst::find_target_arc_direct_addressing` ports `FST.java`'s `ARCS_FOR_DIRECT_ADDRESSING` branch of `findTargetArc` verbatim: node header (label-range `numArcs`/`bytesPerArc` vints), a presence bit-table (`getNumPresenceBytes(labelRange)` bytes, one bit per label in the range, skipped rather than eagerly read -- matching `FST.readPresenceBytes`'s own "don't read them here" comment), then `firstLabel`, then a direct `labelToMatch - firstLabel` index into the range, rejected outright if out of bounds or (if in bounds) not actually present in the bit-table. `Arc` grew `node_flags`/`bit_table_start`/`first_label`/`presence_index` fields; `read_arc`'s label decode (implicit `first_label + arc_idx`, no on-disk label byte at all) and `BIT_TARGET_NEXT` target-skip arithmetic (skip past only the *present* arcs, i.e. the bit-table's set-bit count, not the full label range) both now dispatch on `node_flags` since two encodings share a non-zero `bytes_per_arc`. Four free functions (`bit_table_is_bit_set`/`bit_table_count_bits`/`bit_table_count_bits_up_to`/`bit_table_next_bit_set`) port `BitTableUtil`'s corresponding methods, byte-at-a-time rather than `BitTableUtil`'s own 8-bytes-at-a-time `readLong` fast path (a deliberate simplification, not a correctness gap -- see the `rust-performance` skill on not transliterating Java's perf tricks where a simpler equivalent suffices); `BitTableUtil.previousBitSet`/`FST.readLastArcByDirectAddressing` are now also ported (`bit_table_previous_bit_set`/`read_last_arc_by_direct_addressing`), needed once seek support (below) required "last arc of this node" access for `seek_floor`'s backtracking. **Verified against a real Lucene-written fixture, not just a hand-built one**: `fixtures/src/GenFstDirectAddressing.java` builds an `FST<BytesRef>` with `allowFixedLengthArcs(true)` and seven single-byte root labels chosen dense but not fully contiguous (`a`-`f` plus `h`, skipping `g`) so real `FSTCompiler`'s cost heuristic picks direct addressing over both binary search (too sparse a benefit) and `ARCS_FOR_CONTINUOUS` (which `FSTCompiler` always prefers instead once a label range has zero gaps) -- the generator self-checks this via `arc.toString()` containing `"(da)"` before ever writing the fixture. `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_direct_addressing_fixtures.rs` opens that fixture through this port's unmodified `Fst::read`/`Fst::read_borrowed`/`Fst::iter` and confirms all 7 present keys resolve, 6 absent keys (including `g`, the one gap *inside* the range, specifically exercising the bit-table rejection path rather than just the range-bounds check) are rejected, and enumeration visits every present key in ascending order skipping the gap. Hand-built node tests (`fst.rs::tests::direct_addressing_node_finds_every_present_label`/`direct_addressing_node_rejects_absent_labels_in_and_around_range`/`direct_addressing_node_enumerates_every_present_label_in_ascending_order`) additionally pin down the byte-level field layout (presence bit-table, `first_label`, no per-arc label byte) in isolation. **`ARCS_FOR_CONTINUOUS` is now also supported on read.** `Fst::find_target_arc_continuous` ports `FST.java`'s `ARCS_FOR_CONTINUOUS` branch of `findTargetArc` verbatim: node header (`numArcs`/`bytesPerArc` vints), then `firstLabel` (no presence bit-table at all, unlike `ARCS_FOR_DIRECT_ADDRESSING` -- every label in `[firstLabel, firstLabel + numArcs)` is guaranteed present by construction), then a direct `labelToMatch - firstLabel` index into the range, rejected outright only if out of bounds, otherwise decoded via the same `read_next_real_arc` fixed-size-slot arithmetic `ARCS_FOR_BINARY_SEARCH` already established (`posArcsStart - bytesPerArc * arcIdx`, since both encodings share that addressing once a node header is parsed). `read_arc`'s label decode (implicit `first_label + arc_idx`, shared with `ARCS_FOR_DIRECT_ADDRESSING`) and `BIT_TARGET_NEXT` target-skip arithmetic (uses the full label range, `arc.num_arcs`, same as `ARCS_FOR_BINARY_SEARCH` -- there's no presence count to look up) both dispatch on `node_flags` accordingly. **Verified against a real Lucene-written fixture, not just a hand-built one**: `fixtures/src/GenFstContinuous.java` builds an `FST<BytesRef>` with `allowFixedLengthArcs(true)` and seven single-byte root labels that are fully contiguous (`a`-`g`, no gaps) -- once a label range has zero gaps, real `FSTCompiler`'s cost heuristic always prefers `ARCS_FOR_CONTINUOUS` over both direct addressing and binary search, since no presence bitset is needed at all -- the generator self-checks this via `arc.toString()` containing `"(cs)"` before ever writing the fixture. `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_continuous_fixtures.rs` opens that fixture through this port's unmodified `Fst::read`/`Fst::read_borrowed`/`Fst::iter` and confirms all 7 present keys resolve, 6 absent keys (all strictly outside the label range, since `ARCS_FOR_CONTINUOUS` has no in-range gap to test) are rejected, and enumeration visits every key in ascending order. Hand-built node tests (`fst.rs::tests::continuous_node_finds_every_present_label`/`continuous_node_rejects_labels_outside_the_range`/`continuous_node_enumerates_every_label_in_ascending_order`) additionally pin down the byte-level field layout (no presence bit-table, no per-arc label byte, `first_label` plus direct indexing) in isolation. With this, all three of `FST.java`'s fixed-length-arc node encodings (`ARCS_FOR_BINARY_SEARCH`, `ARCS_FOR_DIRECT_ADDRESSING`, `ARCS_FOR_CONTINUOUS`) are now supported on read -- nothing in the fixed-length-arc node family remains `Error::Unsupported`. Real `FSTCompiler`'s incremental/memory-bounded construction and output-pushing down shared prefixes remain deferred (suffix sharing/minimization itself is now done, see above); and `IntsRefFSTEnum` (`IntsRef`-input enumeration) is also now ported, as `IntsRefFstEnum` -- but honestly scoped as a byte-keyed `BYTE1` `Fst` wearing an `IntsRef`-shaped API (each `i32` key element big-endian-encoded to 4 bytes at the API boundary via `encode_ints_key`/`decode_ints_key`, non-negative elements only), not real Lucene's genuinely wider-alphabet `IntsRefFSTEnum` over a `BYTE4`-keyed FST -- that would require a second, wider-label node/arc codec (`TrieNode`/`read_label`/`find_target_arc`'s direct-addressing and continuous arithmetic all hardcode single-byte labels today), out of scope here. `IntsRefFstEnum` wraps the existing `FstEnum` by composition (`build_fst_from_ints::<O>` delegates to `build_fst_typed`; `next`/`seek_ceil`/`seek_floor`/`seek_exact` are thin encode/decode wrappers around `FstEnum`'s own), so none of the traversal algorithm is duplicated. Tested in `fst.rs::tests` (`encode_decode_ints_key_round_trips`, `encode_ints_key_big_endian_preserves_numeric_order`, `ints_ref_fst_enum_over_empty_fst_yields_nothing`, `ints_ref_fst_enum_over_single_key_fst_yields_that_one_key`, `ints_ref_fst_enum_multi_key_shared_prefix_and_suffix_ascending_order`, `ints_ref_fst_enum_seek_ceil_floor_exact`, `encode_ints_key_rejects_a_negative_element_even_in_release_builds` -- the non-negative restriction is a real `assert!`, not `debug_assert!`, so it is not compiled out under `--release`). **Not yet consumed by any real caller**: `IntsRefFstEnum`/`build_fst_from_ints`/`encode_ints_key`/`decode_ints_key` have no production call site anywhere else in this workspace today, only their own unit tests -- this is new API surface, not wired into a synonym/suggest feature yet. **FST off-heap seek path validation (this task)**: investigated whether `seek_ceil`/`seek_floor`/`seek_exact` (`FstEnum`) and `Fst::iter` enumeration had ever been exercised over a `Fst` opened via `read_borrowed` (off-heap/zero-copy), as opposed to only `Fst::read` (owned). Found a real testing gap, not an implementation bug: `tests/fst_seek_fixtures.rs` and `fst.rs`'s own seek unit tests (`fst_seek_exact_on_present_and_absent_keys`, `fst_enum_seek_ceil_*`, `fst_enum_seek_floor_*`, etc.) exhaustively cover every node encoding and backtracking scenario, but every single one builds its `Fst` via `Fst::read`; conversely every existing `read_borrowed` test (`read_borrowed_matches_read_for_same_bytes`, `read_borrowed_over_a_real_mmap_directory_input`, `read_borrowed_body_is_a_slice_not_a_second_owned_buffer`) only ever called `get`/`get_typed`, never seek or `iter`. Added `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_borrowed_seek_fixtures.rs`, which loads every existing fixture directory (list, binary search, direct addressing, continuous, non-root-array-node, and all three floor-backtrack fixtures) via both `Fst::read` and `Fst::read_borrowed` and asserts `seek_ceil`/`seek_floor`/`seek_exact`/`iter` produce byte-identical results from both, plus one test that opens a borrowed `Fst` over a real `MmapDirectory`-backed file and drives the full seek/enumeration surface (including a backtracking `seek_floor` call) against the mmap-backed slice. All new tests passed on the first run with no code changes needed to `fst.rs` itself: the borrowed path's node/arc reads all go through `SliceInput::slice(from, to)` (arbitrary positional access into the borrowed `&'a [u8]`), so backtracking to a previous arc or ascending to a parent node works identically to the owned path -- there was never a forward-only-access assumption to trip over. **M2 sweep (b4-fst-blocktree), two correctness fixes.** (1) `FSTMetadata`'s `emptyOutput` was decoded as "the recorded bytes, reversed". It is not: `FSTMetadata.save` runs the value through `outputs.writeFinalOutput` first (for `ByteSequenceOutputs`, a `vint` length then the payload), reverses *that* buffer, and length-prefixes the result, so the framing byte is inside the reversed blob and must be consumed. Reading it verbatim left a spurious leading length byte on the empty-string key's output, and `write_fst` made the mirror-image mistake -- so this port round-tripped its own FSTs perfectly while being unreadable by, and unable to read, real Lucene's. No fixture could catch it because no `GenFst*` generator built an FST that *accepts* the empty string; `fixtures/src/GenFstEmptyKey.java` + `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_empty_key_fixtures.rs` now do (including a byte-for-byte `write_fst` == real-Lucene-bytes assertion), and `write_fst_fixture`/`VerifyFst.java` verify the reverse direction (`VerifyFst OK (empty): 5 present keys resolved, 6 absent keys rejected`). (2) `find_target_arc`'s `ARCS_FOR_BINARY_SEARCH` branch did not set `Arc.arcIdx` on a hit, where `FST.findTargetArc` sets `arcIdx = mid - 1` and goes through `readNextRealArc`. `arcIdx` is what `readNextRealArc`/`readNextArcLabel` use to find the *following* slot, so `FstEnum::seek_exact` followed by `next()` resumed from slot 1 of the node regardless of which key was sought -- reachable only through a real `FSTCompiler`-built fixed-length-arc node, since this port's own `build_fst` emits only list-encoded nodes. Regression-tested in `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_binary_search_fixtures.rs`. **Recorded, not changed**: a failed seek leaves this enum fused (Java restarts enumeration from the first key), and the `Outputs`/`PairOutputs` typed layer is not wire-compatible with Lucene's `Outputs<T>` -- both now documented at their definitions. `Util.shortestPaths`/`TopNSearcher`/`readCeilArc`/`toDot` remain unported. **`FSTEnum` sentinel divergence, deliberate (`c41-gates-and-record`)**: Java's `FSTEnum.doSeekFloorArrayDirectAddressing` guards `BitTable.previousBitSet`'s `-1` with `assert floorIndex != -1;`, which is off in production, where the `-1` then makes `readArcByDirectAddressing` derive `firstLabel - 1` -- an arc one label below the range the node declared, and for `firstLabel == 0` exactly `END_LABEL`. This port returns `Error::Corrupt` instead: a rejected input where Java produces a plausible wrong answer. The sibling `findNextFloorArcDirectAddressing`'s `if (floorIndex > 0)` *is* ported faithfully -- there both `0` and `-1` correctly mean "stay put". Both call sites are now recorded per-site by [`docs/mechanical-gates.md`](mechanical-gates.md)'s `sentinel-callers` rule. |

| `search/suggest/fst/WFSTCompletionLookup` (weighted-FST suggester: weight-inverted `PositiveIntOutputs` construction + top-N completion-by-prefix lookup) | `lucene-codecs/src/suggest.rs` | **ported, scoped to the FST shape only.** `build_suggester_fst` builds a `PositiveIntOutputs`-typed FST from `(term, weight: u32)` pairs via the existing `build_fst_typed`/`FSTCompiler` machinery, inverting each weight to a cost (`cost = u32::MAX - weight`, `weight = u32::MAX - cost`) exactly mirroring `WFSTCompletionLookup.encodeWeight`/`decodeWeight`'s `Integer.MAX_VALUE`-based formula (read directly from `WFSTCompletionLookup.java`, widened from `i32`/`Integer.MAX_VALUE` to this port's `u32` weight type) so minimal-output-first FST traversal surfaces the highest-weight terms first. `top_n_completions(fst, prefix, n)` returns the `n` highest-weight completions of a prefix (suffix + weight, descending, ties broken by ascending suffix bytes) by seeking to the prefix via `FstEnum::seek_ceil` and walking only its matching subtree, keeping a bounded (size-`n`) min-heap rather than buffering every match -- **explicitly not** real `Util.shortestPaths`/`TopNSearcher`'s priority-queue-based search that can skip whole non-competitive subtrees without decoding them; see `suggest.rs`'s module doc for the precise disclosure. **Not ported**: `AnalyzingSuggester`-style analyzed/tokenized multi-field surface-form handling (this module takes raw term bytes, matching `WFSTCompletionLookup`'s own contract, not `AnalyzingSuggester`'s), fuzzy/edit-distance suggestion (`FuzzySuggester`), and a dedicated on-disk suggester index format/persistence for the `count` field `WFSTCompletionLookup.store`/`load` carry alongside the FST -- though persistence of the FST body itself needs no new code, since `build_suggester_fst`'s output is a plain `Fst<'static>` that already round-trips through this crate's existing `Fst::read`/`Fst::read_borrowed` wire format. **M2 sweep `b8-automata-analysis`:** `build_suggester_fst` deduplicated by keeping whichever weight came *first in the caller's input*; real `WFSTCompletionLookup.build` reads through a `SortedInputIterator` whose comparator sorts "by BytesRef (ascending) then cost (ascending)", and cost is `Integer.MAX_VALUE - weight`, so the survivor is the **highest** weight ("for duplicate suggestions, the best weight is actually added"). Fixed. `top_n_completions` also gained `WFSTCompletionLookup`'s `exactFirst` flag (its constructor default is `true`): the queried prefix, when it is itself an indexed term, comes back first regardless of weight and never a second time from the weighted search (`allowEmptyString = !exactFirst`). **M2 sweep `c39-codecs-readpath`** re-examined the `Util.shortestPaths`/`TopNSearcher` gap and found the recorded blocker names the wrong thing. The results are already identical -- the bounded heap keeps the same top N under the same `(cost ascending, input ascending)` order `TieBreakByInputComparator` imposes, and `WFSTCompletionLookup` never overrides `acceptResult`, so Java's `TopResults.isComplete` is always true and there is nothing for the port to lose. What a caller loses is **cost**, measured as a single arm (min of 20, release build) over dictionaries of `term%07d` keys: a top-10 lookup with a short prefix takes **1.41 ms at 10 000 entries, 15.0 ms at 100 000 and 75.8 ms at 500 000** -- linear in the prefix subtree, against Java's `O(topN x depth x out-degree)`, which is independent of dictionary size. **Porting `TopNSearcher` alone would not fix that and would introduce a wrong answer**: its pruning is admissible only because a partial path's accumulated output lower-bounds every completion under it, which holds only for an FST whose outputs have been *pushed* toward the root by `FSTCompiler`'s `Outputs.common`/`subtract`. This port's `build_fst` puts the whole output on the accepting node (`TrieNode::final_output`) and `output_add` is byte concatenation, so every partial path's accumulated output is `NO_OUTPUT`; a `maxQueueDepth == topN` best-first search over an all-zero-cost graph returns the **alphabetically first** N completions, not the highest-weighted N. The prerequisite is therefore output pushing plus an `Outputs`-aware reader -- the "much larger change touching the reader too" this file's `Outputs` trait doc already declines -- and is sized in `docs/sweep/m2/c39-codecs-readpath.md`. `Util.readCeilArc` stays unported and has **no consumer in scope**: its only Java callers are `FSTUtil.intersectPrefixPaths` (`AnalyzingSuggester`, unported) and `FSTTermsReader` (the `memory` codec, unported); `TopNSearcher` does not use it. |

**`PositiveIntOutputs`/`PairOutputs` are now ported, layered on top of the single `Vec<u8>` wire output type rather than threaded through `Fst`/`build_fst`/`build_node` as a generic parameter.** Since this builder never does real output-pushing (confirmed above -- each key's whole output already lives on one arc), combining two arcs' outputs when nodes are merged/shared never has to reconcile a partially-pushed prefix from one path with a different one from another: `build_node`'s existing `NodeHash` dedup already keys on each node's exact (encoded) final-output bytes, so structurally-identical subtrees with equal typed values still share correctly and subtrees with differing typed values never incorrectly merge. A new `Outputs` trait (`crates/lucene-codecs/src/fst.rs`) describes an `encode`/`decode`/`zero` codec to/from that `Vec<u8>`; `PositiveIntOutputs` (`i64`, `NO_OUTPUT = 0` encoding to zero bytes, matching real Lucene's sentinel), `ByteSequenceOutputs` (identity codec, the same type `Fst`/`build_fst` already use directly), and `PairOutputs<A, B>` (`Pair<A::Value, B::Value>`, length-prefixing the first component's encoded bytes so both components decode unambiguously; an all-zero pair encodes to zero bytes too) are implemented against it, plus `build_fst_typed::<O>`/`Fst::get_typed::<O>` thin wrappers around `build_fst`/`Fst::get`. Deliberately not ported: real Lucene's `Outputs.add`/`common`/`subtract` operations, since those exist solely to support output-pushing (finding/removing a shared output prefix to hoist onto a shared arc) and this builder has no output-pushing to support. Tested in `fst.rs::tests`: `positive_int_outputs_round_trips_values_including_zero`, `pair_outputs_round_trips_both_components_over_a_shared_prefix_key_set` (a mixed prefix/suffix-sharing key set, both components read back correctly per key), `pair_outputs_shared_suffix_nodes_keep_distinct_first_components` (the highest-risk case: `"bat"`/`"cat"` would share their tail node under plain byte outputs, but distinct per-key `Pair` values on that arc must -- and do -- prevent the node-hash dedup from merging them), and `pair_outputs_zero_value_encodes_to_no_extra_bytes`. **Ported**: `readFirstTargetArc`/`readFirstRealTargetArc`/`readNextRealArc`/`readNextArc` (`FST.java`'s enumeration primitives -- previously written, found to be dead code under the earlier `get`-only scope, and removed; reinstated once `iter`/`FstEnum` below needed them) plus `FstEnum` (`Fst::iter`, a port of `BytesRefFSTEnum`'s `next()`/`doNext`/`pushFirst`): full ascending-order enumeration of every `(key, output)` pair an FST accepts, over list-encoded and all three fixed-length-arc node encodings alike (mirroring `find_target_arc`'s own encoding support). **Seek support (`seekCeil`/`seekFloor`/`seekExact`) is now also ported**, over all four node encodings alike: `Fst::seek_exact` (a thin wrapper delegating to the already arc-by-arc-descending `Fst::get`, matching real `BytesRefFSTEnum.seekExact`'s own short-circuit-on-first-byte-mismatch semantics) plus `FstEnum::seek_ceil`/`seek_floor`/`seek_exact` (`FSTEnum.doSeekCeil`/`doSeekFloor`/`doSeekExact` ported field-for-field, including `rewindPrefix`'s shared-prefix reuse across successive seeks on the same enum, and the backtracking-with-output-accumulation logic for "target falls strictly between two sibling arcs"/"past the last arc of this node" cases -- `rollback_to_last_fork_then_push`/`backtrack_to_floor_arc`/`push_last`, the `seek_floor`-only mirror of the existing `push_first`). Verified both by hand-built-FST unit tests (`fst.rs::tests::fst_enum_seek_*`, including sequential forward-then-backward seeks on one enum and degenerate empty-FST/empty-key-set cases) and by a new differential test file, `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_seek_fixtures.rs`, exercising all four real Lucene-written fixtures above (list, binary search, direct addressing, continuous) for exact hits, a ceil/floor landing strictly between two keys, before-the-first-key, and past-the-last-key cases, plus a fifth fixture, `fixtures/data/fst_seek_non_root_array_node/` (`fixtures/src/GenFstSeekNonRootArrayNode.java`), whose root stays list-encoded while each of the three fixed-length-arc encodings sits one level below it -- specifically exercising the backtrack-past-a-non-root-array-node code paths (`read_last_target_arc`'s array branch, `find_next_floor_arc_binary_search`/`_direct_addressing`/`_continuous`) that the four root-only fixtures above never reach. Fixture-verified against a real `FST<BytesRef>` built via real `FSTCompiler` (`allowFixedLengthArcs(false)`, so the fixture itself never emits the array-node encodings this slice doesn't support) with 7 keys sharing prefixes/suffixes (`app`/`apple`/`application`, `banana`/`band`/`bandana`, `z`) plus 8 deliberately-absent lookups (proper prefixes, over-extensions, a disjoint key) -- see `fixtures/src/GenFst.java` and `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_fixtures.rs`, whose `iter_enumerates_all_keys_in_ascending_order` also confirms `Fst::iter` walks that same fixture in exactly its known sorted order. `find_target_arc`'s `ARCS_FOR_BINARY_SEARCH` decode path is likewise fixture-verified against a real binary-search-encoded root node (`fixtures/src/GenFstBinarySearch.java`, `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_binary_search_fixtures.rs`), and that same fixture's `iter_enumerates_every_key_of_a_binary_search_root_node` proves `Fst::iter` enumerates a genuine fixed-length-arc node correctly, not just the hand-built one in `fst.rs`'s own unit tests. The same is now true for direct addressing: `find_target_arc_direct_addressing` is fixture-verified against a real direct-addressing-encoded root node (`fixtures/src/GenFstDirectAddressing.java`, `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_direct_addressing_fixtures.rs`), and that fixture's `iter_enumerates_every_key_of_a_direct_addressing_root_node` proves `Fst::iter` enumerates a genuine direct-addressing node correctly, including skipping its one deliberate gap. A further fixture, `fixtures/data/fst_deep_trie/` (`fixtures/src/GenFstDeepTrie.java`, `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/fst_deep_trie_fixtures.rs`), closes the "shallow FST only" gap in the seek coverage above: its 9 keys (`abcaa`/`abcab`/`abcz`/`abda`/`abdz`/`acaa`/`aczz`/`baaa`/`bzzz`) share prefixes deeply enough that the path to `"abcaa"` crosses 5 distinct list-encoded trie levels, confirmed by the generator's own manual `readFirstTargetArc`/`readNextArc` walk before the fixture is written (mirroring the existing `"(bs)"`/`"(da)"`/`"(cs)"` self-checks) rather than assumed from "long strings." Every prior seek fixture's interesting structure sits at or one level below the root; this one forces `seekCeil`/`seekFloor`/`seekExact` to actually backtrack across 2-4 levels to resolve absent targets correctly (e.g. `"abcaz"` must fail past `"abca"`'s node and climb back up to the `"abc"` node to find `"abcz"`). Ground truth for all 17 seek targets comes from real Lucene's own `BytesRefFSTEnum.seekCeil`/`seekFloor`/`seekExact` against the reloaded FST, not hand-derived, matching this directory's established standard. |
| `codecs/lucene103/blocktree/Lucene103BlockTreeTermsReader`/`Writer`, `FieldReader`, `SegmentTermsEnum`/`Frame`, `TrieReader`/`TrieBuilder` (`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` term dictionary + term index) | `lucene-codecs/src/blocktree.rs` | **Current design (since the M2 follow-up batch `c1-lazy-blocktree`): a lazy, frame-based reader, the same shape as Java's.** `open` reads only the `.tmd` records and each field's root trie node and touches no `.tim` block; every lookup runs a ported `SegmentTermsEnum`/`SegmentTermsEnumFrame` frame stack. The paragraphs below are the row's accumulated history and narrate, in the present tense, the *earlier* eager design in which `open` materialized every term of every field into one sorted array -- read them as history, and see the `c1-lazy-blocktree` paragraph at the end of this cell (and `docs/sweep/m2/c1-lazy-blocktree.md`) for what replaced it and what that measured. **Historically: ported, scoped to `seekExact` + `docFreq`/`totalTermFreq`, then covering multi-block fields (multi-child `.tip` tries and floor-split `.tim` blocks), not just single-block ones.** Correction to the row this replaces: the pinned Lucene version (10.5.0, `Lucene104PostingsFormat`) uses `Lucene103BlockTreeTermsReader`/`Writer` (package `lucene103.blocktree`), *not* `Lucene90BlockTreeTermsReader` -- that class lives in `backward-codecs` and is out of scope. This version's `.tip` term index is **not an FST** -- Lucene 10.x replaced it with a purpose-built binary trie (`TrieReader`/`TrieBuilder`); `fst.rs` (scoped to the older format) is not used here. Ported: opening a `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` triple (header/footer/checksum framing, the embedded `Lucene104PostingsWriterTerms` sub-header + `BLOCK_SIZE` check, per-field `numTerms`/`sumTotalTermFreq`/`sumDocFreq`/`docCount`/`minTerm`/`maxTerm`), and now a **full trie decode** (all three node shapes -- `SIGN_NO_CHILDREN`, `SIGN_SINGLE_CHILD_WITH_OUTPUT`/`WITHOUT_OUTPUT`, `SIGN_MULTI_CHILDREN` with all three `ChildSaveStrategy` label encodings -- `BITS`/`ARRAY`/`REVERSE_ARRAY`) plus floor-block resolution (`LEAF_NODE_HAS_FLOOR`/`NON_LEAF_NODE_HAS_FLOOR`, the `numFollowFloorBlocks`/`(label, code)` byte layout). **Design**: rather than walking one root-to-leaf trie path per lookup (what real `SegmentTermsEnum.seekExact` does), `open()` recursively visits every reachable trie node, resolves every floor sub-block, decodes every resulting `.tim` block (re-attaching each block's own trie-path prefix, since a block only stores its suffix bytes), and merges everything into one sorted `Vec` -- the same shape the prior single-block slice used, just now fed by a full traversal instead of one root-node read. This eagerly decodes blocks a real `seekExact` for one term would never touch (acceptable for now: no enumeration/streaming consumer exists yet to notice, and correctness came first) -- see the module doc for the full tradeoff writeup. **Real bug this slice's fixture caught**: an interior trie node can have its own output block flagged `hasTerms=false` (a "pass-through" block that itself holds nothing but pointers to its own further-split children, produced by `Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter.writeBlocks` recursing to a deeper prefix instead of floor-splitting) -- the first implementation treated this as an unsupported non-leaf block and errored; the fix is to just skip that one physical block, since `PendingBlock.compileIndex` unconditionally merges every deeper recursion's own trie into the same trie this code already walks, so every term routed through the pointer-only block is independently reachable as a deeper node anyway. Caught by the new "many" field in `fixtures/src/GenBlockTree.java` (400 distinct terms, forcing real `Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter` past the default 25/48 min/maxItemsInBlock thresholds into both a multi-child trie and at least one floor split) -- every other fixture field stayed single-block and would never have hit this. **Still deferred**: automaton intersection (`IntersectTermsEnum`) -- pointer-only (`hasTerms=false`) blocks are skipped rather than opened (see above), and non-leaf `.tim` blocks are no longer in this list; see the "Multi-level blocktree" paragraph below, which superseded this claim once that support landed. **Suffix compression (read-side) is now supported**: `decode_block` dispatches on `code_l & 0x03` (`CompressionAlgorithm.NO_COMPRESSION`/`LOWERCASE_ASCII`/`LZ4`), reusing this port's own `lz4.rs` decompressor for LZ4 (a real, general-purpose decompressor, not a stub) and a small standalone port of `LowercaseAsciiCompression.decompress` for the other mode (`blocktree.rs::decompress_lowercase_ascii`); code `3` (never assigned to a `CompressionAlgorithm` constant) is rejected as `Error::Store(Corrupted)`, matching `CompressionAlgorithm.byCode`'s own `IllegalArgumentException`. This is purely read-side interop with real Lucene-written segments -- this port's own blocktree *writer* still only ever emits `NO_COMPRESSION`. **Verification, stated precisely**: LZ4 is verified against an actual `Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter`-produced fixture (200 terms sharing a long, highly repetitive-suffix common prefix, past the default min/maxItemsInBlock thresholds so the real writer chose LZ4 for several blocks) -- `fixtures/data/blocktree_compressed_index/`, `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_compressed_fixture.rs` (`lz4_compressed_terms_match_real_lucene`, `lz4_compressed_enumeration_matches_real_lucene_terms_enum_next`). `LOWERCASE_ASCII` could **not** be forced out of a real `IndexWriter` in reasonable effort (the writer only takes that branch once LZ4 already failed to save 25% *and* the block's average suffix length still clears its own `prefixLength > 2`/`length > 2*numEntries` gates -- every term shape tried here landed on `NO_COMPRESSION` instead), so it's instead verified against a byte vector produced by directly invoking real Lucene's own `LowercaseAsciiCompression.compress` from the pinned 10.5.0 jar (real Lucene *bytes*, just not embedded in an on-disk segment) -- see `decompress_lowercase_ascii_matches_real_lucene_compress_output`/`decode_block_lowercase_ascii_compressed_suffixes` in `blocktree.rs`. A hand-built (this port's own `lz4::compress`-round-tripped) vector additionally covers the LZ4 dispatch path directly (`decode_block_lz4_compressed_suffixes`). **Ordered enumeration (`TermsEnum.next()`) and nearest-match seeking (`seekCeil()`) are now ported** -- `FieldTerms::iter()` returns a `TermsEnum` cursor (`next()`/`seek_ceil()`/`current()`) over the field's already-sorted `entries` `Vec`. **Design choice**: this is a thin index-bump/binary-search cursor over the Vec the prior slice already materializes, not a reimplementation of real `SegmentTermsEnum`/`SegmentTermsEnumFrame`'s stack-based lazy block-walk -- the eager-whole-field-materialization tradeoff was made by the prior slice, and once every term is already in one sorted Vec, replicating Java's per-frame FST/trie-path push/pop machinery on top of it would only reintroduce complexity this port deliberately avoided (see the `rust-performance` skill). Fixture-verified against the `many` field (400 terms, multi-block/floor-split) via real `TermsEnum.next()` -- the full 400-term ordered sequence (term bytes + docFreq/totalTermFreq) matches exactly, proving enumeration walks block/floor boundaries in the right order and not just within one block -- and `seekCeil()` against four real-Lucene-verified cases (exact match, a between-terms ceiling match, before-the-first-term, and after-the-last-term/`END`); no decode bug surfaced this time, matching on the first real-Lucene run (see `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_fixtures.rs`). Unit tests cover cursor edge cases no real fixture reaches: an empty field, a single-term field, `seekCeil()` followed by `next()` continuing from the landed-on position, and `next()` called repeatedly past the end staying idempotent. Per-term postings-metadata bytes are decoded by `crate::postings::decode_term_metadata` per block (threaded across each block's own entries, `absolute` true only for each block's first entry -- blocks never share metadata state) and threaded into `FieldTerms`, which exposes `FieldTerms::postings(term, doc_in)`/`positions(...)` alongside `seek_exact`'s stats. Fixture-verified against a real `IndexWriter` (`fixtures/src/GenBlockTree.java`): "body"/"id"/"big"/"pos" (unchanged from the prior slice, all single-block) plus the new "many" field above, sampled across its range (first/last, several mid values spanning different trie branches and floor sub-blocks) and checked exhaustively (all 400 terms independently looked up) against real `TermsEnum.seekExact`/`docFreq`/`totalTermFreq`; see `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_fixtures.rs`. Unit tests cover the new trie-decode primitives directly (hand-built single-child-with-output+floor, all three multi-children strategies incl. a `REVERSE_ARRAY` gap case, an end-to-end two-floor-block `open()` round trip, and corruption paths -- invalid strategy code, child-delta-exceeds-parent-fp, trie-nesting-depth guard) that no small real fixture reaches. **Wildcard/prefix automaton-shaped intersection is now ported, scoped to glob matching over the already-materialized `Vec`, not real `IntersectTermsEnum`/`CompiledAutomaton`.** `FieldTerms::intersect(&WildcardPattern) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&[u8], TermStats)>` (`crates/lucene-codecs/src/blocktree.rs`) narrows `entries` to the sorted sub-range starting with the pattern's literal prefix (two `partition_point` binary searches -- free, since `entries` is already sorted for `seek_exact`; see the new `prefix_upper_bound` helper) and then linearly filters that range against `crate::wildcard::WildcardPattern` (`crates/lucene-codecs/src/wildcard.rs`, new module), a from-scratch glob matcher (`*` = zero-or-more bytes, `?` = exactly one Unicode codepoint (1-4 UTF-8 bytes, falling back to 1 byte on invalid UTF-8), everything else literal) plus a `WildcardPattern::prefix()` constructor for pure `PrefixQuery` semantics. **Design tradeoff, stated plainly**: this is *not* `CompiledAutomaton`/`ByteRunAutomaton` DFA compilation and *not* real `IntersectTermsEnum` trie-block-skipping -- real Lucene can visit only the `.tip`/`.tim` blocks a compiled automaton's reachable states could lead to, potentially sub-linear in dictionary size; this port's `intersect` is `O(n)` in the field's term count once the literal-prefix range is applied, degrading to a full field scan for a pattern with no literal prefix (e.g. `*foo`); the per-candidate `WildcardPattern::matches` cost itself is *not* `O(1)` -- it's a backtracking matcher that is multiplicative (`O(term.len()^k)`), not additive, in the number of `*` tokens `k` (see `wildcard.rs`'s own doc comment), so a pattern with several `*`s against long terms is a genuine unguarded pathological case, not just a theoretical one. This mirrors the same "correctness first, honest about what's actually optimized" stance the postings work took (see the `postings.rs` row's lazy-cursor slices) rather than transliterating Java's per-frame automaton-driven stack machine on top of a representation (one eagerly sorted `Vec`) that no longer matches it -- reusing the existing `entries` `Vec`/binary-search idiom `seek_exact`/`TermsEnum` already established, not a new automaton-shaped subsystem. **Also deliberately out of scope this slice**: no regex (`RegexpQuery`) and no fuzzy/Levenshtein automaton (`FuzzyQuery`) -- both need a real automaton/edit-distance representation that's a materially larger scope than glob matching over a sorted `Vec`; (task #34 added `\`-escape support for a literal `*`/`?`/`\` byte, matching real `WildcardQuery.toAutomaton`'s `WILDCARD_ESCAPE` handling exactly -- see the task #34 row below). Fixture-verified against real `WildcardQuery`/`CompiledAutomaton.getTermsEnum` ground truth (`fixtures/src/GenBlockTree.java::appendWildcardIntersectManifest`, extended "many" field, 400 terms) for four patterns: an exact prefix run (`term037*`), a `*`-suffix pattern equivalent to a `PrefixQuery` (`term039*`), a `?` single-char wildcard (`term010?`), and a pattern matching zero terms (`zzz*`) -- `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_fixtures.rs::many_field_wildcard_intersect_matches_real_lucene_wildcard_query`, matched on the first real-Lucene run. Unit tests (`wildcard.rs`) cover the matcher's own edge cases directly: empty pattern (matches only the empty term), pure `*` (matches everything including empty), literal-only pattern, `?` boundary cases (including on an empty term), `*` at the start/middle/end, adjacent `*`s collapsing to one, non-UTF-8 byte matching, and `literal_prefix()` extraction; `blocktree.rs` adds `intersect_prefix_and_wildcard_over_materialized_field` (prefix/`?`/mid-`*`/match-everything/match-nothing-in-range/match-nothing-out-of-range/exact-pattern/`WildcardPattern::prefix()` cases over a hand-built multi-term field) and `prefix_upper_bound_handles_ff_bytes_and_empty` (the `0xFF`-byte-run and empty-prefix corner cases the real fixture's ASCII terms never reach)  **Multi-level blocktree (`.tim` blocks that are themselves non-leaf) is now decoded, not just deferred.** Previously any block with `isLeafBlock == false` (some of its entries are in-block pointers to further-nested sub-blocks, rather than raw term suffixes -- real Lucene's mechanism for a prefix too wide to give every one of its sub-prefixes a separate `.tip` trie arc) was rejected with `Error::Unsupported`; every fixture this port had wasn't large/irregular enough to force real Lucene into writing one. `decode_block`/`decode_block_at_depth` now recurse into every sub-block entry (reattaching that entry's own key bytes as a prefix before merging its sub-entries in), confirmed against a new, dedicated fixture (`fixtures/src/GenBlockTreeMultilevel.java`, 8000 deterministic pseudo-random terms -- a sequential-numeric term set, even at 400+ terms, empirically never triggers a non-leaf block; real Lucene resolves that shape via floor blocks instead) that does reach one, checked structurally (not just "lookups still work") in `blocktree.rs::multilevel_fixture_reaches_a_genuine_non_leaf_block`. One real correctness bug surfaced and was fixed during this: a sub-block can be addressed **both** by its parent's in-block pointer **and** by its own independent `.tip` trie arc (real Lucene's `SegmentTermsEnum` only ever takes one path per lookup and never notices; this module's eager whole-field materialization visits every reachable block and was double-decoding such blocks until `open()` started passing `decode_block_at_depth` the full set of trie-collected top-level block fps, so a sub-block pointer whose target is already in that set is skipped rather than re-decoded). Differential test: `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_multilevel_fixture.rs`. **All three `ChildSaveStrategy` label encodings (`BITS`/`ARRAY`/`REVERSE_ARRAY`) were already implemented and hand-built-unit-tested before this task (`multi_children_array_strategy`/`multi_children_bits_strategy`/`multi_children_reverse_array_strategy`/`multi_children_reverse_array_strategy_with_gap`) -- what was missing was a real-Lucene-fixture differential proof that each strategy is also chosen and decoded correctly against actual `TrieBuilder.ChildSaveStrategy.choose`-selected bytes, not just hand-assembled ones.** Auditing existing fixtures found `REVERSE_ARRAY` already forced for real: `blocktree_multilevel_index`'s "many" field (8000 random lowercase terms) makes the root trie node's children a fully dense `'a'..='z'` range, and `TrieBuilder.ChildSaveStrategy.choose`'s own cost formula (`needBytes`: `REVERSE_ARRAY` = 1, `ARRAY` = 25, `BITS` = 4) picks `REVERSE_ARRAY` -- `multilevel_fixture_reaches_a_genuine_non_leaf_block` now asserts `child_save_strategy == CHILD_STRATEGY_REVERSE_ARRAY` (previously unasserted, just implicitly exercised). `ARRAY` and `BITS`, however, were not forced anywhere: this task adds `fixtures/src/GenBlockTreeChildStrategies.java`, a new generator producing `fixtures/data/blocktree_child_strategies_index/`, whose two fields' leading-byte label sets were hand-picked so the same cost formula picks a distinct winner for each -- "arraystrat" (5 labels spanning printable-ASCII, distance 94: BITS=12, ARRAY=4, REVERSE_ARRAY=90 -> `ARRAY`) and "bitsstrat" (9 labels spaced 5 apart, distance 41: BITS=6, ARRAY=8, REVERSE_ARRAY=33 -> `BITS`) -- confirmed by decoding the real generated bytes before committing to this design (`root.child_save_strategy` matched the predicted code on the first real-Lucene run for both fields, no trial-and-error needed once the arithmetic was worked out from `TrieBuilder.java`'s own `needBytes` overrides). New differential test `child_strategies_fixture_forces_array_and_bits_strategies` (`blocktree.rs`) asserts each field's exact strategy code and `strategy_bytes`, then round-trips every term through the public `open`/`seek_exact` API against the manifest's ground-truth term list. No production code changes were needed -- this was purely closing a fixture-coverage gap for two already-correct code paths. | **Root-block-with-no-terms shape confirmed already handled, one incorrect rejection removed (this task).** `open()` previously errored with `Error::Unsupported("root block with no terms (all sub-blocks) ...")` whenever `collect_leaf_blocks`'s full trie traversal came back empty -- meant to guard the case where a field's root `.tip` trie node's own physical `.tim` block has `hasTerms == false` (holds nothing but sub-block pointers, e.g. because the field's very first terms already diverge enough on their leading byte that every top-level group hits `minItemsInBlock` before the root keeps any loose term of its own). Auditing `Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter.writeBlocks`/`PendingBlock.compileIndex` shows this guard's premise was wrong: the `.tip` trie always structurally mirrors the `.tim` block hierarchy, root included -- every sub-block pointer a block writes always gets a matching trie child merged in by `compileIndex`, regardless of nesting depth -- so `collect_leaf_blocks`'s existing unconditional recursion into a node's trie children (already relied on for the interior no-terms/pointer-only block case, see the paragraph above) already walks straight past a no-terms *root* into its real children with terms; `blocks` was never actually empty for this shape. The check is removed rather than specially handled; if `blocks` is ever empty for genuinely corrupt data, the existing `entries.len() as i64 != num_terms` check catches it with an accurate `Error::Store(Corrupted(...))` instead. Confirmed empirically, not just by source audit: `fixtures/src/GenBlockTreeChildStrategies.java`'s existing "arraystrat"/"bitsstrat" fields (5 and 9 leading-byte groups of 30 terms each) already force exactly this shape and were already passing through `open()` before this task -- the dead check simply never triggered on any fixture this port had. New test `open_field_with_no_terms_in_root_block_still_finds_every_term` (`blocktree.rs`) makes this explicit: asserts `root.has_terms == false` and `root.sign != SIGN_NO_CHILDREN` structurally for both fields (proving the shape, not assuming it), then round-trips every term through both `seek_exact` and full ordered `TermsEnum::next()` enumeration against the same existing fixture -- no new Java generator was needed. **Deep-nesting audit (task #222): a real 4+-level-deep non-leaf `.tim` block chain was producible, and is now fixture-proven, not just structurally argued.** The existing `blocktree_multilevel_index` fixture (8000 random lowercase terms, default `minItemsInBlock=25`/`maxItemsInBlock=48`) only ever reaches one non-leaf layer (root block -> one internal block -> leaves) -- empirically confirmed this plateaus at depth 3 even at 2000-8000 terms, because a 26-letter alphabet fans a large group out into many small children after just one extra prefix byte, shrinking every child below `maxItemsInBlock` almost immediately. Forcing deeper nesting needed both levers real Lucene exposes: a much narrower alphabet (so each extra prefix byte only *halves* a group instead of dividing it by 26) and a smaller `minItemsInBlock`/`maxItemsInBlock` (wired through `Lucene104PostingsFormat`'s two-arg constructor via a custom `Codec.getPostingsFormatForField` override, since `IndexWriterConfig` has no direct knob for it). `fixtures/src/GenBlockTreeDeepNesting.java` (2000 terms over `{a,b}`, 16 bytes each, `minItemsInBlock=2`/`maxItemsInBlock=4`) reaches a genuine depth of 6 chained non-leaf blocks in practice; the new `blocktree.rs::deep_nesting_fixture_reaches_at_least_four_levels` unit test independently re-derives this via its own from-scratch block-chain walk (not sharing code with `decode_block_at_depth`) and asserts a minimum depth of 4, plus the full differential in `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_deep_nesting_fixture.rs`. Audit finding on `decode_block_at_depth`/`collect_leaf_blocks`: reading both functions confirms neither special-cases `depth` beyond the `depth > 10_000` cycle guard -- the sub-block recursion (`decode_block_at_depth(tim, sub_fp, ..., depth + 1, already_trie_indexed)`) and the trie recursion in `collect_leaf_blocks` call themselves identically regardless of how deep they already are, so this fixture's depth-6 chain is a genuine differential proof of the same code path a depth-1 chain exercises, not a structurally distinct one. **M2 sweep (b4-fst-blocktree).** Compared method-for-method against `lucene103/blocktree/{TrieReader,Lucene103BlockTreeTermsReader,FieldReader,SegmentTermsEnum,SegmentTermsEnumFrame,CompressionAlgorithm}`; the trie-node, floor-block, block-header and `decodeMetaData` decoding all agree field for field. Fixed: `.tmd`'s recorded `indexLength`/`termsLength` were only checked for being *too large*, where `CodecUtil.retrieveChecksum(IndexInput, long)` requires exact equality and rejects "file too long" as its own corruption -- now routed through `codec_util::retrieve_checksum_with_expected_length`; and a block's `vint`/`vlong` region lengths were fed straight into `vec![0u8; n]`, which aborts the process on a corrupt length rather than erroring (Java's `new byte[n]` throws). Both now have negative tests. **Still open, unchanged**: the eager whole-field materialization at `open()` (sweep finding A1) and, downstream of it, `intersect`/`fuzzy_intersect`/`regexp_intersect` being a literal-prefix-narrowed linear scan rather than `IntersectTermsEnum`'s automaton-driven block skipping -- that port is blocked on `org.apache.lucene.util.automaton` (no `Automaton`/`CompiledAutomaton`/`ByteRunnable` exists in this port at all; `regexp.rs` is a backtracking matcher) as much as on lazy frames, so it belongs after batch b8, not here. See `docs/sweep/m2/b4-fst-blocktree.md` for the measured over-scan. **M2 sweep `b8-automata-analysis`: `regexp_intersect` now skips.** `RegexpPattern::dead_prefix_len` answers the question a backtracker previously could not -- *is this prefix dead?* -- which is the signal `ByteRunAutomaton` gives `IntersectTermsEnum` when it abandons a trie subtree. Over the sorted term array that becomes a galloping-search jump past the whole run sharing the dead prefix. On a million-term `t0..t999999` dictionary (`benches/regexp_intersect.rs`): `t1[0-9]` **1.72 ms -> 19.6 us (88x)**, `t1*z` -- b4's interior-constrained shape -- **16.4 ms -> 15.4 us (1065x)**. Patterns whose dead runs are short (`t[0-9]{4}`, `t.*99`) would lose to the extra work, so the iterator gives up on skipping after 128 unprofitable attempts and measures 1.00x/1.01x there. This does **not** close A1: Lucene's other win is not *loading* the pruned blocks, and `BlockTreeFields` has already decoded every term at open. **M2 sweep `c1-lazy-blocktree`: finding A1 is closed -- the reader is now lazy, the way `SegmentTermsEnum` is.** `open` reads only the `.tmd` records (per-field counts, min/max term, and the `(indexStart, rootFP, indexEnd)` triple) plus each field's root trie node, and touches no `.tim` block at all; every lookup runs a ported `SegmentTermsEnum`/`SegmentTermsEnumFrame` frame stack -- `TrieReader.lookupChild` one label at a time (all three `ChildSaveStrategy` decodings, now used for lookup rather than enumeration), `scanToFloorFrame` to pick the one floor sub-block covering the target, `loadBlock` for that block alone, `scanToTermLeaf`/`binarySearchTermLeaf`/`scanToTermNonLeaf` to land on the term, and `decodeMetaData` only up to it. `next()` walks blocks and in-block sub-block pointers off the same stack, so the old eager traversal's doubly-addressed-sub-block dedup and both recursion-depth cycle guards are gone -- a sub-block fp is required to be strictly below its parent's, which bounds the stack by construction. Measured on the M1 benchmark corpus' single 579k-term real-Lucene segment (`crates/lucene-codecs/benches/blocktree_open.rs`, `benchmarks/micro/java/TermSeekMicro.java`): `blocktree::open` **35.4 ms -> 0.175 ms (202x)**; per-seek cost was then measured against Lucene itself, interleaved A/B on the same terms in the same order: a cold hit 495 ns vs Lucene's 440 ns (1.13x), a miss 215 ns vs 263 ns (cheaper than Lucene's), a full `next()` scan 28 ns/term vs 22 ns; live heap per opened segment drops from **+39 MB** of materialized dictionary to the shared `.tim`/`.tip` buffers plus a frame stack. `intersect`/`fuzzy_intersect`/`regexp_intersect` now seek to the pattern's literal prefix and walk forward instead of scanning a materialized array, and `regexp_intersect`'s dead-prefix skip re-seeks past the dead range -- which with lazy frames means the blocks under it are never loaded: over a real-Lucene `t0..t999999` dictionary (`benchmarks/corpus/src/GenTermCorpus.java`) `t1[0-9]` is **131x** and `t1*z` **1768x** (b8 measured 88x/1065x with the dictionary already in memory), and the shapes with no dead prefixes (`t[0-9]{4}`, `t.*99`) stay at 1.00-1.17x. **API**: every lookup gained a `Result`-returning form (`try_seek_exact`/`try_next`/`try_seek_ceil`) because a corrupt block is now discovered at lookup time, exactly as in Java; the pre-existing infallible spellings are kept and report such a block as "no such term"/end-of-terms. **M2 sweep `c39-codecs-readpath`** migrated every *production* caller of the four infallible spellings to the `try_*` forms -- 15 sites across `lucene-search`'s scoring, explain, `weight_count` and `multi_segment` entry points, all of which were already inside `Result`-returning functions -- so a corrupt `.tim` block can no longer read as an empty index anywhere a query can reach. Two signatures had to widen for it (`weight_count::count_term_query_shortcut` and `multi_segment::global_term_stats`/`global_boolean_stats`, which returned bare `Option`s). The infallible spellings survive as a **test-only** convenience and say so on each method; that no production caller remains is re-checkable by marking the four `#[deprecated]` and building `--all-targets` (149 remaining sites, every one under `#[cfg(test)]`, `tests/` or `benches/`). `intersect`/`fuzzy_intersect`/`regexp_intersect` also became fallible iterators -- `Item = Result<(Vec<u8>, TermStats)>`, matching `IntersectTermsEnum.next()`'s `IOException` -- because a corrupt block used to end a wildcard/regexp term expansion early, i.e. return a *smaller hit set* rather than an error. `intersect`'s iterator item changed from `(&[u8], TermStats)` to `(Vec<u8>, TermStats)`, which a lazy cursor cannot avoid. `open_shared` takes already-shared `.tim`/`.tip` buffers so a caller can skip `open`'s copy of them. **`c27-arith-codecs-2` (2026-08-29)**: `SegmentTermsEnumFrame.java:701`'s `int mid = (start + end) >>> 1;` was ported as a signed `>>`; the unsigned shift is load-bearing, it keeps the binary-search midpoint correct once the sum passes `Integer.MAX_VALUE`. Also fixed: `if fp + 8 > slice.len()` was the *only* bound on the `.tmd` `rootFP` and formed the very sum it guarded (a negative one arrives as `usize::MAX`, `fp + 8` wraps to 7, the check **passes**); `numFields` sized a `Vec::with_capacity` straight off a vint (498 GB at `i32::MAX`); `minTerm`/`maxTerm` lengths sized `vec![0u8; n]` unbounded; `totalTermFreq = docFreq + readVLong()` overflowed into a negative frequency every scorer downstream would treat as real; and `entCount` was bounded by nothing until it was tied to `numSuffixLengthBytes`, which `Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter.writeBlock` guarantees. A re-signed byte-flip sweep over `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` rejects 391 of 436 with zero panics. **`c42-readpath-perf`**: `TermsEnum` now has Java's split -- `try_next_term()`/`term()` are `SegmentTermsEnum.next()`/`term()` (term bytes only, no `decodeMetaData`) and `try_stats()` is `docFreq()`/`totalTermFreq()`, memoised per frame position by `metaDataUpto` exactly as Java's is. `try_next()` (the fused form) stays for callers that want both. A bytes-only full-field scan went 22.90 -> 10.42 ns/term (2.2x) on the 200 000-term benchmark field, against Lucene's recorded 20.5 ns; the intersect iterators, `check_index`'s intersect/scan comparison and `IndexWriter`'s term-range delete resolution all moved onto it.
| `codecs/lucene104/Lucene104PostingsWriter`, `codecs/lucene103/blocktree/Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter` (`.doc`/`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` write side) | `lucene-codecs/src/postings_writer.rs::write_single_field` | **write path started, narrowly scoped to a single field, single `.tim` block, term-frequency-only postings.** Given one field's already-tokenized/inverted term dictionary (`FieldPostingsInput { field_number, index_options, doc_count, terms: &[TermPostings] }`, each `TermPostings` a term's bytes plus its `(doc_id, freq)` pairs sorted ascending by `doc_id`), produces `.doc`/`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` bytes. **Exactly proven correct, no more**: (1) **single field per call** -- `numFields = 1` in `.tmd`; (2) **single `.tim` block, single trie node** -- every term must fit in one physical leaf block, and `.tip` is one root `SIGN_NO_CHILDREN` node with `hasTerms` set and no floor data (block-splitting, floor sub-blocks, and multi-level tries are not implemented -- a real writer would split once past `Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter`'s min/maxItemsInBlock thresholds, this one never does); (3) **`docFreq < BLOCK_SIZE` (256) for every term** -- only the group-varint "tail block" encoding is ever written (`Lucene104PostingsWriter`'s `flushDocBlock(true)` branch that never reaches `docBufferUpto == BLOCK_SIZE`); no full `ForUtil`/`PForUtil`-bit-packed blocks, no skip data, no impacts are ever produced, and a term at or above `BLOCK_SIZE` docs is rejected with `Error::DocFreqTooLarge` rather than silently producing wrong bytes; (4) **term frequency only, no positions/offsets/payloads** -- only `IndexOptions::Docs`/`DocsAndFreqs` are accepted (`Error::UnsupportedIndexOptions` otherwise), `.pos`/`.pay` are never written; (5) **`docFreq == 1` is pulsed into the term dictionary**, matching the real writer exactly -- no `.doc` bytes at all for a singleton term, `postings::singleton_postings` reads it back purely from the per-term metadata. The per-term postings-metadata bytes always take the bit-clear ("absolute-ish `docStartFP` delta") branch of `decodeTerm`'s encoding, never the zigzag-singleton-delta branch -- a correct but less compact simplification, since this writer has no need for that branch's extra savings. **Caller obligations, not re-validated beyond what's checked**: `terms` must already be sorted ascending by term bytes with no duplicates (checked, `Error::TermsNotSorted`), each term's `docs` must be sorted ascending by doc ID with no duplicates (checked, `Error::DocIdsNotSorted`) and every `freq >= 1` (checked, `Error::NonPositiveFreq`) -- `doc_count` itself (the field-level distinct-docs count written to `.tmd`) is taken as given from the caller, not cross-checked against `terms`. **Does not duplicate the read side's decode logic** -- it only emits bytes; correctness is proven entirely by round-tripping through the *existing, unmodified* `blocktree::open`/`postings::DocInput::open`/`postings::singleton_postings`, the same functions real Java-Lucene fixtures are opened with elsewhere in this port. Unit-tested in `postings_writer.rs` (mixed singleton/multi-doc terms; `IndexOptions::Docs`-only totalTermFreq aliasing; an all-singleton field needing no `.doc` file at all; the `doc_start_fp` delta-threading correctness across 20 terms/5 docs each; every validation-rejection path). **The required end-to-end proof lives in `lucene-search`** (this crate must not depend upward on `lucene-search`, see the `architecture` skill): `crates/lucene-search/tests/postings_writer_round_trip.rs::term_query_finds_correct_docs_over_freshly_written_postings` writes a 3-term field with a mix of singleton and multi-doc terms via `write_single_field`, opens it with the unmodified `blocktree::open`/`DocInput::open`, and runs the unmodified `lucene_search::search_term_query` (the same public query entry point real-fixture tests use) for every term plus a missing term, asserting the exact doc-ID sets Lucene's own `TermQuery`/`PostingsEnum` would return; a second test (`term_query_respects_live_docs_filter`) confirms the written postings compose correctly with a `.liv`-style deletion bitset. **Not verified against real Lucene bytes** -- unlike every read-side row in this table, there is no `fixtures/src/Gen*.java` generator for this writer (there is nothing for it to differentially test against: the point of a first write-side slice is proving this port's own writer and this port's own unmodified reader agree, not that this port's writer produces byte-identical output to `Lucene104PostingsWriter`/`Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter` -- those writers' actual on-wire choices, e.g. singleton run-length compression, real block-splitting thresholds, are known not to be replicated here, see above). **Explicitly deferred, not silently missing**: multi-block terms (`docFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE`, full `ForUtil`/`PForUtil` blocks, skip data, impacts), multi-block/floor-split/multi-child-trie term dictionaries (more than one field or more than one `.tim` block/`.tip` node), positions/offsets/payloads (`.pos`/`.pay`), suffix compression, and singleton run-length stat encoding -- all separate future slices. **Now wired into `IndexWriter::commit()`** (follow-up task, see PLAN.md): `IndexWriter::set_postings_field(Some(field_name))` (`crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs`) opts a writer into calling this function for exactly one field of every segment `commit()` flushes, sourcing `TermPostings` from `crate::indexing_chain::invert_documents` over that field's `FieldValue::String` values -- the same one-field/single-`.tim`-block/`docFreq < BLOCK_SIZE`/term-frequency-only scope above, unchanged, now reachable from a real `IndexWriter::add_document`/`commit()` call rather than only from hand-built test input. A `docFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE` term in one commit's pending-doc batch makes the whole `commit()` fail atomically (nothing written), rather than silently truncating. **Now wired for more than one field per commit too (follow-up task):** `IndexWriter::add_postings_field(field_name)` opts a writer into additional postings fields on top of whatever `set_postings_field` already established (which keeps its original "replace, don't accumulate" semantics); `commit()` batches every configured field's `TermPostings` into exactly one `write_fields` call, so a commit with two or more distinct indexed text fields lands in one `.doc`/`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` file set, matching how `merge_postings` in `merge.rs` already consumed `write_fields`. Proven end-to-end by `crates/lucene-search/tests/index_writer_postings_fixtures.rs::two_distinct_postings_fields_in_one_commit_are_both_searchable` (two fields, real term queries against both, asserting no cross-contamination). **Merge-policy interaction, fixed during review, then superseded (postings/points merge caller wiring task)**: an earlier review found `execute_merge`/`merge_stored_only_segments` had no `.doc`/`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` awareness at all, so `segment_stats()` excluded any segment whose `.si` listed a `.doc` file from `find_merges`' candidate pool entirely, keeping postings-carrying segments permanently un-mergeable rather than mergeable-with-silent-data-loss. **This has since been fixed for real** (see the `execute_merge` entry in the `index/IndexWriter` row below): `execute_merge` now opens each source segment's `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd`/`.doc` (when its `.si` lists them) and builds `crate::merge::SourcePostings` from that real on-disk data, so postings-carrying segments merge like any other, with postings correctly preserved -- `segment_stats()` no longer excludes them. The old `segments_with_postings_are_never_automatically_merged_away` test (asserting postings segments never merge) was replaced by `segments_with_postings_are_automatically_merged_with_postings_preserved`, which asserts the opposite: three postings-carrying one-doc commits under a tight merge policy converge to one segment, and the merged segment's real `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd`/`.doc` still resolve every term correctly (`doc_freq`/postings checked directly via `blocktree::open`/`DocInput`). Required end-to-end proof: `crates/lucene-search/tests/index_writer_postings_fixtures.rs::documents_added_via_index_writer_are_searchable_by_term_query` (single-segment case, pre-existing) plus the new `crates/lucene-search/src/directory_reader.rs::postings_merge_e2e::term_query_finds_docs_from_both_flushed_segments_after_automatic_merge` (two real flushed segments, a real automatic merge triggered by `commit()`, then a real `TermQuery` via `search_term_query_multi_segment` against the merged segment, confirming terms from both original segments resolve correctly post-merge) -- this closes the gap `merge.rs`'s own module doc comment used to describe as "`merge_postings` real and tested on its own, but not yet reachable from a real end-to-end caller." **Points (`merge_points`) remains not reachable end-to-end**, unlike postings: this writer has no points write path at flush time at all (no `set_points_field`-equivalent, no points-carrying `Document` field shape), so there is no real segment it could ever produce with `.kdm`/`.kdi`/`.kdd` files for `execute_merge` to open in the first place -- `merge_points` is still exercised only by `merge.rs`'s own hand-built `MergeSource` fixtures. Wiring points end-to-end needs flush-side points write support added first (a separate, larger follow-up task), not just merge-caller wiring. | **Positions write-side added (this task): `.pos` now supported, still term-frequency-scoped in every other way.** `write_single_field` accepts `IndexOptions::DocsAndFreqsAndPositions` in addition to `Docs`/`DocsAndFreqs` (`DocsAndFreqsAndPositionsAndOffsets` still rejected with `Error::UnsupportedIndexOptions` -- offsets/payloads remain unimplemented on the write side, a further deferred layer). `TermPostings` gained a `positions: Vec<Vec<i32>>` field, parallel to `docs`: each entry is a doc's absolute, ascending occurrence positions (e.g. `[0, 3, 4]`), required to have exactly `docs.len()` entries with `positions[i].len() == docs[i].1` (that doc's freq) whenever `index_options` indexes positions (`Error::MissingPositions`/`Error::PositionsFreqMismatch` otherwise); left empty for `Docs`/`DocsAndFreqs` fields. Scoped identically to the existing `.doc` tail-block restriction: **`total_term_freq < BLOCK_SIZE` (256) for every term with positions** -- only the vint-tail encoding (`refillLastPositionBlock`) is ever written, never a full `ForUtil`/`PForUtil`-encoded `.pos` block; a term at or above that bound is rejected with `Error::TotalTermFreqTooLarge`. A singleton term (`docFreq == 1`, pulsed out of `.doc`) still gets real `.pos` bytes when it indexes positions -- position data is independent of the `.doc` singleton optimization. Per-term metadata (`write_term_metadata`) now also threads a running `pos_start_fp` delta exactly like `doc_start_fp`, written whenever the field indexes positions. **Still not reimplementing the read side**: correctness is proven by round-tripping through the existing, unmodified `postings::read_positions`/`blocktree::FieldTerms::positions`. Unit-tested in `postings_writer.rs` (position round-trip via `read_positions` covering a singleton term with multiple occurrences and a multi-doc term with a position-accumulator reset between docs; every new rejection path). **The required phrase-query end-to-end proof lives in `lucene-search`**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/postings_writer_round_trip.rs::phrase_query_finds_correct_docs_over_freshly_written_positions` writes a 3-term field (`"fox"`/`"jumps"`/`"quick"`) across two docs that share every term but only align adjacently in one of them, opens it through the unmodified `blocktree::open`/`DocInput::open`/`PosInput::open`, and runs the unmodified `lucene_search::search_phrase_query` for `["quick", "fox"]`, `["fox", "jumps"]`, and `["quick", "jumps", "fox"]`, asserting the exact doc each phrase matches -- including the negative case where both docs contain every term but only one doc has them in that exact adjacent order, proving this isn't just a doc-ID conjunction. **`IndexWriter` wiring explicitly NOT touched by this task**: `IndexWriter::build_postings_output` (`crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs`) still only ever builds term-frequency-only `TermPostings` (`positions: Vec::new()`, `set_postings_field` still only accepts `Docs`/`DocsAndFreqs`) -- a real `IndexWriter::add_document`/`commit()` session still cannot produce a `.pos` file yet; wiring positions into `IndexWriter` (sourcing per-doc position lists from `invert_documents`'s already-recorded `PostingEntry::positions`) is left for a follow-up task. **Multi-field support added (this task): `numFields > 1` in `.tmd` is now supported.** `write_single_field` is now a one-element-slice wrapper over the new `write_fields(inputs: &[FieldPostingsInput], segment_id, segment_suffix) -> Result<Output>`, which loops the same per-field write steps (unchanged) over every element of `inputs`, appending each field's `.doc`/`.pos` tail blocks and `.tim` block/`.tip` root node into the *same* shared byte buffers and writing one `.tmd` field record per field. **Per-field limits are exactly as before and apply independently to each field** -- every field is still capped at a single `.tim` block/root trie node, `docFreq < BLOCK_SIZE` (256), and (if it indexes positions) `total_term_freq < BLOCK_SIZE`; none of these bounds are shared or relaxed across fields, and a field violating its own bound still fails the whole call (`Error::DocFreqTooLarge`/`Error::TotalTermFreqTooLarge`) as before. Multi-block terms and multi-level tries remain out of scope (a separate, still-deferred slice). Unit-tested in `postings_writer.rs` (`write_fields_two_fields_share_one_tmd_and_stay_isolated`: two fields, one with positions, one without, opened via the unmodified `blocktree::open` and checked independently). **The required end-to-end proof lives in `lucene-search`**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/postings_writer_round_trip.rs::multi_field_segment_term_queries_are_isolated_per_field` writes a "title" field (`DocsAndFreqs`) and a "body" field (`DocsAndFreqsAndPositions`) in one `write_fields` call, where both fields index a term with the *same bytes* ("rust") but different postings, and runs the unmodified `lucene_search::search_term_query`/`search_phrase_query` against both fields in the same segment, asserting each field's results are correct and that neither field's postings leak into the other's lookups. **`IndexWriter` wiring NOT touched by this task**: `IndexWriter::set_postings_field` still only tracks one configured field per commit (unchanged) -- extending it to multiple fields is a separate follow-up, not attempted here. **Multi-block writer added (this task): a field's terms can now span more than one physical `.tim` block, addressed by a `SIGN_MULTI_CHILDREN` `.tip` root, not just one block/one `SIGN_NO_CHILDREN` root.** Splitting policy, deliberately the simplest one that stays correct without floor blocks or a second trie level: `write_fields` groups a field's (already-sorted) terms by their **first byte** (`group_terms_by_leading_byte`). A field with only one leading byte (or one term, or a term with zero bytes -- there's no leading byte to strip/route on) still gets the original single-block/single-`SIGN_NO_CHILDREN`-root shape, byte-for-byte unchanged (verified: none of the pre-existing single-block tests changed behavior, since their fixtures already exercise both shapes -- e.g. `mixed_singleton_and_multi_doc_terms_round_trip`'s "fox"/"quick"/"the" already has 3 distinct leading bytes and was already taking the multi-block path even before this task added a dedicated test for it). A field spanning 2..=33 distinct leading bytes gets one leaf `.tim` block per group (`write_tim_block`, parameterized by `strip_prefix_len` so the same function serves both the single-block case, prefix length 0, and each group, prefix length 1 -- a block only ever stores the bytes *after* its shared leading byte, matching `crate::blocktree::collect_leaf_blocks`'s "a block only stores its own suffix" contract on the read side), each addressed by its own `SIGN_NO_CHILDREN`/`hasTerms`/no-floor leaf node (`write_leaf_node`), with the field's `.tip` root being a single `SIGN_MULTI_CHILDREN` node with no output of its own (`write_multi_children_root`, always `ChildSaveStrategy::ARRAY` -- the simplest of the three label encodings `crate::blocktree::multi_children_labels_and_fps` supports on the read side; `BITS`/`REVERSE_ARRAY` are never emitted). Each block's per-term metadata delta-threading (`doc_start_fp`/`pos_start_fp`) resets fresh per block, matching `SegmentTermsEnumFrame`'s per-frame reset the read side (`decode_block`) already assumes -- blocks never share metadata state, whether split by floor (not implemented here) or by sibling leaf block (this task). **Explicitly still unimplemented, narrowed further than a first cut might suggest**: floor sub-blocks (a single leading-byte group with too many terms for one block -- this writer has no notion of a per-block term-count cap at all, so an enormous single-leading-byte group still produces one arbitrarily large block, not a floor split; audited again by a later "floor sub-blocks in blocktree writer" task, which confirmed this remains the only gap and that read-side floor support -- `blocktree.rs::expand_floor` -- already has solid real-Lucene fixture coverage including non-first floor sub-blocks, see PLAN.md); a second/deeper trie level (a field needing more than 33 distinct leading bytes is rejected outright with `Error::TooManyLeadingByteGroups` rather than silently misencoding the multi-children node's 5-bit `strategyBytes-1` header field, which can only address 2..=33 children); and the `BITS`/`REVERSE_ARRAY` label-encoding strategies. This is a deliberately narrower scope than "any field exceeding one block's term-count capacity splits automatically" -- the chosen policy (group by leading byte) is the simplest split that maps cleanly onto a *single-level* multi-child trie without floor data, at the cost of only working when a field's terms are naturally spread across enough distinct leading bytes (true of essentially any real natural-language field, false in principle for a pathological field where thousands of terms share one leading byte). **Proof, mirroring the single-block slice's own bar**: `postings_writer.rs::many_leading_byte_groups_force_multi_child_trie_root` (26 terms, one per lowercase letter -- 26 physical `.tim` blocks under one multi-child root, every term independently looked up through the unmodified `blocktree::open`, not just first/last), `rejects_field_needing_more_than_33_leading_byte_groups`, and `empty_term_falls_back_to_single_block_even_with_other_distinct_leading_bytes`. **The required end-to-end proof lives in `lucene-search`**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/postings_writer_round_trip.rs::term_query_finds_correct_docs_across_multiple_tim_blocks` writes the same 26-leading-byte-group field, opens it through the unmodified `blocktree::open`/`DocInput::open`, and runs the unmodified `lucene_search::search_term_query` for terms in the first, a middle, and the last physical block plus a missing term, proving every block is independently reachable and correctly decoded through the real query layer, not just that the writer's own bytes decode in isolation. **Not verified against real Lucene bytes**, same caveat as every other row segment above -- this proves the port's own writer and the port's own unmodified reader agree on a multi-block/multi-child-trie shape, not that it's byte-identical to what `Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter`'s real min/maxItemsInBlock-threshold-driven splitting would produce for the same input. **`IndexWriter` wiring NOT touched by this task**: `IndexWriter::build_postings_output`/`set_postings_field` are unchanged -- a real `IndexWriter::add_document`/`commit()` session still only ever produces single-field output via the (now multi-block-capable) `write_single_field`/`write_fields`, so this new splitting path already reaches `commit()` for whatever field is configured, but wiring a *second* configured field or otherwise changing `IndexWriter`'s own scope is a separate follow-up, not attempted here. **Full-block `.doc` doc-delta/freq emission added (this task): `docFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE` (256) is no longer rejected.** `write_full_block` (new) emits, for every complete 256-doc chunk of a term's postings, a level-0 skip header (`level0NumBytes` skip pointer -- any valid vlong, unused by the reader; `docDelta`/`blockLength` via new `write_vint15`/`write_vlong15` helpers, the exact write-side inverse of `postings::read_vint15`/`read_vlong15`) followed by the doc-delta/freq body, reusing `for_util::for_encode` (doc deltas) and `for_util::pfor_encode` (freqs) directly -- no bit-packing logic is reimplemented here, only called. **Encoding choices, stated plainly**: doc deltas always take the plain positive-`bitsPerValue` `ForUtil` shape; this writer never emits the `bitsPerValue == 0` ("all-256-consecutive") or `bitsPerValue < 0` (dense unary bit-set) alternate encodings the real writer picks for space efficiency (both already decode correctly on the read side, see the `postings.rs` row above, but this writer doesn't choose them) -- one correct, always-decodable shape was chosen over replicating every space-optimization the real writer has. Impacts are always an empty byte region (no competitive-impact computation at all); the reader accepts a zero-length impacts run and decodes it to an empty list, so this is valid, just not space- or query-optimized. The `docFreq % BLOCK_SIZE` remainder still uses the pre-existing group-varint tail-block path, with `prev_doc_id` now threaded from the last full block's last doc ID instead of always `-1`. **The per-term upper bound moved, not disappeared**: `validate_field` now rejects at `docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` (8192, `Error::DocFreqTooLarge`'s condition changed accordingly) rather than `>= BLOCK_SIZE`, since this writer still never emits the level-1 skip entries `postings.rs`'s reader expects to find interleaved above that threshold -- a term that large would round-trip incorrectly, so it is still rejected rather than silently misencoded. **Positions still explicitly out of scope for full blocks**: `total_term_freq >= BLOCK_SIZE` is still rejected with `Error::TotalTermFreqTooLarge`, unchanged -- a term reaching the new `.doc` full-block path can never also need a `.pos` full block in the same call regardless (`total_term_freq >= doc_freq` always), so this is a real, not merely theoretical, non-interaction, not an oversight. **Proof, mirroring the existing bar**: unit tests in `postings_writer.rs` cover `docFreq == BLOCK_SIZE` (one full block, no tail), `docFreq == BLOCK_SIZE + 1` (one full block plus a one-doc tail), `docFreq == 600` (two full blocks plus an 88-doc tail, exercising full-block-to-full-block `prev_doc_id` chaining, not just full-block-to-tail), a full block with `IndexOptions::Docs` (no freqs, no impacts field, no `pfor_encode` call), and small/full-block terms mixed in one field -- every one of these round-trips through the *existing, unmodified* `blocktree::open`/`DocInput::read_postings`, the same functions real Java-Lucene fixtures are opened with elsewhere in this port, asserting every decoded doc/freq exactly, not just "opened without erroring". **No new `fixtures/src/Gen*.java` generator was added for this task** -- deliberately: the existing "big"/"everywhere" fixture field (`docFreq == 300`) already forces the real Lucene *writer*'s own full-block path and fixture-verifies the *reader* side (`postings.rs`) against it; this task's job was proving this port's *own* new writer output is byte-compatible with this port's *own* already-fixture-verified reader, which is a Rust-only round-trip question (no new wire-format understanding is being claimed, unlike a decoder task) -- the same
