reasoning the original single-block writer slice gave for having no generator of its own (see this row's first paragraph). `for_util.rs`'s `for_encode`/`pfor_encode`/`bits_required` lost their `#[allow(dead_code)]` markers now that a real production caller exists. **Still explicitly deferred**: `.pos` full-block emission (`total_term_freq >= BLOCK_SIZE`, see above -- currently provably unreachable from this writer's own `.doc` full-block path, not merely unimplemented); real competitive-impact computation; the all-consecutive/dense-bitset alternate doc-delta encodings; `IndexWriter` wiring (`set_postings_field`/`build_postings_output`) is untouched by this task, so a real `add_document`/`commit()` session still cannot reach a `docFreq >= 256` term without separately tripping `flush_stored_only_segment`'s own pre-existing, unrelated `write_best_speed` `< 128`-docs-per-flush-chunk cap first -- see `crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs`'s `commit_succeeds_below_the_doc_freq_boundary` test doc comment and `crates/lucene-search/tests/index_writer_postings_fixtures.rs`'s trailing comment for the full explanation of why no end-to-end `IndexWriter`-level test of the new boundary exists. **Level-1 skip-entry emission added (this task): `docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` (8192) is no longer rejected, and there is no further per-term docFreq ceiling.** `write_level1_span` (new) emits, before every complete span of `LEVEL1_FACTOR` (32) full level-0 blocks, a level-1 skip entry -- the exact write-side inverse of the existing, unmodified `postings::read_level1_entry`/`LazyDocsCursor::skip_level1_to`. The entry's vlong byte-length field is the one subtle part: `read_level1_entry` computes `doc_end_fp` as that field's value added to the reader's position measured *right after* the vlong itself, i.e. before the freq-gated `skip1EndFP`/`numImpactBytes` fields are read -- so the vlong must span every byte from there through the end of the whole entry plus its 32-block span, not just the span's own bytes (this writer's first draft got that wrong, undercounting by the 4 header bytes, caught by an `advance()` landing 33 docs off from the expected target). **Same encoding choices as level-0**: the level-1 entry's impacts region is always empty (`numImpactBytes = 0`, no competitive-impact computation at either level -- the reader accepts a zero-length impacts run); the `indexHasPos`-gated pos/pay sub-fields `read_level1_entry` supports are never written, because they're provably unreachable from this writer -- a term reaching `docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` has `total_term_freq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS`, which would already have tripped `Error::TotalTermFreqTooLarge` had it indexed positions (the same non-interaction argument `write_full_block`'s own doc comment gives for its level-0 header). **`validate_field`'s docFreq bound is removed entirely, not merely raised**: `postings.rs`'s reader has no level-2 skip structure at all (`Lucene104` postings only ever have levels 0 and 1), so there is nothing left for a further ceiling to protect against -- a term spanning any number of level-1 spans plus a final partial span round-trips exactly like a sub-8192 term always did. **Proof, mirroring the level-0 bar**: unit tests in `postings_writer.rs` round-trip `docFreq == LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` (one span, no remainder), `LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS + 1` (one span plus a one-doc tail), and `2 * LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` (two spans back to back, exercising `level1_last_doc_id`/`prev_doc_id` threading across more than one span) through the existing, unmodified `blocktree::open`/`DocInput::read_postings`, asserting the exact doc/freq lists; the same three boundaries are re-proven through `LazyDocsCursor::advance` (`docfreq_at_level1_boundaries_advance_via_lazy_cursor`); and `writer_level1_span_advance_past_it_skips_corrupted_first_block_header` writes a real span, corrupts its first level-0 block's header bytes in place, and confirms `advance()` past the whole span still succeeds (never decoding the corruption) while `advance()` into the span surfaces it -- the write-side mirror of `postings.rs`'s own `lazy_cursor_advance_skips_whole_corrupted_level1_span_without_decoding_it` proof. **No new `fixtures/src/Gen*.java` generator**, same reasoning as the level-0 task: this is a Rust-only "writer and already-fixture-verified reader agree" question, not a new wire-format claim. **Still explicitly deferred**: real competitive-impact computation (both levels' impacts regions stay empty), the all-consecutive/dense-bitset alternate doc-delta encodings, `.pos` full-block emission, and `IndexWriter` wiring -- `set_postings_field`/`build_postings_output` are untouched by this task, and reaching `docFreq >= LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` via a real `add_document`/`commit()` session was already unreachable before this task (blocked by `flush_stored_only_segment`'s own unrelated `< 128`-docs-per-flush cap) and remains so. **Full-block `.pos` position emission added (this task), superseding the "positions still out of scope for full blocks" caveat above: `total_term_freq >= BLOCK_SIZE` is no longer rejected.** `write_position_tail` now buffers a term's position deltas into one flat, cross-doc sequence (resetting to each doc's absolute first position at that doc's first occurrence, exactly matching `read_positions`'s own flat-then-re-chop shape) and emits every complete 256-occurrence chunk as a full `PForUtil` block via the new `write_full_position_block`, reusing `for_util::pfor_encode` directly. Unlike `.doc` full blocks, a `.pos` full block has **no skip header at all** -- `crate::postings::read_positions`'s `num_full_blocks` loop calls a bare, unframed `for_util::pfor_decode`, so there was no level-0/level-1-equivalent skip-entry inverse to write here; the `total_term_freq % BLOCK_SIZE` remainder still uses the pre-existing vint-tail path. **The real, still-standing ceiling moved from `total_term_freq` to `docFreq`**: `validate_field` now rejects `docFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE` whenever a term indexes positions (`Error::DocFreqTooLargeForPositions`, replacing `Error::TotalTermFreqTooLarge`) -- not because of anything about `.pos` itself (which has no ceiling at all now), but because this writer's `.doc`-side `write_full_block` never emits the pos/pay skip sub-fields `read_full_block_header` expects on a full `.doc` block for a positions-indexing field; since `docFreq <= total_term_freq` always, a term can have arbitrarily large `total_term_freq` from a small number of high-freq docs and still round-trip correctly, as long as `docFreq` itself stays under `BLOCK_SIZE`. **Offsets are now supported too** (`IndexOptions::DocsAndFreqsAndPositionsAndOffsets`). A new `.pay` output stream is opened only when at least one field indexes offsets or payloads; per occurrence, an `offsetStartDelta`/`length` pair follows its position delta -- inlined in `.pos` (reusing the previous occurrence's length unless it changes) for the vint tail, or as two back-to-back bare `PForUtil` arrays in `.pay` for each full 256-occurrence block (`write_full_offset_block`), exactly like `read_positions`'s `has_offsets` branch already expects to decode. `payStartFP` is threaded through the per-term metadata the same running-delta way as `posStartFP`. Proof: `postings_writer.rs` unit tests cover a single position per doc with offsets, multiple positions per doc with offsets (confirming the intra-doc `startOffsetDelta` resets to 0 at each doc boundary), a `total_term_freq` spanning a full `PForUtil` block with offsets, and rejection tests for missing/mismatched/non-monotonic offsets -- all round-tripped through the existing, unmodified `postings::read_positions`/`blocktree::FieldTerms::positions`. **Payloads are now supported too** (`FieldInfo.hasPayloads()`, a per-field bool orthogonal to `IndexOptions` -- unlike offsets, not gated by a distinct `IndexOptions` variant). Payload fields are always written *before* offset fields at each occurrence (matching `Lucene104PostingsWriter`'s own field order), in both the vint tail (length re-written only when it changes since the previous occurrence *within the same term*, reset at `finishTerm`, mirroring the offsets length-suppression convention) and the full `PForUtil` block path (`write_full_payload_length_block`, which never suppresses -- every occurrence's length is written, matching the fixed-size block encoding). A payload is always present (possibly zero-length) for every occurrence once a field has payloads enabled -- there is no "absent payload" state distinct from "present with a zero-length payload". Proof: `postings_writer.rs` unit tests cover a single position per doc with payloads, multiple positions per doc with payloads (same-length-repeated, proving suppression, and varying-length), payloads combined with offsets on the same field (proving the interleaving order and a zero-length-payload case), a `total_term_freq` spanning a full `PForUtil` block with varying/empty payload lengths, and rejection tests for missing/mismatched payloads -- all round-tripped through the existing, unmodified reader with zero reader-side changes needed. **Proof, mirroring the `.doc` full-block bar**: unit tests in `postings_writer.rs` round-trip `total_term_freq == BLOCK_SIZE` (one full position block, no tail) and `total_term_freq == BLOCK_SIZE + 1` (one full block plus a one-occurrence tail), both using genuinely irregular (non-uniform) position deltas spread across several docs, through the existing, unmodified `postings::read_positions`/`blocktree::FieldTerms::positions`, asserting the exact per-doc position sequence, not just counts; a further test proves the previously-rejected "one doc, `freq == BLOCK_SIZE`" shape now round-trips exactly; and a replacement rejection test confirms `docFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE` while indexing positions is still rejected. **No new `fixtures/src/Gen*.java` generator**, same reasoning as every other writer-side task in this row: a Rust-only "writer and already-fixture-verified reader agree" question, not a new wire-format claim. **Still explicitly deferred**: payloads (any full block or tail), real competitive-impact computation, the all-consecutive/dense-bitset alternate `.doc` doc-delta encodings, and `IndexWriter` wiring (`set_postings_field`/`build_postings_output` are untouched by this task, and a real `add_document`/`commit()` session still cannot produce positions at all, per the earlier "IndexWriter wiring explicitly NOT touched" note above) **M2 sweep (b5-postings).** Two wire defects fixed, neither visible to any existing test. (1) **`lastPosBlockOffset` was written as a constant `0`.** Java's `finishTerm` records `posOut.getFilePointer() - posStartFP` *before* writing the vint tail -- the byte length of the term's full `PForUtil` position blocks -- and `Lucene104PostingsReader.reset` turns it into `lastPosBlockFP = posStartFP + lastPosBlockOffset`, the pointer at which `refillPositions` switches to `refillLastPositionBlock`. A `0` makes that true at the term's *first* position block, so real Lucene decodes a `PForUtil` block as a vint tail: garbage positions/offsets/payloads for every term with `totalTermFreq > 256`. Invisible here because this port's own `read_positions` re-derives the split from `total_term_freq` and never reads the field, and invisible to `verify-write-path.sh` because `IndexWriter` cannot write positions yet. Now threaded from `write_position_tail`'s return value through `write_tim_block` into `write_term_metadata`, and tested end to end by walking the real `.tim` leaf block down to its metadata region, decoding it through the unmodified `postings::decode_term_metadata`, and then reading the vint tail out of `.pos` at `posStartFP + offset`. (2) **The doc-delta encoding heuristic compared against the wrong quantity.** Java picks packed FOR when `numBitsNextBitsPerValue <= numBitSetLongs * Long.SIZE`; this port compared against the raw `docRange`, which is `<= numBitSetLongs * 64`, making its packed condition strictly harder and so writing the *bit set* -- the larger encoding -- for every block in the band between. A previous slice had "fixed" this in the wrong direction and pinned it with a test whose doc comment stated Lucene's condition backwards; the test now asserts the packed token and quotes the Java line, and its two siblings bracket the comparison from both sides. **Closed by `c42-readpath-perf`** (was: "`FieldPostingsInput` carries no norms, so impacts are `(maxFreq, norm = 1)`"). `CompetitiveImpactAccumulator` is now ported and `postings_writer::write_fields_with_norms` takes each field's per-document norms; both production callers supply them (`IndexWriter::build_norms_output` hands its columns to `build_postings_output`, and `merge.rs` hands over the same merged norms it writes to `.nvd`). `write_fields` without norms remains Java's `fieldHasNorms == false` path, whose frontier really is one `(maxFreq, 1)` pair. See `docs/sweep/m2/b5-postings.md` and `docs/sweep/m2/c42-readpath-perf.md`. |

**Recommended next slice**: step back to a different Phase -- see below for why the wildcard/prefix intersection slice just landed is intentionally not followed by more work in this same subsystem. Glob-based wildcard/prefix term intersection (`FieldTerms::intersect`, `crate::wildcard::WildcardPattern`) landed this slice: a linear scan (narrowed by a literal-prefix binary-search range) over the already-materialized `entries` `Vec`, covering `PrefixQuery` exactly and `WildcardQuery`'s `*`/`?` glob subset, fixture-verified against real `WildcardQuery`/`CompiledAutomaton.getTermsEnum` output on the "many" field (see the `blocktree.rs` row above for the full writeup). This was explicitly scoped down from real `IntersectTermsEnum`/`CompiledAutomaton` automaton-driven trie-block-skipping (deferred, see that row) and does not include regex (`RegexpQuery`) or fuzzy/Levenshtein (`FuzzyQuery`) automata, both flagged as separate future slices requiring real automaton/edit-distance machinery rather than glob matching. Three reasonable next moves: (1) **regex or fuzzy automaton support**, building a real byte-level automaton/edit-distance representation now that glob/prefix has proven the `FieldTerms::intersect` shape works end-to-end against real Lucene; (2) **real `IntersectTermsEnum`-style trie-block-skipping**, replacing today's "scan the sorted `Vec`" approach with one that walks only the `.tip` trie nodes/`.tim` blocks an automaton's reachable states could lead to -- valuable once a field is large enough that `O(n)` scanning actually shows up in a profile, not before; (3) **step back to a different Phase entirely** -- this subsystem (`postings.rs`/`blocktree.rs`/`for_util.rs`/`wildcard.rs`) has now had a very long consecutive run of slices (level-0/level-1 skip data, positions, lazy cursors, enumeration, impacts, and now wildcard intersection), and PLAN.md's other Phase-1/2 read-path formats (points/BKD query execution, doc-values-backed sorting, or the actual `IndexSearcher`/`Weight`/`Scorer` plumbing that would let any of this get exercised end-to-end against a real query) are likely higher-value to de-risk before this subsystem's next increment -- this is the option this slice's own author recommends. Also still open but narrower: a real-fixture-verified full-`ForUtil`/`PForUtil`-block position path (`totalTermFreq >= BLOCK_SIZE`, currently only unit-tested).

## lucene-index

| Java | Rust | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `index/{DocumentsWriterDeleteQueue,BufferedUpdates,FrozenBufferedUpdates,DocValuesUpdate,BufferedUpdatesStream}` | `lucene-index/src/buffered_updates.rs` | **ported (c7 sweep batch).** Every mutating `IndexWriter` method returns a `long` seqNo starting at 1; deletes buffer with a `docIDUpto` and resolve through a `delGen`-stamped packet against `SegmentCommitInfo.buffered_deletes_gen`. **Deliberate divergence**: the lock-free linked list and `DeleteSlice` heads are not ported -- this port has one indexing thread, and c7's report gives the document-by-document equivalence argument. The ordering contract is proven by breaking each of its three rules in turn and watching the tests fail, not merely asserted. |
| `codecs/lucene99/Lucene99SegmentInfoFormat` (`.si` read + write) | `lucene-index/src/segment_info.rs::{parse, write}` | **both directions ported.** `write` is the exact byte-level inverse of `parse`, emits `numSortFields = 0` unless `SegmentInfo::index_sort` is set (single-field index sort, see below), and does not re-validate that `files` entries are segment-name-prefixed (callers are trusted, matching the parser's own stance) -- exercised via own-reader round-trip unit tests. **Verified against real Lucene in the write→read direction** (unsorted case only, see below): `crates/lucene-index/examples/write_segment_info_fixture.rs` writes two `.si` files (`_0`: compound file, with minVersion, with diagnostics/files/attributes; `_1`: non-compound, with `hasBlocks`, no minVersion, everything else empty) and `fixtures/src/VerifySegmentInfo.java` opens them directly through `Lucene99SegmentInfoFormat.read` and confirms every property matches each segment's manifest -- passed on the first real-Lucene run **b11 sweep (2026-08-28) fixes**: (1) `write` emitted `0` for a false `isCompoundFile`/`hasBlocks`, where Java writes `SegmentInfo.NO == -1` (`0xFF`) -- readers only test `== YES` so it round-tripped, but the bytes differed from Lucene's; caught by a new byte-exact re-encode assertion against the Java-written `.si` fixtures. (2) `read_version` did not validate `Version.fromBits`' `0..=255` per-component range, so a corrupt `.si` yielded a nonsense version later checks then trusted. |
| `index/SegmentInfo.indexSort` / `SortField`/`SortedNumericSortField`/`SortedSetSortField`/`BinarySortField`/`SortFieldProvider` (multi-field index sort, `.si` encoding) | `lucene-index/src/segment_info.rs::{IndexSortField, IndexSortKind, NumericSortKey, StringMissingValue, SortedNumericSelector, SortedSetSelector, SortKeyComparator, read_sort_field, write_sort_field}`, `SegmentInfo::index_sort: Option<Vec<IndexSortField>>` | **byte format now verified against real Lucene, both directions (b11 sweep, 2026-08-28).** Previously this was *this port's own invented encoding* (a 4-byte `field/type-byte/reverse-byte/missing-byte` record) that real Lucene could neither read nor write; it is now the real `Lucene99SegmentInfoFormat` layout -- `writeString(providerName)` followed by that `SortFieldProvider`'s own bytestream. **Read path** decodes all four providers Lucene registers in `META-INF/services/org.apache.lucene.index.SortFieldProvider` (`SortField`, `SortedNumericSortField`, `SortedSetSortField`, `BinarySortField`), every `SortField.Type` name, both multi-value selector enums, and every missing-value encoding (`STRING_FIRST`/`STRING_LAST` markers, raw `INT`/`LONG`, sortable `FLOAT`/`DOUBLE`). **Write path** emits provider `"SortField"`, type `"LONG"`, explicit missing value `Long.MIN_VALUE`/`Long.MAX_VALUE` -- exactly the single-valued long sort this port's `segment_writer::flush_sorted_stored_only_segment` and sort-on-merge actually produce. **Evidence**: `fixtures/data/_2.si` is a real-`Lucene99SegmentInfoFormat`-written two-field sorted `.si` (generated by `fixtures/src/GenSegmentInfo.java`'s `genSorted`); `crates/lucene-index/tests/segment_info_fixtures.rs` parses it *and* asserts our re-encode is byte-identical to Lucene's bytes, and `fixtures/src/VerifySegmentInfo.java` (run by `scripts/verify-write-path.sh`) opens a Rust-written sorted `.si` through real Lucene and compares the parsed `Sort`. **Widened to the whole `SortFieldProvider` model (m2 sweep `c35-norms-and-sort`).** `IndexSortField` is now `(field, reverse, IndexSortKind)` where `IndexSortKind` covers all four providers, every `SortField.Type` that can be an index sort, both selector enums, and every missing-value form -- an arbitrary numeric sentinel, `STRING_FIRST`/`STRING_LAST`, or **none at all** (Java compares such a document as `0`, or as the smallest ordinal for a string sort). `write` is the byte-level inverse of `parse` for every one of them. Before c35 the model was `(field, reverse, missing-first-or-last)` and `parse` *rejected* everything else, so an index a real `IndexWriter` wrote with an ordinary sort could not be **opened at all**. Two things are still refused, and Java refuses both: a `SortField.Type` whose `getIndexSorter()` is null (`SCORE`/`DOC`/`CUSTOM`/`STRING_VAL`/`REWRITEABLE`, which `IndexWriterConfig.setIndexSort` rejects outright) and a `SortedNumericSortField` typed `STRING` (an `AssertionError` inside Java's own `serialize`). `SortKeyComparator` is the comparator for every kind whose per-document key is a single `i64` -- the four numeric types, both numeric selectors, and term ordinals -- and is `None` for `BinarySortField`, whose keys are raw bytes. It also knows which *encoding* each kind's column holds: a NUMERIC FLOAT column holds `Float.floatToRawIntBits` (`FloatDocValuesField`) while a SORTED_NUMERIC one holds `NumericUtils.floatToSortableInt` (`FloatField`), and `SortedNumericSelector.wrap` undoes the latter before `IndexSorter.FloatSorter` sees a value -- comparing the stored form as raw bits instead reverses the whole negative half of the ordering. **Evidence**: `fixtures/data/sorted_index_wide/` is a real `IndexWriter` index sorted three ways (`LONG` descending with missing value `42`, `SortedNumericSortField` `MAX` with no missing value, `STRING` descending by ordinal), and `crates/lucene-index/tests/index_sort_wide_fixtures.rs` opens it, reproduces Lucene's physical order with this port's own comparator, and runs this port's `CheckIndex`; `write_segment_info_fixture`'s `_3` writes all four providers into one `.si` and `VerifySegmentInfo` compares real Lucene's `Sort.toString()` against `describe_index_sort`'s. **What the writers can produce is narrower than what the reader accepts, deliberately**: `IndexWriter::set_index_sort` accepts the numeric kinds (NUMERIC column) and `SortedNumericSortField` (SORTED_NUMERIC column, either selector) and refuses `STRING`/`SortedSetSortField`/`BinarySortField` with `Error::UnsupportedIndexSortKind` -- an ordinal sort needs ordinals this writer only assigns *after* it permutes the buffer, and a binary sort has no single-`i64` key. `CheckIndex.testSort` verifies NUMERIC, SORTED_NUMERIC (both selectors) and SORTED (ordinals), and reports a `SortedSetSortField`/`BinarySortField` sort as **skipped**, naming the field and the reason, rather than either passing it silently or failing a healthy index. `SortedNumericSelector.MIN`/`MAX` are the *first* and the *last* stored value of a document (`MinValue`/`MaxValue`), which is well defined only because a document's SORTED_NUMERIC values are stored ascending -- `IndexWriter::collect_sorted_numeric_values` now sorts them as `SortedNumericDocValuesWriter.finishCurrentDoc` does, and `check_index` gained `CheckIndex.checkSortedNumericDocValues`' "values out of order" check. **`c17-index-sort` (2026-08-29) corrected what the encoding *means*.** A missing value names the sentinel Lucene substitutes (then `SortMissingValue::First`/`Last`, i.e. `Long.MIN_VALUE`/`Long.MAX_VALUE`; since c35 any value at all), and that sentinel is compared like any other value, so `reverse` applies to it too -- a `Last` document under a reversed sort lands **first**. `segment_writer`'s comparator previously pinned missing values to one end regardless of `reverse`, which made the physical order this port produced disagree with the `.si` describing it; real Lucene's `CheckIndex.testSort` rejects such a segment outright (measured). Evidence in both directions: `fixtures/data/sorted_index/` is a real `IndexWriter`-with-`setIndexSort` index whose reversed missing-last tier puts its missing documents first, and `crates/lucene-index/tests/index_sort_fixtures.rs` asserts this port's comparator reproduces Lucene's own physical order from Lucene's own columns; `fixtures/src/VerifySortedSegment.java` runs the reverse direction. |
| `index/IndexWriterConfig.setIndexSort` / `index/IndexingChain.maybeSortSegment` + `validateIndexSortDVType` / `index/Sorter.DocMap` / `index/IndexWriter.validateIndexSort`+`isCongruentSort` (index-sorted flush) | `lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::{set_index_sort, index_sort, validate_index_sort_against_existing_segments, sort_pending_buffer, build_and_write_segment}`, `lucene-index/src/segment_writer.rs::{sort_permutation, permute_in_place, SortKeySpec}` | **ported (`c17-index-sort`, 2026-08-29); this is what made every format's index-sort write path reachable.** `IndexWriter::flush` now takes the sorted path when a sort is configured. **Design divergence, deliberate**: Java threads a `Sorter.DocMap` into *each* format's writer (`SortingStoredFieldsConsumer`, `SortingTermVectorsConsumer`, `NumericDocValuesWriter.sortDocValues`, `Lucene99FlatVectorsWriter.writeSortingField`, ...) and each remaps its own doc ids; this port permutes the **document buffer** once, before any format is built, so stored fields, postings, term vectors, norms, doc values and vectors are put in sort order together and by construction consistently. That removes the per-format remap Java has to repeat -- and forget: c10 found the vector one unreachable here, and a permutation applied to the documents but not the vectors is `CheckIndex`-clean and silently attaches every vector to the wrong document (measured, negative control). **Validation ported**: non-empty sort, sort field's doc-values type must match the kind of sort (`validateIndexSortDVType`: NUMERIC for a numeric `SortField`, SORTED_NUMERIC for a `SortedNumericSortField`; ordinal and byte sorts are refused outright with `Error::UnsupportedIndexSortKind`, since this writer assigns ordinals only after it permutes the buffer), sort field must actually be written (Java gets this free; here doc values are opt-in and `DocValues.getNumeric` substitutes an all-missing column rather than failing, which makes `testSort` vacuous), congruence with every segment already in the index (`validateIndexSort`/`isCongruentSort`), no doc-values update against a sort field, and no document blocks without a parent field (`CorruptIndexException` in Java; this port has no parent-field write path). A segment-private delete's `docIDUpto` is a pre-sort buffer position and is mapped back through `newToOld` before it is compared, as Java does through `ReadersAndUpdates.sortMap`. **Scope limits (c35 widened them)**: the four numeric `SortField.Type`s over a NUMERIC column and `SortedNumericSortField` (either selector) over a SORTED_NUMERIC one, each with any missing value or none. (The old "a merge does **not** preserve the sort" limit recorded here was superseded by `c22-sorted-merge`/`c26-merge-completeness` -- see the `SegmentMerger`/`MergeState`/`MultiSorter` row below, which is the current statement.) **Evidence**: `fixtures/src/VerifySortedSegment.java` (in `scripts/verify-write-path.sh`, 19/19 -> 20/20) opens a 2000-document Rust-written sorted index with a real `DirectoryReader`, checks `LeafMetaData.sort()` tier for tier, re-derives the expected permutation itself and checks every doc id's stored value / both doc-values columns / unique postings term / norm / vector, runs a real `KnnFloatVectorQuery` over the sorted ordinal space, and runs `CheckIndex` at `MIN_LEVEL_FOR_SLOW_CHECKS` (whose `testSort` rebuilds the comparators from the `.si`). |
| `index/DocumentsWriterPerThread` sort-on-flush (physically reordering a flushed segment's documents by the index sort) | `lucene-index/src/segment_writer.rs::flush_sorted_stored_only_segment`, `SortKeySpec` | **partial.** Takes a non-empty, priority-ordered slice of `SortKeySpec`s (one per sort field, each pairing one `IndexSortField` with that field's in-memory `&[Option<i64>]` per-doc sort key), stable-sorts the whole batch by comparing field 0 first and falling through to field 1, field 2, etc. only on a tie at every prior tier (ties/both-missing-at-every-tier keep original relative order), reorders stored fields accordingly, and records the full sort in the flushed `.si`. Each field's `reverse`/missing-value policy is applied independently at its own tier -- one field can be descending with missing-last while another is ascending with missing-first, matching real Lucene's per-`SortField` configuration. This port has no write-side doc-values format yet, so each field's sort keys can't be read back from an already-written file the way real Lucene's `DocValuesConsumer`-fed sort does -- they must already be in memory, parallel to `docs`. No query-side early-termination optimization either (irrelevant to correctness in this port regardless of index sort support). |
| `index/SegmentMerger` + `index/MergeState` + `index/MultiSorter` + `index/DocIDMerger` (one merge, with or without an index sort) | `lucene-index/src/merge.rs::{merge_segments, merge_stored_only_segments, merge_sorted_stored_only_segments, sorted_doc_order, compare_heads, build_doc_id_maps, MergeSortKeySpec, MergeOptions}` | **done (batch `c22-sorted-merge`).** `merge_segments` is the single implementation and `sort_fields: Option<..>` is the *only* difference between the two shapes -- `None` concatenates the sources in order (`MergeState.buildDeletionDocMaps`), `Some` k-way merges them on the shared index sort (`MultiSorter.sort`, comparator reused from `segment_info::SortKeyComparator`, ties broken by source index then doc id exactly as Java's `LeafAndDocID` queue does). `merge_stored_only_segments` / `merge_sorted_stored_only_segments` are thin wrappers kept for existing callers. **Every format is merged either way**: stored fields, postings, term vectors, norms, doc values, BKD points and KNN vectors -- so the sorted entry point no longer trades a preserved sort for lost data (it previously wrote no postings, no points and no vectors, which is why `IndexWriter::segment_stats` had to withhold sorted segments from the merge policy entirely). The order is single-sourced: `doc_order` (the `(source, doc)` pairs in merged doc-id order) drives stored fields / doc values / norms / term vectors by walking it, and `build_doc_id_maps` inverts it into `MergeState.docMaps` for postings / points / vectors, which look documents up. **Doc values are now multi-field**: every merged doc-values field of every type goes into one `.dvm`/`.dvd`/`.dvs` through `doc_values::write_dense_fields` (the real on-disk shape), which a multi-tier index sort requires, and a NUMERIC column may be **sparse** (`SortField.setMissingValue`'s normal case). **Norms are multi-field too as of `c26-merge-completeness`**: every merged norms field goes into one `.nvm`/`.nvd` pair through `norms::write_fields` (the norms analogue of `write_dense_fields`, and what `Lucene90NormsConsumer` does with its one `addNormsField` call per field), so `Error::TooManyNormsFields` is gone; the flush side writes the same set with no opt-in at all as of `c35-norms-and-sort`, and `merge_norms` -- driven by the *merged* `FieldInfos`' `hasNorms()`, exactly as `NormsConsumer.merge` is -- now emits a **sparse** column when a document or a whole source has no value, where it used to be `Error::NormsFieldMissingInSource`. **Merge completeness is mechanically gated** (`merge::SegmentFormat` + `merge::check_format_coverage`, called from `execute_merge`): every format a source segment's own `SegmentCommitInfo::files` lists must be one the caller opened onto that source's `MergeSource`, and an extension no `SegmentFormat` claims is an error -- so a flush path that learns a new format is caught on the first merge instead of having it dropped silently, which is the shape of c22's findings 14/22/23/24. **A segment with a doc-values update merges too** (c22's last withheld case): `execute_merge` resolves each field to its *newest* generation through `field_updates::read_current_field_infos`/`read_current_column`, and the merged segment folds every generation back into one base column, so `segment_stats` now withholds nothing. The merged `.si` claims the index sort exactly when the k-way merge produced it, and lists itself in `SegmentInfo.files` (`Lucene99SegmentInfoFormat.write`'s `si.addFile`, which the merge previously omitted -- `CheckIndex`'s `si.files_lists_itself`, and an `IndexFileDeleter` reference nothing held). `IndexWriter::execute_merge` opens each source's doc values, norms and vectors, reads each sort tier's key out of the very NUMERIC column the merged segment will carry, refuses a merge whose sources disagree about the sort (`IndexWriter.validateIndexSort`), and routes through `merge_segments`. **Precondition, not re-checked inside `merge_segments`:** every source must already be ordered by `sort_fields` (true for anything a sorted flush or a previous sorted merge produced); `execute_merge` establishes it from each `.si`. **Verified against real Lucene**: `write_sorted_merged_segment_fixture` builds the same 2 000-document two-tier-sorted corpus as the flush fixture, but from eight overlapping flushes with one document in fifty-three deleted, merged into one segment, and `fixtures/src/VerifySortedSegment.java` -- the *same* verifier as the flushed case -- checks `LeafMetaData.sort()` tier for tier, the exact expected permutation it re-derives itself, both doc-values columns, every unique postings term's doc id, every norm, every vector component, a real `KnnFloatVectorQuery` over the merged HNSW graph, that deleted documents' terms are gone from the merged dictionary, and `CheckIndex` at `MIN_LEVEL_FOR_SLOW_CHECKS` (whose `testSort` rebuilds the comparators from the `.si`). **`SegmentMerger`'s per-reader metadata (c36)**: `MergeSource` carries this source's `SegmentInfo.minVersion` and `hasBlocks` -- Java's `LeafMetaData` -- and the merged `.si` takes the **minimum** `minVersion` over the sources (`None` if any source records none, seeded with the merging writer's version, `SegmentMerger`'s constructor) and the **disjunction** of `hasBlocks` (`IndexWriter.mergeMiddle`). Until c36 the merged `.si` claimed the merging writer's own version, which is right only while every source was written by this port at that version. `IndexWriter` also now takes `mergeMiddle`'s `shouldMerge() == false` path: a merge whose sources hold no live document writes nothing and `IndexWriter::drop_merge` (`commitMerge` with `dropSegment`) retires the sources with nothing published. **Verified against real Lucene**: `write_merged_metadata_fixture` merges three segments a real `IndexWriter` wrote whose `.si` files record `minVersion` 10.2.0/10.0.0/10.1.0 with `hasBlocks` on one, and `fixtures/src/VerifyMergedMetadata.java` reads both fields back off `LeafMetaData`. |
| `index/SegmentInfos` (`segments_N` read: header/footer, per-segment commit metadata, user data) | `lucene-index/src/segment_infos.rs` | ported (read-only), fixture-verified against a real 2-commit `IndexWriter` output **b11 sweep (2026-08-28)**: added the two `SegmentInfos.readCommit` validations that are performable at this layer -- `luceneVersion.major >= indexCreatedVersionMajor` (a commit cannot claim to have been created by a newer major than wrote it) and `Version.fromBits`' `0..=255` per-component range on both version triples. The remaining `readCommit` validations all need each segment's `maxDoc`/`minVersion` from its `.si`, which this module deliberately never opens; they now live in `check_index.rs` as `commit.*` checks. Also added `SegmentCommitInfo::files()` (`SegmentInfo.files` + the `.liv`, field-infos-generation and doc-values-update files the commit owns), the set every file-walking tool must use. **`SegmentCommitInfo.generationAdvanced()` (c36)**: all four generation-advancing mutators (`advanceDelGen`/`advanceDocValuesGen`/`advanceFieldInfosGen`/`setBufferedDeletesGen`) now give the segment-commit a **new `sci_id`**, which is Java's whole reason for the field -- "an Id that uniquely identifies this segment commit ... This ID changes each time the segment changes due to a delete, doc-value or field update". Nothing in Lucene validates the bytes, so no read broke; what was lost was its use as a **change token** by `openIfChanged`-style reuse, replication and caches. Derived (a hash of the segment id plus all four generations) rather than drawn from a CSPRNG, for the reason `segment_writer::derive_sci_id` already records; `advanceNextWrite*Gen` deliberately does **not** re-id, matching Java. |
| `index/DocumentsWriterPerThread`/`IndexingChain` (tokenize + build an in-memory inverted index from indexed field text) | `lucene-index/src/indexing_chain.rs::{InMemoryInvertedIndex, TermPostingList, PostingEntry, invert_documents}` | **wired all the way through to a persisted, searchable index (task #211) -- superseding this row's earlier "in-memory only" status (task #63).** `invert_documents` runs each `(doc_id, field, text)` triple through a `lucene_analysis::Analyzer` and groups the result into `InMemoryInvertedIndex { terms: BTreeMap<(field, term), TermPostingList> }`, each `PostingEntry` holding a doc's `doc_id` plus its occurrences' resolved absolute positions and character offsets (`term_freq()` = occurrence count) -- the same per-term/per-doc grouping shape real Lucene's `TermsHashPerField`/`FreqProxTermsWriterPerField` build while indexing. `IndexWriter::build_postings_output` (`crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs`) calls `invert_documents` directly for every field opted into postings via `set_postings_field`/`add_postings_field`, and now (task #211) feeds its per-occurrence positions/offsets through to `lucene_codecs::postings_writer::write_fields` whenever that field's `index_options` is `IndexOptions::DocsAndFreqsAndPositions`/`DocsAndFreqsAndPositionsAndOffsets` (previously rejected outright by `resolve_postings_field`, and previously silently dropped even for the `Docs`/`DocsAndFreqs` fields that path did accept). `IndexWriter::write_postings_files` was also fixed in the same task to actually write and register the resulting `.pos`/`.pay` files in `.si` -- before task #211 it built them via `postings_writer::write_fields` but never persisted them, an independent latent bug task #211 closes alongside the positions-wiring gap. A caller can now: add real text via `add_document`, opt a field into `DocsAndFreqsAndPositions`(`AndOffsets`) postings, `commit()`, and run a real `PhraseQuery` against the resulting on-disk segment through `lucene_search`'s existing phrase-query path (`crates/lucene-search/src/directory_reader.rs`'s `phrase_query_e2e` test module) and get correct results -- the first genuine add_document -> tokenize -> invert -> write -> commit -> search round trip for analyzed text in this port. **Sweep c23 (M2)**: payloads are wired through, and the whole positional write path is now read back by real Lucene. `invert_documents_with_payloads` takes a `PayloadSource` (this port's stand-in for `PayloadAttribute`, since `lucene_analysis::Token` has no payload attribute) plus the list of `store_payloads` fields, and fills the payload run -- empty for a field without payloads, otherwise one length per occurrence; `IndexWriter::set_payload_source` installs it and `build_postings_output` now sets `has_payloads` from the field's own `FieldInfo.store_payloads` instead of the constant `false` it used to pass. That constant was a live defect, not just a gap: a `.fnm` declaring payloads over postings written without them makes real Lucene's `Lucene104PostingsReader` either fail to open the segment (no `.pay` at all, when the field has no offsets) or mis-frame every offset it decodes (measured -- see `docs/sweep/m2/c23-positions-writer.md`). `build_term_vectors_output` likewise now records the offsets and payloads the field declares instead of positions only, which is what makes `CheckIndex.testTermVectors`' offset and payload cross-checks bite rather than skip. **Verified end to end**: `crates/lucene-index/examples/write_positions_segment_fixture.rs` writes a 20 000-document, six-field index (one field per `IndexOptions` rung, plus offsets-without-payloads and payloads-without-offsets) whose dense term closes two `LEVEL1_NUM_DOCS` spans, and `fixtures/src/VerifyPositionsSegment.java` walks 51 sampled documents with a fresh `PostingsEnum` each -- so every sample is reached through `advance` and c20's `.pos`/`.pay` skip records -- comparing every occurrence's position, both offsets and its payload, then runs a `PhraseQuery` and `CheckIndex`: `scripts/verify-write-path.sh` 21/21 -> **22/22**. **Measured** (`index-bench`, 50k docs x 40 tokens, interleaved): positions +1.3 us/doc over `DocsAndFreqs`, offsets +3.4, payloads +26.1 -- the payload figure was the per-occurrence `Vec<u8>` slot, not the bytes (an all-empty-payload control cost the same). **Sweep c38 (M2) closed that**: payloads are one flat `(payload_bytes, payload_lengths)` run per `(field, term)` on `TermPostingList`, and the identical shape on `postings_writer::TermPostings`, so `build_postings_output` moves the run across without copying and `write_position_tail` borrows it instead of re-flattening. `PostingEntry` consequently carries no payload field at all, which makes every non-payload field cheaper too. Re-measured as an alternating min-of-N A/B between two binaries, in two independent windows, every arm faster or level and smaller in both, with peak RSS stable to three digits: `freqs` **-18.6%** peak RSS, `offsets` -9.9%, `payloads` **-49.1%**, `payloads-empty` **-46.1%**; and the inverted structure itself, 102.5 MB -> 75.8 MB on 4.9 MB of body text (`crates/lucene-index/examples/invert_memory.rs`). What is left of that structure is 36.8 MB of `Vec<PostingEntry>` slots and 36.6 MB of capacity-4 `Vec<Occurrence>` first allocations -- the `ByteBlockPool`/`IntBlockPool`/`ByteSlicePool` redesign `LEDGER.md` item 15 records, which c38 assessed as a milestone rather than a batch (`docs/sweep/m2/c38-allocation-shape.md`). **Verified by**: `indexing_chain.rs`'s own pre-existing unit tests (unchanged -- composition of task #61's `Analyzer` plus this module's grouping logic), `index_writer.rs`'s new `commit_with_positions_index_options_writes_a_readable_pos_file`/`commit_with_offsets_index_options_writes_readable_offsets` unit tests (real `IndexWriter` commit, decoded back via `blocktree::FieldTerms::positions`), and `lucene-search`'s new `phrase_query_e2e::phrase_query_finds_adjacent_terms_from_a_real_add_document_commit` end-to-end test (real `PhraseQuery` search against a real `add_document`-flushed segment). Coverage: `indexing_chain.rs` 100% lines, `index_writer.rs` 98.21% lines, `directory_reader.rs` 98.46% lines. **Sweep b9 (M2)**: `invert_documents` now accumulates into a `HashMap` and orders the term dictionary once at the end, rather than paying an O(log n) chain of `String` comparisons on every (document, term) touch -- the same split real `TermsHashPerField` makes between a hash-keyed `BytesRefHash` during accumulation and a sort at flush. `IndexWriter` also now calls it **once per commit** for the union of the postings/term-vector/norms fields, matching `IndexingChain.processField`'s single `PerField.invert()` fan-out, instead of once per consumer. Measured on `benchmarks/rust-runner`'s `index-bench` (20k docs x 40 tokens, postings + norms on one field): 43.9 us/doc -> 17.4 us/doc, ~2.5x. **Sweep c28 (M2) -- one deliberate divergence in the position guard.** Java's `IndexingChain.PerField.invert` guards `invertState.position += posIncr` twice: it detects the `int` wrap after the fact (`position < lastPosition`) and throws `IllegalArgumentException("position overflowed Integer.MAX_VALUE")`, and it separately throws for anything past `IndexWriter.MAX_POSITION` (`Integer.MAX_VALUE - 128`). Neither guard existed here, and the bare `+=` was a **panic** in a debug build. Both are now ported as `advance_position`, but with a **divergent outcome**: Java rejects the *document*, while this port **clamps** the position at `MAX_POSITION`, because `invert_documents`/`invert_documents_with_payloads` return an `InMemoryInvertedIndex` and not a `Result`. The consequence is explicit: a document carrying more than 2^31 token positions in one field (a ~2 GB field value) is indexed here where Lucene would have refused it, with every position past the ceiling collapsed onto `MAX_POSITION` -- so a phrase or span query can return a **false positive** on that document. That is chosen over the alternative the unguarded code had (a wrapped negative position, encoded as a garbage vint delta, corrupting the `.pos` file for every document after it), but it is a silently-wrong-answer divergence rather than a faithful port, and closing it means making `invert_documents*` fallible -- a one-line change at its single call site in `index_writer.rs`. Tracked here rather than only in `docs/sweep/m2/c28-arith-index.md`. **Offset unit (c33)**: `invert_documents*` forwards `lucene_analysis::Token`'s `start_offset`/`end_offset` into `Occurrence` untouched, and `IndexWriter` writes them verbatim into `.pos`/`.pay`/`.tvd`, so the unit is whatever the analyzer emits -- UTF-8 bytes until c33, Java `char`s (`OffsetAttribute`'s own unit) now. Nothing downstream could have caught the wrong one: `CheckIndex` only checks that offsets are ordered and in range, never that they index the stored text. Pinned by `offsets_forwarded_to_the_codec_slice_the_source_text_as_java_chars`, which re-slices the source text by UTF-16 code unit and gets each term back. |
| `index/SegmentInfos.write` | `lucene-index/src/segment_infos.rs::write` | **ported.** Byte-level inverse of `parse`, always emits `VERSION_CURRENT` (so the SciId marker byte is always written, matching `parse`'s expectation for this format) and derives the `segments_N` file name from `generation` via the existing `lucene_store::directory::segments_file_name` helper rather than re-deriving the base-36 suffix a second time. Unlike `segment_info::write`/`field_infos::write` (which return `Vec<u8>` and let the caller route bytes through a `Directory`), this one takes a `&dyn Directory` directly and performs the write-then-sync itself: a `segments_N` file's correctness as a *commit* depends on being fsynced before it's durable, mirroring real `IndexWriter.commit()`'s write-then-`Directory.sync` sequencing rather than leaving that easy-to-forget step to the caller. Unit-tested by round-tripping through `parse` via a real `FsDirectory` (empty commit, single segment, multi-segment with SciId/del-counts/DV-update-files/user-data, generation-0 plain `segments` name, negative-generation rejection). **This is also the milestone verified by `crates/lucene-index/examples/write_segment_infos_fixture.rs` + `fixtures/src/VerifySegmentInfos.java`: a genuinely complete single-segment index (`.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm` + `.fnm` + `.si` + `segments_N`, reusing the already-verified `stored_fields::write_best_speed`/`field_infos::write`/`segment_info::write`) opened via real, high-level `DirectoryReader.open(FSDirectory.open(path))` -- not hand-built codec-level access -- succeeded on the first run, confirming doc count and stored field values through ordinary `IndexReader`/`StoredFields` calls.** The fixture's fields are deliberately stored-only (`IndexOptions::None`, no doc values/term vectors/points/vectors): `SegmentCoreReaders` (`org.apache.lucene.index.SegmentCoreReaders`) only opens a `FieldsProducer` when `FieldInfos.hasPostings()` is true, so a segment with zero indexed fields needs no `.tim`/`.tip`/`.doc` postings files at all -- this port has no postings/doc-values/points/vector *write* path yet (those remain read-only, see the rows above), so a fully-indexed segment (a real `TextField`, sorted doc values, etc.) is not yet achievable through `DirectoryReader.open`; only the stored-fields-only case was in scope for this milestone and is what's verified **Sweep b9 (M2)**: split into `write_pending` (serialize + fsync `pending_segments_N`, deleting a truncated one on failure) and `finish_pending` (rename onto `segments_N` + `syncMetaData`), plus `rollback_pending`; `write` is now those two back to back, so every existing caller became crash-safe without changing its call. This is `SegmentInfos.prepareCommit`/`finishCommit`/`rollbackCommit`. |
| `index/IndexWriter` (unifying add/update/delete/commit facade) | `lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::IndexWriter` | **facade over already-built primitives, not a reimplementation of any of them.** `IndexWriter::open(dir, fields, codec_name, lucene_version)` resumes an existing commit (via `lucene_store::directory::last_commit_generation`, distinguishing "no commit yet" from "a commit file exists but is corrupt") or starts a fresh empty index; `add_document` buffers in memory; `commit()` flushes the buffer through the existing `segment_writer::flush_stored_only_segment` and writes the updated segment list via the existing `segment_infos::write`; `update_document`/`delete_documents` delegate straight to the existing `update_document::update_document`/`term_delete::resolve_and_apply_term_delete` + `deletes::apply_deletes`, unchanged; `apply_merge` folds an already-executed `merge::merge_stored_only_segments` result back into the writer's committed state. Every one of those underlying functions existed before this task -- this module's only new logic is the state threading (current `SegmentInfos`, buffered docs, segment-name counter) that previously had to be hand-carried by each caller/test across calls. Segment/commit ids are generated by hashing a monotonic counter with the current time (`DefaultHasher`, not a CSPRNG) since this workspace has no `rand` dependency and the only property a segment id needs here is uniqueness across a session, not statistical randomness. **Update (automatic merge triggering):** `IndexWriter::set_merge_policy(Some(config))` now opts a writer into automatic merging -- every subsequent `commit()`, right after writing its own `segments_N`, loops calling the existing `merge_policy::find_merges` against the writer's now-committed segments (stats built from each segment's `.si` via `segment_info::parse` + `merge_policy::segment_byte_size`, plus `del_count` already in memory) and, for each proposed group, executes it via the existing `merge::merge_stored_only_segments` and folds the result in via the existing `apply_merge`, repeating until `find_merges` proposes nothing further (guaranteed to terminate: every merge strictly shrinks the segment count). With no merge policy set (the default a freshly-opened writer starts with), `commit()` is byte-for-byte unchanged from before this feature, so none of the original tests needed updating. New tests: no-merge-policy commits never auto-merge; a commit sequence staying at/under `segments_per_tier` stays unmerged; a sequence crossing it merges down to fewer segments with every original document still readable; and 20 consecutive single-document commits under a tight policy converge without panicking or looping (segment count never exceeds commit count). **Explicitly deferred, not silently missing:** no merge-policy configurability from `open()` itself (only via the separate `set_merge_policy` setter), no concurrent/background merging, no multi-tier scheduling beyond one `merge_policy::find_merges` call, `update_document`/`delete_documents` do not trigger this check (only `commit()` does), no RAM-based flush triggering (only an explicit `commit()` flushes), no multi-threaded `DocumentsWriterPerThread`-style pooling, and `update_document`/`delete_documents` still only resolve against segments the caller supplies an opened `SegmentDeleteSource` for (inherited from `update_document.rs`/`term_delete.rs` -- no reader pool that opens every segment automatically). **No new Java fixture** (composition of already-verified primitives, same precedent as `update_document.rs`/`merge.rs`): unit tests cover opening a fresh directory, add+commit producing one readable segment (read back both through the returned struct and by re-reading `segments_N` off disk), a no-pending-documents commit still producing a valid next generation, multiple commits producing multiple independent, non-colliding segment names, resuming a writer against an already-committed directory without a segment-name collision, `update_document`/`delete_documents` against the same real-Lucene postings fixture `term_delete.rs`/`update_document.rs` already use (including a failing delete leaving the writer's in-memory state and on-disk `segments_N` untouched), and `apply_merge` folding a real `merge_stored_only_segments` result over two committed segments back into the writer. `SegmentInfos`/`SegmentCommitInfo` gained `PartialEq` (needed to assert a failed call left state byte-for-byte unchanged) -- a mechanical addition, no behavior change. **Update (postings/term-vectors/doc-values wiring):** `set_postings_field`, `set_term_vector_field`, and `set_doc_values_field` (NUMERIC only) each opt a writer into building and writing real postings/term vectors/doc values for one field per commit, respectively -- see this crate's/`lucene-codecs`'s own parity rows above for each feature's exact scope. All three can be configured at once, in the same or different fields, in one commit. `segment_stats()` originally excluded any segment carrying `.doc`/`.tvd`/`.dvd` files from automatic-merge candidacy, since `execute_merge` only merged stored fields. **Update (postings merge-caller wiring, task: wire a real end-to-end caller for postings/points merge logic):** `execute_merge` now opens each source segment's `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` (via `lucene_codecs::blocktree::open`) and `.doc` (via `lucene_codecs::postings::DocInput::open`) whenever its `.si` lists a `.tim` file, and builds one `crate::merge::SourcePostings` per field this writer's own field schema marks postings-eligible (`IndexOptions::Docs`/`DocsAndFreqs`, the same restriction `set_postings_field` enforces) that segment's term dictionary actually has an entry for -- `pos_in`/`pay_in` stay `None`, since this writer never writes positions. `segment_stats()` no longer excludes `.doc`-carrying segments from `find_merges`' candidate pool; `.tvd`/`.dvd`-carrying segments are still excluded (term-vector/doc-values merging is not wired into `execute_merge` at all yet -- a separate, still-open gap). Points (`crate::merge::SourcePoints`) are not wired here: this writer has no points write path at flush time at all, so there is no real segment it could ever produce with points data to open in the first place. See the `SegmentMerger`/`merge.rs` row below for the full postings/points-merge-caller writeup and the new end-to-end test. **Update (term-vectors merge-caller wiring, task #210, closing the gap the previous update explicitly left open):** `execute_merge` now also opens each source segment's `.tvd`/`.tvx`/`.tvm` (via `lucene_codecs::term_vectors::open`) whenever its `.si` lists a `.tvd` file, and sets the resulting `TermVectorsReader` as `crate::merge::MergeSource::term_vectors`, which `merge_stored_only_segments`'s existing `merge_term_vectors` plumbing then reads per doc. Since every segment this writer ever flushes shares this writer's own single `self.fields` schema (field numbers are never reassigned per segment), the field-number remap `merge_term_vectors` applies is the identity mapping in practice here -- same reasoning as the postings wiring above. `segment_stats()` no longer excludes `.tvd`-carrying segments from `find_merges`' candidate pool; only `.dvd`-carrying (doc-values) segments remain excluded, tracked as the one still-open gap. The previously gap-preserving test `segments_with_term_vectors_are_never_automatically_merged_away` is now inverted to `segments_with_term_vectors_are_automatically_merged_with_term_vectors_preserved` (mirroring the postings task's own test-inversion precedent): three one-doc commits with distinct per-doc text, crossing a tight merge policy's `segments_per_tier`, merge down to one segment whose term vectors (reopened for real through `lucene_codecs::term_vectors::open`) still hold every source doc's own distinct terms (checked as a set, since automatic merging doesn't guarantee source-segment order in the merged segment). A real end-to-end test also now exists proving postings and term vectors merge correctly *together* through one automatic merge: `crates/lucene-search/src/directory_reader.rs::postings_merge_e2e::term_vectors_survive_automatic_merge_alongside_postings` flushes three segments with both postings and term vectors on the same field, triggers a real automatic merge, confirms a `TermQuery` still resolves terms from every source segment through the real `DirectoryReader`, and separately confirms the merged segment's term vectors (opened directly, since term vectors have no query/search surface) hold every source doc's content intact. **Update (multiple postings/term-vector fields per commit):** `IndexWriter::add_postings_field`/`IndexWriter::add_term_vector_field` opt a writer into building postings/term vectors for *additional* fields on top of whatever `set_postings_field`/`set_term_vector_field` already established (which keep their original "replace, don't accumulate" semantics) -- `self.postings_fields`/`self.term_vector_fields` are now `Vec`s, not single `Option`s. `commit()` batches every entry in `postings_fields` into exactly **one** `lucene_codecs::postings_writer::write_fields` call (the same multi-field write primitive `merge_postings` in `merge.rs` already used), so a commit with, say, two distinct indexed text fields produces one `.doc`/`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` file set covering both, not two separate file sets. Term vectors follow the same shape into `term_vectors::write_best_speed`, which already accepted multiple `TermVectorField` entries per `TermVectorsDocument` before this task -- the gap was purely on `IndexWriter`'s calling side (`build_term_vectors_output` only ever built a single-field `TermVectorField` per doc), now fixed to build one `TermVectorField` per configured field with content for that doc. New tests (`crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs` unit tests plus `crates/lucene-search/tests/index_writer_postings_fixtures.rs::two_distinct_postings_fields_in_one_commit_are_both_searchable` and `crates/lucene-search/tests/index_writer_term_vectors_fixtures.rs::two_distinct_term_vector_fields_in_one_commit_are_both_readable`) prove two distinct fields committed together are both independently readable/searchable with no cross-contamination, on top of the existing single-field tests (unchanged, still passing byte-for-byte). **Still single-field-per-commit:** `set_doc_values_field` (no `add_doc_values_field` equivalent yet -- no per-field file-suffix machinery there to fan out to more than one field within a single segment); this is an intentional, undone gap, not an oversight. **Update (`rollback()`):** `IndexWriter::rollback(&mut self)` discards every document buffered by `add_document` since the last `commit()` -- a pure in-memory reset of `pending_docs` only, never touching `dir`/`segment_infos` (no `segment_infos::write` call). Writer configuration (`set_postings_field`/`set_term_vector_field`/`set_doc_values_field`/`set_merge_policy`) and any prior commit's segments both survive a rollback untouched, matching real Lucene's split between `IndexWriterConfig` (survives) and buffered documents (discarded). **Not replicated**: real `IndexWriter.rollback()` also closes the writer and releases its write lock permanently; this facade has no open/close lifecycle at all, so `rollback()` leaves the writer fully usable for further `add_document`/`commit()` calls. New tests: rollback discards pending docs so the next commit is empty of them; rollback with nothing pending is a safe no-op; rollback after a prior commit leaves that commit's segment untouched while discarding a second, uncommitted batch; and rollback preserves postings/term-vector/doc-values/merge-policy configuration for a later commit. **Update (two-phase commit, `prepare_commit()`/`finish_commit()`):** `commit()` is now `prepare_commit()` immediately followed by `finish_commit()`, kept as one call for backward compatibility -- every existing caller/test is unchanged. `prepare_commit()` does everything `commit()` used to do (flush `pending_docs` via `flush_stored_only_segment`, build/write postings/term-vectors/doc-values files) *except* the final `segment_infos::write` call, and stashes the resulting in-memory `SegmentInfos` in a new `prepared_commit: Option<SegmentInfos>` field; `finish_commit()` takes that stashed value and calls `segment_infos::write` (the one call that actually produces the next `segments_N`), then runs auto-merge exactly as `commit()` always has. **Honest scope, read before relying on this for anything crash-related**: real `IndexWriter.prepareCommit()`/`SegmentInfos.prepareCommit` write a `pending_segments_N` file plus enough on-disk state that a *new process* opening the index after a crash can discover a prepared-but-not-yet-committed generation and roll it forward or back. This port's `segment_infos::write` has no `pending_segments_N` equivalent and no `segments.gen` pointer file -- there is exactly one kind of `segments_N`, and "what's current" is simply "the highest-numbered `segments_N` that exists" (`lucene_store::directory::last_commit_generation`/`segment_infos::read_latest`). That does make the split genuinely meaningful, not cosmetic: after `prepare_commit()` returns, the new segment's data files are durably on disk and synced, but no `segments_N` references them yet, so a fresh `IndexWriter::open`/reader against the same directory still sees only the previous commit. But `prepared_commit` lives purely in the live `IndexWriter` value's memory -- if the process crashes (or the value is dropped) between `prepare_commit()` and `finish_commit()`, that prepared state is gone with no on-disk trace to recover, discover, or roll forward; the orphaned segment files are just inert bytes no `segments_N` ever points at. So: real caller-visible durability/visibility split, yes; crash-survivable two-phase commit or cross-process/cross-restart handoff, no. `finish_commit()` without a preceding `prepare_commit()` (or called a second time) returns `Error::NoPreparedCommit` rather than silently no-opping. New tests: `prepare_commit()` + `finish_commit()` produces byte-identical on-disk results to calling `commit()` directly (compared against two independent directories fed the same docs), including confirming the prepared generation is invisible to a fresh reader until `finish_commit()` runs; `prepare_commit()` alone (simulating a crash before activation) leaves the previous commit as the only one a fresh `read_latest`/reopened writer sees; `finish_commit()` with no prior `prepare_commit()`, and a second `finish_commit()` right after a successful one, both return `Error::NoPreparedCommit`; and a second `prepare_commit()` before `finish_commit()` replaces the pending prepared state rather than stacking. **Update (FFI exposure):** `open`/`add_document`/`commit`/`prepare_commit`/`finish_commit`/`rollback`/`set_merge_policy` are now reachable over the C ABI too -- see the `lucene-ffi/src/writer.rs` row below for the exact wire shape and what is not exposed.  **Update (task #212, `IndexOptions::DocsAndCustomFreqs` postings, a second postings entry point):** `IndexWriter::set_custom_freq_postings_field(Some(field_name))` opts a writer into building real `IndexOptions::DocsAndCustomFreqs` postings for one field, driven by explicit per-document caller-supplied `(term, custom_freq)` pairs via the new `IndexWriter::add_document_with_custom_freq_terms(doc, terms)` -- unlike `set_postings_field`'s analyzed-text pipeline (`crate::indexing_chain::invert_documents` over a stored `FieldValue::String`), there is no way to derive a genuinely arbitrary per-doc-per-term similarity score from tokenized text, so the caller supplies it directly. `build_custom_freq_postings_output` groups `pending_custom_freq_terms` (kept 1:1-by-index with `pending_docs` by `add_document`/`add_document_with_custom_freq_terms`) into one `TermPostings` per distinct term and calls the same `postings_writer::write_fields` primitive with `index_options: IndexOptions::DocsAndCustomFreqs` (wire-identical to `DocsAndFreqs`, per `postings_writer.rs`'s own module doc comment) -- no positions/offsets/payloads, matching that IndexOptions variant's real scope. **Scope decision, deliberate not silent:** a writer may have *either* `set_postings_field`/`add_postings_field`'s analyzed-text postings field(s) *or* this custom-freq postings field active at a time, never both in the same commit -- attempting to combine them returns `Error::PostingsAndCustomFreqPostingsMutuallyExclusive` (see `set_custom_freq_postings_field`'s doc comment for the full rationale: the two paths buffer per-document input differently and each builds its own standalone `write_fields` input; merging them into one multi-field call is possible in principle but not implemented here). Single-field-only (a single `Option`, not a `Vec` -- no `add_custom_freq_postings_field` yet). A `custom_freq < 1` surfaces as the codec layer's existing `postings_writer::Error` "freq < 1" rejection, same validation `DocsAndFreqs` already had. New unit tests in `crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs` cover field resolution/validation, the mutual-exclusivity error in both directions, a real commit producing readable `.doc`/`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` files with the exact custom freq values read back (not occurrence counts), the empty/no-pending-docs/no-terms-supplied no-op cases, the `freq < 1` rejection leaving the writer's pending state untouched, and `rollback()` discarding `pending_custom_freq_terms` alongside `pending_docs`. A real end-to-end search test, `crates/lucene-search/tests/index_writer_custom_freq_postings_fixtures.rs::custom_freq_postings_round_trip_and_drive_real_bm25_scoring`, commits three docs each supplying exactly one occurrence of the same term with three *different* custom_freq values (5, 50, 1 -- deliberately not all equal to the literal occurrence count of 1), reopens the segment through the existing `blocktree`/`postings::DocInput` read side, confirms the raw postings freqs read back as `[5, 50, 1]` rather than `[1, 1, 1]`, then runs a real `TermQuery` through `lucene_search::search_term_query_scored` (this port's BM25 scorer) and confirms each doc's score matches the BM25 formula evaluated against its own custom_freq value exactly, with the resulting ranking (doc with custom_freq 50 first, then 5, then 1) proving the custom freq genuinely drives real relevance scoring, not a silently-substituted occurrence count. `crate::merge::merge_postings` already accepted `IndexOptions::DocsAndCustomFreqs` in its supported-list before this task (wire-identical to `DocsAndFreqs`, so no merge-logic change was needed), but had zero test coverage for it -- `crates/lucene-index/src/merge.rs::tests::two_sources_no_deletions_merge_docs_and_custom_freqs_correctly` closes that gap, confirming two sources' custom-freq values for the same term merge verbatim (not re-derived as occurrence counts) into the merged segment. **Update (distinct committed-vs-pending doc count accessor):** `IndexWriter::committed_doc_count()` sums `doc_count` across every segment in `self.segment_infos.segments`, read from each segment's own `.si` file via `segment_info::parse` (same source `segment_stats` already uses for `merge_policy::find_merges`). Semantics are **total, not live**: a document deleted via `delete_documents`/`update_document` is still physically present in its segment's stored-fields file (only a merge ever drops it, by omitting it from the merged output) and so is still counted -- callers wanting the live count must separately subtract each `SegmentCommitInfo::del_count`, or open each segment's `.liv` via `lucene_codecs::live_docs::parse` to count set bits. This is distinct from the pre-existing `pending_doc_count()`: `committed_doc_count()` only reflects documents durably written by a prior `commit()`, never buffered-but-uncommitted `add_document` calls. `lucene-ffi/src/writer.rs::ffi_writer_committed_doc_count` wraps it read-only, same "length first" out-param contract as `ffi_writer_pending_doc_count`/`ffi_writer_segment_infos_len`. New tests: `crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::tests::committed_doc_count_sums_across_multiple_commits_and_segments_and_is_distinct_from_pending` commits 3 docs then 2 more (producing a second segment), buffers 2 further docs uncommitted, and confirms `committed_doc_count()` (3, then 5, staying at 5 while buffered docs are pending) never conflates with `pending_doc_count()` (0, then 0, then 2); `delete_documents_marks_matching_docs_dead_and_is_visible_after_commit` additionally confirms `committed_doc_count()` stays at the segment's full `max_doc` across a real delete, proving the total-not-live semantics. `crates/lucene-ffi/src/writer.rs::tests::committed_doc_count_is_distinct_from_pending_doc_count_across_commits` repeats the same distinctness proof through the FFI surface, plus null-out-param and unknown-handle error-path tests for `ffi_writer_committed_doc_count` itself. **Sweep b9 (M2)** changed five things. (1) `prepare_commit`/`finish_commit` now run Java's real two-phase protocol on disk: `prepare_commit` writes and fsyncs `pending_segments_N` (`SegmentInfos.prepareCommit`), `finish_commit` renames it onto `segments_N` and fsyncs the directory (`SegmentInfos.finishCommit`), and `rollback` deletes it (`SegmentInfos.rollbackCommit`) -- so a crash mid-commit can no longer leave a truncated `segments_N` at the highest generation, which previously made the whole index unopenable. (2) A second `prepare_commit()` with no intervening `finish_commit()` is now `Error::PrepareCommitAlreadyCalled` (Java's `IllegalStateException`); it used to replace the prepared state, silently discarding every document the first prepare had already flushed. (3) `update_document` now persists the bumped `segment_infos.counter` in the commit it writes (real `newSegmentName()` bumps the counter as it hands out the name); it used to bump only the in-memory copy, so a reopened writer handed out the same `_N` and overwrote a live segment. (4) `update_document`/`delete_documents`/`apply_merge`/`delete_all` are refused while a commit is prepared (`Error::PreparedCommitPending`) -- they claim the same generation the prepared commit will, so `finish_commit` would have reverted them. (5) Every commit path now stamps a fresh commit id, matching Java writing `StringHelper.randomId()` into each `segments_N` header. **Newly ported**: `deleteAll()` (`delete_all`, in-memory drop of buffered docs + all segments, durable only on the next commit, exactly as in Java) and `setLiveCommitData`/`getLiveCommitData` (`set_live_commit_data`/`live_commit_data`, `SegmentInfos.userData`). **Still not ported** (see `docs/sweep/m2/b9-index-write.md`): `IndexFileDeleter` refcounting -- nothing ever deletes an unreferenced file, so aborted flushes, rolled-back prepares and replaced segments accumulate as orphans; the `DocumentsWriterDeleteQueue` and its sequence numbers (no `addDocument` return value, no buffered deletes); RAM/`maxBufferedDocs` flush triggering; `softUpdateDocument`/`updateDocValues`/`deleteDocuments(Query)`/`addDocuments` (block)/`forceMerge`/`close`/`flush`/`addIndexes`. **Sweep c3 (M2)** closed the two headline gaps that row ended on. (1) **`IndexFileDeleter` is ported** (`lucene-index/src/index_file_deleter.rs`, own row below) and wired into every lifecycle point: `open` reclaims a previous session's orphans (a leftover `pending_segments_N`, an uncommitted flush's segment files) and inflates `generation`/`counter` past them (`IndexFileDeleter.inflateGens`); `flush` takes a non-commit checkpoint; `finish_commit`/`update_document`/`delete_documents`/`apply_merge` take a non-commit checkpoint followed by a commit checkpoint (Java's `checkpoint()` then `deleter.checkpoint(pendingCommit, true)` -- the non-commit half is what releases `lastFiles`' hold on a merge's sources, without it they would never be reclaimed); `rollback` runs Java's `rollbackInternal` sequence (`checkpoint(segmentInfos, false)` + `refresh()`); `delete_all` checkpoints. So superseded `segments_N` generations, superseded `.liv`/doc-values-update generations, a merge's source segments, a rolled-back prepare's segment and a failed flush's partial files are all deleted now, where every one of them used to accumulate forever. `set_deletion_policy` exposes `IndexWriterConfig.setIndexDeletionPolicy` over `KeepOnlyLastCommitDeletionPolicy` (default) and `NoDeletionPolicy`; `delete_unused_files()` is `IndexWriter.deleteUnusedFiles()`. (2) **RAM accounting and automatic flushing are ported**: `set_ram_buffer_size_mb` (default 16.0, `IndexWriterConfig.DEFAULT_RAM_BUFFER_SIZE_MB`), `set_max_buffered_docs` (default `DISABLE_AUTO_FLUSH`), both with Java's exact validation including the refusal to disable both triggers; `ram_bytes_used()` is a real, incrementally-maintained byte count of the buffered documents; and `add_document` now returns `Result<()>` because it flushes once a threshold trips (`FlushByRamOrCountsPolicy.onChange`, document count checked before RAM as in Java). A `flush()` writes a segment that no `segments_N` names until the next commit; `rollback()` discards it and deletes its files. **What the counter measures differs from Java's, deliberately**: Java's `DocumentsWriterPerThread.bytesUsed` counts the *inverted* form because Java inverts per document; this port inverts once at flush, so the counter measures the buffered-document arena and the transient inverted structure is a multiple of it (measured 9.4x on `index-bench`'s corpus -- `InMemoryInvertedIndex::ram_bytes_used()` is the real byte count for that structure). Peak memory is bounded by configuration either way, which is the property that was missing. Measured on `benchmarks/rust-runner`'s `index-bench` at 200k docs: writer peak RSS **862 MB -> 128 MB (6.7x)** and no longer growing with document count (442 MB at 100k, 862 MB at 200k with the trigger disabled; ~128 MB at both with it on), while throughput *improved* 29.0 -> 21.0 us/doc. **Still not ported**: the `DocumentsWriterDeleteQueue` and its sequence numbers; `softUpdateDocument`/`updateDocValues`/`deleteDocuments(Query)`/`addDocuments` (block)/`forceMerge`/`close`/`addIndexes`; multi-threaded DWPT pooling and `DocumentsWriterStallControl`. **Update (c7 sweep, 2026-08-29 -- sequence numbers and buffered deletes):** `crates/lucene-index/src/buffered_updates.rs` ports `DocumentsWriterDeleteQueue`, `BufferedUpdates`, `FrozenBufferedUpdates`, `DocValuesUpdate`/`FieldUpdatesBuffer` and `BufferedUpdatesStream`. Every mutating `IndexWriter` method now returns a `long` **sequence number** (starting at 1, exactly as Java's does), and deletes are **buffered** rather than eagerly resolved-and-committed: `update_document(Term, doc)` and `delete_documents_by_term(&[Term])` put the operation in the delete queue with the buffer position it was issued at (`docIDUpto`) and it takes effect at the next flush/commit. Two mechanisms carry the ordering guarantee, both ported: within the segment being built, the per-entry `docIDUpto` bounds a delete to the documents that preceded it; across segments, `BufferedUpdatesStream` stamps each frozen packet with a `delGen` and `SegmentCommitInfo.bufferedDeletesGen` decides which segments it may touch (`applies_to`: `segment_gen <= packet_gen`), so a delete never reaches a segment flushed after it. **Newly ported on top of it**: `addDocuments`/`updateDocuments` block adds (one seqNo for the whole block, contiguous doc IDs guaranteed by consulting the flush threshold once per call, and `SegmentInfo.hasBlocks` set when the block held more than one document -- verified by real Lucene in `VerifyBlockSegment`); `softUpdateDocument`/`softUpdateDocuments`; `updateDocValues`/`updateNumericDocValue`/`updateBinaryDocValue` (a null value is `DocValuesFieldUpdates.reset(doc)`, i.e. removal, not a write of zero); and `deleteDocuments(Query...)` over a **closed `DeleteQuery` enum** (`Term`/`Prefix`/`TermRange`/`MatchAll`/`Any`/`All`/`Not`) rather than `org.apache.lucene.search.Query`, because `lucene-search` depends on `lucene-index` and naming it here would invert the dependency edge -- the same constraint `term_delete.rs`/`points_delete.rs` already document; `lucene-search`/`lucene-ffi` can lower a richer query onto it. LUCENE-6379's `MatchAllDocsQuery` -> `deleteAll()` specialisation is ported. `IndexWriter` now opens a segment's `.si`/`.liv`/`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd`/`.doc` itself to resolve a buffered delete (the slice of `ReaderPool`/`ReadersAndUpdates` a delete needs), so the caller-supplied `SegmentDeleteSource` list is no longer required; the previous eager entry points are kept as `update_document_with_sources`/`delete_documents_with_sources` for the case a segment's postings live outside the writer's directory. **Explicitly not ported, deliberately**: the lock-free linked list, the `DeleteSlice` heads, the global buffer lock and `FinishedSegments`' in-flight tracking -- all of it exists so many indexing threads can append deletes without a lock, and this port has one indexing thread, for which eager and lazy slice application are provably identical (argument in `docs/sweep/m2/c7-delete-queue.md`, F-1). **Still missing**: `tryDeleteDocument`, `tryUpdateDocValue`, `updateDocuments(Query, ColumnBatch)`, `addIndexes`, NRT `getReader`, `forceMerge`. **Measured**: the sequence-number machinery's per-document cost is below `index-bench`'s noise floor (interleaved 8-pair A/B against a build with it removed: median 20.6 vs 19.9 us/doc, difference alternating in sign). **`c43-final-cleanup`**: `IndexWriter.finishApply`'s **100%-deleted segment drop** is ported (`drop_fully_deleted_segments`). `closeSegmentStates` collects a segment iff `rld.isFullyDeleted()` -- `PendingDeletes.isFullyDeleted` is `getDelCount() == maxDoc()`, hard deletes only -- and `MergePolicy.keepFullyDeletedSegment` is false; `finishApply` then drops each and checkpoints. This port kept such a segment in the commit forever: a segment nothing can ever match, carried by every later open, merge and `CheckIndex`. Only segments this apply touched are candidates, as in Java (`openSegmentStates` filters by generation). It is an in-memory drop plus the deleter checkpoint the flush already runs, not a new commit, so a `rollback()` restores the segment from `rollback_segments`. `MergePolicyConfig::keep_fully_deleted_segments` is the hook (`SoftDeletesRetentionMergePolicy` returns `true` from it), reduced to a flag because this port has no `MergePolicy` trait and no `CodecReader` supplier to hand it. Ground truth: `fixtures/src/GenFullyDeletedDrop.java` runs four scripts through a real `IndexWriter` and records the committed segment count, each segment's `(maxDoc, delCount)` and the visible ids -- including a `partial` control that must **not** drop -- and `a_fully_deleted_segment_is_dropped_exactly_where_real_lucene_drops_it` replays all four, verified to fail against the unfixed code. Not modelled, and said so on the function: `dropDeletedSegment`'s `mergingSegments` guard (no concurrent merges here), `readerPool.drop` and `adjustPendingNumDocs` (neither exists). |
| `index/IndexFileDeleter` + `util/FileDeleter` + `index/IndexDeletionPolicy`/`KeepOnlyLastCommitDeletionPolicy`/`NoDeletionPolicy` | `lucene-index/src/index_file_deleter.rs` | **ported** (sweep c3, M2).
