Reference-counts every file named by every live commit point and by the writer's own in-memory, not-yet-committed segment list, and deletes a file the moment its count reaches zero. `IndexFileDeleter::open` is Java's constructor: list the directory, `initRefCount` every index-looking file (`IndexFileNames.CODEC_FILE_PATTERN`, ported as a hand-rolled matcher rather than a regex dependency, plus the `segments*`/`pending_segments*` prefixes and the `write.lock` exclusion), load every `segments_N` as a `CommitPoint` and incRef its files, delete whatever is left at count 0, apply the deletion policy, then checkpoint the incoming `SegmentInfos`. `checkpoint(infos, is_commit)` is Java's, both halves. `refresh()` (Java's, called from `rollbackInternal`) re-lists and reclaims anything unreferenced, `pending_segments_N` included. `delete_new_files` is `deleteNewFiles`. `inflate_gens` is `inflateGens`, restricted to the two counters this port's `SegmentInfos` carries. Files are deleted `segments_N`-first, then everything else -- Java's two-pass ordering, so a crash mid-delete leaves orphaned segment files rather than a commit file pointing at deleted data. **Rust-side divergences, all deliberate**: `SegmentCommitInfo.files()` needs the segment's `.si` file list, which Java holds in memory on a live `SegmentInfo` and this port must parse from disk -- so the deleter caches it per `(segment_name, segment_id)` (a segment's `.si` is immutable once written, and a new segment always gets both a new name and a new id). `IndexDeletionPolicy` is an enum over Lucene's two *stateless* built-ins rather than a trait with one implementation; `SnapshotDeletionPolicy`/`PersistentSnapshotDeletionPolicy` need a commit-pinning handle type nothing here has a caller for. There is no `IndexCommit` object (Java's `CommitPoint` extends it for `DirectoryReader.listCommits` and for policies to inspect); `CommitPoint` here carries only generation, file list and `segments_N` name. **Explicitly not ported, Linux target**: Java's `deletePendingFiles`, `Directory.getPendingDeletions()` and `FileDeleter.delete`'s `Constants.WINDOWS` branch all exist to model Windows leaving a deleted-but-open file visible in a "pending delete" state. On Linux `unlink` on an open file succeeds and the name disappears immediately, so there is no pending-deletion state to model and a `NoSuchFileException`-equivalent from `delete_file` is a real error here, exactly as it is for Java on a non-Windows platform. `segments.gen` (dropped by Lucene 4.0) is treated as an ordinary unreferenced index file and reclaimed rather than being parsed as a commit. **No Java fixture**: this writes no file format -- it decides which files to delete. Fifteen unit tests cover the codec-file-name matcher (positive and negative), `inflateGens` (pending files, `.tmp` skip, unparsable trash, never lowering a counter), a non-commit checkpoint holding and then releasing a view, a commit checkpoint keeping only the last commit and deleting the rest, `NoDeletionPolicy` retaining every generation and `revisitPolicy` reclaiming them when the policy tightens, `refresh`/`deleteNewFiles` touching only unreferenced files (and never a non-index file), opening over an existing commit, and the three error paths (a commit file under a non-canonical name that nothing references -- Java's `IllegalStateException` -- a `SegmentCommitInfo` whose `.si` is missing, and a corrupt `segments_N`). A further ten tests in `index_writer.rs` assert the **directory listing** after each lifecycle event (rollback after a prepare, a superseded commit, a merge, a `.liv` generation bump, opening over a crashed session's orphans, an auto-flush plus rollback), since an orphan is by definition something no in-memory structure points at. **`c43-final-cleanup`**: the deleter no longer opens a `.si` the writer itself just wrote. `record_segment_files` takes a segment's file list straight off the in-memory `SegmentInfo` `segment_writer::seal_flushed_segment` encoded (which now returns it beside the `SegmentCommitInfo`), which is what Java gets structurally from `SegmentCommitInfo` owning its `SegmentInfo`. `one_commit_writes_the_segments_si_exactly_once` now asserts **zero** `.si` reads during a flush, down from the one c36 left. `commit_files` also stopped cloning each segment's file list per checkpoint (a two-pass fill-then-borrow, one `Vec<String>` per segment per checkpoint removed, on a path that runs twice per commit). **Measured** with the min-of-40 alternating A/B, both arms shipped code in one process (`crates/lucene-index/examples/deleter_checkpoint.rs`; the second arm is `forget_segment_files`, i.e. the pre-c43 cache state): **2.30x-2.35x** on a checkpoint over 8/32/128 segments, a flat **1.75 us per segment** -- one `open` plus a full `segment_info::parse` including the index-header check and a CRC over the whole file. |
| `lucene-ffi` C-ABI wrapper over `IndexWriter` (IndexWriter commit/merge-policy/postings/term-vector/doc-values/update/delete FFI exposure) | `lucene-ffi/src/writer.rs::{ffi_open_writer, ffi_writer_add_document, ffi_writer_commit, ffi_writer_prepare_commit, ffi_writer_finish_commit, ffi_writer_rollback, ffi_writer_set_merge_policy, ffi_writer_set_postings_field, ffi_writer_set_term_vector_field, ffi_writer_set_doc_values_field, ffi_writer_update_document, ffi_writer_delete_documents, ffi_writer_segment_infos_len, ffi_writer_segment_info_name, ffi_writer_pending_doc_count, ffi_close_writer}` | Wraps seven `IndexWriter` methods behind a new opaque handle (`registry::WriterHandle`, `handle::RegistryTag::Writer`), same `guard`/`catch_unwind`/last-error/handle-validation conventions every other module in this crate already follows -- no write-side logic reimplemented. `ffi_open_writer` takes a filesystem path plus a caller's field list as five parallel arrays (name, number, `IndexOptions` ordinal, `DocValuesType` ordinal, store-term-vectors flag); `ffi_writer_add_document` takes one document's fields as four parallel arrays, with a `kind` byte per field selecting how its raw bytes decode (string/binary/i32/i64/f32/f64). Because `IndexWriter<'d>` borrows `&'d dyn Directory` and there is no owned/`'static` `IndexWriter` type, `WriterHandle` owns a heap-boxed `FsDirectory` alongside the writer and builds the writer's borrow against that stable heap address via one contained, documented `unsafe` block (`writer.rs::open_writer_handle`) -- sound because struct field declaration order guarantees the borrowing `writer` field drops before the owning `dir` field ever does. `WriterHandle` also needed explicit (documented) `unsafe impl Send/Sync`, since a bare `&dyn Directory` carries no auto-trait bound the compiler can see on its own, even though this crate's `Mutex`-guarded, one-thread-at-a-time access pattern makes both sound. `SlotMap::get_mut` -- previously `#[cfg(test)]`-only, since every earlier handle type was immutable once inserted -- is now a real (non-test) part of the crate's handle API, since a writer handle's whole point is in-place mutation across calls. **Update (postings/term-vector/doc-values FFI exposure):** `ffi_writer_set_postings_field`/`ffi_writer_set_term_vector_field`/`ffi_writer_set_doc_values_field` now wrap `IndexWriter::set_postings_field`/`set_term_vector_field`/`set_doc_values_field` too, one C function per setter, each taking an `enabled` flag (mirroring `ffi_writer_set_merge_policy`'s own `enabled == 0` -> `None` convention) plus a `(field_name, field_name_len)` pair decoded via this crate's existing `str_from_raw`; `enabled == 0` clears the setting (`None`), ignoring the name/length arguments. Caller-input errors these three can now surface (`UnknownPostingsField`/`UnsupportedPostingsIndexOptions`/`UnknownTermVectorField`/`UnsupportedTermVectorField`/`UnknownDocValuesField`/`UnsupportedDocValuesType`/`MissingDenseDocValue`/`NonNumericDocValue`/`NonBinaryDocValue`) all map to `FfiStatus::InvalidArgument`, extending `map_writer_error`'s existing status-mapping table. Tests: unknown-handle rejection and a disabled (`enabled == 0`) no-op for each of the three, an unknown-field-name rejection for each, and one real end-to-end test per setter (`set_postings_field_end_to_end_writes_readable_postings`, `set_term_vector_field_end_to_end_writes_readable_term_vectors`, `set_doc_values_field_end_to_end_writes_readable_numeric_values`) that opts a field in over FFI, adds documents and commits over FFI, then reads the resulting segment back Rust-side through this crate's own unmodified read side (`lucene_codecs::blocktree`/`postings`, `lucene_codecs::term_vectors::open`, `lucene_codecs::doc_values::parse_meta`/`numeric_value` respectively) to prove the field was genuinely written, not just that the FFI call returned `Ok`. **Update (update_document/delete_documents FFI exposure):** `ffi_writer_update_document`/`ffi_writer_delete_documents` now wrap `IndexWriter::update_document`/`delete_documents`. Both take `(field_name, field_name_len, term_ptr, term_len)` identifying the delete term as raw, already-analyzed bytes (no analysis happens at this FFI boundary, same stance as `query.rs`'s own raw-bytes terms); `ffi_writer_update_document` additionally takes the replacement document's fields via the same four parallel arrays/`kind` byte convention `ffi_writer_add_document` already uses. Neither is buffered -- both commit immediately on success, matching `IndexWriter::update_document`/`delete_documents`'s own atomicity. Since `SegmentDeleteSource` requires an opened `BlockTreeFields`/`DocInput` per segment (`update_document.rs`'s own scope: only segments the caller supplies an opened source for are considered), this FFI layer builds that itself: a new `writer.rs::open_all_segment_sources` reopens every one of the writer's currently-committed segments that has a `.tim` file on disk (i.e. was flushed with `ffi_writer_set_postings_field` enabled at that commit), plus its `.liv` if one exists; a segment with no `.tim` file is skipped, not errored, matching `SegmentDeleteSource`'s own "unlisted segment left untouched" contract. `IndexWriter` gained two small read-only accessors, `dir()`/`fields()`, so this FFI-side helper can reopen those files and know the field schema to decode postings against, without either becoming part of the writer's mutable surface. Tests: unknown-handle and null-field-name rejection for both, a real end-to-end `ffi_writer_update_document` test (add two docs with real postings on `id`, commit, update one by term over FFI, then read every live doc back through this crate's own unmodified `stored_fields`/`live_docs` read side to confirm the old doc is gone and the new one present), a real end-to-end `ffi_writer_delete_documents` test (same shape, proving only the matching doc is removed), and a no-postings-segment no-op test (deleting against a writer whose only segment was flushed stored-only leaves `del_count` at `0`, proving the "skip, don't error" contract). **Update (segment_infos/pending_doc_count introspection FFI exposure):** `ffi_writer_segment_infos_len`/`ffi_writer_segment_info_name` now wrap `IndexWriter::segment_infos` (length-then-per-index-name accessor, the same "length first" shape `results_fragments.rs`'s `ffi_fragment_results_len`/`ffi_fragment_result_text` already established), and `ffi_writer_pending_doc_count` wraps `IndexWriter::pending_doc_count`. Both are read-only, infallible aside from handle/index/buffer validation. Tests: unknown-handle rejection and null-out-pointer rejection for all three, an out-of-bounds segment index, a too-small name buffer (`BufferTooSmall`, nothing written), and one end-to-end test (`segment_infos_and_pending_doc_count_reflect_writer_state_across_commits`) confirming 0 segments/0 pending on a fresh writer, 2 pending after two buffered `add_document` calls with 0 segments before any commit, 1 segment named `_0`/0 pending right after the first commit, and 2 segments after a second commit. **Still deliberately not exposed, tracked here rather than silently missing:** `IndexWriter::apply_merge` has no FFI wrapper. `apply_merge` only makes sense once a caller has already run `merge::merge_stored_only_segments` to produce the `SegmentCommitInfo` it folds in, and this crate exposes no FFI surface to run that merge (merging today only ever happens automatically, inside `commit()`, via `ffi_writer_set_merge_policy`) -- wrapping `apply_merge` alone would be a bookkeeping-only call a JVM caller could never actually drive. Manual merge execution FFI (and `apply_merge` alongside it) is left as a separate, larger task. `ffi_open_writer`'s field-schema wire format is deliberately minimal: every `FieldInfo` flag beyond name/number/index-options/doc-values-type/store-term-vectors (`omit_norms`, `store_payloads`, `soft_deletes_field`, `parent_field`, points/vector dimensions) is fixed at its off/default value, since this task's field list only needs to support stored-fields-only documents. `ffi_writer_set_merge_policy` exposes the five knobs `lucene_index::merge_policy::MergePolicyConfig` has today (`max_merge_at_once`, `segments_per_tier`, `max_merged_segment_size`, `reclaim_weight`, and now `floor_segment_size`, real `floorSegmentMB`'s equivalent, default 16MB) -- `floor_segment_size` was added as a new trailing parameter, a direct signature break with all three in-repo call sites updated (no existing versioned-FFI-export convention in this crate to justify a defaulted overload instead). `forceMergeDeletesPctAllowed` is still not invented here, since this port's `find_forced_merges` has no deletion-percentage-aware `FORCE_MERGE_DELETES`-equivalent merge type for that knob to configure. Tests: null-pointer rejection (`open_writer_null_out_handle_is_null_pointer_error`, `open_writer_null_field_array_with_nonzero_count_is_null_pointer_error`, `add_document_null_array_with_nonzero_count_is_null_pointer_error`), invalid-UTF-8 path rejection, out-of-range enum-ordinal rejection, invalid/unknown-handle rejection for every one of the eight functions, double-close rejection, a cross-registry rejection (a directory handle passed to a writer function), and a real end-to-end test (`open_add_commit_end_to_end_produces_a_readable_segment`) that opens a writer over FFI, adds documents over FFI, commits over FFI, then reopens the same on-disk directory Rust-side (`segment_infos::read_latest` + `lucene_codecs::stored_fields::open`, bypassing the FFI writer handle entirely) and reads every document back to confirm the commit is genuinely valid and queryable, not just `Ok`-returning. Two more end-to-end tests cover the two-phase commit split and the merge-policy convergence behavior the same way. |

## lucene-search

| Java | Rust | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `search/TermQuery` (matching + BM25 scoring) | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::TermQuery`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{search_term_query, search_term_query_scored}` | **ported.** Plain `{ field, term }` struct — no `TermStates`/cross-segment stats attached (nothing to reuse them for yet). `search_term_query` (matching only) is unchanged; `search_term_query_scored` is its new scored sibling (task #13): same field/term/`live_docs` handling, additionally reading each matched doc's `freq` and computing a BM25 score via `similarity::score` (see that module's row for the norms/avg-field-length approximation every score here inherits) into a [`ScoringCollector`]. **Verified against real Lucene**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/scoring_fixtures.rs` scores the real `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` segment and asserts (a) the scored doc set exactly equals the already-fixture-verified unscored doc set, (b) `TopDocsCollector` output is correctly ordered (score descending, doc ID ascending on ties), and (c) every score matches a BM25 formula reimplemented independently in the test file from the segment's real `docFreq`/`doc_count`/per-doc `freq` — not real-Lucene score equality (deferred, see the norms gap above), but full internal-consistency and formula-fidelity verification against real postings data. |
| `index/DocumentsWriterPerThread.flush` + `IndexWriter.commit` (minimal, no-merge multi-segment slice) | `lucene-index/src/segment_writer.rs::flush_stored_only_segment` | **ported, deliberately minimal.** `flush_stored_only_segment` takes an in-memory `Vec<Document>` + `Vec<FieldInfo>` and writes one brand-new stored-fields-only segment (`.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm`/`.fnm`/`.si`, all synced through a real `Directory`), returning the `SegmentCommitInfo` to push into `SegmentInfos::segments`. Calling it more than once against the same `Directory` with distinct segment names/ids and passing all the resulting `SegmentCommitInfo`s to one `segment_infos::write` call produces a single commit describing multiple, independently-flushed segments -- proven by `crates/lucene-index/examples/write_multi_segment_commit_fixture.rs` (two flushes, `_0` with 2 docs + `_1` with 3 docs, one `segments_N`) opened via real `DirectoryReader.open` + `fixtures/src/VerifySegmentInfos.java` (unchanged from the single-segment case -- it was already segment-count-agnostic, and passed first try: `DirectoryReader.open` correctly federates the two Rust-written segments into one 5-doc space). Note `segment_infos::write`/`parse` needed **no changes** for this: `SegmentInfos::segments` was already `Vec<SegmentCommitInfo>` with a plain loop on both the encode and decode side, so describing N segments in one commit was already mechanical; the actual gap this closes is the reusable "flush one batch of documents to one new segment" building block (previously hand-duplicated per fixture example -- `write_segment_infos_fixture.rs` now calls this same function instead of inlining its own copy). **Deliberately not implemented, out of scope for this slice** (see the module doc for the full list): no RAM accounting/automatic flush-triggering, no merging (`TieredMergePolicy`/`ConcurrentMergeScheduler`), no deletes/updates during indexing (`BufferedUpdates`), no NRT reopen, no concurrent `DocumentsWriterPerThread`-per-thread pooling, and -- same constraint as the single-segment fixture below -- every field is stored-only, since there is still no write-side postings/doc-values/points/vectors format. **Compound-file packing (task #38):** `flush_stored_only_segment` now takes a `use_compound_file: bool`. `false` (unchanged) writes the four files loose; `true` packs those same already-complete codec files through `compound_format::write` (task above) into `<name>.cfs`/`.cfe` instead and sets `SegmentInfo.is_compound_file: true` in the `.si`. Real Lucene's `IndexWriter` decides this per-flush via `TieredMergePolicy`'s size-based `noCFSRatio`/`maxCFSSegmentSizeMB` heuristic; this port has no merge policy or segment-size accounting at all yet (`PLAN.md` Phase 5 item 6), so rather than fake a heuristic with nothing real behind it, the caller decides directly with a plain boolean -- `update_document.rs` passes `false`, keeping its existing commits' on-disk shape unchanged. No new Java fixture needed since `compound_format`'s codec itself is already differentially verified (see its row above); this task is Rust-side composition, verified end-to-end by a unit test that flushes with `use_compound_file: true`, recovers the `.fnm`/`.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm` bytes *through* `compound_format::parse_entries`/`open_input` (not the original in-memory buffers), and confirms both `field_infos::parse` and `stored_fields::open`/`.document()` still work reading through those recovered slices, plus a regression test confirming `.si`/loose-file output for `use_compound_file: false` is unchanged (`.cfs`/`.cfe` files absent, `.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm`/`.fnm` present) **`sealFlushedSegment` (c36)**: the segment's `.si` is written **once**, at the end, from the in-memory `SegmentInfo` every format's writer has added its files to -- `write_stored_only_segment_files` produces a `FlushedSegment` (that `SegmentInfo`, its `SegmentCommitInfo`, and the names still to fsync), the caller extends it, and `seal_flushed_segment` writes and syncs. Until c36 a fully configured commit wrote the `.si` seven times, re-`open`ing and re-`parse`ing it before six of them. `flush_stored_only_segment{,_with_blocks}` keep their signatures and are now build + seal. |
| `index/SegmentMerger` | `lucene-index/src/merge.rs::merge_stored_only_segments` | **ported, scoped to exactly what's mergeable today.** Merges N already-flushed segments into one new segment: reads each source's `FieldInfos` (`field_infos::parse`) and `Document`s back out (`stored_fields::open` + `.document()`), drops non-live docs per an optional per-source `FixedBitSet` (`live_docs::parse`), reconciles field numbering across sources by field name (`reconcile_field_numbers`), renumbers surviving docs contiguously by concatenating sources in source order, and writes `.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm`/`.fnm`/`.si`. **Field-number reconciliation**: does the numbering half only (first-seen-across-sources order, one merged `FieldInfo` per distinct name using the first source's metadata) and skips real Lucene's `verifySameSchema` check. **Edge cases**: a source with no `live_docs` is treated as fully live; a fully-deleted source contributes zero docs (merged anyway rather than requiring the caller to pre-filter). Now also merges doc values (NUMERIC, BINARY, SORTED, SORTED_NUMERIC, and SORTED_SET), norms, term vectors, postings, and BKD points, if a caller supplies them per source (`MergeSource::numeric_doc_values`/`binary_doc_values`/`sorted_doc_values`/`sorted_numeric_doc_values`/`sorted_set_doc_values`/`norms`/`term_vectors`/`postings`/`points`, all optional): decodes each source's per-doc data via the existing read-side decoders (`doc_values::numeric_value`, `doc_values::binary_value`, `doc_values::sorted_ord` + `terms_dict::decode_all_terms`, `doc_values::sorted_numeric_values`, and (for SORTED_SET) the same per-value ordinal-to-bytes resolution generalized across `SortedSetKind::Single`/`Multi`, `norms::norm_value`, `term_vectors::TermVectorsReader::document`), drops non-live docs and renumbers contiguously the same way stored fields are, remaps field numbers through the same reconciliation, and re-encodes via the existing single-field write-side encoders (`doc_values::write_single_dense_numeric_field`, `doc_values::write_single_dense_binary_field`, `doc_values::write_single_dense_sorted_field`, `doc_values::write_single_dense_sorted_numeric_field`, `doc_values::write_single_dense_sorted_set_field`, `norms::write_single_dense_field`, `term_vectors::write_best_speed`), writing whichever of `.dvm`/`.dvd`/`.dvs`, `.nvm`/`.nvd`, `.tvd`/`.tvx`/`.tvm` result. **SORTED doc-values merging (`merge_sorted_doc_values`)**: unlike NUMERIC/BINARY, a SORTED field can't be merged by simple concatenation -- each source's term dictionary is built independently, so ordinal `N` in one source's dictionary is generally a *different term* than ordinal `N` in another's (real Lucene's `OrdinalMap` exists to solve exactly this). This port sidesteps building an explicit ordinal-remapping table: for each live doc it resolves that doc's own source's ordinal straight to term bytes (via that source's own `terms_dict::decode_all_terms`), then hands the merge's full per-doc *term-bytes* list to `doc_values::write_single_dense_sorted_field`, which rebuilds the merged, deduplicated, sorted dictionary itself -- so two sources' docs sharing a term land on the same merged dictionary entry purely because the merged dictionary is deduplicated by term bytes, not by ordinal. **SORTED_NUMERIC doc-values merging (`merge_sorted_numeric_doc_values`)**: no shared dictionary to reconcile at all -- each live doc simply contributes its own `Vec<i64>` of values (whatever order/count the source has), concatenated across sources exactly like `merge_numeric_doc_values` generalized from one value per doc to a list per doc. **SORTED_SET doc-values merging (`merge_sorted_set_doc_values`)**: multi-valued SORTED, so it reuses `merge_sorted_doc_values`'s exact resolve-to-bytes-and-let-the-writer-dedupe approach, per-value instead of per-doc -- each of a live doc's own source's ordinals (resolved uniformly whether that source's own entry collapsed to `SortedSetKind::Single` or stayed `Multi`) are turned into term bytes via that source's own dictionary, producing a `Vec<Vec<u8>>` per doc that `doc_values::write_single_dense_sorted_set_field` deduplicates into the merged dictionary itself, same no-remapping-table-to-get-wrong property as SORTED. **Scope of the doc-values/norms merge**: since the underlying write-side encoders only support one field per `.dvd`/`.nvd` file (no multi-field container yet), at most one numeric-doc-values field, at most one BINARY-doc-values field, at most one SORTED-doc-values field, at most one SORTED_NUMERIC-doc-values field, at most one SORTED_SET-doc-values field, and at most one norms field may be merged per call (`Error::TooManyNumericDocValuesFields`/`TooManyBinaryDocValuesFields`/`TooManySortedDocValuesFields`/`TooManySortedNumericDocValuesFields`/`TooManySortedSetDocValuesFields`/`TooManyNormsFields` otherwise) -- and since numeric, BINARY, SORTED, SORTED_NUMERIC, and SORTED_SET all land on the same `.dvm`/`.dvd`/`.dvs` extensions, merging more than one of those five doc-values types in the same call is also rejected (`Error::MultipleDocValuesTypesInOneMerge`); term vectors have no such per-call field-count limit, but do have a per-field content limit -- see below. **Sparse-across-sources rule**: a doc-values/norms field can only be merged if every source contributing at least one live doc supplies decodable, fully-dense data for that field (matches the write-side encoders' dense-only requirement) -- a source missing the field, or only sparsely populating it, is `Error::DocValuesFieldMissingInSource`/`BinaryDocValuesFieldMissingInSource`/`SortedDocValuesFieldMissingInSource`/`SortedNumericDocValuesFieldMissingInSource`/`SortedSetDocValuesFieldMissingInSource`/`NormsFieldMissingInSource` rather than a silent drop; for SORTED_NUMERIC/SORTED_SET this also covers a live doc whose resolved value list/set comes back empty, since the write-side encoders require every doc to have at least one value. Term vectors have no such constraint: a source with no term-vectors reader, or a doc with none, contributes an empty `TermVectorsDocument`. **Term-vectors content scope**: the read side (`TermVectorsReader::document`) fully decodes positions, offsets, and payloads when a source has them, the write side (`term_vectors::write_best_speed`) supports all three (a later task lifted its earlier positions-only restriction -- see that row above), and `merge_term_vectors` now merges all three too: since term-vector data is entirely doc-local (each source's `TermVectorsReader::document` call already returns one self-contained document), merging is just reading each source's doc, remapping its field numbers, and appending it to the merged doc list -- no cross-source term-level combination step is needed, unlike postings. Positions-only, offsets-only, payloads-only, and offsets+payloads term vectors all merge and round-trip correctly. **Postings merging (`merge_postings`)**: each source's term dictionary is independent (same reason SORTED doc values needs the ordinal-avoidance trick above), so this resolves each contributing source's own terms straight to bytes via that source's already-opened `blocktree::FieldTerms`, unions those bytes across sources into one sorted term set, and for each term concatenates every contributing source's `(mergedDocId, freq)` pairs in source order -- ascending overall for free, since merged doc ids occupy disjoint, increasing per-source ranges (`build_doc_id_maps`). Re-encoded via `postings_writer::write_fields`, which (unlike the doc-values/norms writers) already accepts any number of fields per call, so there is no single-field-per-merge-call limit for postings. Scoped to `Docs`/`DocsAndFreqs`/`DocsAndCustomFreqs`, plus positions/offsets/payloads: a field whose merged `index_options` indexes positions (`DocsAndFreqsAndPositions`/`DocsAndFreqsAndPositionsAndOffsets`) has every contributing source's per-term positions (and offsets/payloads, when the merged `FieldInfo::store_payloads` is set / the field has offsets) read back via `blocktree::FieldTerms::positions` (the same read path `lucene-search`'s phrase matching uses) and concatenated in the same source order as docs/freqs, reusing that single read/write path rather than a separate one. Because field-number reconciliation only records the first-seen source's `FieldInfo` as the merged one and never checks agreement across sources sharing a field name, every other live-doc-contributing source's own `index_options` for that field is independently checked against the merged choice (`Error::PostingsIndexOptionsDisagreement`) -- otherwise a source with positions could have them silently dropped whenever an earlier, positions-free source happened to be picked as canonical. Same sparse-across-sources philosophy as doc values/norms applies at the field level (a source missing the postings field entirely while another live-doc-contributing source has it is `Error::PostingsFieldMissingInSource`) -- ordinary per-doc/per-term sparsity within a field is not an error, since that is exactly what a term dictionary already models. **Points merging (`merge_points`)**: a BKD points field has no shared dictionary to reconcile at all, unlike SORTED doc values or postings -- it's a flat, per-doc set of fixed-width packed values, closer in spirit to SORTED_NUMERIC doc values. This reads back every live doc's points via that source's own already-opened `points::PointsReader` (the *exact same* reader `lucene-search`'s `points_query::search_points_range` already uses -- no new BKD decode logic was written for this merge), drops non-live docs and remaps surviving doc ids to the merged id space via `build_doc_id_maps` (the same mechanism `merge_postings` uses), and concatenates the results across sources in source order; `points::write` rebuilds the merged BKD tree (leaf plan, packed index, bounding boxes) from that flat list itself, so there's no tree-merging logic to get wrong here. Like postings and unlike doc-values/norms, `points::write` already accepts any number of fields per call, so there is no single-field-per-merge-call limit for points. A merged field with points data in at least one live-doc-contributing source but not in every such source is `Error::PointsFieldMissingInSource`, matching the sparse-across-sources philosophy applied everywhere else -- but a field has no per-doc denseness requirement of its own here (a live doc contributing zero points for a field is not an error, and a field that ends up with zero points overall after the merge is simply omitted, matching `points::write`'s own `EmptyField` restriction and real Lucene's `finish()` returning `null` in that case). **Scope**: `points::write` supports `num_index_dims <= num_dims` (data-only, non-indexed trailing dimensions), and this merge preserves that -- every point's full `num_dims`-wide packed value round-trips through the merge unchanged. Because field-number reconciliation only records the first-seen source's `FieldInfo`, every other live-doc-contributing source's own BKD tree shape (`num_dims`/`bytes_per_dim`/`num_index_dims`, from that source's own `PointsField`) is independently checked against the merged field's declared shape and rejected with `Error::PointsShapeDisagreement` (for `num_dims`/`bytes_per_dim`) or `Error::PointsIndexDimsDisagreement` (for `num_index_dims`) on a mismatch. Multi-dimension points (e.g. `LatLonPoint`-shaped 2D fields) and multi-valued points (multiple points per doc for the same field) are both supported -- this is exactly what `points::write` already handles, and the concatenation this merge performs preserves both. **Now reachable from a real end-to-end caller for postings and term vectors** (see the `IndexWriter` row above for the exact wiring): `IndexWriter::execute_merge` opens each source's real `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd`/`.doc` files and builds real `SourcePostings` when its automatic-merge path fires, proven by a real end-to-end test (`crates/lucene-search/src/directory_reader.rs::postings_merge_e2e::term_query_finds_docs_from_both_flushed_segments_after_automatic_merge`) that flushes two segments, triggers a real automatic merge, and confirms a `TermQuery` resolves terms originating from both source segments. `execute_merge` also (task #210) opens each source's real `.tvd`/`.tvx`/`.tvm` and sets `MergeSource::term_vectors` when its automatic-merge path fires, proven by `crates/lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::index_writer::tests::segments_with_term_vectors_are_automatically_merged_with_term_vectors_preserved` (term-vector-only) and `crates/lucene-search/src/directory_reader.rs::postings_merge_e2e::term_vectors_survive_automatic_merge_alongside_postings` (postings and term vectors merged together in the same real automatic merge, both verified afterward). Points remain reachable only via hand-built `MergeSource` test fixtures, not `IndexWriter`, since this writer has no points write path at flush time at all -- a separate, prior gap, not something this task skipped. Along the way, this surfaced (and a follow-up task then fixed) a bug: `SegmentReader::open` (`lucene-search/src/directory_reader.rs`) mis-derived the postings codec file-suffix for this port's own no-suffix (`_N.tim`, not `_N_Suffix.tim`) writer convention, treating the segment name's own trailing digit as a bogus suffix and causing `DirectoryReader::open` to fail against a postings-bearing segment this writer produces. Fixed the same way the `.dvm`-suffix derivation already handled it (see the `DirectoryReader.open(Directory)` row below): special-case the no-suffix convention (`tim_file_name == format!("{segment_name}.tim")` -> empty suffix) before the generic strip-and-derive logic runs. The end-to-end test above now opens the merged segment through the real `DirectoryReader::open` -> `open_segments` -> `as_open_segments` path instead of working around the bug by opening files directly. **M2 sweep batch b10 -- cross-source `FieldInfos` reconciliation and Java-tolerant sparsity**: `reconcile_field_numbers` now ports the whole of `FieldInfos.Builder.add`, not just the number unification -- `FieldInfo.verifySameSchema` is enforced across every source sharing a field name (`index_options`, `omit_norms`/`store_term_vectors` when the field is indexed, `doc_values_type`, `doc_values_skip_index_type`, the points dimension triple, and the KNN vector options), and `store_payloads` is ORed in (`if (fi.hasPayloads()) curFi.setStorePayloads()`) rather than rejected, so a payload-bearing segment merges with a payload-free one and the payload-free source's occurrences come through as empty payloads. Previously the merged `.fnm` silently recorded only the first-seen source's view of a field while the merged data files carried another source's. Correspondingly, the module's old blanket "sparse across sources is a hard error" rule was narrowed to match Java: a source whose own `FieldInfos` never saw a field simply contributes nothing for it (`FieldsConsumer.merge`'s `if (terms == null) continue;`, `PointsWriter.merge`'s `if (readerFieldInfo == null) continue;`, and `TermVectorsWriter.merge`'s null-reader -> `addAllDocVectors(null, ...)`), which is the normal state of a segment written before a field was added; a source that *declares* the field but whose `MergeSource` supplied no data for it is still a hard error, since that is a caller wiring bug. The dense-only doc-values/norms writers keep their stricter rule (documented, not silently missing). `build_doc_id_maps` now returns Lucene's dense array-backed `MergeState.DocMap` shape (`Vec<i32>`, `-1` for a deleted doc) instead of a `HashMap`, turning the innermost per-posting/per-point lookup into an index rather than a hash probe. **Tier 2 review follow-ups**: the `store_payloads` OR is gated the way `FieldInfo.setStorePayloads` gates it (`indexOptions.subsumes(DOCS_AND_FREQS_AND_POSITIONS)`), so a source claiming payloads on a positionless field can no longer push the merged `FieldInfo` into a state `checkConsistency` rejects; `FieldInfos.Builder.add`'s third action, `putAttributes`, is ported too (a later source's attributes are `Map.putAll`'d onto the merged field); and a source naming the same field twice is rejected (`Error::DuplicateFieldNameInSource`), matching Java's `FieldInfos(FieldInfo[])` constructor -- previously it silently lost one of the two field numbers. The residual `PostingsFieldMissingInSource`/`PointsFieldMissingInSource` errors for a *declared but unsupplied* field are documented as deliberately stricter than Java (whose `MultiFields`/`PointsWriter.mergeOneField` tolerate a declared-but-empty field): `MergeSource` takes caller-supplied per-field handles rather than a reader, so it cannot tell that apart from a caller wiring bug, and silently merging away a whole source's postings is the worse failure. **Merge fast paths (batch c4)**: the three strategies `Lucene90CompressingStoredFieldsWriter.merge` picks between are now all ported. `matching_readers` is `codecs/compressing/MatchingReaders` (is this source's field name->number mapping the identity after reconciliation?); a matching, deletion-free source whose compressor/`chunkSize` agree and that is not `tooDirty` takes **BULK** (`StoredFieldsWriter::copy_chunks` -- whole already-compressed chunks copied verbatim, only each chunk's `docBase` vint and `.fdx` entry rewritten); a matching source with deletions or a different compressor takes **DOC** (serialized document bytes copied without being parsed into fields); anything else takes **VISITOR**. Measured on 4 x 20 000 documents: BULK 643x, DOC 23x, VISITOR 26x faster than the previous materialise-every-`Document`-and-recompress path (`benchmarks/rust-runner/src/merge_bench.rs`). The merged segment is now verified by real Lucene: `write_merged_segment_fixture` + `VerifyMergedSegment` build three segments, delete from one, merge, then compare every document field by field through `DirectoryReader` and run `CheckIndex` (added to `scripts/verify-write-path.sh`). `merge_postings` is now a streaming k-way `TermsEnum` merge (`FieldsConsumer.merge`/`MultiTerms`): one forward cursor per source, postings decoded from the cursor's own position via the new `TermsEnum::try_current_postings`/`try_current_postings_and_positions`, so the `BTreeSet<Vec<u8>>` of every distinct term and the per-(term, source) dictionary re-seeks are both gone (9.8x). `merge_points` k-way merges each source's already-sorted 1-D point stream (`BKDWriter.merge`'s priority-queue loop, value then docID) and hands the result to `points::write`'s one-pass leaf plan (2.5x). **Also fixed here**: the merged `.fnm` never carried the `PerFieldPostingsFormat.format`/`.suffix` attributes, so real Lucene routed the merged segment's postings nowhere and read the field back as having no terms at all -- silently, with `CheckIndex` clean. `describe_written_files` now makes the merged `.fnm` describe the files the merge actually wrote (per-field format attributes stamped and stale inherited ones stripped, `omit_norms` forced on an indexed field the merge wrote no norms for, `store_term_vectors` cleared when no `.tvd` was written). |

**Important caveat**: this port's only write-side path that produces a full segment, `segment_writer::flush_stored_only_segment`, still only ever writes stored-fields-only segments -- nothing in this port's normal flush path produces doc values/norms/term vectors/postings/points yet, so this merge logic is exercised only by its own unit/round-trip tests supplying synthetic per-source data, not by a real "flush two segments, then merge them" scenario. Unit-tested: field-number reconciliation in isolation; stored-fields merging (two segments no deletions, some docs deleted in each, a fully-deleted source, mismatched field numbers reconciled end-to-end through a re-parsed `.fnm`, zero sources, a real `.liv` round trip); numeric doc values, BINARY doc values, SORTED doc values, SORTED_NUMERIC doc values, SORTED_SET doc values, norms, and term vectors each merged in isolation (two sources, deletions, a source missing the field/reader entirely); two sources' term vectors with POSITIONS+OFFSETS+PAYLOADS merging correctly with exact term/offset/payload values checked after reading the merged output back through the real reader; SORTED-specific correctness tests -- overlapping term dictionaries across sources dedupe into one shared dictionary entry (not two), disjoint term dictionaries produce a merged dictionary containing every term from both sources, and every doc's merged term is resolved back through the unmodified reader stack (`parse_meta` + `sorted_ord` + `terms_dict::decode_all_terms`) and checked against the actual expected term bytes, not just "some valid ordinal"; SORTED_NUMERIC-specific: a multi-value doc keeps both its values (not just one) after merge; SORTED_SET-specific: the same overlapping-dictionary and disjoint-dictionary correctness tests as SORTED, but per multi-valued doc (a doc in one source with `["red","blue"]` and a doc in another with `["red","green"]` dedupe into a 3-entry shared dictionary, with each doc's full resolved value *set* checked, not just dictionary size); the too-many-fields and missing-in-a-live-source error paths for all five doc-values types, plus the multiple-doc-values-types-at-once error path for the numeric+BINARY and SORTED_NUMERIC+SORTED_SET pairs specifically (not all ~10 possible pairs); postings-specific: two sources with no deletions merging correctly (verified by reopening the merged `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd`/`.doc` through the unmodified `blocktree`/`postings::DocInput` reader and checking actual doc ids/freqs, not just "some valid output"), a term shared by both sources merging its postings in ascending merged-doc-id order, deletions dropping a doc's terms from the merge, a fully-deleted source contributing nothing, the missing-in-a-live-source error, the positions-indexed-field-rejected-as-out-of-scope error, and a source disagreeing with the merged field's `index_options` (positions vs. Docs/DocsAndFreqs) being rejected rather than silently dropped; points-specific: two sources with no deletions merging correctly (verified by reopening the merged `.kdm`/`.kdi`/`.kdd` through the unmodified `points::PointsReader` and checking actual doc ids/packed values, plus a full round trip through the unmodified `points_delete::resolve_points_range_doc_ids` range resolver), deletions dropping non-live docs from the merged points, a fully-deleted source contributing nothing, the missing-in-a-live-source error, a cross-source BKD-shape-disagreement error (one source's own points data using a different `bytes_per_dim` than the merged field's declared shape), two sources with consistent `num_index_dims < num_dims` (a `LatLonShape`-style bounding box shape) merging correctly with every point's full packed value -- including the non-indexed trailing dimension -- preserved, and a cross-source `num_index_dims` disagreement (same `num_dims`/`bytes_per_dim`, different `num_index_dims`) being rejected with `Error::PointsIndexDimsDisagreement`; and full round-trip tests -- stored fields + NUMERIC doc values + norms + term vectors, stored fields + BINARY doc values + norms + term vectors, and stored fields + SORTED doc values (with an overlapping term across sources) + norms + term vectors, each together in one call -- via real `Directory` I/O, verified by reading back every format through this port's own readers, including checking the `.si` file lists every written file. **Deliberately not implemented**: no merge *policy* (`TieredMergePolicy`-style "which segments, when" -- the caller picks sources), no background/concurrent merging, no merge-time codec upgrade, no multi-field `.dvd`/`.nvd` support, and postings merging is scoped to `IndexOptions::Docs`/`DocsAndFreqs` only (no positions/offsets/payloads -- see the postings paragraph above), and points merging supports `num_index_dims <= num_dims` but requires it to be consistent across all merged sources (see the points paragraph above) |
| `index/TieredMergePolicy` (`findMerges`, `doFindMerges`, `score`, `getSortedBySegmentSize`, `findForcedMerges`, `findForcedDeletesMerges`, `getMaxAllowedDocs`, `floorSize`, plus `MergePolicy.size`'s pro-rating) | `lucene-index/src/merge_policy.rs::{find_merges, find_merges_excluding, find_forced_merges, find_forced_delete_merges, find_forced_delete_merges_excluding, prorated_size, segment_byte_size}` | **ported faithfully (M2 sweep batch b10; replaces task #47's earlier "real-shaped but simplified" approximation).** The decision function only -- execution stays `merge.rs`'s job and background scheduling (`MergeScheduler`/`ConcurrentMergeScheduler`) remains out of scope. **Now line-by-line**: the level-by-level `allowedSegCount` budget walk; `deletesPctAllowed`'s delete budget and its over-half-`maxMergedSegmentBytes` exclusion rule; `targetSearchConcurrency`'s reserved segment slots and `allowedDocCount` cap; the real `MergeScore` (`skew * totAfterMergeBytes^0.05 * nonDelRatio^2`, skew from *floored* sizes, `nonDelRatio` from pro-rated-over-raw bytes, `1/segsPerTier` when `hitTooLarge`); the "result must be >=50% larger than the biggest input unless that input is delete-heavy" O(N^2) guard; the singleton-with-no-deletes skip; the one-large-merge-per-cycle rule; `findForcedMerges`' `Long.MAX_VALUE`/`*1.25` `maxMergeBytes` derivation and smallest-first bin packing; and `findForcedDeletesMerges` delegating to the *same* `doFindMerges` engine so it respects `maxMergedSegmentBytes` (Java's javadoc: "findForcedDeletesMerges should never produce segments greater than maxSegmentSize") instead of returning one unbounded group. **Constants corrected to Lucene 10.5.0**: `segsPerTier` 8 (was 10), `maxMergedSegmentBytes` 5 GiB (`5 * 1024^3`; was `5000 * 1024^2`), `deletesPctAllowed` 20.0 and `targetSearchConcurrency` 1 (both were absent), `floorSegmentBytes` 16 MiB and `forceMergeDeletesPctAllowed` 10.0 (already correct). `MergePolicyConfig.reclaim_weight` is retained but redefined as the exponent Java hardcodes as `2` in `Math.pow(nonDelRatio, 2)`, default `2.0`. **Two byte quantities, as in Java**: `SegmentStat.size_bytes` is the raw `SegmentCommitInfo.sizeInBytes()` (filled by `segment_byte_size`, or a documented doc-count approximation via `SegmentStat::from_segment_info`), and `prorated_size` derives `MergePolicy.size`'s `bytes * (1 - delRatio)`, which is what every size comparison in the algorithm uses. **Deliberate scope boundaries** (all documented in the module doc): no `MergeContext` (the currently-merging set is an explicit argument -- `find_merges_excluding`/`find_forced_delete_merges_excluding` -- rather than ambient state, and `numDeletesToMerge`/soft deletes come straight off `SegmentStat.del_count`); no `findFullFlushMerges` (only meaningful with a concurrent merge scheduler); `findForcedMerges` treats every supplied segment as `segmentsToMerge`-original and never sees a concurrently running force merge; no compound-file awareness, so `findForcedMerges`' `maxSegmentCount == 1` "already merged" bail-out reduces to "one delete-free segment"; `segmentsPerTier` is an integer (clamped to >= 2, which Java validates in the setter) rather than a double. **Differentially verified against real Lucene**: `fixtures/src/GenMergePolicy.java` emits no index (a merge policy is a pure function of segment *statistics*) but a manifest of 33 `(config, segments, currently-merging set) -> chosen merge groups` scenarios decided by running real `findMerges`/`findForcedMerges(n)`/`findForcedDeletesMerges` over hand-built `SegmentInfos` whose `SegmentCommitInfo.sizeInBytes()` comes from real files of the requested length; `crates/lucene-index/tests/merge_policy_fixtures.rs` replays every scenario and asserts the identical grouping *in the identical order* (which pins the sort order, the candidate construction order and the forced-merge bin walk, not just membership). All 33 matched on the first run. Backed by 40 unit tests written against the Java source -- the exact `MergeScore` formula (both branches), skew/reclaim/floor score orderings, size-descending-then-name sort order, the level budget, the delete budget triggering a merge inside the segment-count budget, the growth guard and its delete-heavy bypass, the merge-factor cap and its below-floor escape hatch, `allowedDocCount` under `targetSearchConcurrency`, forced-merge bin packing/bail-outs, and forced-deletes merges staying under `maxMergedSegmentBytes`. |
| `index/IndexWriter` deletes (doc-ID-level mechanics, plus single-segment delete-by-term resolution) | `lucene-index/src/deletes.rs::{mark_deleted, apply_deletes}`, `lucene-index/src/term_delete.rs::{resolve_term_doc_ids, resolve_and_apply_term_delete}` | **ported: doc-ID-level apply mechanics (task #16) plus single-segment delete-by-term resolution (task #27).** Real Lucene's delete path is two halves: (1) `BufferedUpdates`/`ReaderPool` resolve *which* doc IDs a `deleteDocuments(Term/Query)` call names, per segment, by running the query against that segment's live postings/doc-values reader; (2) `ReadersAndUpdates.writeLiveDocs` takes the resolved doc IDs and turns them into an updated live-docs bitset, a new `.liv` file, and a bumped `delGen`/`delCount`. `deletes.rs` is half (2): `mark_deleted` clears the given doc IDs out of a `FixedBitSet` (starting from "all live" when the segment has no deletions yet, i.e. `del_gen == -1`, or from an existing bitset otherwise), returning the updated bitset plus a count of *newly* deleted docs (already-deleted doc IDs are idempotent, not double-counted); `apply_deletes` wraps that around a `SegmentCommitInfo`, writing the result via the existing `live_docs::write` to a **newly established filename convention** (`liv_file_name`: `_<segment>_<delGen in base36>.liv`, matching real Lucene's `IndexFileNames.fileNameFromGeneration(segment, "liv", delGen)`) and returning a new `SegmentCommitInfo` with `del_gen` incremented (`-1` -> `1` on first delete, else `+1`, matching `SegmentCommitInfo.getNextDelGen()`) and `del_count` increased by the newly-deleted count. Out-of-range doc IDs are a hard `Err`, never a silent no-op or a panic. `term_delete.rs` is (part of) half (1), **scoped to exactly one already-opened segment**: `resolve_term_doc_ids` takes that segment's `BlockTreeFields` + opened `.doc` file + a `(field, term)` pair and returns the matching live doc IDs ascending -- an unknown field or an absent term yields an empty `Vec`, not an error, matching `TermQuery.createWeight`'s `null`-`Scorer` "no matches" outcome. This reimplements the same lookup `lucene-search::term_doc_ids` already does (`field.postings(term, doc_in)` filtered by `live_docs`) directly against `lucene-codecs` primitives (`BlockTreeFields`, `DocInput`) rather than depending on `lucene-search` from `lucene-index`: the intended dependency graph is `util ← store ← codecs ← index ← search ← core ← ffi`, and `lucene-search` already depends on `lucene-index`, so the reverse edge would invert it into a cycle -- since every primitive the lookup needs already lives in `lucene-codecs`, which `lucene-index` depends on directly, reimplementing the handful of lines there avoids both the cycle and a speculative new shared-primitive extraction in `lucene-codecs`. `resolve_and_apply_term_delete` composes `resolve_term_doc_ids` with `apply_deletes` for the full per-segment resolve-then-apply flow, finally closing the "here's a term to delete" -> "here's the actual `mark_deleted`/`apply_deletes` call" gap for one segment. **Deliberately not implemented**: true multi-segment *resolution* (a real `IndexWriter`'s `BufferedUpdates`/`ReaderPool` opens and searches every currently-open segment's live postings reader itself; this port has no reader pool, so a caller of `update_document` — see the row below — still has to hand it whichever segments' `BlockTreeFields` it already has open) and delete-by-query beyond a single exact term. `updateDocument` itself is now implemented as an atomic wrapper — see `lucene-index/src/update_document.rs` below — fanning the per-segment delete-by-term resolution here out across a whole `SegmentInfos`'s segment list and committing it together with a new segment in one `segments_N` write. Unit-tested (`deletes.rs`): `mark_deleted` from an all-live (`None`) state, from an existing partially-deleted bitset, idempotent re-deletion of an already-deleted doc, boundary doc IDs (`0`, `max_doc-1`), out-of-range and negative doc IDs (both `Err`), empty doc-ID batch, plus full round-trip tests via a real `FsDirectory` (first delete round, second round incrementing `del_gen` and unioning with the first, re-deleting the same doc not double-counting, an out-of-range doc ID erroring without writing a file). 99.51% line coverage. Unit-tested (`term_delete.rs`), reusing the checked-in real-Lucene `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture: `resolve_term_doc_ids` for a multi-doc term, a singleton term needing no `.doc` file, a missing term, a missing field, and `live_docs` filtering out an already-deleted doc; `resolve_and_apply_term_delete` end to end against a real `FsDirectory` (`del_gen`/`del_count`/`.liv` bytes all correct for the term's actual matching docs), deleting the same term twice across two rounds confirming `del_gen` increments without double-counting already-deleted docs, and a term with no matches producing a no-op round that still bumps `del_gen`. 98.78% line coverage. **b11 sweep (2026-08-28) fixes**: (1) `liv_file_name` emitted `_<seg>_0.liv` for generation 0, where `IndexFileNames.fileNameFromGeneration` has a dedicated `gen == 0` branch returning `<seg>.liv` with no suffix (unreachable for `.liv` from a real writer, since `getNextDelGen()` goes -1 -> 1, but reachable from a corrupt or hand-built commit). (2) `apply_deletes` could produce a `delCount` past `maxDoc`, which `SegmentInfos.write` refuses to serialize -- now rejected at the source with the segment named. (3) The all-live bitset is built by filling whole `u64` words with a masked tail instead of `maxDoc` individual bit sets. |
| `index/IndexWriter` delete-by-point-range resolution (BKD, single segment) | `lucene-index/src/points_delete.rs::{resolve_points_range_doc_ids, resolve_and_apply_points_range_delete}` | **ported (task #36).** The BKD-range analog of task #27's `term_delete.rs`, same two-halves split: `resolve_points_range_doc_ids` is (part of) half (1), scoped to one already-opened segment's `lucene_codecs::points::PointsReader` + one `field_number` + an inclusive `[min_packed, max_packed]` packed-byte range (`PointRangeQuery`'s own default `newRangeQuery` semantics) — decodes every point via the existing `PointsReader::decode_all_points`, keeps only points where **every** dimension's slice independently satisfies `min[dim] <= value[dim] <= max[dim]` (unsigned byte-wise, matching `Arrays.compareUnsigned` and `PointRangeQuery.matches`), filters by `live_docs`, and returns the matching doc IDs ascending and deduplicated (a multi-valued point field can put more than one matching value on the same doc). An unknown `field_number` yields an empty `Vec`, not an error, same "no matches, not a caller bug" convention `term_delete` uses. `resolve_and_apply_points_range_delete` composes that with `deletes::apply_deletes`, the same resolve-then-apply shape as `resolve_and_apply_term_delete`. **Reused vs. newly written, stated plainly**: unlike delete-by-term, where `lucene-search::term_doc_ids` already existed and `term_delete.rs` just reimplemented its handful of lines locally (to avoid `lucene-index` depending back on `lucene-search` — confirmed again here by reading both `Cargo.toml`s: `lucene-search` depends on `lucene-index`, not the reverse), **no BKD range-query matcher existed anywhere in the workspace to reuse**: `lucene-search`'s only range-shaped queries (`search_numeric_range`/`search_sorted_ord_range`/`search_multi_valued_range` in `doc_value_query.rs`) walk doc-values data, the "slow" `SortedNumericDocValuesField.newSlowRangeQuery` fallback path real Lucene uses when no BKD index exists — not the BKD tree at all; and `lucene_codecs::points` has no intersection/pruning logic either, only the already-existing `PointsReader::decode_all_points` (decodes every point in a field unconditionally, see `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/points_fixtures.rs`). So this module's range-matching logic (`packed_value_in_range`) is genuinely new, reusing only `decode_all_points` as its data source — a real, honest gap against real `BKDReader.intersect`'s tree-pruning traversal (which can skip whole leaves/subtrees the packed index proves are entirely in/out of range): this port's approach is `O(field's total point count)` per call, not sublinear, tracked here rather than hidden. **Cross-engine verification approach**: no new checked-in real-Lucene fixture — the existing `fixtures/data/points_index/` fixture (task #18/#22, `GenPoints.java`) is single-dimension only, and this module's own tests need a hand-built 2D (`LatLonPoint`-shaped) fixture anyway to exercise the "every dimension independently in range" semantics that fixture can't reach; extending it with a new `Append*Manifest.java` would add Java-side machinery without covering anything the in-memory fixture (built via the existing, already real-Lucene-verified `points::write`/`points::open` round trip from task #18/#22) doesn't already cover byte-for-byte. Unit-tested: exact range match, inclusive boundaries on both ends, a zero-match range (no-op, empty `Vec`), a range matching every doc, an unknown field number, `live_docs` filtering an already-deleted doc out of the match set, a 2D multi-dimension field where one dimension alone would over-match but the AND-across-dimensions rule correctly excludes it, and end-to-end `resolve_and_apply_points_range_delete` against a real `FsDirectory` (`del_gen`/`del_count`/`.liv` bytes correct, plus a zero-match round still bumping `del_gen`). 99.38% line coverage. **Deliberately not implemented**: multi-segment resolution and delete-by-query beyond a single field's point range, same scope limits as `term_delete.rs`. **b11 sweep (2026-08-28)**: rewritten onto `points::PointsReader::range_query` (the real `PointValues.intersect` + `PointRangeQuery` visitor ported in b7) instead of `decode_all_points` + an in-memory filter. Two consequences. (1) **Correctness**: the query box is now interpreted over the field's *index* dimensions, as `PointRangeQuery` requires, not its data dimensions -- a field with trailing non-indexed data dimensions (`LatLonShape`, range fields) previously indexed past the end of a correctly-sized box. (2) **Performance**: measured on a 200k-point single-dimension field, 220x faster for a 100-hit range, 200x for a 2k-hit range, 51x at 50% selectivity and 31x for a match-everything range (the pruning traversal never decodes packed values for fully-contained cells). |

| `index/IndexWriter.updateDocument(Term, doc)` | `lucene-index/src/update_document.rs::update_document` | **ported (task #37).** The atomic delete-by-term + add-document composition real Lucene defines `updateDocument` as, built entirely by wiring together already-verified primitives rather than new byte-format work: task #27's `term_delete::resolve_and_apply_term_delete` (delete half, fanned out over every segment in the caller's `SegmentInfos` that it supplies an opened [`SegmentDeleteSource`] for — segments with no supplied source are left untouched, not silently dropped) and task #11's `segment_writer::flush_stored_only_segment` (add half — a brand-new segment for the replacement document). Both halves are applied to an in-memory copy of the segment list; the **only** durable state transition is one `segment_infos::write` call at the very end, after every fallible step has already succeeded — so a reader opening any `segments_N` this port writes either sees the fully-old state (previous generation, if `update_document` errored before its final write) or the fully-new one (old segments with bumped `del_gen`s + the one new segment, all in the next generation's single fsync'd file), never a half-applied mix. This is a state-machine/ordering guarantee, not a new decoder, so no new byte format needed cross-engine fixture verification (same reasoning as task #36's points-delete "reused vs. newly written" note) — what *is* verified against the checked-in real-Lucene `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture (the same one `term_delete.rs`'s own tests use) is that the delete half's resolution is correct within this composition, not just in isolation. **Free function, not a method on a stateful writer**: this port has no long-lived `IndexWriter`-equivalent struct (task #11's "minimal multi-segment writer" is itself a free function, `flush_stored_only_segment`, operating on `Directory` state the caller owns) — `update_document` follows that same style rather than introducing a new stateful abstraction unprompted. **Known, inherited limitation, not new to this task**: this port's only write path (`flush_stored_only_segment`) writes stored-fields-only segments (no write-side postings format exists yet, `PLAN.md` Phase 5 item 2), so a delete-by-term issued against *only* a segment this port wrote itself can never actually match anything — there is nothing to resolve against. Unit-tested: updating replaces a single existing doc (singleton-postings term, no `.doc` file needed) and the deletion is verified on disk via `live_docs::parse` while the replacement document is verified via `stored_fields::open`/`.document()`; a term matching zero existing docs behaves exactly like a pure insert (old segment's `del_count` untouched, `del_gen` still bumped per `resolve_and_apply_term_delete`'s existing no-op-round convention); a term matching multiple docs deletes all of them and adds exactly one new document; a segment with no supplied delete source is left completely untouched in the new commit; and a failing delete step (an out-of-range doc ID from a deliberately-too-small `max_doc`) aborts before any `segments_N`/new-segment file is written, confirmed by asserting neither exists on disk afterward. Also confirms the new commit round-trips through `segment_infos::parse`. 99.25% line coverage. |
| `search/IndexSearcher.search(Query, Collector)` (single-segment slice) | `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::search_term_query` | **ported, scoped to one already-opened segment.** Takes an already-opened `blocktree::BlockTreeFields`, an optional opened `.doc` (`postings::DocInput`, needed only when the matched term's `docFreq > 1`), and an optional `.liv`-derived `FixedBitSet` (`None` = no deletions, matching `IndexSearcher`'s `Bits liveDocs == null` convention) — `seekExact`s the term, decodes its postings eagerly (`FieldTerms::postings`, not the lazy skip-ahead `LazyDocsCursor`: no skip-driven query shape exists yet to need it — see `rust-performance`'s "profile before optimizing"), and feeds every live doc ID to a `Collector` in ascending order. A missing field or a term absent from the dictionary yields zero matches, not an error (mirrors `TermQuery.createWeight`'s null-`Scorer` outcome). **No `SegmentReader`/multi-segment federation existed at the time this function was written** — this function's caller is responsible for opening the segment's files the same way `crates/lucene-codecs/tests/blocktree_fixtures.rs` already does. A minimal `SegmentReader`/`DirectoryReader` now exists (task #45, see its row below `multi_segment.rs`'s), but this single-segment function itself is unchanged -- callers open one segment directly the same way, whether by hand or via `DirectoryReader::segment_readers()`. **Verified against real Lucene**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/term_query_fixtures.rs` runs `search_term_query` against the real `IndexWriter`-produced segment in `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` and asserts the exact doc-ID sets match `manifest.properties`' `postingsDocs` entries (themselves real `PostingsEnum.nextDoc()` output) for fields spanning the singleton (`id`), multi-doc (`body`), multi-block (`big`, `docFreq == 300`), and level-1-skip (`l1`, `docFreq == 8250`) postings shapes already covered at the decode layer. |

Generic (`search_term_query<C: Collector>`), not `dyn`, per `rust-performance`'s "monomorphize per-doc loops" rule. |

Kept as its own trait rather than adding a `score` parameter to `Collector::collect` (which `lib.rs`'s original module doc had flagged as an expected future breaking change) because `CountCollector`/`VecCollector` and the unscored search functions have no use for a score — see `collector.rs`'s module doc for the full reasoning. |

| `search/TopScoreDocCollector` | `lucene-search/src/collector.rs::TopDocsCollector` | **ported (task #13).** Keeps the top `n` `(doc_id, score)` hits, tie-broken by lower doc ID on an exact score tie — verified against `HitQueue.lessThan` (`org.apache.lucene.search.HitQueue`), not assumed. Implemented as a sorted `Vec` with a binary-search insert position rather than real Lucene's binary-heap `HitQueue` — `O(n)` per insert instead of `O(log n)`, an explicit "simple first cut" tradeoff matching this crate's `docid_set::Disjunction` precedent (see that module's doc comment); revisit if `top_n`/query throughput ever demands the heap -- **both pieces now exist**: `lucene_util::TernaryLongHeap` (written for HNSW's `NeighborQueue`) is the heap 10.5.0's `TopScoreDocCollector` uses, and `collector::doc_score_encoder` (c37) is the `DocScoreEncoder` packing it stores, so what is left is a measurement rather than a missing primitive. No `TotalHits`/`TotalHits.Relation` tracking (nothing here does early termination yet — every `collect` call is a fully evaluated hit). Unit-tested for fewer-than-`n`, exactly-`n`, more-than-`n` (eviction), tie-break (both on initial fill and on eviction), and empty-input cases. **b12 sweep (M2)**: gained `total_hits()` (`TopDocs.totalHits`, with `TotalHits.Relation` flipping to `GREATER_THAN_OR_EQUAL_TO` the moment a competitive threshold is published), `with_total_hits_threshold` (`TopScoreDocCollectorManager`'s `totalHitsThreshold`, delaying that publication so the count stays exact), and `score_mode()` reproducing Java's `totalHitsThreshold == Integer.MAX_VALUE ? COMPLETE : TOP_SCORES`. The default threshold is `0` (prune as soon as the queue fills) rather than Java's 1000, documented on the constructor. **c37-search-behaviours** added the last two: `with_after` is `TopScoreDocCollector.after`'s test verbatim (`score > afterScore || (score == afterScore && doc <= afterDoc)`, applied after `totalHits` is incremented so page 2 reports the same total page 1 did), and `MaxScoreAccumulator` is the shared min-competitive score, backed by one `AtomicI64` folded with `fetch_max` over `DocScoreEncoder`'s `(score, ~doc)` packing -- the same design, and for the same reason, as Java's `LongAccumulator(Math::max, Long.MIN_VALUE)`. Java *pushes* the threshold into the scorer every `modInterval == 0x3ff` documents; this port *pulls* it from `pruning_threshold()` at each block decision, so the interval has no work to do and is deliberately not modelled. The doc-id tie-break is: a leaf whose `docBase` is at or past the accumulated hit's global doc id may demand a strictly higher score, expressed under this crate's `bound <= threshold` rule as the score itself, and one ULP lower otherwise -- the same pair Java's `Math.nextUp` produces under its `bound < minCompetitiveScore` rule. Multi-segment entry points: `multi_segment::{merge_multi_segment_scored_after, search_term_query_multi_segment_after, merge_multi_segment_scored_concurrent_shared_max_score}`; C ABI: `ffi_search_term_query_scored_after`. Verified against real Lucene by `AppendSearchAfterManifest.java`, which records three consecutive `IndexSearcher.searchAfter` pages over `blocktree_index` (`big:everywhere`, whose 300 documents take only four distinct scores, so the page boundary is decided by the doc-id half of the rule) and over the two-segment `multi_segment_scoring_index`. |
| `search/similarities/Similarity`/`BM25Similarity` (formula only) | `lucene-search/src/similarity.rs` | **ported: the pure BM25 scoring formula, plus real per-doc/avg norms decoding (this task).** Formula verified against `BM25Similarity.java`/`BM25Scorer.doScore` (Lucene 10.5.0) — `idf(docFreq, docCount) = ln(1 + (docCount - docFreq + 0.5) / (docFreq + 0.5))`, `tfNorm(freq, fieldLength, avgFieldLength, k1, b) = freq / (freq + k1*(1-b+b*fieldLength/avgFieldLength))`, `score = idf * tfNorm`, defaults `k1 = 1.2`, `b = 0.75` (`BM25Similarity()`'s no-arg constructor). **Correctness fix (task #32)**: `tfNorm` previously carried a spurious `(k1+1)` numerator factor (the textbook Robertson/Sparck-Jones BM25 term, not real Lucene 10.5.0's actual formula, which folds no such factor into `BM25Scorer.doScore`'s `weight - weight / (1 + freq * normInverse)`); every self-consistency test in this crate had independently reimplemented the same wrong factor, so none of them could catch it. It was caught by `DisjunctionMaxQuery`'s (task #32) new cross-engine test comparing an absolute score against real `IndexSearcher.explain()` output for the first time in this port, rather than a hand-rederivation of this module's own formula — fixed in `similarity::tf_norm`, with `scoring_fixtures.rs`'s/`phrase_query_fixtures.rs`'s hand-computed expected values updated to match; every dependent test (`scoring_fixtures.rs`, `phrase_query_fixtures.rs`, `boolean_query_fixtures.rs`, `dismax_query_fixtures.rs`) still passes since they only assert relative/self-consistent properties, except the one new test that now checks the literal real-Lucene-comparable magnitude. No query-time boost is folded in (none exists in this port). **The norms gap, previously stated here as open, is now closed**: `crates/lucene-codecs/src/norms.rs`'s read side (`norm_value`) is fixture-verified and complete (see that row above); real Lucene's default `Similarity.computeNorm` encodes a field's token-count length via `SmallFloat.intToByte4` (a lossy 4-bit-mantissa byte encoding), and `BM25Similarity.LENGTH_TABLE`/`SimScorer.score` decode it back with `SmallFloat.byte4ToInt` — this port now has that exact decode (`lucene_util::small_float::byte4_to_int`, wrapped by `similarity::decode_norm`), verified against hand-derived known byte↔length values from `SmallFloat.java`'s algorithm. `lucene-search/src/field_norms.rs::FieldNorms::open` computes `avgFieldLength` once per field per query (summing every live doc's decoded length, not recomputed per scored doc — see the `rust-performance` skill), and `search_term_query_scored`/`search_boolean_query_scored` take an optional `FieldNorms`/`HashMap<String, FieldNorms>`, using it for real per-doc/avg field length when present. **The one remaining, explicitly-scoped gap**: passing `None` (a field with no opened norms — norms disabled for that field, or a caller that hasn't wired norms opening up yet) still falls back to the constant `1.0`/`1.0` substitution (`similarity::UNNORMED_FIELD_LENGTH`) — a documented, deliberate fallback for that one case, not a blanket approximation anymore. Also still open: no cross-engine Java-side BM25-score fixture generator exists, so a full query's *final* score hasn't been diffed byte-for-byte against real Lucene's own output (only the length-normalization term's liveness and the decoded lengths themselves are differentially verified) — deferred. Differential-verified in `crates/lucene-search/tests/scoring_fixtures.rs` against the real `_0.nvm`/`_0.nvd` this fixture's `IndexWriter` wrote: decoded per-doc lengths match hand-derived values from the fixture's own known per-term postings frequencies (a doc's BM25 length is the sum of its terms' frequencies), `avgFieldLength` is their mean, and real-norms scores differ from the constant-fallback scores for the same query/term. Unit-tested with independently hand-computed expected values (not just asserting the implementation's own output) for `idf`/`tf_norm`/`score`/`decode_norm`, plus monotonicity properties (more common term → lower idf, longer field → lower tf_norm, higher freq → higher score, real length ratio → correctly ordered scores). **Configurable `k1`/`b` (task #214, "Configurable BM25 constant from FFI")**: real `BM25Similarity(float k1, float b)`'s constructor lets a caller tune both constants per-`Similarity`-instance; this port previously had no way to do that in-process or over FFI at all — `DEFAULT_K1`/`DEFAULT_B` were baked into every scored call chain. `similarity::Bm25Params { k1, b }` (`Default` reproducing `DEFAULT_K1 = 1.2`/`DEFAULT_B = 0.75` byte-for-byte) and `similarity::score_with_params` are the new configurable primitives, but **only reach the single most fundamental scored search path**: `lucene_search::search_term_query_scored_with_similarity` (a new sibling of `search_term_query_scored`, same matching semantics, existing function's signature/behavior left byte-for-byte unchanged) and its one new FFI entry point, `lucene_ffi::query::ffi_search_term_query_scored_with_similarity`. **Deliberately still hardcoded to `DEFAULT_K1`/`DEFAULT_B`, not wired to this new struct** (a materially larger, riskier change than this task's scope): `search_boolean_query_scored`/`ffi_search_boolean_query_scored`, both MAXSCORE-pruned variants (`search_term_query_scored_maxscore`, `search_boolean_query_scored_maxscore`, and their FFI siblings), `search_phrase_query_scored`/`ffi_search_phrase_query_scored`, multi-segment fan-out (`multi_segment.rs`), and `explain`/`explain_boolean`. Regression-proven: `Bm25Params::default()` through the new parameterized path (both the plain-Rust `score_with_params`/`search_term_query_scored_with_similarity` and the FFI entry point) produces byte-for-byte identical scores to the existing hardcoded-default path; a genuinely different `k1`/`b` (2.0/0.5 in the tests) produces a hand-computed-from-the-formula-above, measurably different score, verified at both the `lucene-search` unit-test layer and the `lucene-ffi` layer against the real `blocktree_index` fixture. | **Corrected in M1.5:** `avgFieldLength` is now computed as real Lucene's `BM25Similarity` computes it -- `sumTotalTermFreq / docCount` from the field's `.tmd` counters (`FieldNorms::from_field_stats`) -- not by averaging *decoded* norms. Norms are `SmallFloat`-quantized to one byte; below length 25 that encoding is exact, which is why every existing fixture (documents of 1-3 terms) passed with the wrong formula, and why only M1's 5M-document corpus with 40-160 term documents exposed it. The old average was 0.1-0.6% off, enough to reorder documents at the top-k boundary: 19 of 20 benchmark queries disagreed with Java on hit sets for this reason alone. `FieldNorms::open`, which scans every doc to average norms, is retained for callers without field stats. **Corrected by the b12 sweep (M2)**: the sentence that stood here -- "is no longer what the search paths use" -- was true of the M1 benchmark runner only. `from_field_stats` has *no* caller outside `benchmarks/rust-runner` and `field_norms.rs`'s own tests; `lucene-ffi::query`'s production entry point and `explain.rs` both still call `FieldNorms::open`, so every FFI-served search still averages lossy decoded norms over *live* docs where Java divides `sumTotalTermFreq` by a `docCount` that includes deleted ones. See `docs/sweep/m2/b12-search-core.md` F-7; owners b13/b15. **b12 sweep (M2)**: the scoring *expression* is now `BM25Scorer.doScore`'s verbatim `weight - weight / (1 + freq * normInverse)` (`similarity::do_score`) over `BM25Similarity.scorer`'s `cache[i] = 1 / (k1 * ((1-b) + b * LENGTH_TABLE[i] / avgdl))` (`similarity::norm_inverse`), not the algebraically-equal `idf * tfNorm` multiply form every scored path used before. That form put every score this engine returns one ULP away from real Lucene's, and made the MAXSCORE block bound and the score it gates use *different* float expressions -- a latent skip-a-real-hit bug. `crates/lucene-search/tests/bm25_scoring_fixtures.rs` now compares term / boolean / exact-phrase / sloppy-phrase / multi-phrase scores against real `IndexSearcher` output **bit for bit** (`f32::to_bits`), sourced from `fixtures/src/AppendScoringManifest.java`. `Bm25Params::new` added with Lucene's own range validation and error strings (`b` outside `[0,1]` breaks the monotonicity every impacts bound rests on). **c6 sweep (M2)**: F-7 is closed -- `explain.rs`'s own call site and the `lucene-ffi` production path both take `from_field_stats` now (b15 did the FFI half), and `FieldNorms::open` is left only for callers that hold a `.nvd`/`.nvm` and nothing else. b13's F-26 is closed too: `avgFieldLength` is now **reader-wide**, `IndexSearcher.fieldStats`' `sumTotalTermFreq`/`docCount` summed over every leaf (`DirectoryReader::avg_field_length`/`field_norms`, `FieldNorms::with_avg_field_length`), where it used to be derived per leaf -- which gave each segment a different length-normalization curve for the same term. Proven by a new genuinely two-segment real-Lucene fixture (`fixtures/src/GenMultiSegmentScoring.java`, per-leaf `avgdl` 1.75 and 40.0 against a reader-wide 20.875) whose recorded `TopDocs` `crates/lucene-search/tests/multi_segment_scoring_fixtures.rs` matches bit for bit, with a negative control asserting per-leaf `avgdl` does *not*. The multi-segment FFI entry points, which passed `None` for norms and so scored every hit unnormed, now pass those reader-wide norms. Still unported: `k3`/`computeQueryTermWeight`, and every non-BM25 `Similarity`.
| `util/SmallFloat` (`intToByte4`/`byte4ToInt` only) | `lucene-util/src/small_float.rs` | **ported**, scoped to exactly the pair BM25 norms need (not the general `byteToFloat`/`floatToByte` 24-bit-mantissa pair used elsewhere in Lucene for unrelated float encodings). Verified byte-for-byte against `SmallFloat.java` (Lucene 10.5.0): `longToInt4`/`int4ToLong`'s "top 4 significant bits plus a biased shift" scheme, `NUM_FREE_VALUES = 255 - longToInt4(Integer.MAX_VALUE) = 24` (asserted as a concrete value, not just trusted from the derivation). Unit-tested: subnormal range (bytes 0-23) decodes exactly, hand-derived known byte→length values, monotonicity (SmallFloat's whole purpose — larger byte never decodes to a smaller length), and encode→decode→re-encode fixed-point round trips for both subnormal and lossy normal-range values. |
| `util/BytesRefHash` (term/byte-sequence interning pool) | `lucene-util/src/term_interner.rs::{TermInterner, TermId}` | **new standalone primitive (task #67), not a byte-for-byte port of `BytesRefHash.java` and NOT yet wired into any indexing or query path.** Real `BytesRefHash` is tied to Lucene's `ByteBlockPool` allocation strategy and exposes sort/compact/rehash machinery for the indexing chain's per-field term dictionaries; this module has none of that. It only carries the core value proposition -- deduplicating recurring byte sequences into a stable, cheap-to-copy handle -- as a from-scratch `HashMap`-backed pool: `TermInterner::intern(&[u8]) -> TermId` returns the same `TermId` for byte-identical input and a fresh one otherwise, `TermInterner::get(TermId) -> Option<&[u8]>` looks the bytes back up, `TermId` is a plain `Copy` `u32` wrapper. No `unsafe` (workspace only permits it in `lucene-util`/`lucene-store`/`lucene-ffi`, and this module doesn't need it).
