Unit-tested: repeated identical bytes intern to the same ID, distinct bytes get distinct IDs, ID→bytes round trip, the empty byte string as a valid distinct term, lookup of an unrecognized/out-of-range `TermId` returns `None` rather than panicking, and a stress case interning 20,000 calls over a 50-word vocabulary that confirms `TermInterner::len()` collapses to exactly 50 stored entries (dedup actually happening, not just returning arbitrary IDs), plus a separate 5,000-all-distinct-terms case. **Explicitly deferred**: wiring this into `lucene-index`'s indexing chain or `lucene-search`'s query terms (future task), `ByteBlockPool`-style bulk/arena allocation, sorting/compaction, and any on-disk format tie-in -- this is purely an in-memory helper. |
| `search/Weight`, `search/Scorer`, `search/BulkScorer` (as a trait hierarchy) | — | **deferred, not started.** This port's scoring is wired directly into `search_term_query_scored`/`search_boolean_query_scored` as plain functions (see the `TermQuery`/`BooleanQuery` rows below), not through a `Weight`/`Scorer` class hierarchy — same "no speculative polymorphism with a single implementation" reasoning `lib.rs`'s module doc already applies to unscored matching. Revisit once a second query type or multi-segment federation gives the hierarchy a real second caller. |
| `search/Weight.count(LeafReaderContext)` + `search/TotalHitCountCollector`'s short-circuit (`TermQuery`, `MatchAllDocsQuery`, `FieldExistsQuery` overrides) | `lucene-search/src/weight_count.rs::{count_term_query_shortcut, count_term_query, count_match_all_docs, FieldExistsLeaf, count_field_exists_leaf, field_exists_rewrites_to_match_all_docs}`, `lucene-search/src/directory_reader.rs::SegmentReader::{num_docs, field_exists_leaf}`, `lucene-ffi/src/query.rs::ffi_count_term_query` | **ported (c37-search-behaviours).** Java's `Weight.count` answers "how many documents match this leaf" from metadata or returns `-1` for "go and iterate"; `TotalHitCountCollector.getLeafCollector` asks first and throws `CollectionTerminatedException` when the answer is real, so `IndexSearcher.count(query)` opens no postings at all for a query that can shortcut. This port has no `Weight`, so each override is a free function taking the same inputs its query's search function already takes, and `Option<i64>` stands in for the `-1` sentinel. `TermQuery`: `termsEnum.docFreq()` **only** when the leaf has no deletions -- `live_docs: Option<&FixedBitSet>` is this crate's `hasDeletions()`, so `Some(_)` declines, exactly as Java does, because `docFreq` counts documents that are no longer live. A missing field or term is `Some(0)`, not `None`. `MatchAllDocsQuery`: `numDocs()`, i.e. the live-docs cardinality. `FieldExistsQuery`: see that query's own row. **Measured** on `benchmarks/.corpus/merged` (~5M documents, `benches/term_count.rs`): `body:t0` (4 997 130 matches) **72.2 ns vs 13.93 ms** collecting every document, and `body:tz` (1 070 709 matches) **96.7 ns vs 1.43 ms** -- the saving is proportional to `docFreq`, which is why both are measured. **Ground truth**: `fixtures/src/AppendCountManifest.java` records real `IndexSearcher.count` over `blocktree_index` (no deletions) and `live_docs_index` (two deleted documents, where `id:1` has `docFreq == 1` and counts 0 -- the number that separates a correct shortcut from one reporting deleted documents as hits); `crates/lucene-search/tests/weight_count_fixtures.rs` asserts against them, and was verified to fail with the deletions gate removed. **Not ported**: `IndexSearcher.count`'s two-clause pure-disjunction inclusion-exclusion optimisation (`isTwoClausePureDisjunctionWithTerms`), which needs a reader-level `Query` object this port does not have, and the `-1` default for every other query shape (a caller just runs the query). |
| `search/BooleanQuery` + `search/BooleanClause` (matching + BM25 scoring, all four `Occur` values, `minimumNumberShouldMatch`, nested `BooleanQuery` clauses, `PhraseQuery` clauses) | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{BooleanQuery, Clause}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{search_boolean_query, search_boolean_query_scored, matched_boolean_docs, resolve_clause_docs, clause_scores, should_match_counts}` | **ported**, including `minimumNumberShouldMatch` (task #24), nested `BooleanQuery` clauses (task #25), and `PhraseQuery` clauses (task #29, closing the gap this row previously noted as deferred). `must`/`filter`/`should`/`must_not` are `Vec<Clause>` plus `minimum_should_match: usize` -- one list per `BooleanClause.Occur` value, which is `clauseSets` (Java's own `Map<Occur, Collection<Query>>`) materialized once instead of re-partitioned per call. **`Occur.FILTER` (c11)**: required for matching, contributing exactly zero to the score -- Java builds a filter clause's `Weight` with `ScoreMode.COMPLETE_NO_SCORES` and `BooleanScorerSupplier.req` puts it in `required` but not `requiredScoring`, so `ConjunctionScorer.score()` never calls it. A filter clause does **not** count toward `minimumNumberShouldMatch` (only `Occur.SHOULD` increments `shouldMatchCount`), and a filter-only query **matches, at score 0** -- it is not a "pure negative" query, since Java's pure-negative test is `clauses.size() == clauseSets.get(MUST_NOT).size()`. Because a filter never enters the sum, it also cannot perturb the scoring clauses' `f32` summation order; `crates/lucene-search/tests/bm25_scoring_fixtures.rs` asserts that bit-for-bit against real `IndexSearcher` for seven filter shapes recorded by `fixtures/src/AppendScoringManifest.java` (`scoring.boolean.filter*`): filter beside a MUST, filter-only, a lone filter, filter + optional, the `minimumNumberShouldMatch` interaction, a filter duplicating a MUST clause, and a filter inside a nested `BooleanQuery`. `BooleanWeight.explain`'s filter arm is Java-verbatim -- a `match on required clause, product of:` node at value 0 whose first child is `# clause` (`Occur.FILTER + " clause"`), with the clause's own explanation as the second. `query_parser` never produces a filter clause, matching `QueryParserBase.addClause`, whose only outcomes are MUST/SHOULD/MUST_NOT. **Deferred**: `lucene-ffi`'s boolean-query entry points still take three flat clause arrays; adding a fourth is a C-ABI signature change across four exported functions and their Java bindings. `Clause` is a closed **three**-variant enum — `Clause::Term(TermQuery)`, `Clause::Phrase(PhraseQuery)`, or `Clause::Boolean(Box<BooleanQuery>)`, recursively (an enum, not a `Weight`/`Scorer`-style trait object, since exactly three query shapes need to nest here today — see the `rust-performance` skill's "enums where the closed set allows" guidance). `BooleanQuery::with_must`/`with_filter`/`with_should`/`with_must_not` take `impl Into<Clause>` items — `Clause`'s `From<TermQuery>`/`From<PhraseQuery>`/`From<BooleanQuery>` impls kept every existing call site compiling unchanged. Matching semantics verified against real `BooleanQuery.rewrite()`/`BooleanWeight.scorer`/`bulkScorer`/`explain` source, not guessed: no `must`/`should` clauses at all (with or without `must_not`) matches **nothing** — real Lucene rewrites both "empty clause list" and "only `MUST_NOT` clauses" ("pure negative BooleanQuery") to `MatchNoDocsQuery`; a pure-exclusion query is *not* "every doc except the excluded ones". `minimum_should_match` gates `should` **regardless of whether `must` is also non-empty** — verified against `BooleanWeight` source specifically because this is the one place the pre-#24 rule ("`should` is score-only once `must` exists") is easy to get backwards: that rule only holds at `minimum_should_match == 0`; for `minimum_should_match > 0`, a doc drawn from `must`'s conjunction (or, when `must` is empty, `should`'s disjunction) is only kept if it also matches at least `minimum_should_match` of the `should` clauses — a genuine behavior change gated purely on `minimum_should_match > 0`. The counting mechanism ([`should_match_counts`]) is a `HashMap<i32, usize>` tally of how many `should` clauses' doc-ID lists contain each candidate doc — needed because a plain `Disjunction` only reports "in the union", not "how many clauses agreed". `minimum_should_match` exceeding `should.len()` needs no special-case branch: real `BooleanQuery.rewrite()` turns that into an explicit `MatchNoDocsQuery` at query-construction time, but no doc's should-match count can ever reach an unreachable threshold either way, so the same threshold comparison already yields the identical "matches nothing" result. `search_boolean_query`/`search_boolean_query_scored`'s previously-duplicated matched-doc-set computation is unified in one shared `matched_boolean_docs` helper. **Nested clauses**: [`resolve_clause_docs`] recursively resolves a `Clause::Boolean` clause via a fresh `matched_boolean_docs` call on the nested query — respecting *that* query's own `must`/`should`/`must_not`/`minimum_should_match` completely independently of the parent's, with no hardcoded nesting-depth limit (verified with 2–3 levels of nesting, not just one extra level). [`clause_scores`] is the scoring sibling: a nested `BooleanQuery`'s own score contribution is the sum of *its own* matching `must`/`should` sub-clauses' scores, restricted to the doc set the nested query itself actually matched — mirroring real Lucene's additive `BooleanScorer` recursion, so a should-clause hit the nested query's own `must_not`/`minimum_should_match` excludes can't leak a score contribution into the parent. **`Clause::Phrase` (task #29)**: `resolve_clause_docs` delegates matching straight to `search_phrase_query` (collected via a local `VecCollector`), and `clause_scores` delegates scoring straight to `search_phrase_query_scored` (collected via a tiny inline `ScoringCollector`) — both reusing the standalone phrase-query functions' logic rather than duplicating it. Because a `Clause::Phrase` needs the segment's opened `.pos`/`.pay` files, `search_boolean_query`/`search_boolean_query_scored` (and every internal helper in the recursive chain — `matched_boolean_docs`, `resolve_clause_docs`, `clause_scores`) now additionally take `pos_in: Option<&PosInput>`/`pay_in: Option<&PayInput>` (a breaking signature change from the pre-task-#29 four/six-argument shape; every call site in this crate and `lucene-ffi` was updated to pass `None`/`None` where no phrase clause is used) — `None` is fine for a query with no multi-term `Clause::Phrase` at any nesting depth, surfacing `Error::MissingPosInput` only if one turns out to need it, same convention `search_phrase_query` already established. **Verified against real Lucene**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/boolean_query_fixtures.rs` computes expected conjunction/disjunction/difference from `manifest.properties`' real `PostingsEnum`-derived doc sets and asserts `search_boolean_query` matches exactly, including a `minimum_should_match`-with-`must`-present case, a nested-`must`-clause case, and a three-level-deep nesting case. **`search_boolean_query_scored`** (task #13) computes the identical matched-doc set, then reports each doc's score as the sum of its BM25 score across every `must`/`should` clause it satisfies (mirroring real Lucene's additive `BooleanScorer`; `must_not` never contributes, matching `Occur.MUST_NOT`'s filter-only contract) — verified in `crates/lucene-search/tests/scoring_fixtures.rs` against the real segment, including a dedicated test that a `must_not` clause never changes the score, a test (task #24) proving `minimum_should_match` gates matching only, not the score sum, a test (task #25) proving a nested `must` clause's own score sums correctly into the parent's additive total, and a test (task #29) proving a `Clause::Phrase`'s own score sums correctly alongside a sibling `Clause::Term`, against the `pos` field's real alpha/beta postings. Unit tests in `lib.rs`/`query.rs` additionally confirm a nested clause's own `minimum_should_match` and the parent's are fully independent settings (no cross-contamination in either direction), plus a `Clause::Phrase` nested inside a `Clause::Boolean` resolves both matching and scoring correctly. **Still deferred**: `lucene-ffi`'s `ffi_search_boolean_query` still only ever constructs flat `Clause::Term` clauses from its four-parallel-array C-ABI wire format — exposing `Clause::Phrase`/`Clause::Boolean` *construction* over that wire format remains a separate, deferred FFI design question (alongside the already-deferred `_scored` FFI wrappers). **Edge cases (task #60)**: six real Lucene `BooleanQuery`/`BooleanWeight` corner cases were individually verified against this port's actual `matched_boolean_docs`/`should_match_counts`/`clause_scores` code (not assumed) and found already correct, locked in with new regression tests rather than requiring a fix: a pure `must_not`-only query matches nothing (confirmed *before* `must_not` ever enters the merge, not via an "everything except excluded" shortcut); `minimum_should_match > 0` with zero `should` clauses at all (a nonsensical-but-configurable combination) correctly matches nothing, not "trivially satisfied"; `minimum_should_match` exceeding `should.len()` has no distinct code path from the ordinary case, so no off-by-one is possible at or past the boundary; a nested `Clause::Boolean`'s own `must_not` never leaks into or is leaked into by an outer level's `must_not` (each nesting level gets a fresh `matched_boolean_docs` call); and — deliberately **not** a bug — a literal duplicate `should` clause (the same `TermQuery` twice) double-counts toward `minimum_should_match` and double-scores, matching real `BooleanQuery`'s own documented non-deduping behavior (duplicating a clause is itself a known, intentional way to boost it in real Lucene), not something this port needed to special-case away. **Rewrite pass (task #79)**: `BooleanQuery::rewrite`/`Clause::rewrite` add a pure, standalone, **opt-in** simplification pass -- never called by `search_boolean_query`/`search_boolean_query_scored` themselves, so existing execution is unchanged. Implements exactly three rules: (1) a single-clause query with no `must_not` collapses to that clause, but only in the two cases proven not to change matching -- `must.len() == 1` with `should` empty and `minimum_should_match == 0`, or `should.len() == 1` with `must` empty and `minimum_should_match <= 1` (both boundary cases where a positive `minimum_should_match` would otherwise turn "matches nothing" into a false positive are deliberately excluded); (2) zero-clause/`must_not`-only "matches nothing" is a documented no-op, since `matched_boolean_docs` already produces that result with no `MatchNoDocsQuery`-equivalent `Clause` to rewrite to; (3) every clause is rewritten recursively, bottom-up, including through nested `DisjunctionMax`/`ConstantScore`/`Boost`. **Duplicate-clause deduplication, resolved precisely (re-audited against `BooleanQuery.java`'s actual `rewrite()` source, not the earlier task #60 assumption)**: real `BooleanQuery.rewrite()` does two genuinely different things here, and this port now matches one and deliberately not the other. (a) **`MUST_NOT`/`FILTER` dedup** is unconditional set-membership dedup (`MUST_NOT`/`FILTER` are stored in a `HashSet`) with no scoring implication, since neither ever contributes to score -- `MUST_NOT` only excludes docs matched by `must`/`should`. **Rule 4, now implemented**: `Clause::rewrite` removes exact duplicate `must_not` clauses (order-preserving, keeps the first occurrence), and since c11 added `Occur.FILTER` it removes duplicate `filter` clauses the same way -- both halves of real Lucene's rule now apply. Proven safe against this port's own executor, not assumed: `matched_boolean_docs` only ever folds `must_not` into a `Disjunction` used purely for exclusion, and `clause_scores` never iterates `must_not` at all (only `must.iter().chain(should.iter())`) -- so a repeated `must_not` clause changes neither the matched-doc set nor any score. (b) **`MUST`/`SHOULD` dedup is correctly left unimplemented, but the earlier justification for that ("real Lucene doesn't dedup these either, duplicating a clause is itself an intentional way to boost it") was wrong** -- real `BooleanQuery.rewrite()`'s `deduplicateClauses` *does* fold duplicate `MUST`/`SHOULD` clauses, replacing every occurrence with **one** copy carrying a recombined boost from `IndexSearcher.getSimilarity().computeQueryTermWeight(count)` for the common unweighted-duplicate case, falling back to a linear boost sum only when explicit per-clause boosts are already present. That `computeQueryTermWeight` call is `Similarity`-specific and is *not* equivalent to "sum each clause's score `count` times" -- it approximates what one token at `count`-times term frequency would have scored. This port's `Clause::rewrite` has **no `IndexSearcher`/`Similarity` in scope** (it runs before any index is opened), so there is no way to reproduce real Lucene's actual `MUST`/`SHOULD` dedup faithfully; a naive alternative (drop the duplicate, or sum boosts unconditionally) would silently produce *different* scores than both this port's current (correct, additive) behavior *and* real Lucene's actual rewritten behavior -- a correctness regression, not a simplification. Left unimplemented until a future task threads a term-weight function into the rewrite path. This is why `clause_scores`'s existing additive-per-clause behavior (a duplicate `should`/`must` clause doubles its score contribution, confirmed against this port's own executor by task #60) is still exactly right for the *unrewritten* query -- it matches real Lucene's own pre-rewrite `BooleanWeight` scoring, which is what this port's non-rewriting `search_boolean_query_scored` always uses. Proven scoring-equivalent, not just structurally simplified: `crates/lucene-search/tests/boolean_query_fixtures.rs`'s `rewrite_produces_identical_scored_results_for_single_must_clause`/`_for_single_should_clause`/`_for_nested_single_clause_boolean`/`_for_duplicate_must_not_clauses` run the same query pre- and post-`rewrite()` through `search_boolean_query_scored` against the real fixture segment and assert identical `TopDocsCollector` output (doc IDs and scores both); `query.rs`'s `rewrite_removes_exact_duplicate_must_not_clauses`/`rewrite_keeps_distinct_must_not_clauses_and_preserves_order`/`rewrite_does_not_deduplicate_must_not_clauses_inside_a_nested_boolean_clause`/`rewrite_deliberately_does_not_deduplicate_duplicate_must_clauses` cover the structural rule and its documented scope boundary. |
| `search/PhraseQuery`/`ExactPhraseScorer`/`SloppyPhraseMatcher` (exact + full sloppy matching incl. term reordering and repeats; BM25 scoring) | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::PhraseQuery`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{search_phrase_query, search_phrase_query_scored, phrase_matches_in_doc, phrase_freq_exact}`, `lucene-search/src/sloppy_phrase.rs::{SloppyMatcher, PhraseRepeats, sloppy_phrase_matches, sloppy_phrase_freq}` | **ported: exact adjacent-position matching (`slop == 0`, task #19), full `SloppyPhraseMatcher` matching and frequency for `slop > 0` including term reordering and repeated terms (c37-search-behaviours), and BM25 scoring (task #29).** `PhraseQuery { field: String, terms: Vec<Vec<u8>>, slop: u32 }` implicitly places `terms[i]` at query-relative position `i` (still no `PhraseQuery.Builder.add(Term, int position)`-style arbitrary/non-adjacent positions -- `slop` widens how far apart the implicitly-numbered terms may drift at match time, it doesn't let a caller assign arbitrary per-term positions). `slop` defaults to `0` (matching real `PhraseQuery.Builder`'s default); `PhraseQuery::with_slop` is the builder method. **Exact alignment algorithm** (`phrase_matches_in_doc`, unchanged from task #19): every position in the first term's list is a candidate base `p`, checked against every other term's sorted position list via `binary_search` for `p+i`. **Sloppy alignment algorithm** (`sloppy_phrase.rs`, c37's replacement for task #28's in-order-only approximation): `SloppyPhraseMatcher` ported statement for statement. Each slot's positions are shifted back by the slot's own index (`PhrasePositions.position = postings.nextPosition() - offset`), which turns "is this a phrase" into "is there a window of width <= slop covering one shifted position per slot" -- a formulation with **no ordering constraint at all**, which is exactly how Lucene admits reordered terms (`pos:"beta alpha"~2` matches a document reading `alpha beta`, at `matchLength == 2`). `nextMatch()` is Lucene's `PhraseQueue`-driven walk: pop the least `PhrasePositions`, record `end - position`, keep advancing that one while it stays below the queue's next element, re-queue, repeat -- so the *sequence* of `matchLength`s, and therefore the non-associative `f32` sum of `sloppyWeight() == 1/(1 + matchLength)` that `PhraseScorer.score()` accumulates, matches Lucene's, not merely the final verdict. Repeated terms are handled by Lucene's own `rptGroups` machinery (`repeatingTerms`/`gatherRptGroups`/`advanceRepeatGroups`/`advanceRpts`/`collide`), including the `hasMultiTermRpts` term-graph union that only a `MultiPhraseQuery` can reach, so `pos:"alpha alpha"~2` still requires two distinct occurrences. **One deliberate divergence**, documented on `PhraseRepeats`: Lucene discovers the repeat groups on the *first candidate document* and, in the single-term case, groups two `PhrasePositions` whose raw positions coincide *in that document*; this port groups by term identity, which is the same partition whenever the repeating slots hold the same term and is stable across documents rather than dependent on which document the scorer met first. `search_phrase_query` computes the doc-level conjunction across every term's live-filtered doc-ID list first (reusing `docid_set::Conjunction`, since phrase match implies term match), then dispatches per candidate doc to `phrase_matches_in_doc` (unchanged fast path) when `query.slop == 0`, or `sloppy_phrase::sloppy_phrase_matches` otherwise. **Edge cases** (both paths): an empty `terms` list matches nothing (mirrors real `PhraseQuery.Builder.build()`'s `MatchNoDocsQuery` result for zero added terms); a single-term "phrase" degenerates to a plain `search_term_query` call regardless of `slop` (trivially aligned wherever the lone term occurs, never needs an opened `.pos` file); a term missing from the field matches nothing, not an error; a repeated term (e.g. "the the") is handled without special-casing. **BM25 scoring (task #29's addition)**: `search_phrase_query_scored` mirrors `search_phrase_query`'s matching exactly, additionally computing each matched doc's **phrase frequency** (in place of a single term's `freq`) and feeding it through the same BM25 machinery `search_term_query_scored` uses (`similarity::tf_norm`, real per-doc/avg norms via the same `FieldNorms` contract). The phrase's overall `idf` is the **sum of each constituent term's own `idf(docFreq, docCount)`** -- verified against `BM25Similarity.idf(CollectionStatistics, TermStatistics[])`'s real source, which sums per-term idf across a multi-term statistics array rather than inventing a single combined docFreq; this is not this port's own invention. **Exact phrase frequency** (`phrase_freq_exact`): counts every valid base position `p0` in the first term's own (sorted, deduplicated) position list for which the rest of the phrase aligns -- one match per distinct starting position of the phrase's first word, matching `ExactPhraseScorer`'s own per-start-position counting granularity (does not suppress overlapping matches, e.g. "the the the" counts 2 matches for phrase "the the"). **Sloppy phrase frequency** (`sloppy_phrase_freq`): `PhraseScorer.score()`'s own `freq = sloppyWeight(); while (nextMatch()) freq += sloppyWeight();` over the walk above -- a graduated `1 / (1 + matchLength)` per match, summed in Lucene's emission order. **Verified against real Lucene**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/phrase_query_fixtures.rs` (task #19) reuses the existing `pos` field checked into `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` for the exact-matching (`slop == 0`) path (doc 8555: "alpha"@0, "beta"@1, adjacent; doc 8556: "alpha"@0 and @1, no "beta" at all); `two_term_phrase_scored_matches_hand_computed_bm25_score` (task #29) hand-derives the expected BM25 score from the manifest's real `docFreq` values and asserts `search_phrase_query_scored` matches, independently of `similarity::score`'s own unit tests. **The sloppy path's cross-engine gap is now closed**: `GenBlockTree.java` was extended with doc7 ("pos" field: "alpha"@0, "beta"@3, a real, known, non-adjacent gap requiring `(3-0)-(2-1) = 2` moves), and at fixture-generation time it *actually runs* real `IndexSearcher.search` with a real `PhraseQuery.Builder().add(alpha).add(beta).setSlop(n)` against that doc for `n` in `{0, 1, 2, 3, 5}`, recording each verdict in `manifest.properties` (`field.pos.sloppyGap.realLuceneSlopResults=0:false,1:false,2:true,3:true,5:true`). `crates/lucene-search/tests/phrase_query_fixtures.rs::sloppy_phrase_gap_matches_real_lucenes_phrase_query_set_slop_at_every_tested_value` reads that manifest key back and asserts `search_phrase_query`'s own sloppy-path verdict agrees with real Lucene's at every one of those five slop values -- confirmed passing, i.e. this port's sloppy-matching formula is now cross-engine verified against actual `SloppyPhraseMatcher`/`PhraseQuery.setSlop(n)` behavior for a known non-adjacent gap, not just self-consistent unit tests. (The formula's own internal correctness was already covered by the hand-computed unit tests in `lib.rs` listed previously; this closes the gap those tests alone couldn't: agreement with real Lucene's actual output.) **b12 sweep (M2)**: the sloppy (`slop > 0`) *frequency* was a flat `1` for "this document matched somehow", a 2.09x scoring error on the fixture's loose match; fixed and pinned bit-for-bit. **c37-search-behaviours** then closed the reordering gap itself. `AppendScoringManifest.java` records real `IndexSearcher` results for `pos:"beta alpha"` at slop 1/2/4 (no hits / doc 8555 at `matchLength` 2 / doc 8555 and doc 8557 at `matchLength` 4, the second scoring *lower*), for `pos:"gamma delta"~2` over the deliberately-reversed doc 8558, for `pos:"alpha alpha"~2` (repeats), and for the same transposition through `MultiPhraseQuery`; `crates/lucene-search/tests/bm25_scoring_fixtures.rs::{reordered_sloppy_phrase_scores_match_real_lucene_bit_for_bit, reordered_sloppy_multi_phrase_scores_match_real_lucene_bit_for_bit}` assert `f32::to_bits` equality against all of them, and were verified to fail (zero hits where Lucene has one) against the previous in-order matcher. **c40-missing-behaviours** closed the highlighter half, and found that the ledger's own prescription for it ("enumerate through `sloppy_phrase`") would have been a second wrong answer: the highlighter does not use `SloppyPhraseMatcher` at all -- see the `uhighlight` offset-strategy row and `lucene-search/src/near_spans.rs`. **What is still out of scope**: `PhraseQuery.Builder.add(Term, int position)`'s arbitrary per-term positions (this port's slots are always `0..n`). |
| `search/DisjunctionMaxQuery`/`DisjunctionMaxScorer` (matching + BM25 scoring, nested `Clause` disjuncts) | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{DisjunctionMaxQuery, Clause::DisjunctionMax}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{search_disjunction_max_query, search_disjunction_max_query_scored, resolve_dismax_docs, dismax_scores}` | **ported (task #32).** `DisjunctionMaxQuery { disjuncts: Vec<Clause>, tie_breaker: f32 }`; `Clause` grows a fourth variant, `Clause::DisjunctionMax(Box<DisjunctionMaxQuery>)`, nesting the same way `Clause::Boolean` already does (arbitrary depth, and a `DisjunctionMaxQuery` disjunct may itself be a `Term`/`Phrase`/`Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax`). Matching is a pure union (`resolve_dismax_docs`, built on the same `Disjunction` merge combinator `BooleanQuery`'s `should`-only case uses) — unlike `BooleanQuery.should`, there is no `minimum_should_match`-style gate; a `DisjunctionMaxQuery` with zero disjuncts matches nothing, mirroring `BooleanQuery`'s own empty-clause-list rule. Scoring (`dismax_scores`) implements real `DisjunctionMaxScorer`'s exact formula, not an approximation: a matching doc's score is `max(disjunct scores) + tie_breaker * sum(every other matching disjunct's score)`, computed via the same recursive per-clause `clause_scores` a `Clause::Boolean` disjunct already uses (so a nested `Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax` disjunct scores correctly to arbitrary depth). `tie_breaker == 0.0` (real `DisjunctionMaxQuery(Collection<Query>)`'s single-arg constructor default) degenerates to pure max-of-disjuncts scoring. **Verified against real Lucene**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/dismax_query_fixtures.rs` reuses the checked-in `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture and its already-fixture-verified `body` postings (`cat`={0,2}, `dog`={0,1}, `bird`={1,4}), plus a genuine `IndexSearcher.search(new DisjunctionMaxQuery(...), 10)` run against that exact on-disk segment (`fixtures/src/AppendDismaxManifest.java`, appended to `manifest.properties` as `dismax.*` keys without perturbing the segment's already-committed random ID) asserting doc-for-doc, score-for-score agreement with real Lucene's own recorded `TopDocs` output — true cross-engine verification of the tie-breaker formula, not just self-consistency. Unit tests in `lib.rs` additionally cover: empty-disjunct-list, a missing-term disjunct contributing nothing, exact tie-breaker arithmetic, a doc matching only one disjunct getting exactly that disjunct's score, and nesting in both directions (`DisjunctionMax` inside `Boolean`, `Boolean` inside `DisjunctionMax`, `DisjunctionMax` inside `DisjunctionMax`). **Deferred, same as `BooleanQuery`**: `lucene-ffi` C-ABI construction of a `Clause::DisjunctionMax` disjunct (the FFI wire format still only builds flat `Clause::Term` clauses). |
| `search/ScoreMode` | `lucene-search/src/collector.rs::ScoreMode` | **ported (b12 sweep, M2).** All five variants with both predicates, matching Java's `(isExhaustive, needsScores)` constructor table exactly. `ScoringCollector::score_mode()` defaults to `Complete` -- the conservative answer, which forbids pruning -- so a collector that has not declared a mode cannot accidentally authorize an early exit; `TopDocsCollector` reports `TopScores` unless its total-hits threshold is `u64::MAX`. `isExhaustive()` is honored end to end: an exhaustive collector publishes no competitive score, so `search_term_query_scored_maxscore` skips no blocks, proven by the crate's block-skip counter rather than by results (which are identical either way). **`needsScores() == false` is modelled but cannot yet be acted on**: `lucene_codecs::postings` has no `PostingsEnum`-flags plumbing, so frequencies are decoded even for a clause that never reads them, where `Lucene104PostingsReader` `PForUtil.skip`s the whole block. That half is the `PostingsEnum`-flags carry-over in `docs/sweep/m2/LEDGER.md`. |
| `search/TotalHits`/`TotalHits.Relation` | `lucene-search/src/collector.rs::{TotalHits, TotalHitsRelation}` | **ported (b12 sweep, M2).** Value plus relation, including Java's `"1000+ hits"` `toString`. `TopDocsCollector::total_hits()` counts every document handed to `collect` (not the kept ones, which are capped at `top_n`) and reports `GreaterThanOrEqualTo` from the moment a competitive threshold is published, since documents can be skipped uncounted from then on. `TopDocsCollector::with_total_hits_threshold` reproduces `TopScoreDocCollectorManager`'s `totalHitsThreshold`, which delays that publication so the count is exact up to the threshold. Before this the port reported no count at all, and the collector's doc comment claimed there was no early termination -- untrue since MAXSCORE landed. |
| `search/MultiPhraseQuery`/`MultiPhraseWeight`/`UnionFullPostingsEnum` | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{MultiPhraseQuery, Clause::MultiPhrase}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{search_multi_phrase_query, search_multi_phrase_query_scored, search_multi_phrase_query_scored_with_stats}` | **ported (b12 sweep, M2).** Every position accepts a *set* of terms, merged into one position stream per document exactly as `UnionPostingsEnum.freq()` does -- **sorted and deliberately not deduplicated**, because that method drains every sub's positions and calls `sort()` with no dedup step, so a position reached by two alternatives (the same term listed twice, or two terms an analyzer stacked) is yielded twice and the alignment is counted twice. Deduplicating looks obviously right and is wrong: real Lucene scores `pos:"(alpha alpha) beta"` at 0.87906057 on this port's fixture where the deduplicated form gives 0.6393168, recorded as `scoring.multiphrase.dup`. Scoring follows `MultiPhraseWeight`: the idf is the sum over **every** term of **every** position, against the merged-position phrase frequency. `MultiPhraseQuery.rewrite`'s degenerate cases are reproduced by the executor rather than by a rewrite step -- an empty `term_arrays` matches nothing, and a **single** position is scored as the `BooleanQuery` of `SHOULD` `TermQuery`s that Java rewrites it to (per-term idf and per-term frequency, summed -- *not* one summed idf against a merged frequency; the first cut here got that wrong and real Lucene's recorded scores caught it). Matching and scoring share one implementation so they cannot disagree about which documents match. Four cross-engine bit-for-bit tests (`scoring.multiphrase.{union,bothslots,single,slop2}`) plus doc-set-agreement, nested-in-`BooleanQuery`, and degenerate-shape tests. **Scoped down**, same two reductions as `PhraseQuery`: implicit positions only (no `Builder.add(Term[], int position)`), and `slop > 0` inherits the in-order-only sloppy restriction. |
| `search/BooleanQuery.rewrite` | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{BooleanQuery::rewrite, Clause::rewrite}` | **ported, 14 of Java's 14 rules (b12 sweep added 5, c11 added the 4 `Occur.FILTER` ones + the lone-filter single-clause case).** Present: empty / pure-negative -> matches nothing, single-clause unwrap, recursion, `MUST_NOT` dedup, and (new in b12) the `MatchNoDocsQuery` clause short-circuit (`MUST` -> whole query, `SHOULD`/`MUST_NOT` -> dropped), required-and-excluded detection (`MUST` clause also in `MUST_NOT`, and `MUST_NOT` containing `MatchAllDocsQuery`) -> `MatchNoDocsQuery`, nested pure-disjunction flattening (Java: "important for block-max WAND to perform well"), required-clause inlining (Java: "helps run filtered conjunctive queries more efficiently by providing all clauses to the block-max AND scorer"), and the `SHOULD`-count-vs-`minimumNumberShouldMatch` pair (fewer -> `MatchNoDocsQuery`, exactly as many -> all `SHOULD` become `MUST`), in Java's order (the count test must follow flattening). **`MUST`/`SHOULD` deduplication, added by c16** -- and the rationale it replaces was a **version defect** caught by `c18-version-audit`: this row and the code both used to justify skipping it by citing `Similarity.computeQueryTermWeight`, which **does not exist in Lucene 10.5.0** (it is a later addition on Lucene `main`). 10.5.0's rule is a pure structural transform with no `IndexSearcher` and no `Similarity` in sight -- unwrap each clause's `BoostQuery` chain multiplying the boosts, sum per distinct query, and rebuild the bucket only when that collapsed the count, wrapping in a `BoostQuery` iff the summed boost is not `1`; `SHOULD` gated on `minimumNumberShouldMatch <= 1`, `MUST` ungated. It is **score-neutral** (BM25 is linear in the clause sum), which two new `AppendScoringManifest` entries pin bit-for-bit against real Lucene (`scoring.boolean.dupshould`, `scoring.boolean.dupmust`); what it changes is query *shape* -- clause count, `maxClauseCount` pressure and the explain tree. Two stated divergences: this port keeps first-seen clause order where Java rebuilds from an unordered `HashMap`, and a rebuild re-enters `rewrite` (Java returns and lets `IndexSearcher.rewrite` loop to a fixpoint), which is load-bearing because summing `a^0.5 a^0.5` back to a bare `a` can make a later rule newly apply. **`Occur.FILTER` rules, added by c11** now that `BooleanQuery` has a fourth clause list: duplicate-`FILTER` dedup (the `HashSet` half of Java's "remove duplicate FILTER and MUST_NOT clauses"); a `FILTER` clause that is also a `MUST` clause, or that is a `MatchAllDocsQuery`, is dropped -- the latter guarded by Java's `filters.size() > 1 || clauseSets.get(MUST).isEmpty() == false`, because dropping the sole filter of a filter-only query would turn "every document, scored 0" into `MatchNoDocsQuery`; a clause that is both `FILTER` and `SHOULD` is promoted to `MUST` with `minimumNumberShouldMatch` decremented (floored at 0); a single `MUST` `MatchAllDocsQuery` alongside filters collapses to `ConstantScoreQuery(BooleanQuery(FILTER.., MUST_NOT..))` carrying the `MatchAllDocsQuery`'s boost; and a lone `FILTER` clause becomes `BoostQuery(ConstantScoreQuery(q), 0)` (Java: "no scoring clauses, so return a score of 0"). The required-and-excluded rule now tests `MUST_NOT` against `MUST` **or** `FILTER` and carries Java's verbatim reason string `"FILTER or MUST clause also in MUST_NOT"` (it was shortened to `"MUST clause also in MUST_NOT"` while `FILTER` did not exist), and required-clause inlining re-labels an inner `MUST` as a parent `FILTER` when the outer clause was a filter, exactly as Java's `assert outerClause.occur() == Occur.FILTER && innerOccur == Occur.MUST` arm does. **Still not implemented**: `ConstantScoreQuery.rewrite`'s "clauses that are not involved in scoring can get some extra simplifications" step (Java wraps every `FILTER`/`MUST_NOT` clause in a `ConstantScoreQuery` before recursing, reaching `BooleanQuery.rewriteNoScoring`), and "Inline SHOULD clauses from the only MUST clause". |
| `search/ConstantScoreQuery`/`BoostQuery` (matching + scoring, nested `Clause` composition) | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{ConstantScoreQuery, BoostQuery, Clause::ConstantScore, Clause::Boost}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{resolve_clause_docs, clause_scores}` | **ported (task #33).** `ConstantScoreQuery { inner: Box<Clause>, score: f32 }` and `BoostQuery { inner: Box<Clause>, boost: f32 }` each wrap any other `Clause`; `Clause` grows two more variants, `ConstantScore(Box<ConstantScoreQuery>)` and `Boost(Box<BoostQuery>)`, nesting the same way `Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax` already do (either direction, arbitrary depth — a `ConstantScoreQuery`/`BoostQuery` can wrap a `Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax`/each other, and a `BooleanQuery`/`DisjunctionMaxQuery` clause can be a `ConstantScore`/`Boost`). Matching for both is exactly the wrapped clause's own matching set (`resolve_clause_docs`'s `ConstantScore`/`Boost` arms recurse straight into the inner clause, no narrowing). Scoring (`clause_scores`): `ConstantScoreQuery` discards the inner clause's own score entirely and reports `score` for every doc the inner clause matched (real `ConstantScoreWeight`/`ConstantScoreScorer`'s behavior — the wrapped query's score never surfaces); `BoostQuery` multiplies the inner clause's own score by `boost` (real `BoostQuery.BoostWeight`'s exact behavior — a pure rescale, not a replacement). **Cross-engine verification scope**: no new Java fixture generator was added — both wrappers are a trivial arithmetic composition (a literal constant; one `f32` multiply) over an inner clause whose own scoring is already cross-engine-verified against real `IndexSearcher`/`TopDocs` output (task #30's `scoring_fixtures.rs`, task #32's `dismax_query_fixtures.rs`), so there was no Lucene-specific formula or wire format left to get wrong that a new fixture could catch. Instead, `lib.rs`'s unit tests use `search_term_query_scored`'s already-real BM25 score for `body:cat`/`body:dog` at specific docs (from the checked-in `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture) as the known-real inner score, and assert the wrapped score is exactly that constant, or exactly that real score times the boost — verifying this task's arithmetic against a real inner score without a second fixture generator. Unit tests cover: matching-set-equals-inner for both wrappers, a missing-inner-term matching nothing, the constant score exactly overriding two different real inner scores, boost multiplying a real inner score, both wrappers composing inside a `BooleanQuery` alongside a sibling clause (additive total), `BoostQuery` nested inside a `DisjunctionMaxQuery` disjunct, `ConstantScoreQuery` wrapping a nested `DisjunctionMaxQuery`, and `BoostQuery` wrapping a nested `ConstantScoreQuery` (confirming the two compose as `inner_constant * boost`, not independently). **Deferred, same as `BooleanQuery`/`DisjunctionMaxQuery`**: `lucene-ffi` C-ABI construction of a `Clause::ConstantScore`/`Clause::Boost` clause (the FFI wire format still only builds flat `Clause::Term` clauses; `ffi_search_boolean_query_scored`'s field-name-extraction match treats both new variants like `Phrase`/`Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax`, contributing no field name). |
| `search/MatchAllDocsQuery`/`MatchNoDocsQuery` (matching + scoring, `Clause` composition) | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{MatchAllDocsQuery, MatchNoDocsQuery, Clause::MatchAllDocs, Clause::MatchNoDocs}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{match_all_doc_ids, resolve_clause_docs, clause_scores}` | **ported.** `MatchAllDocsQuery { max_doc: i32 }` matches every **live** doc in `0..max_doc`, scored flat `1.0` (real `MatchAllDocsQuery`'s `ConstantScoreScorer` always scores `boost`, `1.0` unless wrapped in a `BoostQuery`/`Clause::Boost` — matching this crate's existing `ConstantScoreQuery`/`WildcardQuery`/etc "flat constant, compose a wrapper for anything else" convention). `MatchNoDocsQuery { reason: String }` matches nothing, ever — `resolve_clause_docs`'s `Clause::MatchNoDocs` arm returns an empty `Vec` directly, no segment lookup at all (`reason` is purely informational, mirroring real `MatchNoDocsQuery(String reason)`'s documented rewrite-provenance string; nothing in this crate inspects it). **Why `max_doc` lives on the query struct, not threaded as a new parameter through `resolve_clause_docs`/`clause_scores`/`search_boolean_query` and the rest of that call graph**: every other leaf `Clause` variant resolves its matched-doc set from a term dictionary lookup (a term's own postings already enumerate exactly the docs it needs) — `MatchAllDocsQuery` is the first clause with nothing to seek into, so rather than a wide, mechanical signature change across every function in `resolve_clause_docs`'s call graph (touching call sites in other crates' tests too) for a value only this one variant needs, the caller supplies `max_doc` once at construction time, the same way it already knows and passes `live_docs` per search call. `match_all_doc_ids` (the shared `0..max_doc` sweep both `resolve_clause_docs` and `clause_scores` call) is the same shape as `doc_value_query::search_numeric_range`'s own `[0, max_doc)` sweep — no dictionary, so a full sweep is the only correct option. `Clause` grows two more leaf variants, `MatchAllDocs(MatchAllDocsQuery)` and `MatchNoDocs(MatchNoDocsQuery)`, usable anywhere a `Clause` is, including nested inside `BooleanQuery`/`DisjunctionMaxQuery`/`ConstantScoreQuery`/`BoostQuery`. **Tests**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/match_all_no_docs_fixtures.rs` reuses the real `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` segment (via `boolean_query_fixtures.rs`'s own manifest-driven open helper) and a hand-built `live_docs` bitset (the checked-in fixture itself has no deletions) to prove: `MatchAllDocsQuery` returns exactly the live doc set with some docs marked deleted (not the deleted ones), and every doc with no deletions; `MatchNoDocsQuery` always returns empty regardless of deletions; `MatchAllDocsQuery` as a `must` clause alongside a real `TermQuery` behaves identically to that `TermQuery` alone; `MatchNoDocsQuery` as a `must` clause makes the whole `BooleanQuery` match nothing; and both compose correctly through `search_boolean_query_scored`/`TopDocsCollector` (`MatchAllDocsQuery` scores every live doc flat `1.0`, `MatchNoDocsQuery` produces zero hits). Plus `query.rs` unit tests for construction/`Clause::from`/builder methods. **Deferred, same as every other `Clause` variant since task #29**: `lucene-ffi` C-ABI construction of a `Clause::MatchAllDocs`/`Clause::MatchNoDocs` clause. |
| `search/TermInSetQuery` (matching + scoring, `Clause` composition) | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{TermInSetQuery, Clause::TermInSet}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::term_in_set_doc_ids` | **ported.** `TermInSetQuery { field: String, terms: Vec<Vec<u8>> }` matches a doc if it contains ANY of the given same-field terms — implemented as a union over each term's existing single-`TermQuery` postings resolution (`field_terms.postings(term, doc_in)` per term, deduplicated/sorted), not a new low-level postings walk. **Scoring, verified against the actual Java `Scorer` code, not just the class javadoc**: real `TermInSetQuery`'s default rewrite (`CONSTANT_SCORE_BLENDED_REWRITE`) builds a `ConstantScoreScorer` whose score is always the fixed boost (`1.0` unless wrapped in a `Clause::Boost`) regardless of how many of the terms a doc actually matched — NOT the max score across matched terms, and NOT a summed `BooleanQuery`-OR score, even though the matching semantics are exactly that of a same-field disjunction. This crate's `clause_scores` arm for `Clause::TermInSet` matches: flat `1.0` per matching doc. An absent term contributes nothing (no error); every term absent, or an empty `terms` list, correctly matches nothing (the union of zero/empty sets is empty, no special-casing needed). **Tests**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/term_in_set_query_fixtures.rs` (9 tests) against the real `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture — any-not-all matching, a 3-term union, absent-term/all-absent-terms/empty-term-set/missing-field (all correctly empty, no error), composition as both a `must` and `should` `BooleanQuery` clause, and a scoring test explicitly proving a doc matching two of the given terms still scores `1.0`, not `2.0` (disproving the summed-should hypothesis). **Deferred, same as `MatchAllDocsQuery`/`MatchNoDocsQuery` above**: `lucene-ffi` C-ABI construction of a `Clause::TermInSet` clause (the two exhaustive field-name-extraction matches in `lucene-ffi/src/{explain.rs,query.rs}` were extended to treat it as fieldless, same no-op treatment those files already give `MatchAllDocs`/`MatchNoDocs`). |
| `search/WildcardQuery` (matching + constant scoring, `Clause` composition) | `lucene-codecs/src/wildcard.rs::WildcardPattern` (escape support), `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{WildcardQuery, Clause::Wildcard}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{wildcard_doc_ids, resolve_clause_docs, clause_scores}` | **ported (task #34).** `WildcardQuery { field: String, pattern: Vec<u8> }` is a leaf `Clause::Wildcard(WildcardQuery)`, matching every doc that has at least one term (for `query.field`) `lucene_codecs::wildcard::WildcardPattern` accepts, **union**ed across every matching term (`wildcard_doc_ids`: `FieldTerms::intersect(&pattern)` enumerates matching terms — reusing task #1's `WildcardPattern`/`intersect` machinery unchanged rather than building a parallel matcher — then every matching term's postings' doc IDs are collected, sorted, and deduplicated, since a multi-valued field can have more than one matching term in the same doc). Scoring (`clause_scores`'s `Clause::Wildcard` arm): a flat, unscored `1.0` per matching doc — real Lucene's `MultiTermQuery` has no single term's frequency/idf to score a multi-term match against by default; a caller wanting a different constant or a rescale already has `ConstantScoreQuery`/`BoostQuery` (task #33) to compose on top, same as real Lucene's own default rewrite chain. **This task also closed a gap task #1 explicitly deferred**: `WildcardPattern::new` now supports `\`-escaping (`\*`/`\?`/`\\` match a literal `*`/`?`/`\`; a trailing unpaired `\` falls back to matching a literal `\`), matching real `WildcardQuery.toAutomaton`'s `WILDCARD_ESCAPE` handling exactly — escaping any other byte is a harmless no-op, same as real Lucene's `default` fallthrough. `Clause` grows a sixth variant, `Wildcard(WildcardQuery)` (a leaf, not a wrapper — it does not nest another `Clause` the way `Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax`/`ConstantScore`/`Boost` do), usable anywhere a `Clause` is (a `BooleanQuery`/`DisjunctionMaxQuery` clause, or wrapped in `ConstantScoreQuery`/`BoostQuery`). **Verified against real Lucene**: `fixtures/src/AppendWildcardManifest.java` (same append-only pattern as `AppendDismaxManifest.java` — opens the already-committed `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` directory read-only, no segment regeneration/ID perturbation) runs eight real `org.apache.lucene.search.WildcardQuery`s against `body`'s real terms/postings (`cat`={0,2}, `dog`={0,1}, `bird`={1,4}) — a literal, a trailing `*`, a `?`, a bare `*` (matches every term), a no-match pattern, an escaped `*` (no term contains a literal `*`, so it matches nothing), an escaped non-special byte (`do\g`, a no-op escape, matches `dog`), and a `?` landing on `bird`'s last byte (`bir?`) — recording real Lucene's own matched doc IDs into `manifest.properties`' `wildcard.*` keys. `crates/lucene-search/tests/wildcard_query_fixtures.rs` re-runs the equivalent `Clause::Wildcard` query for every recorded case and asserts doc-for-doc agreement, the actual cross-engine proof (not a self-consistency check) — all eight cases matched real Lucene's output on the first run. Additional unit tests (in the same file) cover an empty pattern (matches nothing, since `body` has no empty term), pure-wildcard patterns (`**`/`?`), a missing field, and composing `Clause::Wildcard` inside a `BooleanQuery`'s `must` alongside a plain `Clause::Term`. New escape-specific unit tests in `wildcard.rs` cover `\*`/`\?`/`\\`, a trailing unpaired `\`, and escaping a non-special byte. **Deferred, same as every other `Clause` variant since task #29**: `lucene-ffi` C-ABI construction of a `Clause::Wildcard` clause (the FFI wire format still only builds flat `Clause::Term` clauses; `ffi_search_boolean_query_scored`'s field-name-extraction match now also treats `Wildcard` like `Phrase`/`Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax`/`ConstantScore`/`Boost`, contributing no field name). No real `IntersectTermsEnum`/`CompiledAutomaton` trie-block-skipping (unchanged from task #1 — `intersect` is still an `O(n)`-in-field-term-count scan narrowed by a literal-prefix range); no regex/`FuzzyQuery` (still need real automaton/edit-distance machinery, unchanged scope from task #1). **M2 sweep `b8-automata-analysis`: swept clean.** `WildcardQuery.toAutomaton`'s `WILDCARD_ESCAPE` handling, `?` as one codepoint and the lenient trailing-`\` fallthrough all match. The one divergence is intentional and byte-level: `*` here matches any byte sequence, where Lucene's `Automata.makeAnyString` composed with `UTF32ToUTF8` matches any well-formed UTF-8 one. |
| `search/PrefixQuery` (matching + constant scoring, `Clause` composition) | `lucene-codecs/src/wildcard.rs::WildcardPattern::prefix`, `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{PrefixQuery, Clause::Prefix}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{prefix_doc_ids, resolve_clause_docs, clause_scores}` | **ported (task #35).** `PrefixQuery { field: String, prefix: Vec<u8> }` is a leaf `Clause::Prefix(PrefixQuery)`, matching every doc that has at least one term (for `query.field`) starting with `prefix`'s literal bytes, **union**ed across every matching term (`prefix_doc_ids`, structurally identical to task #34's `wildcard_doc_ids` — `FieldTerms::intersect` enumerates matching terms, then every matching term's postings' doc IDs are collected, sorted, and deduplicated). Scoring (`clause_scores`'s `Clause::Prefix` arm): the same flat, unscored `1.0` per matching doc as `Clause::Wildcard`, for the same reason (no single term's frequency/idf to score a multi-term match against; `ConstantScoreQuery`/`BoostQuery` compose on top if a caller wants otherwise). **Design decision, made explicitly rather than defaulted to**: real `PrefixQuery` is functionally "match every term starting with X," which could be built as (a) a thin wrapper constructing a `WildcardQuery` pattern by escaping `prefix`'s bytes and appending an unescaped trailing `*`, reusing `WildcardPattern::new`'s glob parser, or (b) a direct prefix match bypassing glob syntax entirely. This port takes (b): `PrefixQuery` calls `lucene_codecs::wildcard::WildcardPattern::prefix` directly — a constructor that already existed (task #1, for exactly this purpose) and builds its token list straight from `prefix`'s raw bytes as `Literal` tokens plus a trailing `AnyMany`, **never invoking `WildcardPattern::new`'s escape/glob-parsing loop**. Option (a) was rejected because it would require re-escaping `prefix` byte-by-byte before matching, which is exactly the kind of edge case this task's own scoping called out: a prefix like `a*b` must match every term starting with the 3 literal bytes `a`, `*`, `b`, not be reinterpreted as "`a`, then anything, then `b`" — building on `WildcardPattern::prefix` sidesteps that risk entirely (there is no escaping step to get wrong) rather than mitigating it with careful escaping. **Verified against real Lucene**: `fixtures/src/AppendPrefixManifest.java` (same append-only pattern as `AppendWildcardManifest.java` — opens the already-committed `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` directory read-only, no segment regeneration/ID perturbation) runs six real `org.apache.lucene.search.PrefixQuery`s against `body`'s real terms/postings (`cat`={0,2}, `dog`={0,1}, `bird`={1,4}) — a prefix matching one term (`ca` → `cat`), a prefix matching several (`b` → `bird`), the empty prefix (matches every term in the field), a prefix equal to a full existing term (`cat`, must still match, plus any longer terms sharing it — none exist in this fixture, but the case pins the boundary), a no-match prefix (`zzz`), and a prefix containing literal `*`/`?` bytes (`a*b?c`, no term contains these bytes, so it's a no-match case, but it proves the bytes are matched literally rather than panicking or being glob-reinterpreted) — recording real Lucene's own matched doc IDs into `manifest.properties`' `prefix.*` keys. `crates/lucene-search/tests/prefix_query_fixtures.rs` re-runs the equivalent `Clause::Prefix` query for every recorded case and asserts doc-for-doc agreement, the actual cross-engine proof (not a self-consistency check) — all six cases matched real Lucene's output on the first run. Additional unit tests (in the same file) cover a missing field, a prefix containing literal `*`/`?`/`\` bytes confirmed not glob-interpreted (including a bare trailing `\`, which would trigger `WildcardPattern::new`'s trailing-unpaired-escape fallback if this query mistakenly routed through it), and composing `Clause::Prefix` inside a `BooleanQuery`'s `must` alongside a plain `Clause::Term`. Unit tests in `query.rs` cover `PrefixQuery::new`, equality, the `Clause::From` conversion, and `BooleanQuery::with_must` accepting a bare `PrefixQuery`. **Deferred, same as every other `Clause` variant since task #29**: `lucene-ffi` C-ABI construction of a `Clause::Prefix` clause (the FFI wire format still only builds flat `Clause::Term` clauses; `ffi_search_boolean_query_scored`'s field-name-extraction match now also treats `Prefix` like `Wildcard`/`Phrase`/`Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax`/`ConstantScore`/`Boost`, contributing no field name). No real `IntersectTermsEnum`/`CompiledAutomaton` trie-block-skipping (unchanged from task #1); no regex/`FuzzyQuery` (unchanged scope from task #1). **M2 sweep `b8-automata-analysis`: swept clean.** `PrefixQuery.toAutomaton` is byte-level (`isBinary = true`: the prefix bytes then a `0..255` self-loop), which is exactly `WildcardPattern::prefix`. |
| `search/FuzzyQuery` (matching + constant scoring, `Clause` composition) | `lucene-codecs/src/fuzzy.rs::{edit_distance, FuzzyMatch}`, `lucene-codecs/src/blocktree.rs::FieldTerms::fuzzy_intersect`, `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{FuzzyQuery, Clause::Fuzzy}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{fuzzy_doc_ids, resolve_clause_docs, clause_scores}` | **ported (task #42; `max_expansions` bound added task #221).** `FuzzyQuery { field: String, term: Vec<u8>, max_edits: u8, prefix_length: usize, transpositions: bool, max_expansions: usize }` is a leaf `Clause::Fuzzy(FuzzyQuery)`, matching every doc that has at least one term (for `query.field`) within `max_edits` edit distance of `term`, restricted to terms sharing `term`'s first `prefix_length` bytes exactly, **union**ed across every matching term (`fuzzy_doc_ids`, structurally identical to task #34/#35's `wildcard_doc_ids`/`prefix_doc_ids` — `FieldTerms::fuzzy_intersect` enumerates matching terms, then every matching term's postings' doc IDs are collected, sorted, and deduplicated). Defaults mirror real `FuzzyQuery` exactly: `max_edits = 2` (`FuzzyQuery.defaultMaxEdits`/`LevenshteinAutomata.MAXIMUM_SUPPORTED_DISTANCE`), `prefix_length = 0`, `transpositions = true`, `max_expansions = 50` (`FuzzyQuery.defaultMaxExpansions`, exposed as `FuzzyQuery::DEFAULT_MAX_EXPANSIONS`). **`max_expansions` (task #221)**: caps how many matching terms `fuzzy_doc_ids` expands into postings lookups, closing the previously-unbounded-scan gap flagged for this query (the same untrusted-input/DoS-shaped class of concern task #198's regexp step-budget fix addressed) — `fuzzy_doc_ids` calls `.take(query.max_expansions)` directly on `fuzzy_intersect`'s iterator, which lazily filters candidate terms one at a time as they're pulled from the (already prefix-narrowed) sorted range, so the `take` genuinely stops the term-dictionary walk early rather than collecting every match and truncating afterward. **Selection policy, explicitly scoped down from real Lucene's default**: when more than `max_expansions` terms match, this port keeps the first `max_expansions` terms in **sorted term-dictionary order** (`fuzzy_intersect`'s natural iteration order); real Lucene's default rewrite (`TopTermsBlendedFreqScoringRewrite`) instead keeps the highest-scoring/highest-docFreq terms via a priority queue over the whole match set — replicating that would require scoring every match before truncating, defeating this task's early-exit goal, so "first N in dictionary order" is used instead and disclosed as not equivalent to real Lucene's selection. Scoring (`clause_scores`'s `Clause::Fuzzy` arm): the same flat, unscored `1.0` per matching doc as `Clause::Wildcard`/`Clause::Prefix`, for the same reason. **No `LevenshteinAutomata`/`CompiledAutomaton`/`IntersectTermsEnum` machinery** — this port instead computes edit distance directly with a plain `O(n*m)` DP (`fuzzy::edit_distance`) each time a candidate term is tested (narrowed first to the sorted range starting with `term`'s `prefix_length`-byte literal prefix, the same binary-search-then-linear-filter shape `FieldTerms::intersect` already established for `WildcardQuery`/`PrefixQuery`), closing `wildcard.rs`'s last remaining explicitly-deferred gap from task #1. **Damerau-Levenshtein-with-transpositions, verified against real Lucene's actual default, not assumed**: `edit_distance(a, b, transpositions: bool)` implements the "restricted"/"optimal string alignment" variant — substitution/insertion/deletion/adjacent-transposition each cost 1 edit when `transpositions = true` (real `FuzzyQuery`'s own default), degrading to plain Levenshtein (an adjacent swap costs 2, not 1) when `transpositions = false`. `prefix_length` is an exact-match requirement on the candidate's leading bytes, held entirely outside the edit-distance budget (real `FuzzyQuery`'s `prefixLength` semantics), not a "free" edit region within it. **Byte-vs-codepoint scope decision, stated explicitly**: real Lucene's `LevenshteinAutomata` operates on UTF-32 codepoints (`FuzzyQuery` decodes the target term via `UnicodeUtil.codePointAt`/`codePointCount` before building the automaton, so a multi-byte character costs one edit unit, not 2-4), but this port's `edit_distance` operates on raw bytes — a deliberate shortcut, not an oversight, since this port's terms are `Vec<u8>` with no guaranteed UTF-8 validity and every fixture/test term is ASCII (where one byte and one codepoint coincide, so byte-level distance is pragmatically equivalent here); full codepoint decoding (mirroring `wildcard.rs`'s `utf8_codepoint_len` approach) is deferred as a documented gap for non-ASCII terms, see `fuzzy.rs`'s module doc for the full writeup. **Verified against real Lucene**: `fixtures/src/AppendFuzzyManifest.java` (same append-only pattern as `AppendWildcardManifest.java`/`AppendPrefixManifest.java` — opens the already-committed `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` directory read-only, no segment regeneration/ID perturbation) runs eleven real `org.apache.lucene.search.FuzzyQuery`s against `body`'s real terms/postings (`cat`={0,2}, `dog`={0,1}, `bird`={1,4}) — exact match, single substitution/insertion/deletion, **the single most important case**: a transposition (`"cta"` vs. target `"cat"`, `maxEdits=1`) matching with `transpositions=true` and *not* matching with `transpositions=false` (confirmed against real Lucene: it does, and it doesn't, exactly as this port's own `edit_distance` predicts), `prefix_length` excluding an otherwise-in-range candidate, and `max_edits` exactly-at-vs-one-over the boundary — recording real Lucene's own matched doc IDs into `manifest.properties`' `fuzzy.*` keys. `crates/lucene-search/tests/fuzzy_query_fixtures.rs` re-runs the equivalent `Clause::Fuzzy` query for every recorded case and asserts doc-for-doc agreement, the actual cross-engine proof (not a self-consistency check) — all eleven cases matched real Lucene's output on the first run, including the transposition case in both directions. Additional unit tests (in the same file) cover a missing field, an exact-match case against the real fixture, and composing `Clause::Fuzzy` inside a `BooleanQuery`'s `must` alongside a plain `Clause::Term`. `fuzzy.rs`'s own unit tests cover `edit_distance` directly (identical strings, single substitution/insertion/deletion, the transposition-with-vs-without case, multiple transpositions, a multi-edit case (`"kitten"`/`"sitting"`, distance 3)) and `FuzzyMatch` (max-edits boundary, prefix-length exclusion and zero-requirement cases, a candidate shorter than `prefix_length`, exact match, `literal_prefix` extraction including the longer-than-term clamp case). Unit tests in `query.rs` cover `FuzzyQuery::new`'s defaults (including `max_expansions = 50`/`DEFAULT_MAX_EXPANSIONS`), the builder methods (`with_max_expansions` included), the `Clause::From` conversion, and `BooleanQuery::with_must` accepting a bare `FuzzyQuery`. `max_expansions` truncation itself (task #221) is covered at two levels: `blocktree.rs::fuzzy_intersect_take_truncates_the_lazy_walk` builds a 60-term dictionary all matching one fuzzy pattern and asserts `.take(50)` yields exactly the first 50 in sorted order (plus the untruncated count is 60, and a cap above the total match count is a no-op); `fuzzy_query_fixtures.rs::fuzzy_max_expansions_caps_matched_terms_to_first_n_in_sorted_order`/`fuzzy_max_expansions_default_is_unaffected_when_matches_are_few` exercise the same cap end-to-end through `Clause::Fuzzy` against the real fixture (`max_expansions = 1` keeps only `bird`'s postings out of three otherwise-matching terms; the untouched default is confirmed unaffected when only three terms ever match). **Deferred, same as every other `Clause` variant since task #29**: `lucene-ffi` C-ABI construction of a `Clause::Fuzzy` clause (the FFI wire format still only builds flat `Clause::Term` clauses; `ffi_search_boolean_query_scored`'s field-name-extraction match now also treats `Fuzzy` like `Wildcard`/`Prefix`/`Phrase`/`Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax`/`ConstantScore`/`Boost`, contributing no field name). No real `IntersectTermsEnum`/`CompiledAutomaton`/`LevenshteinAutomata` trie-block-skipping (unchanged scope from task #1/#34/#35 — `fuzzy_intersect` is an `O(n)`-in-field-term-count scan, narrowed by the `prefix_length` literal-prefix range, with an `O(n*m)` DP per candidate); full Unicode-codepoint edit distance for non-ASCII terms (see the byte-vs-codepoint scope decision above). `RegexpQuery` is now ported too — see the next row. **M2 sweep `b8-automata-analysis`: four correctness fixes.** (1) `edit_distance` counted UTF-8 **bytes**; `FuzzyAutomatonBuilder` decodes to `int[]` codepoints first, so substituting one 3-byte character for another is one edit, not three. (2) `prefixLength` was applied as a byte prefix *and* the DP still ran over the whole terms; Java splits the codepoints at `prefixLength` and hands only the **suffix** to `LevenshteinAutomata`, which is a strictly stronger test (a shared prefix can absorb part of an alignment). (3) `FuzzyQuery` is **not** constant-scoring: its default rewrite is `TopTermsBlendedFreqScoringRewrite`, so `FuzzyMatch::boost` now implements `FuzzyTermsEnum`'s `1 - ed/min(len(term), len(query))` similarity and `lucene_search::fuzzy_doc_scores` sums `max(0, boost) * BM25(df_blended, ...)` over the expanded terms, with `df_blended = max` over them as `BlendedTermQuery.rewrite` does. This closes finding P4 in `docs/sweep/findings.md`. (4) `max_expansions` kept the first N terms in term-dictionary order; `TopTermsRewrite.collect`'s priority queue keeps the highest-boost N, ties broken by ascending term bytes. Also added: a banded DP with an early length-difference exit (`edit_distance_at_most`), and `MAXIMUM_SUPPORTED_DISTANCE`, which `FuzzyQuery`'s constructor enforces and this port deliberately does not. **`c43-final-cleanup`** closed the remaining half of the reader-wide-statistics gap: `FuzzyQuery`'s expansion is now computed **across every leaf**, as `TermCollectingRewrite.collectTerms` does (one `TopTermsRewrite` collector driven over `topReaderContext.leaves()`, so the `maxExpansions` queue selects one term set for the whole reader, a term's `docFreq` accumulates across leaves, an evicted term loses what earlier leaves contributed -- Java's own behaviour -- and the `MaxNonCompetitiveBoostAttribute` the pruning loop reads carries from one leaf's enum to the next, with each new `FuzzyTermsEnum` re-tightening from it in its constructor). `FuzzyTopTerms` is that collector; `multi_segment::global_fuzzy_stats` drives it and folds `BlendedTermQuery.rewrite`'s `df = Math.max(df, ctx.docFreq())`; `GlobalStats` became a struct carrying `FuzzyCollectionStats` beside the per-term ones (keyed by the *query*, since a fuzzy clause and a `TermQuery` on the same `(field, term)` mean different things), and `global_boolean_stats` walks `Clause::Fuzzy`. A second defect fell out of the fixture: the boost was multiplied into the finished score where `BM25Similarity.scorer` folds it into the **weight** (`this.weight = boost * idf`), which is one ULP out on any fuzzy hit -- `fuzzy_doc_scores` now evaluates `similarity::do_score(boost * idf, freq, normInverse)`. Ground truth: `fixtures/src/AppendMultiSegmentFuzzyManifest.java` records the rewritten query's own selected terms and boosts, each term's reader-wide `docFreq`, and real `IndexSearcher` `TopDocs` as raw float bits over the two-segment `multi_segment_scoring_index` (`dog`'s `docFreq` is 3 in each leaf and **6** reader-wide); `crates/lucene-search/tests/multi_segment_fuzzy_fixtures.rs` asserts both the selection and the scores, verified to fail (0.400 against Lucene's 0.365) against the per-segment blend. |
| `search/RegexpQuery` (matching + constant scoring, `Clause` composition) | `lucene-codecs/src/regexp.rs::{RegexpPattern, RegexpError}`, `lucene-codecs/src/blocktree.rs::FieldTerms::regexp_intersect`, `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{RegexpQuery, Clause::Regexp}`, `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{regexp_doc_ids, resolve_clause_docs, clause_scores, Error::Regexp}` | **ported (task #43), scoped to a restricted Lucene-regexp syntax subset, not real `o.a.l.util.automaton.RegExp`/`CompiledAutomaton`.** `RegexpQuery { field: String, pattern: String }` is a leaf `Clause::Regexp(RegexpQuery)`, matching every doc that has at least one term (for `query.field`) the compiled `pattern` accepts **in full** (real `RegexpQuery`'s whole-term-match convention — a pattern like `ca` does *not* match indexed term `cat` as a substring), **union**ed across every matching term (`regexp_doc_ids`, structurally identical to task #34/#35/#42's `wildcard_doc_ids`/`prefix_doc_ids`/`fuzzy_doc_ids` — `FieldTerms::regexp_intersect` enumerates matching terms, then every matching term's postings' doc IDs are collected, sorted, and deduplicated). Scoring (`clause_scores`'s `Clause::Regexp` arm): the same flat, unscored `1.0` per matching doc as `Clause::Wildcard`/`Clause::Prefix`/`Clause::Fuzzy`, for the same reason. **Scope decision, made explicitly rather than defaulted to**: no crate in this workspace depends on the `regex` crate (checked — no `Cargo.toml` reference anywhere), so this task had a real choice between (a) a hand-built parser/matcher for Lucene's own restricted `RegExp` syntax, or (b) pulling in `regex` and translating/restricting its semantics to Lucene's conventions. **(a) was chosen**: real Lucene's `RegExp` is deliberately not PCRE/Perl regex (no anchors as zero-width assertions, no lookahead/backreferences, and its own `~`/`&` operators standard `regex` doesn't have), so reusing `regex` directly risked either silently accepting syntax Lucene rejects or needing a translation/validation layer nearly as large as writing a purpose-built parser — continuing the `fuzzy.rs`/`wildcard.rs` "hand-build a small, honestly-scoped matcher" precedent instead of reaching for an off-the-shelf crate. **Exact syntax subset supported**: literal bytes (with `\`-escaping of any byte, mirroring `WildcardPattern::new`'s escape convention), `.` (any single byte), `*`/`+`/`?` postfix quantifiers on the immediately preceding atom, **`{n}`/`{n,}`/`{n,m}` bounded repetition** (also postfix, mutually exclusive with `*`/`+`/`?` on the same atom — added in a follow-up slice after task #43 shipped the rest of this subset; `{0,0}` legally matches zero occurrences, `m < n` or a non-numeric/missing bound is `RegexpError::MalformedRepeat`), `[...]` character classes (literal bytes and/or `a-z`-style ranges, optional leading `^` negation, a trailing unescaped `-` before `]` treated as a literal dash), `(...)` grouping (precedence/quantifier-scope only, no capture), `\|` alternation. **Exact subset deliberately NOT supported, rejected with a `RegexpError` parse error rather than silently mis-parsed**: `~` (complement) and `&` (intersection) — Lucene-specific operators that would need real automaton complementation/intersection, materially beyond this slice's backtracking-matcher scope (see the follow-up task's investigation note below) — and named classes (`\d`, `\w`, POSIX `[:alpha:]`). Anchors `^`/`$` are not special-cased at all outside `[^...]`'s negation position (irrelevant given whole-term matching is already always in effect, matching real Lucene's own treatment of `^`/`$` as ordinary literals in `RegExp`). **Implementation**: `Parser` (recursive-descent, grammar in `regexp.rs`'s doc comment) builds a small AST (`Literal`/`AnyByte`/`Class`/`Concat`/`Star`/`Plus`/`Ques`/`Repeat`/`Alt`); `RegexpPattern::matches` is a continuation-passing backtracking matcher (`node_match`/`concat_match`/`star_match`/`repeat_match`) enforcing whole-term matching by only succeeding when the candidate is consumed to its exact end — same "match predicate over already-materialized `FieldTerms`" shape as `fuzzy.rs`/`wildcard.rs`, not a `CompiledAutomaton`/`ByteRunAutomaton` DFA. `RegexpPattern::literal_prefix()` extracts the pattern's guaranteed leading literal byte run (only from a `Concat` root, stopping at the first non-`Literal` node) for `FieldTerms::regexp_intersect`'s binary-search-then-linear-filter narrowing (same trick `wildcard.rs`/`fuzzy.rs` already use); an alternation or a pattern starting with `.`/a class/a quantifier conservatively returns an empty prefix, falling back to a full-field scan — always *correct*, just not optimized, the same documented tradeoff `wildcard.rs`'s own `literal_prefix` makes for `*foo`-shaped patterns. **Byte-vs-codepoint scope decision, stated explicitly**: same tradeoff `fuzzy.rs` already documents for edit distance — real Lucene's `RegExp`/`Automaton` machinery is codepoint-wide, this module's `.`/class matching is byte-wide, a deliberate shortcut since every fixture/test term and pattern is ASCII (one byte == one codepoint); a multi-byte UTF-8 character under `.` would consume only its first byte here. **Verified against real Lucene**: `fixtures/src/AppendRegexpManifest.java` (same append-only pattern as `AppendFuzzyManifest.java`/`AppendWildcardManifest.java` — opens the already-committed `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` directory read-only, no segment regeneration/ID perturbation) runs real `org.apache.lucene.search.RegexpQuery`s against `body`'s real terms/postings (`cat`={0,2}, `dog`={0,1}, `bird`={1,4}) — exact literal, **the single most important case**: the whole-term-match convention (`ca` against `cat`, confirmed against real Lucene: no match, exactly as this port's own whole-term-match design predicts), a `.` wildcard matching only 3-byte terms, `*`/`+`/`?` quantifiers, a `[...]` class, two-way and three-way alternation, a no-match case, a missing-field case, and (**follow-up slice**) four `{n,m}` bounded-repetition cases — `{n}` exact count (`do{1}g`), `{n,}` unbounded max (`bi{1,}rd`), `{n,m}` both bounds (`ca{1,2}t`), and `{0,1}` behaving like `?` (`do{0,1}g`) — recording real Lucene's own matched doc IDs into `manifest.properties`' `regexp.*` keys. `crates/lucene-search/tests/regexp_query_fixtures.rs` re-runs the equivalent `Clause::Regexp` query for every recorded case and asserts doc-for-doc agreement, the actual cross-engine proof (not a self-consistency check) — every case matched real Lucene's output. Additional tests in the same file cover a missing field standalone, the whole-term-match convention standalone, composing `Clause::Regexp` inside a `BooleanQuery`'s `must` alongside a plain `Clause::Term`, and a malformed pattern (`a~b`, the `~` complement operator — updated from an earlier `{n,m}` example now that bounded repetition is supported) surfacing as `Err(Error::Regexp(_))` rather than panicking. `regexp.rs`'s own unit tests cover every supported construct directly (literal, whole-term-match, `.`, `*`/`+`/`?`, `{n}`/`{n,}`/`{n,m}` bounded repetition incl. exact-count/unbounded-min/both-bounds/zero-min/`{0,0}`/composition with other operators/repeat-on-a-group, classes incl. ranges/negation/trailing-dash-literal, alternation, nested grouping, escapes incl. inside a class) plus every parse-error case (unmatched paren/bracket, empty class, dangling quantifier, malformed repeat — missing close brace, non-numeric bound, empty braces, `max < min`, dangling `{` with no preceding atom, each unsupported operator) and `literal_prefix` extraction (pure literal, stops at first non-literal, stops at a bounded repeat, alternation is empty, leading wildcard/class is empty, empty pattern). `blocktree.rs` adds `regexp_intersect_over_materialized_field` (literal-prefix-narrowed range, no-useful-prefix alternation, whole-term-match no-substring case, out-of-range no-match) over the same hand-built multi-term field `intersect_prefix_and_wildcard_over_materialized_field` already uses. **Deferred, same as every other `Clause` variant since task #29**: `lucene-ffi` C-ABI construction of a `Clause::Regexp` clause (the FFI wire format still only builds flat `Clause::Term` clauses; `ffi_search_boolean_query_scored`'s field-name-extraction match now also treats `Regexp` like `Fuzzy`/`Wildcard`/`Prefix`/`Phrase`/`Boolean`/`DisjunctionMax`/`ConstantScore`/`Boost`, contributing no field name). No real `IntersectTermsEnum`/`CompiledAutomaton` trie-block-skipping (unchanged scope from task #1/#34/#35/#42 — `regexp_intersect` is an `O(n)`-in-field-term-count scan, narrowed by the literal-prefix range, with a backtracking match per candidate); full Unicode-codepoint matching for non-ASCII terms/patterns (see the scope section above). **`~`/`&` explicitly investigated and deferred, not silently skipped**: real Lucene's `RegExp` builds these via `Automaton` complement/intersection over the *entire* language `Operations.complement`/`Operations.intersection` operate on (a full DFA construction, including determinization/minimization for complement — `Operations.complement` explicitly builds a *total* automaton over the whole alphabet before subtracting), which has no cheap backtracking-matcher analogue: this module's matcher only ever asks "can some path consume the input," a question with no natural encoding of "and no other pattern could also consume it" (`&`) or, far worse, "match everything this pattern does NOT match" (`~`, which requires enumerating/complementing over the whole byte alphabet at every position, not just refusing a match). A subtly-wrong hand-rolled complement (e.g. one that only complements at the top level, or one that mishandles a class/group boundary) would be worse than an honest, typed `RegexpError::UnsupportedOperator` — so `~`/`&` remain rejected at parse time pending an actual automaton-based engine, which is a materially larger undertaking (full NFA/DFA construction + determinization) than this module's scope. **Bounded-repeat nesting DoS mitigation**: adding `{n,m}` introduced a new pathological case a backtracking matcher over the prior operator set never had -- small, ordinary-looking nested bounds (e.g. `(a{1,15}){1,15}`) can combine multiplicatively and hang on a long failing input, confirmed by reproduction (a 40-byte all-`a` term against that pattern hung before this fix). `Pattern::matches` now runs every match under a fixed `MATCH_STEP_BUDGET` (1,000,000 node visits, charged in `node_match` itself so it bounds total work regardless of which node shape drives the blowup) and reports "no match" rather than hanging once exhausted -- the same fail-fast direction real Lucene's automaton construction takes (rejecting a pathological pattern up front) rather than exploring it at query time. Regression-tested directly (`nested_bounded_repeat_does_not_hang_on_a_failing_match`). **M2 sweep `b8-automata-analysis`: the parser now implements Lucene's whole `RegExp` grammar**, verified pattern-by-pattern and term-by-term against real `RegExp` + `ByteRunAutomaton` by a new `GenRegexp` fixture. Previously it implemented a PCRE-flavoured subset, and six constructs were **silently different**: `#` (empty language) and `@` (any string) were literals, `"..."` quoted strings were literals, `<n-m>` numeric intervals were literals, `&` intersection and `~` were hard parse errors (`~` is an ordinary character under `RegExp.ALL`, since `DEPRECATED_COMPLEMENT` is not in it), `\d \D \s \S \w \W` were unsupported, `()` was not the empty string, and Lucene's leniencies -- a leading `*`/`{`/`\|`/`&` as an ordinary character, stacked quantifiers (`a**`) -- were errors. `.` and `[...]` are now codepoint-wide rather than byte-wide, matching the `UTF32ToUTF8` conversion, and an ill-formed UTF-8 term matches nothing, as it does in Lucene. Error variants now mirror `RegExp`'s `IllegalArgumentException` messages one-for-one, positions included. Still unported: the `CASE_INSENSITIVE`/`CASE_INSENSITIVE_RANGE` *match* flags, which `RegexpQuery(Term)` never sets. |
| `queries/spans/SpanTermQuery`/`SpanNearQuery`/`SpanOrQuery` (matching + flat scoring, `Clause` composition) | `lucene-search/src/query.rs::{SpanQuery, Clause::Span}`,
