| `index/{NumericDocValuesFieldUpdates,BinaryDocValuesFieldUpdates,ReadersAndUpdates.writeFieldUpdates,SegmentDocValuesProducer}` + `IndexWriter.updateNumericDocValue`/`updateBinaryDocValue` (doc-values field updates, end to end: resolve, write a new generation of `Lucene90DocValuesFormat` files, record it, read it back) | `lucene-index/src/field_updates.rs`, `lucene-codecs/src/doc_values_updates.rs::{merge_numeric_column, merge_binary_column, write_numeric_generation, write_binary_generation}`, `lucene-index/src/index_writer.rs::write_doc_values_update_generation`, `lucene-search/src/directory_reader.rs::SegmentReader::doc_values_for_field`; the older overlay encoding (`write_numeric_updates`/`read_numeric_updates`/`numeric_value_with_generations`, +BINARY) survives only as `lucene-search/src/soft_deletes.rs`' standalone marking serialization | **ported (task #54, extended to N generations in a follow-up pass) -- unblocks task #48's documented write-side gap.** Real Lucene marks a doc's doc-values field with a new value without rewriting the whole segment's `.dvd`/`.dvm` triple by appending a small "generation" file of sparse `(docId, value)` deltas (`NumericDocValuesFieldUpdates`/`FieldUpdatesWriter`), applied as an overlay over the base doc-values at read time, with `SegmentCommitInfo.docValuesGen` tracking which generations exist and newest-generation-wins when the same doc is touched across many rounds. This port implements the core mechanism for **any number** of sequential overlay rounds: `write_numeric_updates` encodes a sparse `(docId -> newValue)` map (sorted, de-duplicated keeping the last write per doc) into a small standalone file per generation, reusing this crate's `codec_util` header/footer/CRC conventions for structural integrity; `read_numeric_updates` decodes each generation's file back to a `HashMap<i32, i64>`, validating strictly-ascending doc ids; `numeric_value_with_updates` composes a single already-decoded overlay map with the existing base `doc_values::numeric_value` decode (overlay winning when present), and `numeric_value_with_generations` generalizes that to a whole ordered chain of overlay maps (oldest-to-newest, matching `SegmentCommitInfo.docValuesGen`'s own counter order), checking newest-to-oldest so a later generation's write always overrides an earlier one for the same doc, falling through past generations that didn't touch a given doc, and finally falling back to the base decode. **Byte format is this port's own invention, not a real-Lucene port**: there is no checked-in fixture for `NumericDocValuesFieldUpdates`' actual on-disk generation-file bytes (same honest situation as task #49/#52's index-sort format), and there's no plan to derive one either -- only the header/footer/CRC shell reuses `codec_util`, the field layout in between is invented for this task and documented as such in the module's own doc comment; layering multiple generations reuses that same per-generation format unchanged, one file per generation, composed only at read time. **Scope decision, still explicit**: `SegmentCommitInfo`/`.si` `docValuesGen` metadata wiring (tracking which generation number each file corresponds to, and `IndexWriter` actually writing a new generation against a committed segment) is deliberately deferred -- this is "the update-overlay mechanism itself works and is correct across arbitrarily many chained rounds", not the full commit-lifecycle integration; callers are responsible for keeping the slice of decoded overlay maps passed to `numeric_value_with_generations` in ascending-generation order themselves. **Wired into task #48's soft-deletes flow**: `mark_soft_deleted_via_overlay` marks a doc soft-deleted by writing *only* an overlay file (any value, per task #48's presence-based semantics) with zero base-file I/O; `is_soft_deleted_with_overlay`/`effective_live_docs_with_overlay` extend task #48's existing presence check and combined-bitset computation to consult the overlay first, falling back to the base decode -- the concrete, real "incremental update without full rewrite" property task #48's own write-side gap called for (still single-generation at this integration point; not yet upgraded to consume a generation chain). **Verified**: unit tests cover overlay round-trip (including unsorted input and duplicate-doc last-write-wins), an empty overlay round-tripping to an empty map, wrong-segment-id and truncated-file rejection, and a hand-built out-of-order-doc-ids rejection (exercising the validation path no honest writer output can reach); overlay-vs-base composition tests cover overlay-overrides-base, base-fallback-when-absent-from-overlay, and empty-overlay-is-a-no-op, all against a base numeric field built through the real `write_single_dense_numeric_field`/`parse_meta` round trip (not hand-rolled bytes). Multi-generation layering is covered by dedicated unit tests: three chained generations all touching the same doc (newest wins), overlapping-but-distinct doc sets across three generations (each doc resolves to its own newest write), a doc untouched by any generation (falls back to base), an empty generation chain (degenerates to plain base decode), a doc whose newest-touching generation isn't the chain's last entry (falls through past an unrelated later generation to an earlier one), and a test proving the chain composes with the *unmodified* `write_numeric_updates`/`read_numeric_updates` per-generation I/O (each generation really is its own standalone file). The soft-deletes integration is verified against the real checked-in `fixtures/data/doc_values_index/` sparse fixture (task #4/#21/#48's own real-Lucene-written bytes, docs 0/2/4 soft-deleted in the base, 1/3 not): marking doc 3 soft-deleted via `mark_soft_deleted_via_overlay` alone (no base rewrite) makes `is_soft_deleted_with_overlay`/`effective_live_docs_with_overlay` correctly exclude it while the plain base-only `is_soft_deleted` still (correctly) reports it as not soft-deleted, proving the base bytes were genuinely untouched; additional tests cover the empty-overlay-matches-plain-version case and the no-base-soft-deletes-field-but-overlay-present case. **Merge-awareness audit (follow-up task)**: investigated whether this overlay composes correctly with `merge.rs`'s doc-values-aware segment merging. Finding: **there is no production code path to compose in the first place** -- `doc_values_updates` has exactly one consumer today, `lucene-search/src/soft_deletes.rs`'s query-time overlay-aware live-docs check, which never touches `merge.rs`; `IndexWriter` has no `update_numeric_doc_value`-style method that writes an overlay against a committed segment (`SegmentCommitInfo::doc_values_gen` is set to `-1` everywhere and never wired up); and `merge.rs`'s `MergeSource`/`SourceNumericDocValues` (and the sibling BINARY/SORTED/SORTED_NUMERIC/SORTED_SET source structs) carry no overlay field at all -- the merge's numeric-field code path calls `doc_values::numeric_value` directly on each source's base `data`/`entry`, so there is no parameter a caller could even use to plug an overlay in. A new regression test, `merge::tests::numeric_doc_values_update_overlay_does_not_survive_a_merge_today` (`crates/lucene-index/src/merge.rs`), proves this concretely through both features' real public APIs: it writes a real overlay marking doc 0 with a new value, confirms `numeric_value_with_updates` genuinely sees it, then runs an actual `merge_stored_only_segments` call over that same source (necessarily using only the base data, since that's all `SourceNumericDocValues` can express) and confirms the merged segment keeps the **stale** pre-update value -- i.e. if a future task ever wires an overlay-writing update method into `IndexWriter` without also teaching whichever call site assembles `MergeSource`s to resolve each source's "effective" (overlay-folded) values first, updates would be silently dropped by any subsequent merge. **Scope conclusion**: this is not a bug to fix in the current codebase -- there is no real caller today that both writes an overlay against a committed segment and later merges it, so "does merge see the overlay" isn't yet a well-formed question against production code. It is documented here as the explicit boundary any future `IndexWriter`-level update-write API must account for: fold overlays into base values before constructing `MergeSource`s, keeping `merge.rs` itself unaware of update generations. **m2 sweep (b6-docvalues)**: `DocValuesFieldUpdates.reset(docId)` is now supported -- an update that *removes* a doc's value rather than changing it, which `IndexWriter.updateDocValues` reaches whenever a field is passed with a null value (`FrozenBufferedUpdates` line 309, `IndexWriter` line 1770), and which this port could not express at all: every entry was a bare `i64`, so a reset was indistinguishable from "absent from this generation". Entries are now `Option<i64>` end to end (`None` == reset), encoded on disk as a per-entry `has_value` byte at a new `VERSION_HAS_VALUE`; `VERSION_START` files, which predate the byte, still read. A reset in the newest generation that touched a doc shadows both older generations and the base decode, so `numeric_value_with_updates`/`_with_generations` return `Ok(None)`. `lucene_search::soft_deletes`'s overlay-aware checks were updated to match: a reset entry means the doc's soft-deletes value was removed, so the doc is *not* soft-deleted and the base value is not consulted. **Update (c7 sweep, 2026-08-29):** the BINARY half is ported too -- `write_binary_updates`/`read_binary_updates`/`binary_value_with_updates`/`binary_value_with_generations` are `index/BinaryDocValuesFieldUpdates`, with semantics identical to the numeric side's (ascending by doc, last write per doc wins within a generation, newest generation wins across them, a `None` value is `DocValuesFieldUpdates.reset(doc)`). `Some(vec![])` and `None` are deliberately distinguishable, because an empty `BytesRef` is a legal `BinaryDocValues` value while a reset is the absence of one; and the binary format carries its own codec name so an overlay handed to the wrong reader fails the header check instead of decoding garbage. Both are now reachable from the public API: `IndexWriter::update_numeric_doc_value`/`update_binary_doc_value`/`update_doc_values`/`soft_update_document` buffer a `DocValuesUpdate`, and `IndexWriter` writes one overlay file per `(generation, field)` named `<segment>_<gen>_LuceneRustDVU_<fieldNumber>.dvu`, bumping `SegmentCommitInfo.docValuesGen` and recording the file in `dv_update_files` so the deleter, `check_index` and `checksum_verify` all see it. The name is shaped as Java's four-part `segment_gen_codec_suffix.ext` so `IndexFileNames.parseGeneration` reads the generation back out of it. **The consequence of the format still being this port's own invention is now load-bearing and must be stated**: a segment that carries doc-values updates is **not readable by real Lucene**, because `CheckIndex` and any doc-values read of that field would try to open the recorded files as `Lucene90DocValuesFormat` output. A segment without them still is, which is why every `scripts/verify-write-path.sh` case (none of which writes a doc-values update) is unaffected. Closing that needs a generational `.dvd`/`.dvm` writer plus the `docValuesGen` file-naming contract -- the work b6 scoped out of this module in the first place; the semantics above it (which docs, which value, which generation wins, reset vs zero) are ported and tested. **Update (c14 sweep, 2026-08-29) -- the invented format is gone from the index.** `ReadersAndUpdates.writeFieldUpdates` is now ported for real, in the new `lucene-index/src/field_updates.rs`: an update rewrites the updated field's **whole column** through `Lucene90DocValuesConsumer`'s Rust equivalent into a generation-suffixed `_<segment>_<base36 gen>_Lucene90_0.{dvm,dvd,dvs}` triple, writes a `FieldInfos` generation `_<segment>_<base36 gen>.fnm` recording that field's `FieldInfo.docValuesGen`, and records both in `SegmentCommitInfo` (`docValuesGen`/`fieldInfosGen`, `dvUpdatesFiles` **replacing** the field's previous entry rather than accumulating, since a generation is the field's complete column). Each updated field takes its **own** `docValuesGen` (Java advances it inside `handleDVUpdates`' per-field loop) while the round shares one `fieldInfosGen`; a failed round rolls back and advances both next-write counters, as Java's `finally` block does. The read side (`SegmentReader::open`) reads the generational `.fnm` when one exists and resolves each field to its own generation (`SegmentDocValuesProducer`), so a never-updated field still comes out of the base column. **Verified in both directions**: `fixtures/src/GenDocValuesUpdates.java` writes a real three-round-updated index that `crates/lucene-index/tests/doc_values_updates_fixtures.rs` and `crates/lucene-search/tests/doc_values_updates_reader_fixtures.rs` decode value-for-value against Lucene's own answers, and `scripts/verify-write-path.sh`'s new `VerifyDocValuesUpdates` case (19/19) has real Lucene read every document's updated value back out of a **Rust-written** index and run `CheckIndex` on it. The `.dvu` overlay files are no longer written into any index; the encoding remains only as `soft_deletes.rs`' standalone marking serialization and is documented as not-an-index-format. **Closed by c29**: `SegmentReader::doc_values_for_field` is the per-field generation resolution (`SegmentDocValuesProducer.dvProducersByField`), and `lucene-ffi` now has its own — `SegmentHandle::doc_values_for_field`, fed by the additive `ffi_segment_add_doc_values_generation(segment_handle, dir_handle, fnm_name, fnm_suffix, field, dvm_name, dvd_name, dv_suffix)` (the `.liv` pattern: a generation changes without the segment being rewritten, so it attaches to an already-open handle). Every doc-values read in `sort.rs`, `facets.rs` and `range_sort.rs` now resolves the entry **and its bytes together** through it, so an updated field's entry can no longer be decoded against the superseded base file; `doc_value_query::search_numeric_range_sorted_by_field` and `multi_segment::DocValueSegment` grew separate range/sort buffers for the same reason (two fields of one segment need not share a column). Proven against the `GenDocValuesUpdates` fixture, with the pre-fix behaviour asserted as the negative control. **One gap still open**: this port's own `check_index` reads the base `.fnm`, so it never opens a generation column (the generational files' *checksums* are verified, via `SegmentCommitInfo::files()`, and both a Rust-written and a Lucene-written updated index come back clean today -- it is a coverage gap, not a false failure). |
| `index/DirectoryReader.openIfChanged(DirectoryReader)` (NRT reopen against an already-committed `Directory`, not the `ReaderPool`-backed writer overload) | `lucene-search/src/directory_reader.rs::DirectoryReader::open_if_changed` | **ported (task #46), scoped precisely below.** `reader.open_if_changed(dir)` reads `dir`'s latest `segments_N` and compares its generation to the one `reader` was opened from: identical generation returns `Ok(None)`, matching real `openIfChanged`'s "nothing changed" convention (callers keep using their existing reader rather than rebuild it). A changed generation builds a new `DirectoryReader` through a shared helper, `open_at_reusing`, which for each segment in the new commit looks for an already-open `SegmentReader` in `reader`'s own segment list matching on segment name **and** segment id **and** `del_gen`, reusing it in-memory (`SegmentReader::clone_reader`, a plain struct clone -- `BlockTreeFields`/`FixedBitSet` are already `Clone` -- no file I/O) only when all three match, and opening fresh from disk otherwise (`SegmentReader::open`, the same path `DirectoryReader::open_at` already used). `del_gen` is now tracked on `SegmentReader` (a new private field, populated from the owning commit at open time) precisely so a delete-only change on an otherwise-unchanged segment -- same name/id, bumped `del_gen` -- is never mistaken for "safe to reuse": that segment's `.liv`/`live_docs` genuinely differ under the new commit, and serving it from the old reader would silently show deleted documents as live. A merged-away or replaced segment always falls into "open fresh," since its replacement has a different name/id. **Verified**: `open_if_changed_returns_none_when_generation_unchanged` (no writes since open -> `None`); `open_if_changed_reuses_unchanged_segment_and_opens_new_one` (a second `flush_stored_only_segment` call adds segment `_1` under a new `segments_N` -- the reopened reader has both segments with correct `doc_base`s, and genuine reuse of segment `_0` is proven, not assumed, by deleting `_0`'s on-disk `.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm`/`.fnm` files before calling `open_if_changed`: a fresh open of `_0` would then fail with a file-not-found error, so the call succeeding is only possible if the original in-memory reader was reused); `open_if_changed_reopens_segment_whose_del_gen_changed` (the correctness-critical case -- `deletes::apply_deletes` bumps segment `_0`'s `del_gen` with no new segment added, and the reopened reader's `live_docs` reflects the new deletion rather than the original "no deletions" state). **Deliberately scoped down from real Lucene's actual `openIfChanged`**: this is one reader reopening itself, not the `ReaderPool`-wide sharing real `IndexWriter`/`StandardDirectoryReader` provide (there, *every* open `DirectoryReader` in a process benefits from another reader's already-open segments, not just the one being reopened); no `SearcherFactory`/`ReaderManager` warm-up-listener hook runs on newly-opened sub-readers before they're handed back; and there is still no `openIfChanged(IndexWriter)` overload (reading uncommitted flushed segments directly from an in-memory writer rather than a committed `segments_N` on disk) -- that remains PLAN.md §2 item 7's larger, writer-integrated NRT scope, unaddressed here. **Doc-values audit ("NRT reopen after sparse doc-values commits"): found and fixed a real gap.** `SegmentReader::open` never opened a segment's `.dvm`/`.dvd` at all before this audit -- confirmed by reading it end to end and by this module's own prior doc comment, which said doc-values were "deliberately excluded... irrelevant here." Fixed: `SegmentReader` now opens `.dvm`/`.dvd` (present together or not at all, same contract as `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd`, transparently through a `.cfs`/`.cfe` archive when compound), parsed via the unchanged `doc_values::parse_meta`, exposed via new `SegmentReader::field_infos`/`doc_values_meta`/`doc_values_data` accessors that feed directly into the existing dense/sparse-agnostic `doc_values::{numeric_value, binary_value, sorted_ord, sorted_numeric_values}` readers -- no new low-level codec logic. Because these live on `SegmentReader` alongside every other per-segment file `open_if_changed` already reopens-or-reuses, NRT reopen refreshes them for free. One genuine bug found and fixed while wiring this in: the doc-values codec suffix must be derived from the actual `.dvm` file name (like `.tim`'s already is), not hardcoded to `""` -- a real Lucene-written segment (`fixtures/data/compound_index/`) gives doc values their own codec suffix (`Lucene90_<n>`) independent of the postings suffix, and the first cut broke that fixture's existing `compound_file_segment_opens_and_is_queryable` test with a suffix-mismatch error before the fix. **Verified end-to-end through the real `IndexWriter` -> commit -> NRT reopen -> read-back path**: two new tests, `directory_reader.rs::tests::sparse_doc_values_nrt::{nrt_reader_reads_sparse_numeric_field_after_first_commit, nrt_reopen_reads_new_segments_sparse_numeric_values_correctly}` -- the first commits a sparse NUMERIC field (present/absent/present) and reads it back through a freshly opened `DirectoryReader`; the second adds a second commit with a brand-new, differently-sparse second segment and confirms `open_if_changed` correctly serves both the reused first segment's original values and the newly opened second segment's own sparse values, with correct `doc_base`s. **Not yet consumed by existing doc-values query code**: `doc_value_query.rs`/`facets.rs` still take raw, separately-hand-loaded `.dvm`/`.dvd` bytes exactly as before this audit -- neither was changed to call the new `SegmentReader::field_infos`/`doc_values_meta`/`doc_values_data` accessors, so today those accessors have no production consumer, only the two new tests above use them directly. NRT-reopen-correctness for doc-values is proven at the `SegmentReader` level; wiring the query-side readers to actually go through it is a separate, still-open follow-up. **M2 sweep b13 -- one CORRECTNESS fix and one PERF fix.** (1) The reuse predicate matched on `segment_name`/`segment_id`/`del_gen` only, where Java's is `delGen == commitInfo.getDelGen() && fieldInfosGen == commitInfo.getFieldInfosGen()` (`StandardDirectoryReader.createOrReuseSegmentReader`). A numeric doc-values update bumps `docValuesGen` and `fieldInfosGen` and leaves `delGen` alone, so every reopen after such a commit reused a reader holding the *previous* generation's parsed `.dvm`/`.dvd` -- the update was permanently invisible to any reader open across it. `SegmentReader` now records both generations and matches on all three. (2) Reuse was a deep copy: `clone_reader` copied the decoded `BlockTreeFields` (FST and all per-field term metadata), `FieldInfos`, live docs and `DocValuesMeta`, and a `del_gen` bump fell through to a full fresh open that re-read and re-decoded every file to change one bitset. Those four members are now `Arc` (Java's `oldReader.incRef()` -- reuse is a refcount bump, asserted via `Arc::ptr_eq`), and a new `reopen_with_new_live_docs` is Java's "only liveDocs changed" branch: shares the core and reads only the new `.liv`, needing nothing from `.si`. Both directions are pinned by tests that delete the segment's own files before reopening, so reuse and non-reuse are each provable rather than assumed. |
| `index/IndexReader.getTermVector(int doc, String field)` (query-facing read API over term vectors) | `lucene-search/src/term_vectors_query.rs::{term_vector_for_doc, matched_term_offsets}` | **ported (task #39), a thin adapter -- no new byte-format decoding.** `term_vector_for_doc` resolves a `(doc, field name)` pair to `crates/lucene-codecs/src/term_vectors.rs`'s `(doc, field number)` shape via `FieldInfos` and returns that already-decoded `TermVectorField` (every term with its frequency, and whichever of positions/offsets/payloads that field had turned on at index time -- all already fully decodable per task #3/#26's work, nothing new added here). Mirrors real `IndexReader.getTermVector`'s `null`-return convention: `Ok(None)` for an unknown field name, a doc with no term vectors at all, or a doc missing that particular field; `Err` only propagates straight from `TermVectorsReader::document` (doc ID out of range, or a corrupt `.tvd`/`.tvx` region), the same passthrough convention every other `lucene-search` query function already uses for its codec reader. **Highlighting primitive, precisely scoped**: `matched_term_offsets` computes `(term, start_offset, end_offset)` spans for a set of matched terms in one field -- exactly what a `Highlighter`/`UnifiedHighlighter` needs to slice source text at match boundaries -- but only this one primitive; there is no fragment selection, snippet assembly, or `PassageFormatter`-equivalent, and it returns `None` outright (not a fabricated guess) when the field wasn't indexed with offsets (`has_offsets == false`), since this port never decoded any to begin with. Positions and payloads are equally already-decodable but aren't surfaced through a dedicated function here -- callers read them straight off the `TermVectorField`/`TermVectorTerm` `term_vector_for_doc` returns. **No new fixture generated**: per the `differential-testing` skill's precedent for composition/wiring tasks (#36-38), this task's logic (field-name resolution, error passthrough, span computation) touches no new byte format, so its tests reuse the real, already cross-engine-verified `fixtures/data/term_vectors_index/` fixture (task #3's) rather than generating new Java output. Unit-tested: field resolved by name with real offsets returned; unmatched term yields no spans; unknown field name, a field known in `FieldInfos` but not stored for that doc, and a doc with no term vectors at all all return `None`; an out-of-range doc ID surfaces as `Err`; a field without offsets makes `matched_term_offsets` return `None`. **Deliberately out of scope**: multi-segment federation (this function still takes one already-opened segment's `TermVectorsReader` directly; task #45's `DirectoryReader` doesn't open term-vectors files at all -- `OpenSegment` has no term-vectors field, see that row above). The write side's offsets/payloads gap this row used to note here has since been closed by a later task (`write_best_speed` now supports all three) -- unrelated to this read-side task either way, tracked in the write-side row above. **Fragment selection/snippet assembly, the one thing this row's own note above deferred, is now addressed by task #56 -- see the next row.** **Swept in `b14-search-features` (m2): CORRECTNESS fix.** `matched_term_offsets` sorted by `start_offset` alone; real `OffsetsEnum.compareTo` orders by start offset, then end offset, then term, which is what makes the result deterministic when two terms start at the same offset (the synonym/overlapping-token case). Now sorts on all three keys. |
| `search/{KnnFloatVectorQuery,KnnByteVectorQuery,AbstractKnnVectorQuery,AcceptDocs,VectorScorer}` + `search/knn/{KnnCollectorManager,TopKnnCollectorManager,KnnSearchStrategy}` + the dispatch half of `codecs/lucene99/Lucene99HnswVectorsReader.search` | `lucene-search/src/vector_query.rs::{KnnFloatVectorQuery, KnnByteVectorQuery, VectorsInput, KnnSegment, search_knn_float_vector_query, search_knn_byte_vector_query, search_knn_float_vector_query_multi_segment, search_knn_byte_vector_query_multi_segment, search_knn_float_vector_query_multi_segment_concurrent, search_knn_byte_vector_query_multi_segment_concurrent, per_leaf_top_k, accept_bitset}` | **ported (M2 sweep `c16-knn-query`), single-segment and multi-segment, unfiltered and filtered.** The *query-level* layer over c5's codec layer: which collector size, which accept set, and graph-walk-versus-exact. `TopKnnCollector`/`AbstractKnnCollector` are **not** re-ported here -- they are `lucene_codecs::hnsw::KnnCollector` already, as is `VectorScorer` (`hnsw::VectorScorer` + `vectors::{FloatVectorScorer,ByteVectorScorer}`) and the graph walk (`hnsw::HnswGraphSearcher`). **Per-leaf `k` is pro-rata, not `k`**: `TopKnnCollectorManager.isOptimistic()` is `true` in 10.5.0, so `rewrite` wraps it in an `OptimisticKnnCollectorManager` that sizes each leaf's collector at `perLeafTopKCalculation(k, leafMaxDoc/indexMaxDoc)` = `max(1, k*p + 16*sqrt(k*p*(1-p)))` (ported bit-for-bit, float/double split included, because a difference of one moves which documents an approximate search returns), and a second **re-entrant pass** re-searches with a full-`k` collector every leaf whose worst phase-1 hit is still at or above the merged top-`k`'s worst. The merge is this crate's own `multi_segment::merge_multi_segment_scored` (`TopDocs.merge`), not a second copy. **Filtered KNN is ported**: `AcceptDocs` in *ordinal* space (which makes Java's implicit `FieldExistsQuery(field)` conjunct free and `cost()` exact, the same exact `BitSet.cardinality()` Java uses), Java's `cost <= perLeafTopK` exact-search short circuit, the `acceptOrds` graph walk at `visitedLimit = cost + 1`, and its `scoreDocs.length < perLeafTopK` fall-back to `exactSearch`. Deletions take the same `acceptOrds` path Java uses, so a deleted node never enters the collector. The filter *query* is resolved by the caller and passed in as a per-segment bitset (this port has no `IndexSearcher`/`Weight`); `accept_bitset` converts a `resolve_clause_docs` doc-id list into it. **Evidence**: `crates/lucene-search/tests/vector_query_fixtures.rs` reproduces real Lucene doc-for-doc and score-for-score over both `fixtures/data/vectors_index` (80 single-segment queries) and the new `fixtures/data/vectors_multi_index` (four unequal segments; 80 multi-segment queries plus 40 filtered ones), including five queries that can only be answered by the re-entry pass. Two further fixtures (`c21-hnsw-seeded`): `fixtures/data/vectors_seeded_index` (1400/700/700/40 documents at `k = 100`, where a 700-document leaf **with a graph** has `perLeafTopK = 93 < k` and holds a cluster every target sits next to, so the re-entry pass fires on a leaf the seeded searcher actually runs on -- `vectors_multi_index` only ever re-enters its graphless 40-document leaf, where Java ignores the search strategy) and `fixtures/data/vectors_filter_index` (one 1200-document segment with a term dictionary, giving single-leaf filtered ground truth for both of Java's filtered branches and both encodings). **The re-entrant pass is seeded (M2 sweep `c21-hnsw-seeded`)**, as Java's is: `ReentrantKnnCollectorManager` wraps phase 1's per-leaf hits in a `KnnSearchStrategy.Seeded` and `HnswGraphSearcher.search` delegates to `SeededHnswGraphSearcher`, so level 0's beam restarts from the nodes phase 1 reached instead of descending the graph again. The seeds are phase 1's own hit ordinals ascending, which is what Java's `TopDocsDISI` + `MappedDISI` pair computes from its doc ids -- this port keeps them from the walk that already had them rather than re-deriving them. `c21` also deleted `vector_query::hnsw_search`, the ~40-line copy of `hnsw_vectors::search` that existed only because that function had no `acceptOrds`/`filteredDocCount` parameters; it has them now. **Not ported**: `PatienceKnnVectorQuery`, `SeededKnnVectorQuery`, the `*VectorSimilarityQuery` family, `KnnFloat16VectorQuery`, and `TimeLimitingKnnCollectorManager`. **Measured** (4000 documents, `k = 10`, 20 queries per iteration): one segment 5.4 us/query; four unequal segments 38 us/query sequentially -- the fan-out is not the cost, the pro-rata collectors are (30+24+24+5 = 83 slots against one leaf's 10); a selective filter is *cheaper* than no filter at all (7.8 us/query, since every leaf takes the exact scan over <= 10 accepted vectors) and a permissive one costs 52 us/query. The rayon fan-out is a **pessimization at this size** (198 us/query at `k = 10`, 232 at `k = 100` against 128 us/query sequential) -- see `benches/knn_multi_segment.rs` |
| `search/uhighlight/UnifiedHighlighter` + `PassageFormatter` (fragment assembly / text-snippet highlighting; not `BreakIterator`-based sentence detection or term-density passage scoring) | `lucene-search/src/highlighter.rs::{Fragment, FragmentConfig, assemble_fragments}` | **ported (task #56), a real but honestly-scoped MVP -- simplified passage-boundary heuristic, not `BreakIterator`.** Given the original field text and a set of task #39's `TermOffsetSpan`s, `assemble_fragments` slices out fixed-size (`window_chars`-configurable) character windows around each match (or cluster of nearby matches), wraps matches in caller-configurable `pre`/`post` markers (default `<b>`/`</b>`, real Lucene's `PassageFormatter` default), and merges overlapping/abutting windows into one fragment rather than emitting duplicates -- capped at `max_fragments`. **Passage-boundary heuristic, documented as a simplification, not silently pretended sentence-aware**: window edges are snapped outward to the nearest whitespace boundary so a fragment doesn't start/end mid-word, but there is no term-density scoring; fragments are simply emitted in left-to-right document order and truncated at `max_fragments`. **Sentence-boundary snapping, opt-in via `FragmentConfig::snap_to_sentence` (default `false`, prior behavior unchanged)**: when set, a fragment's rendered edges snap to the sentence(s) containing its match(es) instead of the fixed window -- a real but deliberately narrow terminator rule (`.`/`!`/`?` followed, after skipping whitespace, by an uppercase letter or the text's end), **not** ICU/`BreakIterator`-grade Unicode sentence segmentation: no locale rules, no comprehensive abbreviation dictionary. **Extended (still not ICU-grade)**: a small hardcoded English-only abbreviation list (`ABBREVIATIONS` -- "mr"/"mrs"/"ms"/"dr"/"jr"/"sr"/"vs"/"etc"/"inc"/"st"/"prof"/"capt"/"co"/"ltd"/"gen") now suppresses a sentence break after these specific words' trailing `.`, closing the previously-documented "Mr. Smith" false positive (re-tested by `sentence_snap_abbreviation_list_closes_mr_smith_false_positive`, now asserting no break); any abbreviation not on this short fixed list still produces the old false positive (`sentence_snap_unlisted_abbreviation_still_breaks`, using "Cmdr."), so this is a targeted fix, not universal period-suppression. **Found in review**: "st" on the list (for "St." as in a street name) would otherwise also swallow the sentence break after any ordinal number ending in "st" (21st., 1st., 91st., ...) -- e.g. "He finished 21st. She started next." incorrectly merging into one fragment. Fixed by excluding an alphabetic run directly preceded by a digit from the abbreviation check (an ordinal suffix, not a real abbreviation), covered by `sentence_snap_ordinal_number_is_not_mistaken_for_st_abbreviation`. A terminator immediately followed by a closing quote (`"`/`'`/U+201D/U+2019) or paren/bracket (`)`/`]`/`}`) before any whitespace is now recognized as a sentence end too (the closing punctuation is skipped before the whitespace+uppercase check) -- previously e.g. `He said "Stop." Then left.` wasn't recognized as a break at all, since the character right after `.` wasn't whitespace (`sentence_snap_closing_quote_after_terminator_is_recognized`, `sentence_snap_closing_paren_after_terminator_is_recognized`). Decimal numbers ("3.5", "$5.00") were already handled correctly by the base terminator rule and needed no change. **Marker-insertion correctness for multi-match fragments**: `render_cluster` inserts `pre`/`post` around each match within a merged fragment working from the last match backward, so an earlier (leftward) insertion's byte offsets are never invalidated by a later one -- the one part of this logic genuinely easy to get subtly wrong, and covered by a dedicated test with two nearby matches merging into one fragment with both terms highlighted and neither original word corrupted. **Offset-unit finding**: term-vector offsets are decoded verbatim by task #3/#39 and never reinterpreted, carrying whatever unit real Lucene's `Analyzer` wrote at index time (UTF-16 code units in real Lucene); this port's checked-in fixture is ASCII-only so byte/UTF-16/scalar counts are indistinguishable there. `highlighter.rs` treats incoming offsets as Unicode-scalar (`char`) counts (equivalent to UTF-16 for the entire Basic Multilingual Plane) and converts to UTF-8 byte offsets via `char_indices()` before any slicing, so a match spanning a multi-byte UTF-8 character cannot panic even on out-of-range/mis-unitted input (offsets are clamped to the text's char count first). **No new Java fixture needed for the fragment-assembly logic itself** (there is no "real Lucene bytes" to check string-slicing/highlighting against -- correctness here means "produces a sensible readable snippet," checked by direct unit tests), but one test composes task #39/#3's real, cross-engine-verified fixture offsets (`fixtures/data/term_vectors_index/`, doc 0's "text" field: "cat"/"car"/"cat" at char offsets 0..3/4..7/8..11) with the real text those exact offsets denote (`"cat car cat"`, per `fixtures/src/GenTermVectors.java`'s `CannedTokenStream` token list), not a made-up string. Unit-tested: single match windowed/highlighted correctly; two nearby matches merge into one fragment with both terms highlighted; two far-apart matches stay separate; window clamping at text start/end without panicking; `max_fragments` truncation; a multi-byte UTF-8 match; out-of-range/invalid spans (negative, end-before-start, past text end) dropped defensively rather than panicking. **Deliberately out of scope**: term-density passage scoring/ranking, and full ICU/`BreakIterator`-grade Unicode sentence segmentation (locale rules, abbreviation dictionaries) even though a real, scoped-down sentence-boundary heuristic is now implemented (see above) -- real, substantial follow-on work, not silently skipped. ~~`lucene-ffi` C-ABI exposure~~ -- closed by the Highlighter FFI exposure task: see the `lucene-ffi` section's `highlighter.rs`/`results_fragments.rs` row for `ffi_assemble_fragments`. **Swept in `b14-search-features` (m2): CORRECTNESS + MISSING fixes.** (1) `max_fragments` truncated in plain document order, silently discarding the *best* passages whenever they sat late in the text; real `FieldHighlighter.highlightOffsetsEnums` keeps the top `maxPassages` by score and only then re-sorts by offset. Added `PassageScorer` -- a verbatim port of Java's `k1 = 1.2`/`b = 0.75`/`pivot = 87` defaults and its `weight`/`tf`/`norm`/`score` formulas, with each term's whole-document frequency derived by counting its occurrences across every supplied span (what `OffsetsEnum.freq()` reports for spans from `matched_term_offsets`) -- and wired it into selection. `Fragment` now carries `score`, `start_offset` and `end_offset` (char offsets, `Passage.getScore/getStartOffset/getEndOffset`). (2) Marker insertion ran back-to-front and produced nested, interleaved markers for *overlapping* matches; `render_cluster` is now a port of `DefaultPassageFormatter.format`'s forward loop, coalescing any run of overlapping matches into one `pre`/`post`-wrapped span (`while (i + 1 < numMatches && matchStarts[i + 1] < end) end = max(end, matchEnds[++i])`). (3) Added the rest of `DefaultPassageFormatter`: `FragmentConfig::ellipsis` (default `"... "`) with a new `format_fragments` joining non-contiguous passages, and `FragmentConfig::escape` applying Java's exact six-character HTML escaping to content while leaving the markers raw. **Superseded by c12** on the sentence-segmentation point: `sentence_boundaries` is now UAX #29 via `unicode-segmentation`, the specification the JDK's `BreakIterator.getSentenceInstance` implements, pinned against the JDK's own output by `fixtures/src/GenBreakIterator.java`. See the `uhighlight/SplittingBreakIterator` row below. |
| `index/CheckIndex` (standalone segment-consistency verifier, self-check only -- not the differential-testing harness) | `lucene-index/src/check_index.rs::{Check, CheckResult, check_segment, check_directory}` | **ported (task #57), scoped to the cross-file/cross-structure checks this port's current write-side scope makes valuable -- not full `CheckIndex`.** Deliberately *not* built on top of `lucene-search`'s `DirectoryReader`/`SegmentReader` (task #45): those types expose only the curated subset of state a query needs and hide exactly what a self-check must cross-reference (`SegmentInfo.files`, per-field flags, raw `.si`/`.fnm`/stored-fields bytes). Lives in `lucene-index` (not `lucene-search`, which it has no actual dependency on -- every type it composes is already available here), and reuses `crate::segment_infos::read_latest` for the shared "find the latest commit, list its segments" step, then opens each segment's files directly through the same lower-level decoders `lucene-search`'s `directory_reader.rs` itself calls. Each check reports independently as a named pass/fail (`Check { name, passed, message }`), matching real `CheckIndex`'s own per-check `Status` reporting style rather than a single boolean. **Implemented**: every file `.si` lists opens and passes `CodecUtil.checksumEntireFile` (`codec_util::check_whole_file_footer`, a full-payload CRC -- until the c9 sweep this was `retrieve_checksum`, a footer-*shape* check that a mid-file byte flip passed; it doubles as a "forgot to write/list a file" check); `.si` doc_count vs `.liv`'s byte-size-implied word count, derived independently of `si.doc_count` (not by passing it into the decode call, which would make the check vacuous by construction); `live_docs` cardinality vs `SegmentCommitInfo.del_count` (surfaced through `live_docs::parse`'s own `DelCountMismatch`, since that cross-check is already enforced at decode time -- this module reports it under its own named check rather than re-deriving the same arithmetic); `.fnm`'s per-field flags (doc values, norms, term vectors, postings via `index_options != None`) cross-checked in both directions against which of `.dvd`/`.dvm`/`.nvd`/`.nvm`/`.tvd`/`.tvx`/`.tvm`/`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` the segment's file list actually includes (a field claiming a feature with no matching files is flagged, and so is a file group present with no field claiming it); stored-fields reader's own `max_doc()` vs `.si`'s declared `doc_count`; **postings term-by-term re-derivation (revisited from a prior deferral -- see below)**: for every field with postings and every term in its dictionary, walks the term's actual postings via `blocktree::BlockTreeFields::iter_fields` (added by this task) and `DocInput::read_postings`/`postings::singleton_postings` -- the same read-side API `lucene-search`'s query path already uses -- and independently recomputes `totalTermFreq` (sum of decoded per-doc freqs), cross-checking it against the term dictionary's own recorded `TermStats`; each decoded doc ID is also checked for being in-range and strictly increasing (`postings.doc_ids_valid:<field>`), the meaningful proxy for `docFreq` corruption given this decode API's structure (see below for why a plain `docFreq` recount would be vacuous). A `.si` that fails to open/parse short-circuits every other check for that segment (nothing else can be trusted without a valid file list) and reports a single `si.open` failure. **Revisited scope decision**: task #57 originally deferred postings re-derivation with the stated reason "requires walking per-format internals this port's read-side decoders expose in different shapes per format -- genuinely a separate, large task." Re-examined this task: that blocker no longer holds for postings specifically -- `blocktree::FieldTerms::iter()` already yields every `(term, TermStats)` pair, `DocInput::read_postings`/`postings::singleton_postings` already fully materialize a term's `(docID, freq)` pairs (both already exercised by `lucene-search`'s query path, task #45), so only a one-line accessor (`iter_fields`) was missing; no new decode logic was needed. Investigating the decode API surfaced a genuine structural finding, not just a rediscovery: `read_postings`/`singleton_postings` are *parameterized by* the claimed `docFreq` (same as real Lucene's own `PostingsEnum.reset`/`TermState.docFreq`), so `postings.docs.len()` is always exactly equal to the claimed `docFreq` whenever decoding succeeds at all -- a plain recount can never disagree, so it isn't implemented as one; a wrong claimed `docFreq` instead surfaces as an out-of-range/non-monotonic decoded doc ID (`postings.doc_ids_valid`) or an outright decode error (`postings.open`), which is what the implemented checks actually catch. A `docFreq == 1` singleton term stores no per-doc freq on disk at all (`singleton_postings` reconstructs it from the term dictionary's own recorded `total_term_freq`), so `totalTermFreq` re-derivation is honestly vacuous for singletons -- a real format fact, not a port-specific gap. The same vacuity extends to any `IndexOptions::Docs` (freq-less) field with `docFreq > 1`, for a related-but-distinct reason: `blocktree.rs`'s meta parsing sets `total_term_freq = doc_freq` for such fields (no independent stat is ever written) and the postings decoder synthesizes freq `1` for every doc when a field stores no freqs at all, so the comparison there is also always `doc_freq == doc_freq` -- `postings.doc_ids_valid` remains meaningful for these fields regardless, since it depends only on decoded doc IDs, not freqs. **Points-tree (BKD) structural invariants (revisited from the same prior deferral -- see below)**: for every field with points data (`.fnm`'s `point_dimension_count != 0`), walks the field's actual BKD tree leaf-by-leaf via `lucene_codecs::points::PointsReader::decode_leaves` (a new per-leaf accessor this task added, backed by a new `points::Leaf` type, and by actually reading -- rather than skip-decoding -- the multi-index-dim per-leaf bounding box `read_leaf_block` had always parsed past) and checks: every point's packed value (over the index dimensions) falls within the field's own `.kdm`-declared `min_packed_value`/`max_packed_value` (`points.value_within_field_bounds:<field>`, meaningful because the field-level bound comes from `.kdm`, a file entirely separate from the `.kdd` bytes a point's value decodes from); for fields with more than one index dimension, each leaf's own embedded bounding box is itself a subset of that field-level bound (`points.leaf_bounds_subset_of_field:<field>`, skipped for single-index-dim fields since no such per-leaf box exists on disk in that case); and the leaves' decoded point counts sum to `.kdm`'s own declared field-level `point_count` (`points.point_count_matches:<field>`). A field claiming points whose segment is missing one of `.kdm`/`.kdi`/`.kdd` is a single `points.open` failure; a field number `.fnm` claims that has no matching entry in `.kdm` is `points.field_present:<field>`. **Revisited scope decision**: task #57 originally deferred this for the same "separate per-format task" reason as postings, and it's closed here for the same reason that blocker no longer holds -- `lucene_codecs::points`'s existing read-side decoder (already exercised by `lucene-search`'s points range queries, task #199) does all the packed-index/leaf-block parsing; only the per-leaf accessor was missing, no new BKD parsing logic was needed. **Doc-values value-range sanity** was deferred here and closed by the b11/c9 sweeps (see the addendum at the end of this cell). **Vectors-graph (HNSW) structural invariants** were out of scope here because this port had no vector/HNSW write path at all; batch c5 landed one in `lucene-codecs`, and c9 ported `testVectors`/`testHnswGraphs` on top of it (see the addendum at the end of this cell). **No new Java fixture generated for either revisited check**: both are self-consistency logic layered over already-differentially-verified decoders, not new byte-format decoding, so direct unit tests (including deliberately-corrupted inputs) are the right verification method, per the `differential-testing` skill's precedent -- the postings re-derivation negative test builds its corruption via the existing `postings_writer::write_single_field` API (swapping in a term dictionary that claims a different `totalTermFreq` than the real `.doc` bytes it's paired with actually sum to); the points-tree negative tests build theirs via `lucene_codecs::points::write` the same way (swapping in `.kdd` bytes decoded from a different point set/count than the paired `.kdm`/`.kdi` declare) -- neither needs raw byte mutation. Unit-tested: the real `blocktree_index`, `live_docs_index`, and `points_index` fixtures (the latter covering a single-index-dim field, a 2-dim field, and a 4-dim/2-index-dim "shape" field) pass every check cleanly; deliberately corrupted cases confirm each failure mode reports clearly rather than panicking or false-passing -- a hand-mutated `SegmentCommitInfo.del_count`, a truncated `.liv` file, a `.si` listing a nonexistent file, a `.si` that won't parse at all, a `.liv` whose byte size disagrees with `si.doc_count`, a file with a corrupted footer, a partial `.fdt`/`.fdx`/`.fdm` file set, a `si.doc_count` that disagrees with a real stored-fields reader's `max_doc()`, a term dictionary whose recorded `totalTermFreq` disagrees with its actual postings' summed freqs, a term dictionary whose real postings decode to an out-of-range/non-monotonic doc ID while `totalTermFreq` still agrees, a segment with postings but no `.doc` file, a BKD point value decoded outside the field's declared bounds while its leaf's decoded point count still agrees, a BKD leaf whose actual decoded point count disagrees with `.kdm`'s declared `point_count` while its values still stay in bounds, a mismatched points field number, a missing points file, and a compound-file segment correctly skipping points checks entirely. Coverage: `lucene-index/src/check_index.rs` (96.9% lines) and `lucene-codecs/src/points.rs` (99.7% lines), `cargo llvm-cov --fail-under-lines 95` passing workspace-wide. **b11 sweep (2026-08-28) greatly widened the check set** after a method-by-method comparison with `CheckIndex.java`. Added: `testStoredFields`' real per-document scan (every doc decoded, not just `max_doc` compared); `testTermVectors` (doc count, every doc decoded, per-doc fields cross-checked against `.fnm`'s `storeTermVectors`); `testDocValues` (every field's every per-doc value decoded from `.dvd`, SORTED/SORTED_SET ordinals bounds-checked against the terms dictionary, SORTED_SET ordinals checked strictly increasing per doc, BINARY lengths against `.dvm`'s min/max, decoded docs-with-value against `numDocsWithField`); `testSort` (a segment declaring an `indexSort` really is in that order, via the sort fields' doc values and `segment_info::SortKeyComparator`); `checkSoftDeletes` (live docs carrying the soft-deletes field vs `softDelCount`); `testPostings`' term/field invariants (terms strictly increasing, `docFreq > 0`, and `.tmd`'s `numTerms`/`sumDocFreq`/`sumTotalTermFreq`/`docCount`/`minTerm`/`maxTerm` all re-derived); and the `SegmentInfos.readCommit` cross-`.si` validations this port's `segment_infos::parse` structurally cannot do (`delCount`/`softDelCount` vs `maxDoc`, their sum, `delCount == 0` without a delete generation, segment version vs `minSegmentLuceneVersion`/`indexCreatedVersionMajor`, `minVersion` present, total docs vs `IndexWriter.MAX_DOCS`, unique segment names, `SegmentInfos.counter` ahead of every segment name). `check_directory` now returns a leading commit-level `CheckResult` before the per-segment ones. The file set checked is now `SegmentCommitInfo.files()` (adds `.liv` and generational field-infos/doc-values-update files), plus a new `si.files_lists_itself` check. Still unchecked: HNSW/vector graphs (no write path exists) and norms *values*.  **Widened by the `b11-index-meta` and `c9-check-index` sweeps, which walked `CheckIndex.java` method by method; three statements above are now out of date and are corrected here.** (1) The per-file check is no longer `codec_util::retrieve_checksum` (footer *shape* only) but `codec_util::check_whole_file_footer` -- `CodecUtil.checksumEntireFile`, the same helper `merge.rs` runs before a byte-copy merge and `checksum_verify.rs` uses -- so a byte flipped in the middle of any segment file is now detected. (2) Doc-values value-range sanity is no longer deferred: `doc_values.entry_present`/`values_decode`/`ords_dense`/`terms_sorted` decode every field's every per-doc value, bounds-check SORTED/SORTED_SET ordinals, require the ordinal space to be hole-free and the ordinal-to-term dictionary to be strictly increasing, and check BINARY lengths and `numDocsWithField`. (3) Vectors are no longer "not a checker gap at all": batch c5 landed real `Lucene99FlatVectorsFormat` and `Lucene99HnswVectorsFormat` in `lucene-codecs`, which is the layer this module reads through, so `testVectors` and `testHnswGraphs` are ported (`vectors.dimension_positive`/`field_entry_matches_fnm`/`values_decode`/`ord_to_doc`, `hnsw.nodes_in_range`/`neighbors_on_level`/`neighbors_sorted`/`entry_point_reachable`) and verified against the real Java-written `fixtures/data/vectors_index`; batch c10 then wired `IndexWriter` to write vector fields, so `check_index`'s vector blocks now run over segments this port produced as well as Java-written ones (`our_own_check_index_passes_over_a_writer_produced_vector_segment`). Also now performed: `testFieldNorms` (every norm value, plus `checkFields`' terms-vs-norms cross-check), `checkFields`' positional/offset ordering, term-statistic bounds, seek-and-reseek consistency, `Terms.intersect` agreement with a linear scan, and skip-data agreement; `testTermVectors`' slow-level vectors-vs-inverted-index cross-check; `testStoredFields`' full document scan; `testPoints`' `docCount` half; `testSort`; `checkSoftDeletes`; and `SegmentInfos.readCommit`'s cross-`.si` validations. Counts are cross-checked against real Java `CheckIndex -level 3` output on five fixtures. The Java checks that remain unported are each named with their reason in `docs/sweep/m2/c9-check-index.md` -- almost all are agreements between two APIs where this port has one. **c15 sweep**: `checkDocValueSkipper` is ported (`doc_values.skipper:<field>`) -- the `.dvs` skip index's global value range, `maxValueCount`, per-interval start, per-level doc ranges and per-level value ranges nested inside the global one, and the level-0 doc counts summing to the declared `docCount`. It was c9's finding 11, recorded there because the skipper's `advance`-shaped read API did not exist yet; b6 has since ported `DocValuesSkipper` with Java's exact state machine. Also added: `norms.entries_name_real_norms_fields`, the `Lucene90NormsProducer.readFields` `FieldInfos` validation (see the `Lucene90NormsFormat` row). Both negative controls re-sign the file's footer over the corrupted bytes, so the semantic check is the only thing that can catch them -- 428 of 574 single-byte `.dvs` corruptions are rejected by the skipper checks, and all 574 passed before this batch. **Two labels in this row are corrected by the c18 version audit, handed to c15**: (a) `testVectors` is a real 10.5.0 check and is ported, but the `hnsw.*` checks are **not** a port of `testHnswGraphs` -- 10.5.0's `CheckIndex` has no HNSW check at all (`testHnswGraphs`/`testHnswGraph` were added to Lucene after the pinned tag), so they are this port's own diagnostic addition, kept because they catch real graph corruption and because Lucene's later equivalent settled on the same three properties; (b) the module's "still out of scope" list named the `Float16` vector encoding, which does not exist in 10.5.0's `VectorEncoding` (`BYTE` and `FLOAT32` only). Both corrected in `check_index.rs`'s module and function docs; no behaviour changed. **c19 sweep**: `vectors.dimension_positive` is **withdrawn** -- Java's `dimension <= 0` guard is unfalsifiable in Java as well as here (`hasVectorValues()` *is* `vectorDimension > 0`, and `FieldInfo`'s constructor rejects a negative one), so it could only ever pass; c9 finding 9 already established this module does not ship such checks. Five negative controls (`.doc`/`.tip`/`.nvm`/`.vex`/`.dvd`) now re-sign the file's footer over each corruption and assert a *measured floor* plus an isolation case instead of `caught > 0` -- 85/99 `.nvm`, 44/99 `.tip`, 138/318 `.vex`, 18/99 `.dvd` and 5/2034 `.doc` corruptions are rejected by the named check, with the `.doc` control additionally pinning that only 3 of 2 034 are accepted by every check. Two arithmetic hardening fixes in this file (the `.nvm` docs-with-field region's `offset + length`, and `checkDocValueSkipper`'s `maxDocID(0) + 1`) and the whole file now carries `#![deny(clippy::arithmetic_side_effects)]` -- see `docs/arithmetic-gate.md`. |
| `index/CheckIndex` fast checksum-only mode (real Lucene's `-fastCheck`-style footer-only pass, distinct from the full structural verifier above) | `lucene-index/src/checksum_verify.rs::{FileCheck, VerifyReport, verify_directory}`, CLI wrapper `lucene-index/examples/verify_segment_checksums.rs` | **ported (task #217).** A separate, deliberately lighter-weight companion to the `CheckIndex` row above, not a duplicate of it: opens the latest commit via `segment_infos::read_latest`, then for every file every segment's `.si` lists, re-reads the whole file and recomputes its CRC-32 via `codec_util::check_whole_file_footer` (`CodecUtil.checksumEntireFile`, a full-payload recompute, not `retrieve_checksum`'s shape-only check) -- catches bit-rot/truncation/corruption in file payloads, nothing about encoding-logic correctness. Does none of the structural cross-checks the full `CheckIndex` port does (no `.si`-vs-`.liv` doc-count comparison, no `.fnm`-flags-vs-files, no postings/points re-derivation) -- that scope narrowness is the point: it's meant as a cheap, fast pre-flight (or a tight-loop/every-commit check) ahead of the full verifier, not a replacement for it. Reports per-file pass/fail plus a summary (`VerifyReport::{total, failed_count, all_passed, failures}`); the example binary is a thin CLI wrapper exiting non-zero when any file fails or the commit can't be read, exit 0 when everything passes. Unit-tested against a real 2-segment commit written via `segment_writer::flush_stored_only_segment` (the same fixture shape as `write_multi_segment_commit_fixture.rs`): a clean directory passes every file; a single deliberately flipped payload byte (not the footer itself) inside a real `.fdt` file is caught with a "checksum failed" message; a missing file and a truncated file are each reported as a failure rather than panicking; a directory with no commit is a top-level `Err`. Coverage: `lucene-index/src/checksum_verify.rs` (95.65% lines). **b11 sweep (2026-08-28)**: previously verified only `SegmentInfo.files`, silently skipping every file recorded on the *commit* -- the `.liv`, generational `.fnm` and doc-values-update files, i.e. exactly the files a delete/update round writes last -- and the `segments_N` file itself. Now walks `SegmentCommitInfo::files()` plus the commit file.  **c9 sweep**: `verify_file` was a third hand-rolled copy of the seek-to-footer-then-`check_footer` sequence; it now calls the shared `codec_util::check_whole_file_footer` (`CodecUtil.checksumEntireFile`), the same helper `merge.rs` runs on a merge source before a byte-copy and that `check_index`'s `file:*` checks were switched to. Behaviour unchanged -- it was already a full-payload CRC -- so this is de-duplication, and the parenthetical above about the `CheckIndex` row using a shape-only check is no longer true of that row either. |
| `search/LRUQueryCache` (per-segment matching-docs bitset cache) | `lucene-search/src/query_cache.rs::QueryCache`, `query_cache.rs::search_term_query_cached` | **new standalone primitive, not a byte-for-byte port of `LRUQueryCache.java`, now wired into one real search entry point (`TermQuery` only) via an opt-in cached wrapper -- the underlying `search_term_query`/`QueryCache::get_or_compute` logic itself is unchanged.** Real `LRUQueryCache` tracks per-segment `IndexReader.CacheKey` identity via weak references, bounds itself by both entry count and estimated RAM usage, and applies a `shouldCache` heuristic to skip caching queries that aren't worth it; none of that is implemented. This module keeps only the core value proposition: given a `(segment, query)` pair, hand back a previously computed `FixedBitSet` of matching doc IDs instead of recomputing it. `QueryCache<S, Q>` is generic over any segment identifier `S: Eq + Hash + Clone` (this port has no `IndexReader.CacheKey`-style segment identity object yet, so a caller-supplied key such as a segment name or generation number stands in) and any query representation `Q: Eq + Hash + Clone`; `query::TermQuery` picked up an additive `Hash` derive (alongside its existing `PartialEq + Eq`) so it can be used directly as `Q`. API: `QueryCache::new(max_entries)`, `get_or_compute(segment, query, || -> FixedBitSet)`, `invalidate_segment(&segment) -> usize`, `remove(&segment, &query) -> bool`, `clear()`, `len()`/`is_empty()`. Eviction is bounded by entry count only (no RAM-based sizing), least-recently-used-first, tracked via a monotonic access counter rather than an external LRU-list crate. No `unsafe` (`lucene-search` is `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`). Unit-tested (`QueryCache` itself): cache miss computes and stores; cache hit reuses the stored bitset without recomputing (verified via a call counter); distinct queries against the same segment get distinct entries; the same query against different segments get distinct entries; inserting past `max_entries` evicts the correct least-recently-used entry; `invalidate_segment` removes only that segment's entries, leaving other segments untouched; a `max_entries == 0` cache never actually retains anything; `clear()` empties every entry; `TermQuery` used end-to-end as a concrete `Q`. **Wired entry point**: `search_term_query_cached(cache, segment, fields, doc_in, live_docs, num_docs, query, collector) -> Result<()>` composes `QueryCache::get_or_compute` with the existing, unchanged `search_term_query` -- opt-in, `search_term_query` itself and every existing caller of it are unaffected. Real-fixture-tested (`blocktree_index`, not synthetic data): the same query run twice against the same segment returns identical, correct doc IDs on both calls, with the second call proven to be a genuine cache hit (not a silent recompute) by omitting `.doc` input on the second call -- both fixture terms tested (`docFreq == 2`) require `.doc` input to actually execute, so a real recompute without it fails with `Error::BlockTree`, making an `Ok` result on the second call proof the cache path was taken; a different query against the same segment key, and the same query against a different segment key, are each proven to be genuine fresh misses the same way; `invalidate_segment` is proven to force a real recompute on the next call for that segment, also the same way. **Explicitly deferred**: RAM-based cache sizing, automatic per-segment invalidation hooks tied to real segment open/close/merge lifecycle events (`invalidate_segment` exists and is correct, but nothing in this port calls it automatically yet -- a caller of `search_term_query_cached` still invokes it manually), cache-worthiness heuristics like real `LRUQueryCache.shouldCache`, and wiring any query type other than `TermQuery` (in particular `BooleanQuery`, blocked on its `Clause::DisjunctionMax` variant embedding a non-`Eq`/`Hash` `f32 tie_breaker`) into a cached entry point. **M2 sweep b13 -- substantially closer to `LRUQueryCache.java`, and three of the "explicitly deferred" items above are now done.** (a) **Stored representation**: entries are `Arc<CachedDocIdSet>` (see the `ConjunctionDISI` row), applying `cacheImpl`'s 1%-density bitset-vs-`RoaringDocIdSet` rule -- previously a raw `FixedBitSet` sized to `maxDoc`, i.e. `maxDoc / 8` bytes per entry regardless of match count. (b) **`maxRamBytesUsed`**: `with_ram_limit(max_entries, max_ram_bytes)` with exact per-entry accounting and Java's `requiresEviction()` (`size > maxSize || ramBytesUsed > maxRamBytesUsed`) and its insert-then-`evictIfNecessary` order. (c) **Caching policy**: `QueryCachingPolicy` trait plus `UsageTrackingPolicy`, a port of `UsageTrackingQueryCachingPolicy` -- a `FrequencyTrackingRingBuffer` of the last 256 used query hashes and Java's per-shape thresholds (2 costly / 4 composite / 5 ordinary), with `shouldNeverCache` expressed as a `CachingCost` trait implemented for `TermQuery` (Java refuses to cache term queries outright). `leaf_is_worth_caching`/`DEFAULT_MIN_LEAF_SIZE` port `MinSegmentSizePredicate(10000)`. Applied via `get_or_compute_with_policy`. (d) A cache **hit** no longer deep-copies the set (it was `entry.bits.clone()`: 1.25 MB of memcpy per hit on a 10M-doc segment, more work than re-running many queries), and iterating a hit is `O(matches)` / `O(maxDoc/64)` rather than an `O(maxDoc)` bit scan. (e) LRU selection is `O(log n)` via a recency `BTreeMap`, not an `O(n)` scan -- which mattered once the RAM bound can evict many entries per insert. (f) `get_or_compute` is generic over the error and takes `impl FnOnce() -> Result<FixedBitSet, E>`, so a failed computation inserts nothing; the old code inserted an empty placeholder and removed it afterwards, leaving a window where a poisoned "matches nothing" entry was live. **Still deferred**: automatic lifecycle-driven invalidation (needs a segment-identity object this port lacks), the 16-way lock partitioning (this cache is `&mut`-exclusive by design -- ownership over locks), `skipCacheFactor` (needs iterator `cost()` estimates), and the observability callbacks. Note `search_term_query_cached` caches unconditionally and so caches exactly the query type Java's policy refuses; that is deliberate and documented (it is the only query type in this port satisfying `Eq + Hash`). Two further corrections from b13's own Tier 2 review: `QueryCachingPolicy::on_use` is the **caller's** to call, once per query execution, mirroring `CachingWrapperWeight`'s `used.compareAndSet(false, true)` guard -- calling it per leaf (as the first cut did) bumps a query's tracked frequency once per segment, so an N-segment reader would cross the 5-observation threshold after 5/N executions; and `search_term_query_cached` now caches a **deletion-agnostic** set with `live_docs` applied while iterating it, matching `cacheIntoBitSet`/`cacheIntoRoaringDocIdSet` scoring with `acceptDocs == null` and the cache keying on the *core* cache helper -- baking deletions in made the entry silently wrong for the same key under any other `live_docs`, since a hit never consults `live_docs`. **Added, no Java counterpart: `Error::CachedDocOutOfRange`.** Java's `LRUQueryCache` builds its set from a `LeafReaderContext` that owns both the doc-id space and the `maxDoc`; here `num_docs` is a separate parameter from the segment whose postings produce the doc ids, so a disagreement is reported rather than absorbed into a ghost bit. A `live_docs` bitset shorter than the cached set hides the docs it does not cover, matching `soft_deletes::is_live`. (`c41-gates-and-record`.) |

## lucene-ffi

| Java file/class | Rust module | Status/notes |
|---|---|---|
| — (no direct Java equivalent; this is the JNI/C-ABI boundary itself, task #20) | `lucene-ffi/src/directory.rs::{ffi_open_directory, ffi_close_directory}` | **new.** Opens/closes a real `lucene_store::directory::FsDirectory` at a caller-given filesystem path, behind an opaque `u64` handle (`lucene-ffi/src/handle.rs::SlotMap`, a hand-rolled generation-tagged slotmap — see that module's doc comment for why it isn't reused from elsewhere in the workspace). Each handle also carries an 8-bit registry-type tag (`RegistryTag::{Directory,Segment,Results,ScoredResults,SortedResults,DirectoryReader,FacetResults}`, the last one added by the Faceted search FFI exposure task) alongside its index/generation bits, so a handle from one of this crate's seven registries is rejected outright by another registry's `get`/`remove` on a tag mismatch, rather than only being caught if its index/generation also happens to miss. |
| — | `lucene-ffi/src/segment.rs::{ffi_open_segment, ffi_close_segment}` | **new, partial.** Opens one segment's `.fnm`/`.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` term dictionary (`lucene_codecs::blocktree::open`) plus, optionally, its `.doc`/`.pos` postings files, from already-known file names/segment ID/suffix/`maxDoc` (a real caller is expected to have already parsed `segments_N`/`.si` to discover these — see that module's doc comment for why this doesn't parse `.si` itself). **Task #30**: `ffi_open_segment` grew optional `nvm_name`/`nvd_name` parameters, opening the segment's `.nvm`/`.nvd` norms pair (`lucene_codecs::norms::{parse_meta, check_data_header_footer}`, always validated against the empty codec suffix — real Lucene's norms format has none, unlike `.tim`/`.doc`/etc) so `ffi_search_*_query_scored` can use real per-doc/avg field lengths instead of always falling back to `similarity::UNNORMED_FIELD_LENGTH`; `SegmentHandle` now also carries the parsed `field_infos` (needed to map a scored query's field *name* to the field *number* `NormsEntry`/`Norms` are keyed by — `BlockTreeFields` has no such mapping of its own). ~~**No `.pay` (payload) parameter yet**~~ — closed by the M2 sweep (`c13-ffi-surface`): `ffi_open_segment` takes `pay_name`/`pay_name_len` (null = open none, which stays correct for a field whose `IndexOptions` carry neither payloads nor offsets), validates the file with `postings::PayInput::open` at open time, and `query.rs`'s two phrase entry points plus `explain.rs`'s phrase explain pass the opened `PayInput` through instead of `None`. **Task #40**: `ffi_open_segment` grew optional `dvm_name`/`dvd_name`/`dv_suffix` parameters, opening the segment's `.dvm`/`.dvd` doc-values pair (`lucene_codecs::doc_values::{parse_meta, check_data_header_footer}`) so `ffi_sort_by_doc_value`/`ffi_sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value` (see below) have real doc-values bytes to sort by; unlike `.nvm`/`.nvd`, `.dvm`/`.dvd` carry their own per-field codec-suffix component in their index header, so `dv_suffix` is a separate parameter from `segment_suffix` rather than reusing it. `SegmentHandle` grew matching `dv_data`/`dv_meta` fields, both `None` when the segment was opened without doc values (the two sort entry points then return `FfiStatus::InvalidArgument` — unlike norms, there's no meaningful constant fallback for "nothing to sort by"). **Points range query FFI exposure task**: `ffi_open_segment` grew optional `kdm_name`/`kdi_name`/`kdd_name` parameters, opening the segment's `.kdm`/`.kdi`/`.kdd` BKD points meta/index/data files (`lucene_codecs::points::open`, validated once at open time then stored as owned bytes on a new `SegmentHandle::points_data` field) so `ffi_search_points_range` (see below) has points data to search; like `.nvm`/`.nvd`, always validated against the empty codec suffix (`""`), not `segment_suffix`/`dv_suffix` — real Lucene's points format has no per-field codec-suffix component in its index header either. A segment opened without them can't serve `ffi_search_points_range` (`FfiStatus::InvalidArgument`, same "no sensible fallback" story as doc values' absence). |
| `search/SortField.Type.LONG`/`INT`/`SortedNumericSortField`/`SortedSetSortField` (single-key ascending doc-value sort, C-ABI wrapper) | `lucene-ffi/src/sort.rs::{ffi_sort_by_doc_value, ffi_sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value}` | **new, task #40.** Thin `catch_unwind`-guarded wrappers around `lucene_search::{sort_by_numeric_doc_value, doc_value_query::sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value}`, following task #30's exact FFI shape: resolve a field name to a NUMERIC/SORTED_NUMERIC `.dvm` entry via the segment's `field_infos` (same name -> number lookup `open_field_norms` already does for norms), run the sort over a caller-given candidate doc-ID list, and store the resulting ascending `(doc_id, value)` pairs under a new `SortedResultsHandle`. The missing-value policy (`lucene_search::MissingValue::Exclude`/`Default(i64)`) crosses the wire as `missing_is_default: bool` + `missing_default: i64` (ignored when the bool is `false`) rather than a tagged union; `ffi_sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value`'s `ValueSelector` (MIN/MAX) crosses as an `i32` (`0`/`1`, anything else is `FfiStatus::InvalidArgument`). Same "also serves SORTED_SET" reuse `doc_value_query.rs` documents for the multi-valued function applies here too — passing a SORTED_SET field's ordinal-array entry works unchanged. **Ascending-only, single-sort-key-only** (see the deferred row below) — matches `doc_value_query.rs`'s own documented scope; a `descending` flag isn't exposed since the wrapped Rust functions don't support it and a reverse-then-relabel hack wouldn't be real Lucene sort semantics. |
| `index/NumericDocValues.advanceExact`/`.longValue` (single-doc "does this doc have a value, and what is it" read — C-ABI wrapper) | `lucene-ffi/src/sort.rs::ffi_numeric_doc_value_for_doc` | **new, FFI exposure audit for sparse doc-values reading.** Before this, every doc-values FFI entry point (`ffi_sort_by_doc_value`/`ffi_sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value` above, `range_sort.rs`'s range-sort pair, `facets.rs`'s facet counters) operated over a *set* of docs and already correctly threaded the sparse "no value" case through as an aggregate policy (`MissingValue::Exclude` drops the doc from the output, `MissingValue::Default` substitutes a caller-given value) — but none of them let a caller ask "does *this one* doc have a value for this field" and get back a real present/absent distinction; a caller wanting that had to sort a one-doc candidate list and infer presence from whether the result set came back empty, which is indirect and doesn't work at all for a field with `MissingValue::Default` baked in. `ffi_numeric_doc_value_for_doc` closes that specific gap: a thin `catch_unwind`-guarded wrapper directly around `lucene_codecs::doc_values::numeric_value` (already `Result<Option<i64>>`-shaped — `Ok(None)` means "doc legitimately has no value", already distinct from `Ok(Some(0))` at the Rust level), reusing `sort.rs`'s existing `numeric_entry_for` field lookup. Crosses the wire as two out-parameters: `out_has_value: *mut bool` and `out_value: *mut i64`, the latter left untouched (not zeroed) whenever `*out_has_value` comes back `false`, so a caller can never mistake "no value" for a stored `0` (or any other sentinel) — verified by a test that deliberately pre-poisons `*out_value` with a non-zero sentinel before the call and confirms it's still there after a "no value" result. Same field-lookup/entry-kind-validation and error-status conventions as `ffi_sort_by_doc_value` (unknown field/wrong entry kind/segment without doc values → `InvalidArgument`). **Review-confirmed bug fixed before landing**: `lucene_codecs::doc_values::numeric_value`'s own `DocOutOfRange` check only bounds-checks `doc` for DENSE entries -- a sparse entry has no `max_doc` of its own, so a doc ID far past the segment's real document count silently fell through the docs-with-field bitset lookup and came back as `has_value == false` instead of an error. Fixed by checking `doc < 0 || doc >= segment.max_doc` directly against the segment's own `max_doc` inside this FFI function, uniformly for dense and sparse entries, before ever calling into the codec -- proven by a dedicated test (`numeric_doc_value_for_doc_out_of_range_positive_doc_on_sparse_field_is_invalid_argument`) against the same sparse fixture with a doc ID far past its real document count. Any other decode error still maps to `FfiStatus::Decode`. **NUMERIC fields only** — BINARY/SORTED/SORTED_NUMERIC/SORTED_SET per-doc "does this doc have a value" lookups are not exposed through FFI yet (genuine, explicitly out-of-scope gap for a follow-up task; the underlying `lucene_codecs::doc_values::{binary_value, sorted_ord, sorted_numeric_values}` functions already exist and are equally `Option`-shaped, so closing that gap is "more of the same wiring", not new design). Tested against the same real-Lucene `fixtures/data/doc_values_index/`'s `sparse` field (5, *no value*, 15, *no value*, 25 across docs 0..4) already used by `sort_by_doc_value_missing_default_policy_real_fixture`: a present-value doc, a missing-value doc (confirming `out_value` is left untouched), a dense field's present value, plus the usual unknown-field/wrong-entry-kind/negative-doc/unknown-handle/null-out-param/decode-error status-code paths. |
| `search/TopFieldCollector` + `PointRangeQuery`/`SortedNumericDocValuesField.newSlowRangeQuery` (numeric range query, ranked by a doc-value field, ascending or descending — C-ABI wrapper, single- and multi-segment) | `lucene-ffi/src/range_sort.rs::{ffi_search_numeric_range_sorted_by_field, ffi_search_numeric_range_sorted_by_field_multi_segment}` | **new, TopFieldCollector FFI exposure task.** Thin `catch_unwind`-guarded wrappers around `lucene_search::doc_value_query::search_numeric_range_sorted_by_field` (single segment, task #80) and `lucene_search::multi_segment::search_numeric_range_sorted_by_field_multi_segment` (multi-segment fan-out/merge, task #88) — no range-matching, sorting, or doc-ID-translation logic reimplemented, only C-ABI marshaling. **Result-handle shape reuses the existing `SortedResultsHandle`/`registry::sorted_results()` (task #40)**, not a new handle type: both wrapped functions return `Vec<FieldValueDoc>`, i.e. exactly the same `(doc_id: i32, value: i64)` wire shape `SortedResultsHandle` already carries for `ffi_sort_by_doc_value`/`ffi_sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value` — read back via the existing `ffi_sorted_results_len`/`ffi_sorted_results_copy`/`ffi_close_sorted_results` trio, no new accessor functions needed. `direction` crosses the wire as an `i32` (`0` = `SortDirection::Ascending`, `1` = `SortDirection::Descending`, anything else is `FfiStatus::InvalidArgument`), the same `0`/`1`-selector convention `ffi_sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value`'s `ValueSelector` already established; `range_field`/`sort_field` are looked up via the existing `sort.rs::numeric_entry_for` helper (now `pub(crate)`, reused rather than duplicated), and `missing_is_default`/`missing_default` reuse `sort.rs::missing_value` unchanged. **Multi-segment wire format is a flat array of already-open segment handles plus a parallel caller-supplied `doc_base` array, not a `DirectoryReaderHandle`**: task #51's `DirectoryReaderHandle` carries no `.dvm`/`.dvd` doc-values data per segment at all, so there is nothing for a reader-handle-based entry point to read a doc-value from — each already-opened `ffi_open_segment` handle already carries its own `dv_data`/`dv_meta` (task #40), so this function instead looks up each handle in the existing `segments()` registry and builds the exact `lucene_search::multi_segment::DocValueSegment` list that function already expects, with the caller supplying each segment's `doc_base` explicitly (a segment has no way to know its own position in a larger commit on its own). `live_docs` is always `None`, the same documented "no `.liv` FFI surface yet" behavior every other query entry point in this crate already has. **Verified**: differential test asserting the FFI wrapper's output matches calling `search_numeric_range_sorted_by_field` directly against the same real `fixtures/data/doc_values_index/` fixture bytes; both sort directions and top-`n` truncation; the missing-value default policy; multi-segment doc-ID translation and tie-break (the same fixture segment opened twice with different `doc_base`s, mirroring `multi_segment.rs`'s own cross-segment tests); and the usual null-pointer/unknown-handle/decode-error/invalid-direction/invalid-field/segment-without-doc-values error paths for both entry points.
