Term-density passage scoring and full ICU/`BreakIterator`-grade sentence segmentation remain out of scope, matching `highlighter.rs`'s own already-documented scope boundary. `ffi_assemble_fragments` now also takes a `snap_to_sentence: u8` flag (C-`bool`-style) so `highlighter.rs`'s opt-in sentence-boundary-snapping mode is reachable across the FFI boundary, cross-checked against a direct `lucene_search::highlighter::assemble_fragments` call in `highlighter::tests::assemble_fragments_snap_to_sentence_flag_matches_direct_call`. |
| `search/IndexSearcher.explain(Query, int)` + `search/Explanation` (C-ABI wrapper, term/phrase/flat-boolean clauses only) | `lucene-ffi/src/explain.rs::{ffi_explain_term_query, ffi_explain_phrase_query, ffi_explain_boolean_query}`, `lucene-ffi/src/results_explain.rs::{ffi_explain_results_len, ffi_explain_node_value, ffi_explain_node_matched, ffi_explain_node_description, ffi_explain_node_child_count, ffi_explain_node_child_at, ffi_close_explain_results}` | **new (Explain FFI exposure).** Thin `catch_unwind`-guarded wrappers around task #59's `lucene_search::explain::explain_clause` — **no explain logic is reimplemented here**, `lucene-search/src/explain.rs` is unchanged by this task. Each of the three functions builds exactly the same `Clause` `query.rs`'s matching `ffi_search_*_query_scored` sibling already builds (same wire formats, same norms-map construction, reused via a newly-`pub(crate)` `query::open_field_norms`) and hands it straight to `explain_clause`. **Scope deliberately matches `query.rs`'s existing clause-construction surface, not `explain_clause`'s full ten-variant `Clause` enum**: only `Clause::Term`/`Clause::Phrase`/flat-`Clause::Term`-only `Clause::Boolean` are wrapped, since those are the only three shapes `query.rs` can build from FFI input at all — `DisjunctionMax`/`ConstantScore`/`Boost`/`Wildcard`/`Prefix`/`Fuzzy`/`Regexp`/`Span`/`MultiPhrase` explanations have no wrapper because those clauses can't be *searched* through this ABI either, not a gap this task introduced. ~~truly-nested-`Boolean`~~ is no longer among them: the M2 sweep (`c13-ffi-surface`) replaced the clause wire format with `query.rs::read_boolean_query`'s flat, `Occur`-tagged, parent-indexed clause array, which builds arbitrarily nested `Clause::Boolean` trees (and `Occur.FILTER`, and `minimumNumberShouldMatch`), and `ffi_explain_boolean_query` shares that decoder — so a nested boolean query is now both searchable and explainable over this ABI. **The recursive-tree problem**: `Explanation` is a recursive tree (`details: Vec<Self>`, e.g. a single term explanation is already three levels deep — `weight(...)` → `score(freq=...)` → `idf`/`tfNorm`), unlike every prior FFI result (`facets.rs`/`highlighter.rs` both flat lists). Chosen scheme: depth-first, pre-order flattening into `Vec<registry::ExplainNode>` at construction time — each node keeps its own `value`/`matched`/`description` plus a `Vec<usize>` of *its children's indices into that same flat `Vec`* (a child-index list per node rather than a parent-index-per-node scheme, so "this node's Nth child" is an O(1) lookup, not an O(total nodes) scan); pre-order guarantees the root is always flattened first, so **node index `0` is always the root**. `ffi_explain_results_len`/`ffi_explain_node_value`/`ffi_explain_node_matched` read the fixed-size per-node fields; `ffi_explain_node_description` is a per-node string accessor reusing this crate's existing `buf`/`buf_len`/`out_written`/`BufferTooSmall` contract; `ffi_explain_node_child_count`/`ffi_explain_node_child_at` (the same "length first, then per-index accessor" shape `results_fragments.rs` established for a fragment's matched-terms list, applied here to a node's children) let a caller walk the whole tree starting at node `0`. New `RegistryTag::ExplainResults`/`registry::ExplainResultsHandle` (own registry — an explain node's shape has no correspondence to any existing handle's element). **Verified**: `explain::tests` cross-checks the term/phrase/boolean FFI paths against calling `lucene_search::explain::explain_clause` directly on the same real `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture, walking the **entire** flattened tree via the FFI accessors node-by-node (not just the root value) and independently re-deriving every `ffi_explain_node_child_at` link against a from-scratch Rust-side re-flattening of the same `Explanation`, plus a non-matching doc collapsing to a single no-match node, a single-term phrase delegating to the same tree a direct term-explain call produces, a missing-`.pos`-input multi-term phrase surfacing as `FfiStatus::Search`, an empty boolean query as a single no-match node, and unknown-handle/null-pointer/invalid-UTF-8 cases for each of the three functions. `results_explain::tests` covers a full tree round-trip (value/matched/description/child-count/child-at on both an internal and a leaf node), out-of-bounds node/child index on every accessor, buffer-too-small on the description accessor, null-pointer variants, and unknown/double-close handle rejection. `cargo test -p lucene-ffi`: 261 tests pass (up from 221). New-file coverage: `explain.rs` 97.05% lines, `results_explain.rs` 100.00% lines (the handful of missed `explain.rs` lines are `.doc`/`.pos` reopen-decode-error branches identical in shape to `query.rs`'s own already-accepted untested branches, unreachable without hand-corrupting already-validated segment bytes). **Deferred, not a gap introduced by this task**: every clause kind `query.rs` itself doesn't expose (see above) has no explain wrapper either — a follow-up to `query.rs`'s own wire format, not to `explain.rs` (either crate). |
| `search/IndexSearcher.search(Query, Collector)` (single-segment slice, C-ABI wrapper) | `lucene-ffi/src/query.rs::{ffi_search_term_query, ffi_search_boolean_query, ffi_search_phrase_query, ffi_search_term_query_scored, ffi_search_boolean_query_scored, ffi_search_phrase_query_scored}` | **new** (unscored); **new, task #30** (scored). Thin `catch_unwind`-guarded wrappers around `lucene_search::{search_term_query, search_boolean_query, search_phrase_query}`, collecting every matching doc ID into a plain `lucene_search::VecCollector` (entirely Rust-side — no callback ever crosses back into the caller) and storing the result under a new results handle. `BooleanQuery`'s The `BooleanQuery` clause list crosses the boundary as **one** flat, `Occur`-tagged, parent-indexed clause array (`clause_occurs`/`clause_kinds`/`clause_fields`/`clause_field_lens`/`clause_terms`/`clause_term_lens`/`clause_parents`/`clause_params`, length `clause_count`, plus a `minimum_should_match` scalar) — see `query.rs::read_boolean_query`'s doc comment for the full table. The M2 sweep (`c13-ffi-surface`) replaced the three separate `must`/`should`/`must_not` four-array buckets with it, because those could express neither `Occur.FILTER` nor a nested `Clause::Boolean` and would have cost a fresh C-ABI break per additional `Occur`; under the new format an `Occur` and a leaf clause kind are *values*, so the next of either costs no ABI change. `live_docs` comes from the segment handle's own `.liv` (attached by `ffi_segment_set_live_docs`), so no single-segment entry point returns deleted documents. **The `_scored` siblings (task #30)** wrap `lucene_search::{search_term_query_scored, search_boolean_query_scored, search_phrase_query_scored}`, feeding a `lucene_search::TopDocsCollector` (constructed from a caller-given `top_n`) instead of a `VecCollector`, and storing the `(doc_id, score)` hits under a new `ScoredResultsHandle`. Each scored function's segment-handle norms (see the `segment.rs` row above) are looked up per call via a small `open_field_norms` helper (name -> field number -> `NormsEntry` -> `FieldNorms::from_field_stats`, recomputing `avgFieldLength` every call rather than caching it on `SegmentHandle` — a deliberate simplicity-over-micro-optimization call, documented in that helper's doc comment). **Corrected in `c34-ledger-reconcile`**: this row said `FieldNorms::open` (an average of *decoded, live-doc-only* norms) long after `b15` had moved `open_field_norms` onto `from_field_stats` (`sumTotalTermFreq / docCount` off the field's `.tmd`, Java's own `avgdl`); the code was right and the row was the last carrier of b12's F-7 claim; `ffi_search_boolean_query_scored` builds one `HashMap<String, FieldNorms>` from every distinct field name across its flat `must`/`should`/`must_not` term-clause arrays. Same deferred-nested-clause-construction limitation as the unscored sibling (flat `Clause::Term` only). **`ffi_search_term_query_scored_maxscore` (MAXSCORE FFI exposure task)**: before this task, this port's real block-level MAXSCORE dynamic pruning (`lucene_search::search_term_query_scored_maxscore` — streams a single `TermQuery`'s postings through a `LazyDocsCursor` and skips whole level-0 blocks a `TopDocsCollector`'s current worst kept score proves are unreachable, instead of eagerly decoding every block via `DocInput::read_postings`) had zero FFI exposure: every existing scored FFI function above (`ffi_search_term_query_scored`/`ffi_search_boolean_query_scored`/`ffi_search_phrase_query_scored`) calls the *eager*, non-pruning sibling instead — this was a genuine gap, not something already transparently reachable, since (unlike, say, an optimizer pass that fires automatically inside one shared code path) `search_term_query_scored_maxscore` is a wholly separate function from `search_term_query_scored` that no existing FFI wrapper called. Closed the single-`TermQuery` gap with a new `ffi_search_term_query_scored_maxscore` entry point, same `(segment_handle, field, term, top_n) -> ScoredResultsHandle` shape and `open_field_norms`/fallback conventions as `ffi_search_term_query_scored`, wired straight to `lucene_search::search_term_query_scored_maxscore`. **Proof it's genuinely reached and genuinely prunes, through the FFI boundary itself** (`crates/lucene-ffi/src/query.rs::tests::term_query_scored_maxscore_matches_eager_ffi_path_and_actually_skips_blocks`): reuses the same real, Java-written fixture term as `lucene-search`'s own Rust-level proof (`big`/`"everywhere"`, `docFreq == 300`, real per-doc norms opened), asserts `ffi_search_term_query_scored_maxscore`'s results are byte-for-byte identical to `ffi_search_term_query_scored`'s (the eager FFI path) across several `top_n` values, and — reusing `lucene_search::test_only_maxscore_block_skip_counter` verbatim rather than reimplementing it — confirms real level-0 block skips actually happened for small `top_n` and zero skips happened when `top_n` equals the full `docFreq`. That counter is normally `#[cfg(test)]`-private to `lucene-search` and invisible to a different crate's own tests; it's made reachable here via a new `test-support` Cargo feature on `lucene-search` (`crates/lucene-search/Cargo.toml`) that only `lucene-ffi`'s `[dev-dependencies]` edge enables (`crates/lucene-ffi/Cargo.toml`) — resolver-2 feature unification scopes that to `lucene-ffi`'s own test/bench targets, never its normal library/cdylib build, so nothing test-only reaches a production binary. Two more tests cover the unknown-segment-handle and missing-term/field error/empty-result branches, mirroring `ffi_search_term_query_scored`'s own equivalents. **`ffi_search_boolean_query_scored_maxscore` (BooleanQuery WAND/MAXSCORE dynamic pruning task)**: the boolean-side gap noted above is now closed too — a new entry point mirroring `ffi_search_boolean_query_scored`'s exact four-parallel-array clause wire format and `ScoredResultsHandle` contract, wired to `lucene_search::search_boolean_query_scored_maxscore` (see the "Impacts/competitive-scoring"/`WANDScorer` row above for that function's exact scope: pure SHOULD-disjunction of plain term clauses only, simplified two-tier essential/non-essential-style skip, not full multi-way WAND pivot selection). Any `BooleanQuery` outside that fast path's scope (a `must`/`must_not` clause, `minimum_should_match > 1`, a nested/non-term clause, a singleton `docFreq == 1` term) transparently falls back to the same exhaustive `search_boolean_query_scored` path `ffi_search_boolean_query_scored` calls. **Proof, through the FFI boundary itself** (`crates/lucene-ffi/src/query.rs::tests::boolean_query_scored_maxscore_matches_eager_ffi_path_and_actually_skips_blocks`): a three-clause SHOULD query (`big`/`"everywhere"` plus `body`/`"cat"`/`"dog"`) asserts byte-for-byte identical results to `ffi_search_boolean_query_scored` across several `top_n` values and confirms `test_only_maxscore_block_skip_counter` (shared with the single-term MAXSCORE path) records a real skip for a small `top_n`. Further tests cover the unknown-segment-handle, null-out-pointer, and `must`-clause-present fallback branches. |
| `search/KnnFloatVectorQuery`, `search/KnnByteVectorQuery`, `search/AbstractKnnVectorQuery` (C-ABI wrapper) | `lucene-ffi/src/vectors.rs::{ffi_open_vectors, ffi_knn_float_vector_search, ffi_knn_byte_vector_search, ffi_knn_float_vector_search_filtered, ffi_knn_byte_vector_search_filtered, ffi_vectors_set_live_docs, ffi_close_vectors}` | **new (M2 sweep `c13-ffi-surface`).** Exposes `c5-vectors`' `Lucene99FlatVectorsFormat` reader and `Lucene99HnswVectorsFormat` graph over the C ABI: a field, a target vector, `k`, an `ef_search` beam (OpenSearch's `num_candidates`; `0` reproduces `KnnFloatVectorQuery` exactly, which searches with a collector of exactly `k`), and a `visited_limit` (`0` = Java's unlimited default). Both encodings (FLOAT32/BYTE) and all four similarity functions. Hits come back as `(doc_id, score)` through the existing `registry::ScoredResultsHandle` and `results_scored.rs`'s accessor trio — a KNN hit *is* a scored doc, so no new results shape was invented. **Its own handle/registry, not `SegmentHandle`**: `ffi_open_segment` requires `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd`, and a vector field needs no term dictionary at all (real Lucene's `KnnVectorsReader` is a per-segment reader independent of `FieldsProducer`; `fixtures/data/vectors_index` is exactly such a segment), so folding vectors into it would have made a vectors-only segment unopenable. **`similarity` is a cross-check, not an override** (`-1` = use the field's own, any other value must equal it): the HNSW arcs encode the build-time similarity, so walking them under a different one silently degrades recall with no error. **Deletions are honoured**: `ffi_vectors_set_live_docs` attaches the segment's `.liv` through the same `segment::decode_live_docs` helper `ffi_segment_set_live_docs` uses, and the search layer hands the bitset to the graph walk as Java's `acceptOrds`, so a deleted node never enters the collector at all. **Now a thin wrapper (M2 sweep `c16-knn-query`)**: Java's query-level policy — the `k >= 1` bound, the collector sizing, the ordinal→doc translation, the accept set — moved down into `lucene-search/src/vector_query.rs` (see that row), and this module owns only handle validation, argument decoding (including the `similarity` ordinal) and the results handle, like `points_query.rs` and `sort.rs`. The exported symbols and their behaviour are unchanged; the differential test below is what proves it. Tested differentially against real Lucene: every query `fixtures/data/vectors_index/manifest.properties` records is run through the exported symbols and compared to the exact `(doc, score)` list Java's `KnnFloatVectorQuery`/`KnnByteVectorQuery` returned, plus the graphless (exact brute-force) branch, the sparse ord→doc mapping, and every rejected argument. **Filtered KNN is exposed (M2 sweep `c21-hnsw-seeded`)**: `ffi_knn_float_vector_search_filtered`/`ffi_knn_byte_vector_search_filtered` take the filter as the **same** occur-tagged, parent-indexed clause array `read_boolean_query` already decodes for every `ffi_search_boolean_query*` entry point — no second encoding was invented, which is what `c13-ffi-surface` rebuilt that format for — plus the same segment's `SegmentHandle` for the term dictionary a clause needs and `ffi_open_vectors` deliberately does not open. The two handles are cross-checked on `maxDoc`, because a filter resolved against a different segment yields doc ids that silently mean something else. An empty clause list is Java's `MatchNoDocsQuery` rewrite (no hits, no error); a match-all filter is the *unfiltered* entry point, which is Java's own `rewrite` short circuit. Tested differentially against real Lucene over `fixtures/data/vectors_filter_index` — 80 filtered queries across both encodings and both of Java's filtered branches, doc for doc and score for score through the exported symbols, plus every hit asserted to come from the term's own recorded postings. |
| — | `lucene-ffi/src/results.rs::{ffi_results_len, ffi_results_copy, ffi_close_results}`, `lucene-ffi/src/results_scored.rs::{ffi_scored_results_len, ffi_scored_results_copy, ffi_close_scored_results}` (task #30), `lucene-ffi/src/results_sorted.rs::{ffi_sorted_results_len, ffi_sorted_results_copy, ffi_close_sorted_results}` (task #40) | **new.** Reads a results handle's collected doc IDs back out via a caller-allocated buffer (`ffi_results_len` sizes it, `ffi_results_copy` bulk-copies — chosen over a per-index accessor since a JNI caller almost always wants the whole array at once, see that module's doc comment), then releases the handle. No Rust-side-allocated memory ever crosses the boundary in this slice — `ffi_results_copy`/`ffi_scored_results_copy`/`ffi_sorted_results_copy` only ever write into a buffer the caller already allocated. **`ffi_scored_results_copy` (task #30)** writes into two caller-allocated *parallel* buffers (`out_doc_ids: *mut i32`, `out_scores: *mut f32`, same `buf_len`) rather than one interleaved buffer — see `results_scored.rs`'s module doc for why; backed by a new `ScoredResultsHandle`/`registry::scored_results()` registry (a separate `RegistryTag`, not a widened `ResultsHandle`) so a results handle from the wrong search flavor is rejected by the handle-tag check rather than misread. **`ffi_sorted_results_copy` (task #40)** is the same parallel-buffers shape again (`out_doc_ids: *mut i32`, `out_values: *mut i64`), backed by yet another separate `SortedResultsHandle`/`registry::sorted_results()` registry — a sort's second element is an arbitrary doc-value, not a BM25 score, so it gets its own handle type rather than being folded into `ScoredResultsHandle`. |
| — | `lucene-ffi/src/error.rs::{FfiStatus, guard}`, `lucene-ffi/src/lib.rs::ffi_get_last_error_message` | **new.** Every exported function's body runs inside `guard`, which wraps `std::panic::catch_unwind` and converts a caught panic into a stable `FfiStatus::Panic` status code plus a thread-local last-error message (readable via `ffi_get_last_error_message`) instead of letting the unwind cross into the JVM. **Notable implementation detail**: the panic message is captured via a process-wide panic hook (installed once, active only while the calling thread is inside `guard`) rather than by downcasting `catch_unwind`'s `Box<dyn Any + Send>` payload to `&str`/`String` — the ordinary downcast approach was tried first and found to intermittently fail to match either type in this environment even though the same payload's type is correctly observable from a hook at panic time; see `error.rs`'s `install_panic_hook` doc comment for the full investigation. Every handle-taking function validates its handle(s) against the relevant `SlotMap` before doing anything else, returning `FfiStatus::InvalidHandle` on a miss (stale/closed/unknown handles never dereference freed state). Every call site locks its registry's `Mutex` via `registry::lock_recovering` (`.lock().unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner())`) rather than a bare `.lock().unwrap()` — a panic caught by `guard` while a registry lock was held would otherwise poison that `Mutex` permanently, making every later `.lock().unwrap()` on it panic again forever (turning one bad call into a permanently broken registry); recovering the poisoned guard is sound here since every `SlotMap` mutation is a single non-panicking sequence with no possibility of a torn write. **Task #30's regression test for the scored path** (`registry_mutex_recovers_from_poisoning_after_a_panic_mid_scored_query`) uses a `thread_local!`-scoped panic-injection switch rather than reusing task #20's process-wide `AtomicBool` one (`PANIC_ON_NEXT_TERM_QUERY`) — `cargo test`'s default parallel execution means a process-wide flag armed by one test can fire inside a different, concurrently-running test's call to the same function, a real (if narrow) flakiness source task #29's review flagged in the pre-existing unscored-path test; the new thread-local flag is immune to that race by construction (arming and firing always happen on the same test's own thread). The pre-existing `PANIC_ON_NEXT_TERM_QUERY` itself was not changed by this task — fixing it is tracked as a separate follow-up. |
| `index/DirectoryReader.open(Directory)` + `IndexSearcher.search` fan-out, sequential and concurrent (C-ABI wrapper) | `lucene-ffi/src/directory_reader.rs::{ffi_open_directory_reader, ffi_close_directory_reader, ffi_search_term_query_multi_segment, ffi_search_boolean_query_multi_segment, ffi_search_term_query_multi_segment_concurrent, ffi_search_boolean_query_multi_segment_concurrent}` | **new, task #51.** Exposes task #45's `lucene_search::directory_reader::DirectoryReader` and task #41's multi-segment fan-out/merge over the C ABI, following tasks #20/#30/#40's exact FFI pattern (opaque `u64` handles, `catch_unwind`-guarded `extern "C" fn`s via `guard`, `lock_recovering` registry-mutex access, bounds-checked out-buffers). A new `RegistryTag::DirectoryReader`/`registry::directory_readers()` registry backs a `DirectoryReaderHandle`; `ffi_open_directory_reader` takes a filesystem path directly (not an already-open `ffi_open_directory` handle -- a `DirectoryReader` copies every segment's bytes into its own owned buffers at open time and needs the directory for no longer than that one call, unlike `ffi_open_segment`'s directory-handle-reuse case). `ffi_search_term_query_multi_segment`/`ffi_search_boolean_query_multi_segment` reuse task #30's existing `ScoredResultsHandle`/`ffi_scored_results_len`/`ffi_scored_results_copy`/`ffi_close_scored_results` trio rather than inventing a new results type, since multi-segment search returns the exact same `Vec<ScoreDoc>` shape single-segment scored search already does. Every per-segment norms slot passed to the wrapped `lucene_search` functions is `None` -- task #45's `DirectoryReader` carries no `.nvm`/`.nvd` data, so every multi-segment scored query here falls back to `similarity::UNNORMED_FIELD_LENGTH`, the same documented fallback single-segment scored queries already use for a bare `None`. **Avoiding task #29's flakiness pattern**: the new poison-recovery regression test arms a **thread-local** `Cell<bool>` switch (`PANIC_ON_NEXT_MULTI_SEGMENT_QUERY`), following `query.rs`'s post-task-#29 `PANIC_ON_NEXT_SCORED_TERM_QUERY` precedent rather than task #20's older process-wide `AtomicBool`, since `cargo test`'s parallel thread pool can otherwise let one test's armed flag fire inside a different, concurrently-running test's call to the same function. **Verified**: happy-path multi-segment term and boolean search against the real `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture; wrong-tag handle rejection in both directions (a `ScoredResultsHandle` id passed as a reader handle, and a `DirectoryReader` handle passed to `ffi_scored_results_len`); null-pointer/unknown-handle/double-close cases for every new entry point; the poison-recovery regression test above. No new Java fixture needed -- this is FFI wiring around already cross-engine-verified `lucene-search` logic (tasks #41/#45), not new correctness surface. Coverage: `directory_reader.rs` 96.04% lines.

**Progress (Concurrent segment search FFI exposure):** `ffi_search_term_query_multi_segment_concurrent`/`ffi_search_boolean_query_multi_segment_concurrent` expose task #68's `search_term_query_multi_segment_concurrent`/`search_boolean_query_multi_segment_concurrent` (`lucene-search/src/multi_segment.rs`, the `rayon`-based sibling row above) over the identical wire format, handle validation, and `ScoredResultsHandle` readback the sequential wrappers above already use -- no search/merge logic reimplemented at this layer, only the fan-out function called differs (`_concurrent` instead of the sequential one). **Verified**, including the critical byte-for-byte proof at the FFI boundary itself: `term_query_multi_segment_concurrent_matches_sequential_ffi_wrapper`/`boolean_query_multi_segment_concurrent_matches_sequential_ffi_wrapper` each open two independent `DirectoryReader` handles against the same real `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture, run the sequential wrapper through one handle and the concurrent wrapper through the other, and assert the read-back `(doc_id, score)` vectors are equal -- confirming the identical-output property already proven for the underlying Rust functions still holds with the FFI marshaling/handle/registry layer in the loop. Plus the same real-fixture-happy-path/unknown-reader-handle/null-out-handle coverage every other entry point in this crate carries. `cargo test -p lucene-ffi`: 269 tests pass (up from 261). Coverage: `directory_reader.rs` 95.81% lines. |

**Progress (MAXSCORE multi-segment search FFI exposure, task #213):** `ffi_search_boolean_query_multi_segment_maxscore`/`ffi_search_boolean_query_multi_segment_maxscore_concurrent` expose the new `search_boolean_query_multi_segment_maxscore`/`search_boolean_query_multi_segment_maxscore_concurrent` (`lucene-search/src/multi_segment.rs`, see their dedicated row above) over the identical `Clause::Term`-only flat wire format, handle validation, and `ScoredResultsHandle` readback every other multi-segment boolean entry point in this file already uses -- no new wire format, handle type, or results type, only the `lucene_search` function called differs. Same `None`-norms fallback as every other entry point in this module (task #45's `DirectoryReader` carries no `.nvm`/`.nvd` data). **Verified**: `boolean_query_multi_segment_maxscore_matches_exhaustive_ffi_wrapper` opens two independent `DirectoryReader` handles against the real `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture, runs the exhaustive `ffi_search_boolean_query_multi_segment` through one and the new MAXSCORE wrapper through the other with the same three-clause query (`big`/`"everywhere"`, `body`/`"cat"`, `body`/`"dog"`) used by the underlying Rust-level differential test, and asserts the read-back `(doc_id, score)` vectors are equal byte-for-byte -- confirming the identical-output property proven one layer down still holds through the FFI marshaling/handle/registry layer. `boolean_query_multi_segment_maxscore_concurrent_matches_sequential_ffi_wrapper` gives the same proof for the concurrent sibling, mirroring the existing `boolean_query_multi_segment_concurrent_matches_sequential_ffi_wrapper` pattern. | — | **deferred, not started** | (1) ~~**`.liv` (live docs/deletions)**~~ — closed by the M2 sweep (b15). `lucene-ffi/src/segment.rs::ffi_segment_set_live_docs(segment_handle, dir_handle, liv_name, liv_name_len, del_gen, del_count)` reads the generation-suffixed `.liv` file through an already-open directory handle, decodes it with `lucene_codecs::live_docs::parse` (which cross-checks the bitset’s cardinality against the commit’s `del_count`, so a `.liv` that disagrees is `FfiStatus::Decode`, not silently wrong results), and stores it on `registry::SegmentHandle::live_docs`; a null `liv_name` clears it again for a `del_gen == -1` segment. It is its own additive call rather than four more `ffi_open_segment` parameters because a `.liv` name is generation-suffixed, needs `del_gen`/`del_count` to validate, and changes without the segment being rewritten — so a caller can refresh deletions without reopening the term dictionary and postings. Every single-segment entry point that *produces* a doc set now passes it: `query.rs`’s nine term/boolean/phrase searches (unscored, scored, MAXSCORE, custom-similarity), `points_query.rs::ffi_search_points_range`, `explain.rs`’s three explain calls, and `range_sort.rs`’ two range-then-sort calls. `sort.rs`/`facets.rs` deliberately still do not — they take a caller-supplied *candidate list* rather than producing one, so filtering it is the caller’s decision. `query.rs::open_field_norms` also deliberately keeps `live_docs: None` when computing `avgFieldLength`: real Lucene’s BM25 `avgdl` comes from `CollectionStatistics` (`Terms.getSumTotalTermFreq()`/`getDocCount()`), which still count deleted documents, so subtracting deletions there would make scores diverge from Java’s. The multi-segment `directory_reader.rs` entry points needed no change — task #45’s `DirectoryReader::open` already reads each segment’s `.liv` from the commit and `OpenedSegments::as_open_segments` already passes it through, so those have honoured deletions since they landed (the old "every query call passes `live_docs: None`" wording was stale for them). Verified against the real Java-written `fixtures/data/live_docs_index/` (5 docs, docs 1 and 3 deleted at `del_gen == 1`): `segment::live_docs_tests` asserts a term query matches the deleted doc before attaching and not after, that the decoded bitset equals the fixture manifest exactly, and that a wrong `del_gen`/`del_count` is rejected. (2) ~~**`.pay` (payloads)** for phrase queries~~ — closed by the M2 sweep (c13-ffi-surface). `ffi_open_segment` gained `pay_name`/`pay_name_len` (null = open none, the pre-c13 behaviour and the right one for a field whose `IndexOptions` carry neither payloads nor offsets); the file is validated with `postings::PayInput::open` at open time, so a wrong file is `FfiStatus::Decode` there rather than at the first query, and `query.rs`'s two phrase entry points plus `explain.rs`'s phrase explain now pass the opened `PayInput` through instead of `None`. It became reachable once `lucene-search`'s `DirectoryReader` started opening `.pay` per segment: the multi-segment path already honoured payloads while the single-segment one structurally could not, which is two entry points to the same index disagreeing about whether a field is searchable at all. (3) ~~**A unified `.si`-driven "open everything" entry point**~~ — closed by task #51: `lucene-ffi/src/directory_reader.rs::{ffi_open_directory_reader, ffi_close_directory_reader, ffi_search_term_query_multi_segment, ffi_search_boolean_query_multi_segment}` (see its own row below). `ffi_open_segment` itself is unchanged and remains available for a caller that already has one segment's files named. (3b) **Writer/highlighter capability gaps closed by the M2 sweep (b15)**: `ffi_writer_delete_all` (`IndexWriter.deleteAll()`), `ffi_writer_set_live_commit_data`/`ffi_writer_live_commit_data_len`/`ffi_writer_live_commit_data_entry` (`setLiveCommitData`/`getLiveCommitData`, four parallel `(key, value)` arrays in, a length-then-per-index accessor out), the three `MergePolicyConfig` knobs `ffi_writer_set_merge_policy` used to silently default (`force_merge_deletes_pct_allowed`, `deletes_pct_allowed`, `target_search_concurrency` — all eight are now parameters, the two percentages validated to `0..=100` and the concurrency to `>= 1`), the three `FragmentConfig` fields `ffi_assemble_fragments` could not reach (`ellipsis` — a null pointer selects Java’s `"... "`, a non-null empty one the empty string; `escape`; and `PassageScorer`’s `k1`/`b`/`pivot`, validated), and `ffi_fragment_result_span` for the `Passage` `start_offset`/`end_offset`/`score` `Fragment` gained. Eight already-exported `#[no_mangle]` functions that were missing from `lib.rs`’s `pub use` list are also re-exported now (the C symbols were always present; the Rust-level surface and `cargo doc` view were not). `IndexWriter::apply_merge`/`add_postings_field`/`set_custom_freq_postings_field` remain unwrapped — see `writer.rs`’s module doc. (4) **The JNI wrapper class itself** — out of scope for this Rust repo; this crate only exposes a stable C ABI. (5) **Phrase/`f32`-carrying `BooleanQuery` clause *construction*** over the C ABI — the *nested* half was closed by the M2 sweep (c13-ffi-surface): `lucene-ffi/src/query.rs::read_boolean_query` replaced the three `must`/`should`/`must_not` four-array clause lists with one flat, `Occur`-tagged, parent-indexed clause array, so `Occur.FILTER` (which the old format could not express at all, and which `c11-occur-filter` measured 44% cheaper than the equivalent `MUST`), arbitrarily nested `Clause::Boolean` trees, and `minimumNumberShouldMatch` (per query and per nested clause) are all constructible over the C ABI, at all eight boolean entry points and `ffi_explain_boolean_query`. Under that format a new `Occur` or a new `(field, term)`-shaped leaf kind (`Prefix`/`Wildcard`/`Regexp`/`Fuzzy`/`TermInSet`) is a new tag value, not another ABI break; `clause_params` covers an `i32` attribute (it is how nested `minimumNumberShouldMatch` needed no new array). What is still deferred is a clause kind carrying something the format has no room for: a `PhraseQuery`'s ordered term *list*, or a `BoostQuery`'s / `DisjunctionMaxQuery`'s `f32`, each of which needs one more parallel array. Nesting is capped at depth 32 (`MAX_CLAUSE_DEPTH`) — not a Java limit, a boundary-safety one: nested clauses are evaluated and dropped recursively, so caller-controlled nesting is caller-controlled stack depth, and a stack overflow aborts where `catch_unwind` cannot contain it. (6) **Multiple sort fields / MIDDLE_MIN/MIDDLE_MAX selectors** (task #40) — `ffi_sort_by_doc_value`/`ffi_sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value` only expose what `doc_value_query.rs` itself supports (single-key, MIN/MAX); no `Sort`/`SortField` composition exists anywhere in this port yet. ~~Descending sort~~ — closed for the range-query-then-sort-by-field path by `lucene-ffi/src/range_sort.rs::{ffi_search_numeric_range_sorted_by_field, ffi_search_numeric_range_sorted_by_field_multi_segment}` (see that row above); `ffi_sort_by_doc_value`/`ffi_sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value` themselves (a plain candidate-list sort, no range query) still wrap only the ascending-only `sort_by_numeric_doc_value`/`sort_by_multi_valued_doc_value`, unchanged. Tested (`crates/lucene-ffi/src/*.rs`'s `#[cfg(test)]` modules, ≥95% line coverage per file): real round-trip queries against `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` through the exact exported `extern "C" fn` entry points (not just the underlying Rust functions), stale/closed-handle rejection, generation-tag reuse correctness, and a genuine caught-panic-surfaces-as-a-status-code test (deliberately panicking inside `guard`) — now for the unscored, scored (task #30), and sort (task #40) query paths. **Progress (M2 sweep `c13-ffi-surface`)**: three further capability gaps closed. **KNN/vector search** — `lucene-ffi/src/vectors.rs::{ffi_open_vectors, ffi_knn_float_vector_search, ffi_knn_byte_vector_search, ffi_vectors_set_live_docs, ffi_close_vectors}` expose `c5-vectors`' `Lucene99FlatVectorsFormat` reader and `Lucene99HnswVectorsFormat` graph over the C ABI, mirroring `KnnFloatVectorQuery`/`KnnByteVectorQuery` for both FLOAT32 and BYTE encodings and all four similarity functions, returning `(doc_id, score)` through the existing `ScoredResultsHandle`. It gets its own handle and registry rather than fields on `SegmentHandle` because `ffi_open_segment` requires `.tim`/`.tip`/`.tmd` and a vector field needs no term dictionary at all (real Lucene's `KnnVectorsReader` is a separate per-segment reader; `fixtures/data/vectors_index` is exactly such a segment). `ef_search` is OpenSearch's `num_candidates` and `0` reproduces `KnnFloatVectorQuery` exactly; `similarity` is a cross-check against the field's own (`-1` = use the field's), not an override, because the HNSW arcs encode the build-time similarity. Deletions are honoured through Java's own `acceptOrds` graph walk, so a deleted node never enters the collector. Since `c16-knn-query` this module is a thin wrapper: the query itself is `lucene-search/src/vector_query.rs`. Tested differentially: every query `fixtures/data/vectors_index/manifest.properties` records is run through the exported symbols and compared to the exact `(doc, score)` list real Lucene's `KnnFloatVectorQuery`/`KnnByteVectorQuery` returned. **`IndexWriter` write surface** — `ffi_writer_{add_postings_field, add_term_vector_field, set_custom_freq_postings_field, omit_norms_field, add_document_with_custom_freq_terms}` close b15's recorded gaps (`ffi_writer_set_norms_field` was the opt-*in* until `c35-norms-and-sort` made norms the default and replaced it with the opt-out Lucene actually offers), and `c7-delete-queue`'s new APIs are exposed as `ffi_writer_{add_documents, update_documents, soft_update_document, update_numeric_doc_value, update_binary_doc_value, delete_documents_by_query}`; `deleteDocuments(Query...)` uses the same kind-tagged, parent-indexed node-array wire format the boolean clause array uses, so a new `DeleteQuery` variant is a new tag value. Every mutating writer entry point now writes Java's `long` seqNo to a nullable `*mut i64` out-parameter (c7's finding A7). `IndexWriter::apply_merge` is still unwrapped, for the unchanged reason: this crate exposes no way to *run* a merge, so there is no way to produce the `SegmentCommitInfo` it takes. |

| `queryparser/classic/QueryParser`, `queryparser/flexible/standard/StandardQueryParser` | `lucene-search/src/query_parser.rs::{parse_query, parse_query_with_analyzer, ParseError}` | **a minimal parser inspired by classic `QueryParser`'s syntax (task #44), not a port of `QueryParser.java`/`StandardQueryParser` themselves.** Both real classes are large grammars (JavaCC-generated in the classic case) covering range queries, configurable boolean-operator precedence, per-field analyzers, fractional fuzzy similarity, and a great deal of escaping-edge-case handling; this module is a from-scratch, hand-written recursive-descent parser (same "small, honestly-scoped hand-built parser" precedent `wildcard.rs`/`regexp.rs`/`fuzzy.rs` already established) turning a deliberately small syntax subset directly into this port's existing `Clause` tree — `parse_query(input: &str, default_field: Option<&str>) -> Result<Clause, ParseError>` reuses every already-ported constructor (`TermQuery`/`PhraseQuery`/`BooleanQuery`/`WildcardQuery`/`PrefixQuery`/`FuzzyQuery`/`RegexpQuery`/`BoostQuery`), adding no new query representation. **Boolean-operator style, a deliberate single-style choice**: real classic `QueryParser` supports *both* `+`/`-` prefixes *and* `AND`/`OR`/`NOT` operators simultaneously, with configurable default-operator precedence — exactly the "half-supported mix" this task's scoping explicitly called out to avoid. This parser supports **only** `+`/`-`/bare-is-SHOULD (`+term` = `Occur::MUST`, `-term` = `Occur::MUST_NOT`, bare = `Occur::SHOULD`, mapped straight onto `BooleanQuery`'s existing `must`/`should`/`must_not` buckets with no translation layer); `AND`/`OR`/`NOT` tokens parse as ordinary bare terms, never as operators — unambiguous to parse (every clause's role comes from its own leading character, never from what's between clauses), so no precedence table is needed at all. **Supported grammar**: `field:term` and bare terms (`default_field: Option<&str>`; `None` plus a bare term is a clean `ParseError::MissingField`, never a silent "search every field" guess), quoted `"phrase terms"` → `PhraseQuery`, `field:term~`/`field:term~N` (`N` in `0..=2`, matching `FuzzyQuery`'s own `LevenshteinAutomata.MAXIMUM_SUPPORTED_DISTANCE` range) → `FuzzyQuery`, `field:prefix*` (a single trailing unescaped `*` and no other wildcard char) → `PrefixQuery` vs. `field:c?t`/any other `*`/`?` combination → `WildcardQuery` (mirroring real `QueryParser`'s own `PrefixQuery`-vs-`WildcardQuery` split), `field:/pattern/` (Lucene's own regexp delimiter convention, `\/` escaping a literal slash without ending the pattern) → `RegexpQuery`, `(...)` grouping recursing to arbitrary depth (not just one level — "one grouping construct" was the scoping ask, not a depth cap), and a `^boost` suffix on any atom (term, phrase, group, wildcard, etc.) → `BoostQuery`. Escaping: inside a bareword, `\` followed by any byte inserts that byte literally, stripped from the resulting term — the same convention `wildcard.rs::WildcardPattern::new` already documents. **Deferred explicitly, rejected with a `ParseError` rather than silently mis-parsed**: `AND`/`OR`/`NOT` as real operators with precedence rules (not implemented at all), fractional-similarity fuzzy (`term~0.8`, the `StandardQueryParser` float-similarity convention — only a bare `~` or `~` plus a plain `0..=2` integer is accepted), and any escaping edge case beyond the single `\`-then-any-byte rule (e.g. `\uXXXX` Unicode escapes). **Verification**: no cross-engine Java fixture applies here (there's no "real Lucene bytes" for query-string *syntax* to decode — the correctness question is "does this string parse to the `Clause` a human would expect," per this task's own scoping note) — unit tests in `query_parser.rs` cover every supported grammar construct (single term, `field:term`, phrase, `+`/`-` combination, wildcard, prefix, fuzzy with/without explicit edit distance, regexp with/without escaping, one level and nested parenthesized groups, boost on a term and on a group) plus every malformed-input case (unclosed quote/paren/regexp, including after a trailing escape; unmatched close paren; missing field; invalid/non-finite boost; invalid fuzziness including out-of-range and fractional; unsupported range syntax; empty/whitespace-only input; empty group) confirming a clean `Err`, never a panic. `crates/lucene-search/tests/query_parser_fixtures.rs` is the honest substitute for a Java fixture: it parses queries against the real `IndexWriter`-produced `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` segment `term_query_fixtures.rs`/`boolean_query_fixtures.rs` already validate, executes them via `search_boolean_query`, and confirms the result matches the equivalent hand-built `Clause` (`TermQuery`/`PhraseQuery`/nested `BooleanQuery`) executed the same way — proving the parser's output is not just structurally right but genuinely executes to the same doc set as the already-differentially-verified query layer. **Deferred, same as every other `Clause`-producing task**: no `lucene-ffi` C-ABI entry point accepts a query string yet (the FFI wire format still only builds flat `Clause::Term` clauses); no query-string support for `Clause::DisjunctionMax`/`Clause::ConstantScore` (real classic `QueryParser` has no direct syntax for either anyway — both are Java-API-only constructs). **Analyzer wiring (task #62)**: closes the gap the `lucene-analysis` row above previously flagged (nothing wired an `Analyzer` into this module). `parse_query` is unchanged and now delegates to a new additive entry point, `parse_query_with_analyzer(input, default_field, analyzer: Option<&lucene_analysis::Analyzer>)` — `None` reproduces every prior literal-term behavior exactly (all 41 pre-existing tests pass unmodified, plus a new test directly comparing `parse_query`'s and `parse_query_with_analyzer(.., None)`'s output). When an analyzer is supplied it runs over a bareword's text before `Clause::Term`, and over each whitespace-separated word of a quoted phrase before `Clause::Phrase` (real `QueryParser` analyzes phrase text word-by-word, not as one blob), but **never** over wildcard/prefix/fuzzy/regexp pattern text — matching real classic `QueryParser`, which doesn't analyze pattern syntax either, since tokenizing/lowercasing/stopword-filtering a glob or regex would corrupt it (verified with pattern text containing uppercase letters and a stopword-shaped substring that must survive untouched). Zero/one/multi-token-per-word handling (a deliberately simplified subset of real `QueryParserBase.newFieldQuery`'s fuller handling, which can also build position-aware `SynonymQuery`s — out of scope): one token → `Clause::Term`/one phrase position; zero tokens (the word analyzed away entirely, e.g. it was itself a stopword) → an empty `BooleanQuery` (no `must`/`should`/`must_not`), already this crate's established "matches nothing" shape per `matched_boolean_docs`'s own doc comment, so no new `Clause` variant was needed; more than one token → `Clause::Phrase` in order (bareword) or spliced into the phrase's term list (one phrase word). No new Java fixture — this composes two already independently cross-engine-verified pieces (this row's own parser grammar, the `lucene-analysis` row's analyzer chain); the added zero/one/multi-token control flow is new Rust-level logic covered directly by unit tests, not new byte-format decoding. **Numeric range syntax (`PointRangeQuery`'s classic-`QueryParser` grammar)**: `field:[min TO max]` parses into a leaf `crate::query::Clause::PointsRange(PointsRangeQuery)` carrying `field`/`min: i64`/`max: i64`. **Exclusive bounds now supported (follow-on task)**: `[`/`]` and `{`/`}` are recognized independently per side, matching real classic `QueryParser` exactly — `[a TO b]` (inclusive/inclusive), `{a TO b}` (exclusive/exclusive), `[a TO b}` and `{a TO b]` (mixed) all parse; only a genuinely mismatched/missing closing delimiter (anything other than `]`/`}`) is a `ParseError::InvalidRangeBound`. Since `PointsRangeQuery` still only stores one inclusive `[min, max]` pair (no separate min/max-inclusive flags), an exclusive *literal* bound is converted to the equivalent inclusive one at parse time in `Parser::parse_range` — stepped one value toward the other bound (`min + 1`/`max - 1`, saturating so a bound already at `i64::MIN`/`i64::MAX` can't overflow) — mirroring real Lucene's own `QueryParserBase.getRangeQuery`, which performs the identical adjustment before constructing its inclusive-only `PointRangeQuery`. An exclusive `*` (open-ended) bound is left unstepped, since `*` denotes "no bound" rather than a literal value (`{* TO 100}` is still fully open on the low end). Either bound may be `*` (mapped to `i64::MIN`/`i64::MAX`, real Lucene's own open-end convention) or a plain, optionally-negative decimal integer; a non-numeric bound, a missing/misspelled `TO`, or a missing/mismatched closing delimiter is a `ParseError::InvalidRangeBound` rather than a panic or a silently-wrong clause. Only `i64` bounds are supported — string/date range queries (non-numeric bounds, real `TermRangeQuery`'s territory) are also `ParseError::InvalidRangeBound`, not a fallback to lexicographic comparison. **Execution now wired (task #199), resolving the previously-tracked gap.** `resolve_clause_docs`/`clause_scores` in `lucene-search/src/lib.rs` both gained a real `Clause::PointsRange` arm (a shared `points_range_doc_ids` helper): given an optional new `points: Option<&points_query::PointsInput<'_>>` parameter — threaded through `search_boolean_query`/`search_boolean_query_scored`/`search_disjunction_max_query`/`search_disjunction_max_query_scored` and every internal helper between them (`matched_boolean_docs`/`resolve_dismax_docs`/`dismax_scores`), the same "new optional input, `None` fine until a clause needs it" precedent `pos_in`/`pay_in` already established — a `Clause::PointsRange` looks its field's number up from `PointsInput::field_infos` (mirroring `lucene-ffi/src/points_query.rs::field_number_for`'s existing name-to-number lookup, one layer down from the C ABI), packs the query's `i64` `min`/`max` via a new `points_query::pack_i64` helper (real `LongPoint`'s sign-bit-flipped big-endian encoding, previously only hand-rolled in test code), and delegates straight to the already-existing, already-tested `points_query::search_points_range` — no BKD traversal or packed-value comparison logic duplicated. `points: None` for a query that turns out to contain a `Clause::PointsRange` is `Error::MissingPointsInput(field)` (renamed from `Error::UnexecutablePointsRange` now that the clause genuinely is executable given the right input — same "caller didn't open a resource this call needed" shape as `Error::MissingPosInput`); an unknown field name (present in `points.field_infos`? no) matches nothing, not an error, same convention every other clause follows. `explain::explain_clause` has no `PointsInput` parameter (out of scope for this task) and so always surfaces `Error::MissingPointsInput` for a `Clause::PointsRange` — an existing, documented limit, not a regression. Multi-segment federation (`multi_segment.rs`) passes `points: None` at its two `search_boolean_query_scored` call sites, unchanged — same "single already-opened segment" scope this whole module already documents. **End-to-end proof**: `lib.rs`'s `points_range_query_string_matches_real_segment_end_to_end` test parses a real query *string* (`"price:[20 TO 40]"`, `"price:{20 TO 40}"` exercising task #195's exclusive syntax, and an unknown-field case) via `query_parser::parse_query`, builds a real `PointsInput` from `lucene_codecs::points::write`/`open` plus a hand-built `FieldInfos`, and confirms `search_boolean_query`/`search_boolean_query_scored` return the correct doc set (and constant `1.0` score) against a small indexed fixture (docs valued 10/20/30/40/50) — the full query-string-to-results path, not just a direct `search_points_range` call. Covered by unit tests in `query_parser.rs` as before (all four inclusive/exclusive combinations, negative bounds, `*` on either/both ends, saturating exclusive bounds, bare default-field range, boosted range, every malformed shape), plus the pre-existing `exclusive_bound_excludes_boundary_value_end_to_end` test, plus `lib.rs`/`explain.rs` unit tests pinning the (renamed) `Error::MissingPointsInput` path for a `None` `points` input. **Swept in `b14-search-features` (m2): MISSING fixes.** (1) `AND`/`OR`/`NOT` (and the `&&`/`||`/`!` aliases) are now real operators, a port of `QueryParserBase.addClause`'s truth table including both of its retroactive rewrites of the *preceding* clause (an `AND` promotes it to `MUST` unless prohibited; an `OR` under `AND_OPERATOR` demotes it to `SHOULD`), plus Java's `clauses.size() == 1 && firstQuery != null` unwrapping. They are recognized only as whole uppercase bareword tokens, matching JavaCC's longest-match-then-first-rule tokenization (`ANDROID`/`NOTHING`/`and` stay terms). (2) `DefaultOperator` + `parse_query_with_operator` port `setDefaultOperator`; `parse_query`/`parse_query_with_analyzer` keep Java's `OR` default. (3) Phrase slop `"a b"~n` was a hard parse error; it now sets `PhraseQuery::slop` via `handleQuotedTerm`'s `(int) Float.parseFloat(...)` (a fractional slop truncates, a bare `~` leaves 0). (4) The boost and the `~` suffix now parse in **either** order, matching `[ <CARAT> boost [ fuzzySlop ] | fuzzySlop [ <CARAT> boost ] ]`, and a `~` after a wildcard/prefix bareword is ignored rather than applied, matching `handleBareTokenQuery`'s wildcard-before-fuzzy precedence. **`MultiFieldQueryParser` is ported by c12** -- see its own row below. Still out of scope: `TermRangeQuery`-shaped string/date ranges, and the `allowLeadingWildcard` guard (always allowed here). |

| `search/IndexSearcher` constructed with an `ExecutorService` (concurrent per-leaf search) | `lucene-search/src/multi_segment.rs::{merge_multi_segment_scored_concurrent, search_term_query_multi_segment_concurrent, search_boolean_query_multi_segment_concurrent}` | **ported (task #68), a `rayon`-based parallel sibling of the existing sequential fan-out/merge core — not a port of real `IndexSearcher`'s `Executor`/`LeafSlice` machinery itself.** Real Lucene's `IndexSearcher(IndexReader, Executor)` constructor searches each `LeafReaderContext` (or `LeafSlice` group of leaves) on the supplied `Executor`, then merges the partial `TopDocs` once every leaf finishes. This port has no thread-pool abstraction of its own, and `rayon` is already a workspace dependency used elsewhere in this crate, so `merge_multi_segment_scored_concurrent` uses `rayon::prelude::*`'s `par_iter` over segments in place of an `Executor`: each segment's `TopDocsCollector`, per-segment search call, and doc-base translation run independently inside the parallel closure (no shared mutable state between segments, so no locking needed), producing one `Vec<ScoreDoc>` per segment. Those per-segment vectors are then merged **sequentially**, through the identical `TopDocsCollector` type [`merge_multi_segment_scored`] (the pre-existing sequential core, unchanged by this task) already uses for its own merge step — the same merge logic, not a second implementation of it, and `rayon`'s `.collect()` preserving input order regardless of which worker thread computed which element is what makes the two paths' outputs provably identical rather than merely usually-the-same. `search_term_query_multi_segment_concurrent`/`search_boolean_query_multi_segment_concurrent` are thin wrappers mirroring the existing `search_term_query_multi_segment`/`search_boolean_query_multi_segment` exactly, differing only in which core they call. **Additive only**: the pre-existing sequential functions (`merge_multi_segment_scored`, `search_term_query_multi_segment`, `search_boolean_query_multi_segment`) and all of their tests are unchanged. **Verified**: sequential and concurrent results are asserted byte-for-byte identical (same doc IDs, same order, same scores — `assert_eq!`, not an epsilon compare) for the same input across an empty index (zero segments), a single segment, 16 synthetic segments (enough for rayon's global pool to plausibly run more than one in parallel), a `top_n`-truncation case, a case where every segment ties on score at the same local doc ID (exercising the cross-segment tie-break through the concurrent path too), and both real end-to-end query types (term, boolean) against the real `fixtures/data/blocktree_index/` fixture. **Explicitly deferred**: any thread-pool configuration/sizing knob (real Lucene's `Executor` lets a caller supply an arbitrarily sized/shaped pool; this always uses rayon's process-global pool, with no equivalent knob exposed), work-stealing tuning beyond what that global pool already provides out of the box, and any I/O-bound async concern — this is CPU-parallel per-segment scorer evaluation only. ~~No `lucene-ffi` C-ABI entry point exposes the concurrent path yet~~ — closed by the Concurrent segment search FFI exposure task: `lucene-ffi/src/directory_reader.rs::{ffi_search_term_query_multi_segment_concurrent, ffi_search_boolean_query_multi_segment_concurrent}` (see the `directory_reader.rs` row below for that FFI wiring's own details). **Deadline/timeout support (follow-up, additive only)**: `merge_multi_segment_scored_with_deadline`/`merge_multi_segment_scored_concurrent_with_deadline` (same file) are new sibling functions -- not modifications to the two functions above, which stay byte-for-byte unchanged and untouched by any existing caller -- taking an `Option<Instant>` deadline checked once per segment (sequential) or once per `rayon` closure, before that segment's own `per_segment_search` call. This port has no `TotalHits`/exact-vs-partial relation type, so expiry is signaled by a plain `timed_out: bool` rather than inventing new hit-count-relation API surface. Best-effort, not preemptive, matching real `TimeLimitingBulkScorer`'s own granularity: an expired deadline stops FURTHER segments from starting, it does not abort one already in flight. `deadline: None` degrades to byte-for-byte the same code path as the non-deadline siblings. **Not yet wired into any query-level entry point** (`search_term_query_multi_segment`/`search_boolean_query_multi_segment`/their FFI exposures all still call the non-deadline core) -- these are additive fan-out-loop primitives only, a caller must opt in explicitly; no `lucene-ffi` exposure yet either. |

| `search/IndexSearcher.explain(Query, int)` + `search/Explanation` | `lucene-search/src/explain.rs::{Explanation, explain_clause}` | **ported (task #59), introspection only — does not change any scoring behavior.** `Explanation { matched, value, description, details }` mirrors real `Explanation` exactly (`matched` stands in for `isMatch()`; `Explanation::match_(value, description)`/`.with_details(vec)` and `Explanation::no_match(description)` mirror the real `Explanation.match(...)`/`Explanation.noMatch(...)` factory split). `explain_clause(fields, doc_in, pos_in, pay_in, live_docs, clause, doc, norms)` recomputes each node's `value` via the *exact same* `similarity::idf`/`similarity::tf_norm` calls (and the same `term_doc_freqs`/`term_doc_positions`/`phrase_freq_exact`/`matched_boolean_docs`/`resolve_clause_docs` helpers) `search_term_query_scored`/`search_boolean_query_scored`/`search_phrase_query_scored`/`search_disjunction_max_query_scored` already call — its top-level `value` is bit-for-bit identical to those functions' own output for the same doc, verified directly (`assert_eq!`, not an epsilon compare) in this module's own unit tests rather than re-derived independently. **Detailed explanations** (weight → score → idf/tfNorm sub-trees, or the query's own composition formula): `Clause::Term` (idf with `docFreq`/`docCount` leaf details, tfNorm with `freq`/`k1`/`b`/`fieldLength`/`avgFieldLength` leaf details), `Clause::Phrase` (same shape, phrase idf = sum of each term's own idf, tfNorm keyed on phrase frequency), `Clause::Boolean` (sum of matching `must`/`should` sub-clause explanations, `no_match` when a `must` clause fails, `minimum_should_match` isn't met, or the query has no `must`/`should` clauses at all), `Clause::DisjunctionMax` (real `max + tie_breaker * sum(rest)` formula over matching disjuncts' own explanations), `Clause::ConstantScore` (matches iff the wrapped clause matches, always reports the constant, discarding the wrapped clause's own score), `Clause::Boost` (wrapped clause's own explanation value multiplied by `boost`). **Flat explanations** ("1.0 = matches, unscored constant score" or a clean `no_match`, no per-term breakdown): `Clause::Wildcard`/`Clause::Prefix`/`Clause::Fuzzy`/`Clause::Regexp`/`Clause::Span` — same "no single term's frequency/idf to score against" rationale each query type's own `query.rs` doc comment already gives for why they score a flat `1.0`. No new Java fixture generator needed (per the `differential-testing` skill's precedent for presentation-layer logic over already-differentially-verified scoring — there's no "real Lucene bytes" to check a description string against, only the numeric equality this task's tests assert directly against this crate's own already-verified ground truth). Coverage: `lucene-search/src/explain.rs` 97.82% lines (workspace total 97.28% lines, `cargo llvm-cov --fail-under-lines 95` passing). **Swept in `b14-search-features` (m2): CORRECTNESS fixes -- every `description` string is now real Lucene's verbatim**, because downstream tooling parses them. Previously divergent, now fixed: the term/phrase root (`weight(field:term in DOC) [BM25Similarity], result of:`, was `weight(field:term), result of:`), the score node (`score(freq=2.0), computed as boost * idf * tf from:`, was `computed as idf * tfNorm`, and the freq now renders Java-style with a decimal point), the idf node (`idf, computed as log(1 + (N - n + 0.5) / (n + 0.5)) from:` with `n, number of documents containing term`/`N, total number of documents with field`, was a `docCount`/`docFreq` spelling with the values inlined into the description), the tf node (`tf, computed as freq / (freq + k1 * (1 - b + b * dl / avgdl)) from:` with `dl, length of field`/`dl, length of field (approximate)` and `avgdl, average length of field`, was `tfNorm`/`fieldLength`/`avgFieldLength`), the phrase idf (`idf, sum of:` with per-term children in Java's own format, and a `phraseFreq=X` leaf), `BooleanWeight`'s `sum of:` (was `{value} = sum of:`) with its four distinct no-match descriptions and per-clause `no match on required clause (...)`/`no match on optional clause (...)`/`match on prohibited clause (...)` children, `DisjunctionMaxWeight`'s `max of:` vs `max plus {tie} times others of:` and `No matching clause`, `ConstantScoreWeight`'s `{query}`/`{query}^{score}`/`{query} doesn't match id {doc}`, and `TermWeight`/`PhraseWeight`'s bare `no matching term`/`no matching terms`/`no matching phrase`. Added `impl Display for Explanation` (a port of `Explanation.toString()`'s two-space-per-level indented `{value} = {description}` rendering) and an internal `describe_clause` mirroring each Java query class's `toString()`. Recorded, not fixed: `Clause::Boost` still explains as its own `product of:` node, where real `BoostQuery` folds the boost into the wrapped weight's `boost * idf * tf` (this port's `clause_scores` multiplies after the fact, so an explanation matching Java's shape would no longer match this port's own score). |

| `facet/FacetsConfig` (read side plus the `SortedSetDocValuesFacetField` half of `build`) | `lucene-search/src/facets.rs::{FacetsConfig, DimConfig, DrillDownTermsIndexing, build_sorted_set_facet_fields, BuiltFacetField, FacetBuildError, path_to_string, path_components_to_string, string_to_path, DEFAULT_INDEX_FIELD_NAME, DELIM_CHAR, ESCAPE_CHAR}` | **ported (c12 read side; c29 write side).** The per-dimension configuration Lucene deliberately does *not* store in the index (`hierarchical`, `multiValued`, `requireDimCount`, `drillDownTermsIndexing`, `indexFieldName`), plus the delimiter-joined / escape-escaped dim+path encoding (`U+001F` / `U+001E`) every SORTED_SET facet term uses. c29 added `build_sorted_set_facet_fields`, `FacetsConfig.build(Document)`'s `processSSDVFacetFields` + `indexDrillDownTerms`: hierarchical dims index every path prefix, a flat dim must have exactly one component beside the dim, a flat `multiValued` + `requireDimCount` dim also indexes the bare dimension, and the five `DrillDownTermsIndexing` modes select which `StringField` drill-down terms go beside the values. It returns the fields rather than a `Document`, so no `IndexWriter` dependency enters `lucene-search`. **Not ported: the taxonomy half** (`processFacetFields`, which needs a `TaxonomyWriter` and a taxonomy index) **and `AssociationFacetField`** — both are outside this port's SSDV-only faceting scope, not oversights. Verified against real Lucene via `fixtures/src/GenFacets.java` (`crates/lucene-search/tests/facets_fixtures.rs`), whose manifest now records the exact fields `FacetsConfig.build` emitted for eleven configurations.

| `facet/sortedset/{SortedSetDocValuesReaderState,DefaultSortedSetDocValuesReaderState}` (`OrdRange`, `DimTree`, `getDims`) | `lucene-search/src/facets.rs::{FacetsState, OrdRange, DimTree, DimTreeChildren, FacetsStateError, INVALID_ORDINAL}` | **ported (c12), both the flat and the hierarchical dim shape.** Built from one global ordinal space (a single segment's SORTED_SET terms, or an `OrdinalMap`-merged dictionary). `createOneFlatFacetDimState`'s rejection of a deeper-than-`dim/child` term under a flat dim is `FacetsStateError::NotHierarchical`, Java's `IllegalArgumentException`. `getDims()` yields hierarchical dims then flat ones, each in **ascending name** order rather than Java's unspecified `HashMap` order — a deliberate determinism divergence; `getAllDims` sorts its own output regardless. **Not ported: `ramBytesUsed`/`getChildResources`** (`Accountable`, no counterpart in this port) and the `OrdinalMap` reader cache (`cachedOrdMap`, keyed on `IndexReader.CacheKey`, which does not exist here).

| `facet/sortedset/{SortedSetDocValuesFacetCounts,AbstractSortedSetDocValueFacetCounts}` (`getTopChildren`/`getAllChildren`/`getSpecificValue`/`getAllDims`/`getTopDims`) | `lucene-search/src/facets.rs::{SortedSetFacetCounts, FacetResult, merge_segment_counts}` | **ported (c12), completing what b14 recorded as "genuinely one layer up".** `adjustPathCountIfNecessary`'s three cases are reproduced exactly, including Java's `-1` for a multi-valued dim without `requireDimCount`; `prepareChildIteration` walks an `OrdRange` for a flat dim and a `DimTree` for a hierarchical one, skipping the dim's own ordinal for a multi-valued+`requireDimCount` dim. `merge_segment_counts` is `countOneSegment`'s `counts[(int) ordMap.get(ord)] += count` remap. `getTopDims` is implemented as the base `Facets.getTopDims` contract (`getAllDims` truncated), not `AbstractSortedSetDocValueFacetCounts`' optimizing override, which exists to avoid per-dim work this port's single-pass `all_dims` does not do. Every result is pinned against real `SortedSetDocValuesFacetCounts` output over a three-segment index (`fixtures/src/GenFacets.java`).

| `index/OrdinalMap` | `lucene-search/src/ordinal_map.rs::OrdinalMap` | **ported (c12).** Merge-sorts every segment's terms into one global ordinal space and records `segmentOrd -> globalOrd` per segment — the thing whose absence made cross-segment faceting *unavailable* (b14 §3.7). **Divergences, all deliberate:** the map is a plain `Vec<i64>` per segment rather than `PackedLongValues` deltas over `globalOrdDeltas`/`firstSegments` (same mapping, no `acceptableOverheadRatio`); Java's weight-based segment reordering is not ported because it is invisible in `segmentToGlobalOrds` and only exists to help the packed representation, so `getFirstSegmentNumber` reports the lowest *input* segment index; there is no `IndexReader.CacheKey` owner. **Home:** Java's is in `lucene/core/index`; here it sits beside its only consumer (faceting) in `lucene-search`, a one-file move if doc-values merging ever needs it. Pinned against real Lucene's own `MultiDocValues.getSortedSetValues(...).mapping` table. **Measured (c29), then closed (c38)**: the materialized input, not the map, dominated — 5 segments x 1 M 17-byte terms cost 267 MB for the term lists against 51 MB for the map (319 MB peak). `OrdinalMap::build_streaming` now takes `&mut [&mut dyn TermCursor]`, exactly as Java's `build` takes `TermsEnum[]`, and holds one reused term buffer per segment plus the map: **318.2 MB peak -> 51.4 MB** on that shape, and 290 ms -> 140 ms end to end (terms produced + built), measured one arm per process with `examples/ordinal_map_memory.rs`. `OrdinalMap::build` still takes materialized lists for a caller that has them, and runs the same merge over a slice-backed cursor rather than a second algorithm. The cursor is `lucene_codecs::terms_dict::TermsCursor`.

| `facet/range/RangeFacetCounts` multi-valued counting + `totCount` | `lucene-search/src/facets.rs::{multi_valued_range_facet_counts, multi_valued_double_range_facet_counts, range_facet_counts_with_total, double_range_facet_counts_with_total, RangeCounts}` | **ported (c12), closing b14 §3.8.** A document with several values inside one range is counted **once** for that range (Java's `startMultiValuedDoc`/`addMultiValued`/`endMultiValuedDoc`), and `totCount` counts it once overall no matter how many ranges it hit — a number a caller cannot derive from the per-range counts once ranges overlap, and which the older `range_facet_counts` signature made the caller invent. Java's consecutive-duplicate skip (`j == 0 || val != previous`) is reproduced. Verified against `LongRangeFacetCounts`/`DoubleRangeFacetCounts` over both a single-valued NUMERIC field and a genuinely multi-valued SORTED_NUMERIC one.

| `search/uhighlight/{FieldOffsetStrategy,PostingsOffsetStrategy,TermVectorOffsetStrategy,AnalysisOffsetStrategy,TokenStreamOffsetStrategy,NoOpOffsetStrategy}` + `UnifiedHighlighter.getOffsetSource`/`getOptimizedOffsetSource` | `lucene-search/src/highlighter.rs::{OffsetSource, offset_source_for_field, optimize_offset_source, offsets_from_postings, offsets_from_analysis, phrase_match_offsets, offsets_from_phrase}` (term-vector source: `term_vectors_query::matched_term_offsets`) | **ported (c12), closing b14 §4.6's offset-strategy half.** The source is chosen from the field's own `FieldInfo` in Java's order and then adjusted for the query (`Postings` + a multi-term part downgrades to `Analysis`; `PostingsWithTermVectors` without one upgrades to `Postings`; nothing to highlight is `NoneNeeded`). All three producers emit `TermOffsetSpan`s in `OffsetsEnum.compareTo` order, so they are interchangeable inputs to `assemble_fragments`. `PhraseHelper`'s offset half is ported (c29) as `phrase_match_offsets`/`offsets_from_phrase`: only the occurrences inside an actual phrase match are returned, collected per term and deduplicated exactly as `OffsetSpanCollector`/`SpanCollectedOffsetsEnum.add` do; its query-walking half (`WeightedSpanTermExtractor`, deciding *which* sub-queries are position-sensitive) is the caller's job here. **Match enumeration is `SpanNearQuery`'s, not the scorer's (c40)**: `WeightedSpanTermExtractor.extract` rewrites a `PhraseQuery` as `SpanNearQuery(clauses, slop + positionGaps, inOrder = slop == 0)`, so `slop == 0` is `NearSpansOrdered` (the in-order greedy walk c29 already had) and `slop > 0` is `NearSpansUnordered` (`lucene-search/src/near_spans.rs`), whose budget `maxEndPosition - top().startPosition() - totalSpanLength` is a *different quantity* from `SloppyPhraseMatcher`'s slot-shifted `matchLength` -- they differ by two positions on a transposition, and `SpanNearQuery` has no `rptGroups`, so two slots may share one occurrence. The consequences are Lucene's own: a reordered pair is highlighted at slop 1 where the scorer needs slop 2, and `"alpha alpha"~2` is highlighted in a document with a single `alpha` that the query does not match. Until c40 this port enumerated in order at every slop, so a reordered occurrence was scored and never highlighted. Verified against real Lucene's own `PhraseHelper.createOffsetsEnumsForSpans` over thirteen cases (`fixtures/src/AppendHighlightManifest.java`'s `highlight.*` keys, `crates/lucene-search/tests/highlighter_offsets_fixtures.rs::phrase_helper_offsets_match_real_lucene`), and against `IndexSearcher`'s own per-slop match results in `GenBlockTree.java`'s `field.pos.sloppyGap.realLuceneSlopResults` for the in-order case where the two agree. **Not ported: `MemoryIndexOffsetStrategy` (needs a `MemoryIndex`), `MultiFieldsOffsetStrategy`, and `getSummaryPassagesNoHighlight`. `offsets_from_postings` verified against real Lucene's own per-occurrence offsets (`GenBlockTree.java`'s `field.pos.term.*.occurrences`). **Offset unit (c29):** everything in this module is measured in UTF-16 code units — Java `char`s, which is what `OffsetAttribute` and `Passage.getStartOffset()` are — not Unicode scalars, which is what this port used until c29 and which differs for every supplementary-plane character. `offsets_from_analysis` converts nothing: as of c33 `lucene_analysis::Token` reports the same Java `char`s, so there is one unit end to end (the boundary conversion c29 added came out with the producer's fix). Pinned against real Lucene's `StandardAnalyzer` offsets over non-ASCII text in `fixtures/src/GenBreakIterator.java` (`crates/lucene-search/tests/highlighter_utf16_offsets_fixtures.rs`).

| `search/uhighlight/SplittingBreakIterator` + `BreakIterator.getSentenceInstance` | `lucene-search/src/highlighter.rs::{sentence_boundaries, split_sentence_boundaries}` | **ported (c12), replacing a heuristic that diverged from Java.** Sentence boundaries are UAX #29's, via the `unicode-segmentation` crate — the same specification the JDK's `BreakIterator.getSentenceInstance` implements. Pinned against the JDK's *actual* output by `fixtures/src/GenBreakIterator.java` (eleven texts, both `Locale.ROOT` and `Locale.ENGLISH`, compared as sliced substrings so UTF-16-vs-UTF-8 offsets never enter into it), so a JDK or CLDR data bump surfaces as a failing check rather than as passages silently drifting from Lucene's. Until c12 this module carried a hand-rolled terminator scan plus a hardcoded English abbreviation list ("Mr", "Dr", "St", ...) suppressing breaks; running the JDK shows it applies **no** abbreviation suppression in `Locale.ROOT` or `Locale.ENGLISH`, so that list was a divergence and is gone. **One tailoring is applied, toward Java**: a boundary starting a whitespace-only run is dropped, because the JDK folds a trailing paragraph-separator run into the preceding sentence where pure UAX #29 (rule SB4) makes it its own sentence. Six JDK outputs are pinned as tests. **Not ported:** locale tailorings (`getSentenceInstance(Locale)`), `WholeBreakIterator`, `CustomSeparatorBreakIterator`, `LengthGoalBreakIterator`.

| `queryparser/classic/MultiFieldQueryParser` | `lucene-search/src/query_parser.rs::{parse_multi_field_query, parse_multi_field_query_with_boosts}` | **ported (c12), closing b14 §7.7.** A bare (unqualified) atom fans out across the configured fields as a `SHOULD` disjunction (`getMultiFieldQuery`), with an optional per-field `BoostQuery`; an atom that names its own field is untouched. The disjunction sits at the **leaf**, under the outer query's conjunctions and modifiers, so `cat dog` with `AND_OPERATOR` is Java's documented `+(title:cat body:cat) +(title:dog body:dog)`. Implemented by re-parsing each bare atom's own span once per field, which is faithful here because this parser takes a single `Analyzer` for all fields (Java calls `super.getFieldQuery(fields[i], ...)` per field precisely so a *per-field* analyzer can differ, then zips the i-th sub-clause across fields via its `maxTerms` loop; with one analyzer that zip degenerates to "expand each leaf"). **Divergence, algebraically equivalent:** a `^n` written in the query text lands inside each expansion here and outside the disjunction in Java — identical scores, since a `SHOULD` `BooleanQuery` sums and `sum(b*s_i) == b*sum(s_i)`. **Not ported: per-field analyzers.**

| `search/FieldExistsQuery` | `lucene-search/src/doc_value_query.rs::{DocValuesField, doc_values_field, doc_has_value, search_field_exists, FieldExistsSource, field_exists_source, search_field_exists_norms, search_field_exists_vectors, field_exists_leaf_is_complete}` | **ported (c12 doc-values source; c29 the rest).** `field_exists_source` is Java's three-way `FieldInfo` switch in its own order (norms, then vectors, then doc values), with the "indexes neither" case an error as Java's `IllegalStateException` is; `search_field_exists_norms` iterates a `FieldNormsCursor` (one forward `IndexedDISI` walk for a sparse field), `search_field_exists_vectors` iterates the field's *ordinals* and maps each to its doc, which visits `size()` documents rather than `maxDoc`; `search_field_exists` is the five-way doc-values switch plus `ConstantScoreScorerSupplier.fromIterator`. A field with no doc-values entry is Java's `null` iterator: no matches, not an error. **There is no points *source*** — 10.5.0's `scorerSupplier` never touches `PointValues`; points appear only as a docCount proxy in `rewrite`/`count`, which is `field_exists_leaf_is_complete` (the per-leaf half of the `MatchAllDocsQuery` shortcut; the whole-reader decision is the caller's). c12 recorded a points iterator as a deferred source; re-reading 10.5.0 shows that source does not exist. **`count` and the whole-reader `rewrite` landed in c37-search-behaviours**, in `lucene-search/src/weight_count.rs`: `count_field_exists_leaf` is `ConstantScoreWeight.count`'s full ladder (`fieldInfo == null` -> 0; the norms branch's `docCount == maxDoc ? numDocs : -1`; then vectors/skipper/points/terms for a raw count, reconciled as `0 -> 0`, `== maxDoc -> numDocs`, `>= 0 && !hasDeletions -> count`, else `-1`), and `field_exists_rewrites_to_match_all_docs` is `rewrite`'s per-leaf loop -- including Java's asymmetry of reading `getDocCount(field)`/`maxDoc()` off the **top-level** reader for the norms branch while the other two read the leaf's. `SegmentReader::field_exists_leaf` gathers the counts (`vector_size` is a parameter, not a lookup: this port's vector values are opened by the caller, not held by the reader). Ground truth is `AppendCountManifest.java`'s `count.fieldexists.*`/`rewrite.fieldexists.*` over five committed indexes -- `norms_index` for both the complete (rewrites to `*:*`) and partial norms cases, `doc_values_index` for the arm where nothing gives `count` a doc count at all, `doc_values_skip_index` for `DocValuesSkipper.docCount()`, and `blocktree_index` for a field on 4 of 8959 documents. Norms and vector sources verified against `fixtures/data/norms_index` (a sparse field with `NONE` documents) and `fixtures/data/vectors_index` (a 1334-of-4000 sparse vector field).

| `search/IndexOrDocValuesQuery` | `lucene-search/src/doc_value_query.rs::{IndexOrDocValuesPlan, plan_index_or_doc_values}` | **the planner ported (c12); the cost estimate it consumes does not exist here.** `cost() >>> 3 <= leadCost ? index : doc values` is reproduced verbatim, including Java's own justification for the 8x doc-values penalty. Java's `cost()` for a point range query is `PointValues.estimateDocCount(visitor)`, a BKD tree walk this port has not ported (b14 §1.4 recorded it as absent for want of a consumer; this is the consumer). **`index_cost: None` plans `Index`** — not a guess but Java's own answer whenever the query is the lead iterator (`leadCost == cost()` makes the test true for every non-negative cost) and unconditionally what `bulkScorer()` does. Choosing doc values without an estimate would be the guess. **c29** ported `PointValues.estimateDocCount`'s arithmetic as `points_query::estimate_doc_count` (the all-docs / single-valued / multi-valued-urn three-branch formula, floored at 1) and pinned what changes with a real estimate: nothing while this query leads, and `DocValues` exactly when another clause leads with fewer than `cost/8` documents. `estimatePointCount`'s BKD walk itself is still absent — it needs each node's subtree `size()` mid-traversal, which `lucene-codecs/src/points.rs` does not expose.

| `index/PostingsEnum` flags on the unscored search paths (`PostingsEnum.NONE`) | `lucene-search/src/lib.rs::{term_docs_only, try_conjunction_lazy}`, `stream_constant_score_clause` | **wired (c12), from c8's `PostingsFlags`/`for_util::pfor_skip`.** Every matching-only path now asks for doc ids without frequencies, so the `.doc` file's frequency blocks are skipped (`PForUtil.skip`) rather than unpacked: `term_doc_ids`, `prefix_doc_ids`, `wildcard_doc_ids`, `fuzzy_doc_ids`, `regexp_doc_ids`, `term_in_set_doc_ids`, `multi_phrase_slot_docs`, the constant-score streaming union, and every `Occur::FILTER` leg of a lazy conjunction. Scored paths are unchanged. The contract is enforced structurally rather than by convention: `term_docs_only` returns a bare `Vec<i32>` (the `1`-filled `freqs` array `DocsOnly` produces is never in scope), the streaming union's cursors are wrapped in a local newtype exposing only `next_doc`, and a conjunction leg's scoring inputs live inside a `LegRole::Scoring` variant so the only way to reach the `freq()` call is to have matched a leg that decoded frequencies.
